# Thornswood working notes

On 15 October 2026 the name of another SkyMP server, the one Thornswood-Content.esp was salvaged from, was taken out of this file (issue #400; Patrick: "no records may keep it"). Where it stood in prose it now says "the other server", and file, folder and code names that carried it say OtherServer. git history holds the original wording.

On 28 September 2026 the old multiplayer mod's name was taken out of this file (issue #497). Where it stood in prose it now says "the old mod", and file, folder and code names that carried it say OldMod (OldTradeMod, OldMorphMod and OldMessagingPlugin for its add-ons). Where its initials named one of our own files, the file is described instead. git history holds the original wording.

One block per entry in the launch list. Written so somebody who was not here, or a fresh agent, can pick up any single entry cold. Generated 17 September 2026 from a 17-agent audit that fact-checked every entry against the repository.

## 1. The dev game server is driving the LIVE voice service right now

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Substance verified: the dev gamemode does talk to the live voice service. The line list is slightly off. A literal 7778 is at tools\gamemode.js lines 148, 657, 694, 1878 and 2810 (five sites, not six). Line 3337 is not a literal: it reads `port: VOICE_PORT` from the `var VOICE_PORT = 7778;` declared at 2810 in the same IIFE, and 1895, 1908, 2029 and 2817 use the constant too. tools\Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1:82 is `& scp @ssh $Gamemode "$User@${Vps}:$Root/gamemode.js"`, verbatim, so the file does ship unchanged. "nothing points at it" is wrong: tools\app\DevBuild.cs:27 is `public const string DevVoicePort = "7789";` and tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1:12 and :24 create the dev voice on 7789. It is the dev gamemode alone that never points at it, and grep confirms 7789 appears nowhere in gamemode.js. The claim that the live queues can be drained is supported by the code: GET /commands at gamemode.js:3333-3345 and GET /choices at 657 both shift items off the voice service's arrays (voice-server.js:386, 410), so a read by the dev server removes them for the live one. The live claim that both services answer was not re-tested here (it needs the VPS). Note version.json already carries a `voicePort` key holding 7778, so a settings key for this exists on the launcher side already.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo says "fall back to 7778 on anything falsy or on a throw"; doneWhen says "`grep -n 7778 tools/gamemode.js` returns nothing".  ->  The two cannot both be satisfied. A resolver with 7778 as its default puts the literal 7778 into the file, so the grep can never come back empty. The test as written fails the moment the fix is done correctly, and passing it means writing a resolver with no fallback, which is the opposite of what the howTo asks for.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "GET /commands at gamemode.js:3333-3345 and GET /choices at 657 both shift items off the voice service's arrays (voice-server.js:386, 410), so a read by the dev server removes them for the live one."  ->  /choices does not remove anything. tools\voice\voice-server.js:386-391 is the whole route: a loopback check, then `res.end(JSON.stringify(choices))`. No splice, no shift, no delete. Removal happens only in POST /claimed at :394-406, which the gamemode calls from tools\gamemode.js:694 (claimChoice). Only /commands drains, at voice-server.js:412, and by `commands.splice(0, commands.length)` rather than shift. The harm to a live person's character choice is real, but it comes from the dev gamemode POSTing /claimed, not from it reading /choices, and the correction stamps the wrong mechanism as "supported by the code". Anyone fixing this will go looking at line 386 for a mutation that is not there.

**Read first:** 1) tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1 lines 1-25, which is the only place the whole live and dev port map is written down in one block (live game UDP 7777 with resources on TCP 3000, dev game 7787 with resources 7788, live voice 7778, dev voice 7789). 2) tools\gamemode.js lines 140-160, 650-700, 1870-1900, 2805-2820 and 3330-3345, because the port is copied into three separate IIFEs and each has its own declaration. 3) tools\Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 end to end; it is short and shows nothing rewrites the file in flight, which is why the dev copy carries live values.

**How:** In tools\gamemode.js add one resolver beside the other small helpers (after the log block, near line 90): read server-settings.json with fs.readFileSync, take cfg.voicePort, coerce with Number, fall back to 7778 on anything falsy or on a throw. Then replace the literal at 148, 657 and 694 and the two `var VOICE_PORT = 7778;` declarations at 1878 and 2810 with a call to it. On the VPS side add `"voicePort": 7789` to C:\Thornswood-Server-Dev\server-settings.json, ideally by having tools\Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 write it the same way tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1:50-55 already rewrites port, name and loadOrder for the dev root. Patrick runs anything that reaches the VPS.

**Done when:** `grep -n 7778 tools/gamemode.js` returns nothing and `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. Then prove the resolver without a server: in a scratch directory write a server-settings.json holding {"voicePort":7789}, run node against a copy of the resolver, and confirm it returns 7789, then delete the file and confirm it returns 7778. The live confirmation, for Patrick, is that the dev server's thornswood.log carries the line gamemode.js:2029 prints, "voice position feed running, pushing to 127.0.0.1:7789", naming 7789.

**Traps:** gamemode.js is loaded by skymp5-server through requireTemp(), which copies it to a temp directory and requires it there; relative require() breaks, which is why everything lives in one file with all data inlined (header, lines 5-10). Do not split the resolver into a module. The working directory is the server's own folder, stated at gamemode.js:64-65, which is why three other places already read server-settings.json by bare relative name. The file is hot reloaded and mp.clear() runs first, so do not cache the port in a cross-reload global. The voice service is a separate process on the live box: tools\Deploy-Server.ps1:195-200 carries a long comment about why stopping the game server must never take 7778 down, and that comment records a real incident. Finally, gamemode.js ships through Deploy-Gamemode, not through a launcher pack, so this still falls under the CLAUDE.md rule that launcher, game files and server go out as one release.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/http.html , https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html#fsreadfilesyncpath-options

## 2. The game server's data folder is served to the whole internet, and the site's bot key is in it

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The design rule and the file layout are verified; two line numbers are off by one or two. The rule is stated at tools\gamemode.js lines 51-52, verbatim: "Beside server.log in the server's own folder, deliberately NOT under data. The resources server hands out anything under data over plain HTTP to anybody who asks". That is line 51, not "sixty lines from the top", close enough. allowed.json is at gamemode.js:2307 and door.log at 2308 (the entry cites 2306-2308; 2306 is the closing catch). door.json is read at 2406, verified exactly. The api\servers manifest publisher is at 2536-2538, so the cite of 2538 lands on `var DST` rather than `var DIR`. characters.json is at gamemode.js:2067, which the entry does not cite but which is the file that matters most: it holds names and locations. The 32 character bot key is verified at tools\Install-Bot.ps1:238, `$botKey = NewKey 32`. The live "returns 200 anonymously" test needs the VPS and was not repeated here, but the 15 September backup zip confirms the folder layout: game/data/characters.json (3,219 bytes), game/data/manifest.json, game/data/online.json and game/data/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json are all there.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "and then tools\Set-Door.ps1 to rewrite door.json on both the live and dev roots."  ->  Set-Door.ps1 cannot target the dev root. Its param block at tools\Set-Door.ps1:29-38 has $Vps, $User, $Site, $DocsRoot and the switches, and no server root. It finds the root itself at :59-67 by listing C:\ on the VPS and taking `Select-Object -First 1` of whatever holds server-settings.json, which is C:\Thornswood-Server. There is no way to point it at C:\Thornswood-Server-Dev. Two further gaps: the switch that writes door.json is -Connect, which the howTo never names, and rotating the bot key on live alone leaves the dev door holding the old key (tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1:37 copies door.json across once), so the dev door stops refreshing with nothing to say so.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: move door.json, allowed.json, door.log and characters.json to a sibling private folder, with nothing said about what else reads them.  ->  characters.json is hardcoded as `data\characters.json` in three more places that the move would break without a word on screen: tools\Wipe-Saves.ps1:63, :122 and :150, tools\Clean-Everything.ps1:259, and tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1:45. The worst of those is Wipe-Saves, which would report a clean wipe while the real character register survives untouched in the private folder. Note also that gamemode.js declares `var dataDir` three separate times, in three independent IIFEs (2061, 2301, 2530), so "introduce a second constant beside dataDir" is three edits, not one.

**Read first:** 1) tools\gamemode.js lines 45-60, the comment that already states the rule this entry is enforcing, so you argue from the file's own words. 2) tools\gamemode.js lines 2295-2320 and 2400-2420, which show every consumer reads its path from `dataDir` out of server-settings.json, so moving the private files is a settings change plus one new path constant, not a rewrite. 3) tools\Set-Door.ps1 lines 90-100 and 150-160, the script that writes door.json on the VPS and would need the new path.

**How:** Rotating first, because it is the part that cannot wait: Patrick runs tools\Install-Bot.ps1 with -RotateKey, which mints a new 32 character key, and then tools\Set-Door.ps1 to rewrite door.json on both the live and dev roots. Then in tools\gamemode.js introduce a second constant beside `dataDir`, for example `privDir`, defaulting to 'private' and overridable from server-settings.json, and point FILE (2307), LOG (2308), the door.json read (2406) and the characters roster path (2067) at it. Leave manifest.json, online.json and the api\servers tree under dataDir, because the client fetches those over the resources port. On the VPS the files move with a plain move once the new server is in place. Do not try to firewall port 3000: the client needs it.

**Done when:** Without the VPS: `grep -n "dataDir" tools/gamemode.js` shows only manifest, online and api paths still under dataDir, and `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. With the VPS, for Patrick: `curl -si http://<host>:3000/door.json` returns 404, and `curl -si http://<host>:3000/online.json` still returns 200. tools\Check-Manifest.ps1 is read-only and should still pass, since the manifest stays where it was.

**Traps:** dataDir is read from server-settings.json in three separate IIFEs (2300-2305, 2530-2534 and near 2060); change one and miss another and the door reads a file the roster no longer writes. Moving characters.json changes where the character register lives, which touches entry 3 and entry 7 at the same time, so agree the new path in #dev first. The resources port is how the SkyMP client pulls plugin files; anything moved out of the served folder that the client needs will break sessions silently. Rotating the bot key breaks the site's door refresh until both door.json copies are rewritten, so do the site, the live root and the dev root in one sitting. CLAUDE.md requires a line in #dev before touching anything both machines edit, and this touches gamemode.js, which both do.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/path.html#pathjoinpaths , https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits

## 3. One four digit string is a dead join password, the live character register secret, and in public git history

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Most of it is verified, one instruction is already done. server-secret.txt is exactly 4 bytes and all digits (checked by length and isdigit, value never read out). tools\Install-Voice.ps1:79-121 is the block, and its comment does still say the value "is the game server's own password, out of a private repo, so it is not public", which is the wrong reason the entry calls out. tools\app\Config.cs:72-76 is the dead ServerPassword field with its old-mod comment, verified exactly, and it is still protected and unprotected by DPAPI at Config.cs:246 and :265. tools\docs-app\server.js:1292 is `password: cfg.serverPassword || ''` inside the ticket reply, verified exactly. The published Thornswood-Releases history at 4ac2786..6dbc8e2 could not be checked: those commits are not in this clone and that is a different repository. What can be said from here is that the local version.json no longer has a serverPassword key at all, and tools\Publish-Update.ps1:918-921 actively removes one on every publish and writes the value to server-secret.txt instead. So "delete the dead field from version.json" is already done at the publisher; what is left is the launcher field, the site's join reply, and the fact that the value in use on the VPS is the one that was published.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** doneWhen: "`grep -rn \"ServerPassword\\|serverPassword\" tools/` returns only tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 (or nothing...)".  ->  That grep currently also hits tools\Publish-Update.ps1:918-920, which is the code that strips serverPassword out of version.json on every publish. Its comment at 910-917 records why: "Any copy already sitting in version.json is taken out here, so the next publish removes it from the public repo rather than leaving it there forever." Passing this test means deleting the fix for exactly the leak entry 3 is about. It also hits tools\Characters.ps1:66 and tools\Install-Voice.ps1:109 (the documented old-checkout fallback), tools\Add-SkyrimUnbound.ps1:212-213, and tools\app\Config.cs:389, none of which the write-up mentions.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "Write it to server-secret.txt, which is gitignored. Patrick then runs tools\Install-Voice.ps1 to push it and restart the..."  ->  server-secret.txt is not a file you own. tools\Publish-Update.ps1:918-928 rewrites it from config.json's ServerPassword on every publish ($cfg is config.json, read at :845). Write 32 random bytes into it, push with Install-Voice, then run Ship before config.json is dealt with, and the file is back to the old four digits and the next Install-Voice pushes the old secret again. The howTo never mentions config.json. Confirmed separately that the old value is the live one: the 15 Sep backup's voice/secret.txt is byte-identical to the local server-secret.txt (compared by bytes, never read out); both are 4 bytes, all digits.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 900-931, which is where this was half fixed already and explains in its own comment why the value moved out of version.json. 2) tools\Install-Voice.ps1 lines 79-121, the comment that has to be rewritten and the scp that puts secret.txt on the VPS. 3) tools\app\Config.cs lines 68-80 and 240-280, so you can see ServerPassword is DPAPI-protected on disk and removing it needs a migration path for older config.json files.

**How:** Generate 32 random bytes and hex or base64 them (PowerShell: `[Convert]::ToBase64String((New-Object byte[] 32 | ForEach-Object { $_ }))` is wrong; use `$b=New-Object byte[] 32; [Security.Cryptography.RandomNumberGenerator]::Create().GetBytes($b); [Convert]::ToBase64String($b)`). Write it to server-secret.txt, which is gitignored. Patrick then runs tools\Install-Voice.ps1 to push it and restart the voice task. Rename it in the code for what it is: it guards the character register, not a join. Delete `[DataMember] public string ServerPassword` at Config.cs:76 along with the three places it is handled at 205, 246, 262-277, and drop `password: cfg.serverPassword` from docs-app\server.js:1292 plus the cfg default at server.js:79 and the startup line at 1488. Rewrite the Install-Voice comment to say it is a purpose-made secret held only on the VPS and on the shipping machine.

**Done when:** `grep -rn "ServerPassword\|serverPassword" tools/` returns only tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 (or nothing, if that script is retired too), and the launcher still compiles: run tools\Check-Build.ps1, which is read-only, or let the GitHub build workflow do it. Confirm the new secret is not four digits with `(Get-Content server-secret.txt -Raw).Trim().Length` and never print the value. Confirm version.json still has no serverPassword key with a jq or ConvertFrom-Json key listing.

**Traps:** Never paste the value anywhere, including a commit message or a Discord line; name the file instead. Config.cs:246 unprotects and :265 re-protects with DPAPI, so deleting the field without touching the migration will throw on an older config.json that still holds a protected string; handle a missing member rather than assuming. Rotating breaks the character register until Install-Voice has run against the VPS, so the launcher's character page will fail for anybody mid-session. The launcher is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. The published value is in git history on a public repository, so rotating is the fix; deleting the old commits is not, and should not be attempted without agreement in #dev.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html#cryptorandombytessize-callback , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.cryptography.randomnumbergenerator

## 4. The voice port accepts any WebSocket: a stranger is believed about who they are, hears everyone, posts as anyone, and learns every person's home address

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every cite checks out. tools\voice\voice-server.js:462-475 is the upgrade handler: it takes sec-websocket-key, writes the 101 response and constructs a Sock, with no origin check, no token, no allow list. :180-189 is onJoin, where `const profileId = Number(msg.profileId) || 0;` takes identity straight from the message. :192-206 is the 200ms broadcast that sends every peer the full snapshot of everyone's position, angle, cell and name. :495-534 is the chat branch, which ends `for (const [, p] of peers) p.ws.send(out);`, so a connected socket both reads and writes as anybody. STUN with no TURN is at tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:654 and tools\launcher\voice-host.html:134, both exactly `new RTCPeerConnection({ iceServers: [{ urls: 'stun:stun.l.google.com:19302' }] })`, verified line for line. The file is 545 lines total. Whether the port is open on all interfaces is a VPS fact and was not re-tested here.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** Marked [verified] with a fix written against tools\voice\voice-server.js line numbers ("make onJoin at :180 verify the t..."), with no note that this file is not what is running.  ->  tools\Install-Voice.ps1:77 is `& scp (Join-Path $src 'voice-server.js') "${target}:.../voice-server.js"`, which copies the repo file straight over the deployed one. The deployed build is 26,612 bytes and 723 lines; the repo copy is 20,011 bytes and 545 lines. Entry 4 is ordered before entry 7, so following it in order writes a token check into the repo copy and deploys it, deleting /whoami, /claim, /owners and the owners.json register. Entry 4 never says entry 7 has to be settled first.

**Challenged (minor):** "STUN with no TURN is at tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:654 and tools\launcher\voice-host.html:134, both exactly ... verified line for line."  ->  Both cites are right, but there is a third copy the correction missed: tools\voice\public\voice.js:112, the same `new RTCPeerConnection({ iceServers: [{ urls: 'stun:stun.l.google.com:19302' }] })`. A TURN change that touches only the two named files leaves the voice service's own page on STUN alone.

**Read first:** 1) tools\voice\voice-server.js end to end; it is 545 lines and the whole of the problem is in it. 2) tools\app\Creation.cs lines 665-700, because the same service takes a Discord ticket on /choose over plain HTTP and that is entry 17, which has to be designed with this one. 3) tools\Install-Voice.ps1 lines 190-215, which registers the scheduled task and shows how the service is hosted today, since putting it behind Caddy changes how it is started and which interface it binds.

**How:** Mint a short-lived token where identity is already known. The site already signs a Discord cookie (docs-app\server.js around 415-425 and 1280-1295), so add a route there that returns a token binding profileId plus an expiry plus an HMAC over both, using a key held in the site config and shipped to the voice service the way secret.txt already is. In voice-server.js, make onJoin at :180 verify the token and take profileId from the token payload, never from msg, and drop the socket when it does not verify. Bind the listener to 127.0.0.1 at :541 and put `reverse_proxy /voice/* 127.0.0.1:7778` (with the dev equivalent) in the Caddyfile, then change the two client URLs to wss://. Add a TURN server, or accept that pairs behind symmetric NAT stay silent, which the code already admits.

**Done when:** Without the game: run `node tools/voice/voice-server.js` locally on a spare port and drive it with a tiny ws client script. A join with no token must be closed; a join with a token whose profileId differs from the signed one must be closed; a valid token must get a welcome. `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` must pass. On the VPS, for Patrick, `curl -si http://<host>:7778/health` should fail to connect once the bind is loopback, while `curl -si https://<host>/voice/health` answers.

**Traps:** voice-server.js on the VPS is not the file in this repo: the deployed build answers /health with owners-4 and holds an account register, and the 15 September backup contains voice/owners.json and voice/secret.txt that no route in the repo copy ever writes. Entry 7 is that gap, and it has to be closed before this change, or you will overwrite a deployed feature nobody has read. This service also carries the launcher's character choices and the chat command queue the lockpick fix rides on (comment at voice-server.js:500-508 and the /commands route at :410), so a restart during a session drops those. Position culling is entry 55 and the relay is entry 56; do not fold them in here. The whole thing has to ship as one release with the two client files, because a client asking for wss:// against a service still on ws:// simply fails silent.

**Links:** https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/reverse_proxy , https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6455 , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/RTCPeerConnection/RTCPeerConnection

## 5. The door is switched off on the live server, so a ban does nothing

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both cites land exactly. tools\gamemode.js:2326 is `list = { enforce: doc.enforce === true, ids: ids, count: Object.keys(ids).length };`, so enforce defaults to false for any value that is not literally true and for a missing key. tools\gamemode.js:2385 is `mp.kick(userId);`, reached only after the `if (!conf.enforce)` branch at 2369-2375 logs "WOULD TURN AWAY" and continues. The file states the watch-only choice in its own words at 2279-2284: it starts in WATCH mode because it was written without a way to test it against a live server. tools\app\Door.cs exists (9,772 bytes) and is the launcher-side Discord check, with the offline grace at :140-170. The live claim that allowed.json currently reports enforce false with 20 profiles is a VPS fact and was not re-checked here; allowed.json is not in the repo and is not in either backup zip.

**Challenged (minor):** "reached only after the `if (!conf.enforce)` branch at 2369-2375".  ->  gamemode.js:2369 is blank. The branch is 2370-2375. The two cites that matter are exact: 2326 is the enforce read and 2385 is `mp.kick(userId);`. Same class of slip in entry 2 ("2306 is the closing catch": 2305 is the catch, 2306 is blank) and entry 6 ("_NO prefix at 341-342, _YES prefix at 343-344": they are 342-343 and 344-345). Harmless one at a time, but the correction presents these as hand-verified traces.

**Read first:** 1) tools\gamemode.js lines 2274-2400, the whole door, including the comment that says what enforce true means and why it is off. 2) tools\app\Door.cs, so you understand that today's check happens in the launcher and can simply be skipped by not using the launcher. 3) tools\Set-Door.ps1, the read-write script that manages allowed.json and door.json on the VPS, since flipping enforce is done there and not by editing the server.

**How:** The interim is one field: Patrick runs tools\Set-Door.ps1 against the live root to write enforce true, having first read door.log to confirm nobody legitimate is on the WOULD TURN AWAY list. The real change is that the server refuses the session rather than kicking an actor already in the world: hook the earliest event where a connection is known (the same place entry 15's token check goes) and refuse there, then have the refresh at gamemode.js:2403 sever any live session whose profile has just dropped off the list, rather than waiting for the next sweep. Create the Banned role first, which is entry 67, because docs-app\server.js:75 expects a role by that name and Build-Discord.py never makes one.

**Done when:** Read-only and checkable now: `grep -n "enforce" tools/gamemode.js` shows the read at 2326 and the branch at 2369, and after the change the kick path is reachable without the enforce guard. For the live state, Patrick reads C:\Thornswood-Server\data\door.log through tools\Get-Logs.ps1 and confirms lines change from "WOULD TURN AWAY" to "turned away". A test account removed from the allow list must fail to enter, not enter and then be removed.

**Traps:** A ban here is keyed on profileId, and gamemode.js:2917-2918 says in its own words that the profile id "is a claim and not a fact". Turning enforce on without entry 15 gives a door that a stranger walks around by editing one number in a settings file, so say that plainly rather than reporting this as closed. The door refresh throws the whole allow list away on one failed lookup (entry 57), so enforcing before that is fixed risks a Discord hiccup locking everyone out at once; that is exactly the outage the comment at 2280-2283 was written to avoid. tools\app\Door.cs is on the do-not-touch-without-a-line-in-#dev list in CLAUDE.md. And this is a coordinated release: the server decides, the launcher explains, so both go out together.

**Links:** https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits

## 6. The age gate passes a sixteen year old who answers in a sentence

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Correct in every particular, with one small cite adjustment: said_yes is defined at tools\discord\bot.py:328 and returns at 352; lines 323-325 are the _NO and _YES tuples, so the cited 324-352 covers the lists plus the function. The order is exactly as described: empty at 340, _NO prefix at 341-342, _YES prefix at 343-344, and only then the digit scan at 346-351. Traced by hand: "16" reaches the digit scan and returns False, so it is flagged; "I'm 16" matches "i'm " at 343 and returns True; "im 16" matches "im "; "yeah 14" matches "yeah". The call site is tools\discord\bot.py:447, `if not said_yes(gave):`, verified exactly, and the only consequence is prefixing the ticket with CHECK THIS ONE at 448-450. Nothing auto-rejects and nothing auto-admits, so the entry's framing is right.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** doneWhen: `python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/discord'); import bot; print([(s, bot.said_yes(s)) for s in [...]])"`  ->  That cannot run. tools\discord\bot.py has no `if __name__ == '__main__':` guard: line 851 is `try:` and the next line is `client.run(TOKEN)` at column zero, so importing the module starts the bot. It also imports discord at module level. On Patrick's machine it either dies on the missing discord package or reaches client.run with an empty DISCORD_TOKEN and hits the LoginFailure branch, `raise SystemExit(1)`, before the print. The function has to be tested by copying said_yes out, or by adding a main guard first.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo: "`if any(n < 18 for n in nums): return False`", with no note of what it costs.  ->  Any number under 18 anywhere in the sentence turns the answer into a flag. "yes, I'm 21, turning 22 on the 3rd" and "I'm 20, born 5/12/2005" both come back False. The cost is only a CHECK THIS ONE prefix, so it is not dangerous, but it inverts what the file says it is for, at tools\discord\bot.py:332-337: "Generous on purpose... A guess about how a stranger types is not a good enough reason to throw an application away." A change that reverses a written design decision should say so. Also `if n.isdigit()` in the comprehension is dead: re.findall(r'\d+') only ever yields digits.

**Read first:** 1) tools\discord\bot.py lines 316-352, the comment above AGE_Q and the function, because the comment states the design intent (it is a record, not a gate) that any change must not break. 2) tools\discord\bot.py lines 435-460, the one call site, so you see the failure mode is a missing warning line and not a rejection. 3) tools\discord\content.py, which holds the channel copy, in case the question text changes alongside.

**How:** In tools\discord\bot.py, move the digit scan above the prefix tests and make any number under 18 return False. Concretely, inside said_yes after the empty check, run `nums = [int(n) for n in re.findall(r'\d+', t) if n.isdigit()]`, then `if any(n < 18 for n in nums): return False`, then `if any(n >= 18 for n in nums): return True`, and only then fall through to the existing _NO and _YES prefix tests. Keep the docstring honest about the new order. Guard the int() against absurd lengths, since re.findall will happily return a 40 digit run.

**Done when:** Add a plain unit test or run it inline: `python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/discord'); import bot; print([(s, bot.said_yes(s)) for s in ['16','I\\'m 16','im 16','yeah 14','27','im 34','yes','no','','under 18','I am 18']])"`. Every string containing a number below 18 must print False, and 27, 'im 34', 18 and a bare 'yes' must print True. If importing bot.py pulls in discord.py and fails, copy said_yes and its two tuples into a scratch file and test it there.

**Traps:** Importing tools\discord\bot.py outside the VPS may need discord.py installed; test the function in isolation rather than installing things. Do not turn this into an auto-reject: the comment at lines 316-321 records the deliberate decision that a guess about how a stranger types is not a good enough reason to throw an application away, and changing that is a design call for Patrick and Djinn, not a patch. Numbers that are not ages will now flag: "I have 2 characters in mind" would be caught, which is a false positive on a warning line and is the safe direction, but say so when you hand it over. The bot runs on the VPS under tools\Install-Bot.ps1; a person deploys it, never a Claude.

**Links:** https://docs.python.org/3/library/re.html#re.findall

## 7. The voice service that is running is not the one in the repository, so the code that accepts a character claim cannot be read

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified from this machine without touching the VPS, and more strongly than the entry claims. tools\voice\voice-server.js has exactly six routes: /choose (342), /choices (386), /claimed (394), /commands (410), /health (444) and a static file handler. There is no /owners route, no account register and no reference to a secret anywhere in the file (grep for secret, owners and register returns only a comment at line 34). Independent corroboration that the deployed build differs: the 15 September backup zip at C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Backups\thornswood-backup-20260915-0956.zip contains voice/owners.json and voice/secret.txt pulled off the VPS, and nothing in the repo copy of voice-server.js ever reads or writes either. tools\Install-Voice.ps1:79-121 does scp a secret.txt to the VPS, which explains that file, but owners.json has no writer in the repo at all. The health line reporting build owners-4 was not re-fetched here.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** "Patrick runs the fetch: scp <vpsuser>@<host>:C:/Thornswood-Voice/voice-server.js ..." and the LIST row's "nobody can review it". The correction says the owners-4 health line "was not re-fetched here".  ->  The deployed file is already on this machine. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Backups\thornswood-backup-20260915-0956.zip contains voice/voice-server.js at 26,612 bytes against the repo's 20,011 (tools\voice\voice-server.js). The correction opened that zip (it cites voice/owners.json and voice/secret.txt from it) and missed the file sitting beside them. Extracted and read: 723 lines, `const BUILD = 'owners-4';` at line 42, POST /whoami at 507, POST /claim at 529, POST /owners at 584, the owners.json register at 83-113, SECRET read from secret.txt at 93-97. The owners-4 build string could have been confirmed from disk without the VPS, and the review the entry says is impossible can start today.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** "Commit it or redeploy the repository copy" is offered as two equal options, and the correction adds nothing against redeploying.  ->  Redeploying the repo copy breaks shipping code in this repo. tools\app\Account.cs:66 posts /whoami and :100 posts /claim; tools\Characters.ps1:88 posts /owners. None of those three routes exist in tools\voice\voice-server.js. Redeploy silently removes the character-ownership register the launcher and the admin script call.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "tools\voice\voice-server.js has exactly six routes: /choose (342), /choices (386), /claimed (394), /commands (410), /health (444) and a static file handler."  ->  Seven routes plus the static handler. It misses POST /positions at :300 and POST /say at :420. /say is the route tools\gamemode.js:148 posts to, which entry 1 is partly about, and /positions is the broadcast feed entries 4 and 55 rest on. The repo states the correct list in its own words at tools\app\Shell.cs:3727-3728: "its routes are /positions, /choose, /choices, /claimed, /commands, /say, /health and the page".

**Challenged (minor):** "grep for secret, owners and register returns only a comment at line 34".  ->  A case-insensitive grep for secret, owners or register in tools\voice\voice-server.js returns nothing at all. Line 34 is inside a comment block about who "owns" a profile and contains none of the three words. The conclusion (the repo copy has no register and no secret) is right; the check quoted as evidence for it did not happen.

**Read first:** 1) tools\voice\voice-server.js, all 545 lines, so you know exactly what the repo thinks the service is. 2) tools\Install-Voice.ps1, the whole script, which is how the file gets to the VPS and where a commit stamp and a dirty-tree refusal would go. 3) tools\Get-Logs.ps1, the existing read-only way to pull files off the VPS, so you do not invent a second one.

**How:** Patrick runs the fetch: `scp <vpsuser>@<host>:C:/Thornswood-Voice/voice-server.js C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\voice-server.live.js` and the same for C:/Thornswood-Voice-Dev. Diff both against tools\voice\voice-server.js. If the deployed build is ahead, commit it on a branch (`git checkout -b patrick/voice-service-as-deployed`) and open a pull request so it can be read; if it is a dead end, redeploy the repo copy through Install-Voice. Then in Install-Voice.ps1, before the scp at line 77, capture `git rev-parse --short HEAD` and `git status --porcelain` from the repo, refuse to deploy when the tree is dirty or the file is untracked, and write the commit into a build field the /health route returns.

**Done when:** `git diff --no-index tools/voice/voice-server.js C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\voice-server.live.js` prints nothing. After the stamping change, `curl -s https://<host>/voice/health` (or the raw port, until entry 4 moves it) returns a build field whose value matches `git rev-parse --short HEAD` on the shipping machine. Until the diff is empty, treat every other voice finding, entries 4, 17, 55, 56, 58, 64 and 65, as written against code nobody has read.

**Traps:** This is the blocking dependency for the whole voice half of the list, so do it before any of them. Redeploying the repo copy over a newer deployed build would delete a working feature (the character register the launcher's Creation.cs:670-700 depends on) and break character claiming for everybody; read before you write. secret.txt and owners.json on the VPS hold live data, so do not overwrite them when installing. Never paste the contents of secret.txt anywhere, name the path. Install-Voice.ps1 talks to the VPS, so a person runs it, not a Claude. Adding a build field to /health changes the service's public surface, which under CLAUDE.md means a line in #dev before the code.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-parse , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html#httpcreateserveroptions-requestlistener

## 8. The installer payload is publicly downloadable and redistributes about forty named third-party mods, including Bethesda's own assets

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The size is right and the risk is real, but the two specific claims are not supported by the built payload. C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip is 527,227,436 bytes, so 527 MB is verified. Its payload/ tree holds 1,151 entries, of which 1,149 are under Data. There are zero .esp, .esm or .esl files in it and exactly one .bsa (Data/MarketplaceTextures.bsa), so "about forty named third-party mods" does not describe this build: the plugins are not in it, because tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:260-306 leaves out everything Vortex recorded as deployed, and Die's at 272 rather than shipping a build with an empty collection record. "Including Bethesda's own assets" is also not supported: the exclusion list at 132-138 names Skyrim.esm, Update.esm, Dawnguard.esm, HearthFires.esm, Dragonborn.esm, _ResourcePack.esl and .bsa plus the patterns 'Skyrim - *.bsa' and cc*, and no Bethesda esm or bsa is in the zip. What IS in it and does need a lawyer's eye: 392 MB under Data/Platform (SkyrimPlatform plus CEF and libnode, which is the GPLv3 dependency from entry 10), 369 MB of loose textures and 178 MB of loose meshes from named third-party mods including LostArk, 'LoA Reborn Paladin Legendary', leifcrops, pulcharmsolis and weapons/rpcustom, and, separately alarming, folders named the other server and kzlcustommeshs, which are another server's content that Ship-Rules is supposed to keep out. The anonymous 200 on the download route needs the VPS and was not re-tested.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "Its payload/ tree holds 1,151 entries, of which 1,149 are under Data", presented as the account of what the installer redistributes.  ->  The payload counts are right (I opened the zip: 1,151 under payload/, 1,149 under payload/Data/, zero .esp/.esm/.esl, one .bsa at payload/Data/MarketplaceTextures.bsa, Platform 393 MB, textures 369 MB, meshes 178 MB, the other server and kzlcustommeshs present). But the zip holds 1,168 entries, and the 17 outside payload/ are the ones the write-up needed: profile/Plugins.txt, profile/loadorder.txt, and three other servers' load-order backups shipped to customers, profile/Plugins.txt.otherserver-20260910-130852.bak, profile/loadorder.txt.mereth-20260910-104610.bak and profile/Plugins.txt.mereth-20260910-104610.bak, plus vanilla.json, collection.txt, applied.txt, config.json and manifest.json. So the installer does carry a load order, and it carries two other servers' as well. That belongs in entry 8 and in the load-order entries.

**Challenged (minor):** "tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:260-306 leaves out everything Vortex recorded as deployed, and Die's at 272 rather than shipping a build with an empty collection record."  ->  The Die is at line 273, and it only guards one of the two packaging paths. It sits inside `if ($null -ne $ownCollection)` at 271, the own-game-folder branch. The Vortex/Steam branch at 281 onward does not die: at 302-305 it prints "no Vortex record found, so this installer carries every mod file." and carries on. CLAUDE.md says the own-game-folder shape is not on main yet and what ships is the Steam copy with Vortex, so the safety net the correction leans on is branch-specific and the write-up does not say which branch it belongs to.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 lines 130-310, which is the whole decision about what ships, in order: Bethesda files, other people's frameworks, runtime folders, optional packs, then the Vortex record. 2) tools\Ship-Rules.ps1, the shared filter that the installer and the packs both use, and the place a rule about other servers' folders belongs. 3) C:\Thornswood\Profile\OwnGame\files.txt (30,746 lines), the launcher's own record of what the collection provides, since it is what Test-FromCollection consults.

**How:** First re-run the measurement so the finding is about today's build, not September's: `python -c "import zipfile,collections;z=zipfile.ZipFile(r'C:\\Thornswood\\_dist\\Thornswood-Setup.zip');print(collections.Counter(i.filename.split('/')[2] if i.filename.count('/')>2 else i.filename for i in z.infolist()).most_common(40))"`. Then in tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 add the other-server folder names (the other server, kzlcustommeshs, DaedricData is already handled at Package-Thornswood.ps1:254) to Test-ShipMachine so both the installer and the packs drop them. For the remaining third-party loose assets, the correct fix is that they belong in the Nexus collection, not the payload: move each one into the collection and let Test-FromCollection drop it, rather than adding names to a deny list. Put the download behind the same Discord door the rest of the site uses (docs-app\server.js:60-74 holds the access lists).

**Done when:** Rebuild is Patrick's call, but the check is read-only: after a rebuild, the same python one-liner must show no folder named OtherServer or kzlcustommeshs, and the mod-asset folder count under payload/Data/meshes and payload/Data/textures must be smaller than it was. `grep -n "OtherServer" tools/Ship-Rules.ps1` must find the new rule. For the door, `curl -si https://<host>/<download route>` without a session cookie must not return 200 with the zip.

**Traps:** Do not add a deny list of mod names to Package-Thornswood: the file's own comment at 258-262 says the Vortex record, not a guess at which folders count as mods, is what decides, and a hand list will drift. Package-Thornswood already refuses rather than shipping when the OwnGame record is empty (line 272), so a build that suddenly grows is the signal that the record went stale, not that the filter broke. This is tangled with the settled design in CLAUDE.md: what ships today is the Steam copy with Vortex, and the own-game-folder branch changes which record is authoritative, so do not mix the two shapes in one release. Data/Platform is skyrim-platform, which is GPLv3 and is entry 10, so those two go out together or the notices file is wrong twice.

**Links:** https://help.nexusmods.com/article/18-do-i-need-permission-to-use-a-mod-in-my-video-or-mod-pack , https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-3.0.html

## 9. README.md still hands out a 10.7 GB Google Drive copy of the entire mod list

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is right; one number in it is not. README.md is 97 lines, its title is "# the old mod", and line 8 is the Google Drive folder link, verified. But the README itself describes the zip as "about 9 GB", not 10.7 GB; 10.7 GB may be the current size of the Drive folder, which cannot be checked from here. Lines 1-40 is the right range for the download section. The two supporting files exist: tools\Message to friends.txt and tools\READ ME FIRST.txt. Two further contradictions in the same 97 lines worth folding in: line 63 describes an F2 party menu, and line 92 says to screenshot an error and send it to a name that CLAUDE.md and this project's memory both correct. This overlaps entry 86, which covers the whole README rewrite; entry 9 is only the link, and doing 9 first is right because it is the redistribution.

**Read first:** 1) README.md, all 97 lines, so you see how much of it is the old product and not just the link. 2) tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md, the current and correct install story, which is what any replacement text should point at. 3) CLAUDE.md, the Words section, because the replacement has to avoid em dashes and the word this project does not use for the people in the world.

**How:** Delete line 8 of README.md and the table beneath it that names OldMod-Setup.exe and .zip. Do it on a branch: `git checkout -b patrick/readme-drop-the-drive-link`, edit, `git commit -m "Take the Drive copy of the mod list off the front page"`, push, `gh pr create --fill`. Patrick runs the push and the pull request. Keep the change to the link alone so it is one fix per pull request as CLAUDE.md requires; the full rewrite is entry 86. Separately, Patrick should unshare or delete the Drive folder, because deleting the link does not un-publish the files.

**Done when:** `grep -rn "drive.google" .` from the repo root returns nothing. `git log --oneline -1` shows the commit on a branch and not on main. The Drive folder itself, checked by Patrick in a signed-out browser, should no longer be readable; that is the half that actually stops the redistribution.

**Traps:** The repository is private, but this README is also what any collaborator and any future public mirror sees, and the file is the first thing a new second developer opens. Removing the link does not remove the files: the sharing setting on the Drive folder is the real control and it is outside this repo. Do not fold entry 86's whole rewrite into this pull request; CLAUDE.md is explicit that a branch changing eight things is eight reviews nobody has time for. Do not push to main: main is what ships and only Patrick's machine ships it, through Ship.ps1.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-readmes

## 10. The modified GPLv3 SkyMP client is shipped with no source offer, and the copy being shipped is another server's build

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The repo-side facts check out and the upstream licence position is confirmed as of today. tools\Patch-Client.ps1 is 280 lines and its header at 1-45 states plainly that the bundle is not built here, that it is another server's build (the other server's until 16 September, Daedric Online's since), and lists the three patches with the exact text each expects. That means the diff the entry says exists really does exist, in that file. On the upstream side: the proposal to relicense skymp5-server and skymp5-client from GPL to MIT is pull request 2720 on skyrim-multiplayer/skymp and it is still open and paused, because a contributor objected that GPL code from another project is in the tree. So the GPL obligation stands today and will not quietly go away. The repo's own TERMS.md summarises it as "disclose the source code of your forks".

**Read first:** 1) tools\Patch-Client.ps1 lines 1-60, which is the patch set and also the record of what went wrong when the bundle was edited by hand. 2) CLAUDE.md, the settled-design bullet that says skymp5-client.js is not built here and the copy in the game folder is patched and shipped as is, because that constrains every possible answer. 3) tools\Ship.ps1 around line 299-330, where Patch-Client is run before every publish, so you know when the patched bytes are produced.

**How:** Publish the patch set rather than the whole bundle, which is the honest form here because the bundle is not built in this repo. Create a small public repository or a folder in Thornswood-Releases holding tools\Patch-Client.ps1 plus a plain statement naming the upstream project and, as precisely as can be determined, the build the bundle came from. Add the written offer in two places: a NOTICE or THIRD-PARTY file in this repo (entry 87 is the same file) and a paragraph on the site, generated from tools\docs-app\data\site\credits.md. Put a line in the installer's READ ME FIRST.txt pointing at it. Section 6 of GPLv3 is the part to read for what a valid offer must say and how long it must stand.

**Done when:** A stranger can reach, from the download page and from inside the installed folder, a page that names skymp5-client, skyrim-platform and skymp5-server, states their licences, and either links the corresponding source or carries an offer with an address. Check it the way a stranger would: open the site's credits page in a signed-out browser and follow the link. In the repo, `ls NOTICE THIRD-PARTY LICENSE 2>/dev/null` currently returns nothing, and should return the notices file afterwards.

**Traps:** Do not try to rebuild the client to dodge this: CLAUDE.md settles that the bundle is patched and shipped as is, and changing that is a #dev conversation between Patrick and Djinn, not a code change. Do not hand-edit skymp5-client.js; the Patch-Client header records that hand edits on 14 September were silently overwritten on 16 September when another launcher wrote a fresh build into the shared folder, and nobody noticed until doors unlocked again. Naming the exact upstream commit may be impossible, since the bundle came from another server's build; say so rather than guessing at a commit hash, because a wrong hash in a licence notice is worse than an honest "derived from an unknown build of". This pairs with entry 87 (the notices file) and entry 8 (what else is in the payload), and the three read best as one pass.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/TERMS.md , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/pull/2720 , https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-3.0.html , https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-faq.en.html

## 11. 164 of the 192 update packs point at a repository that is now private

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Directionally right, but the numbers describe two different files and the entry mixes them. The version.json in this clone is 59,496 bytes and holds exactly 164 pack entries, and all 164 point at https://api.github.com/repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/releases/assets/..., so zero of them are on the public host. There is no 192 in this file and no Thornswood-Releases URL in it at all. The published feed on Thornswood-Releases may well hold 192, since this local copy is dated 15 September and the last release was 1.1.47 today, but that cannot be checked from here. Note the direct contradiction with entry 59, which attributes 192 pack entries to a 59,496 byte file; only one of those can be true of the same file. The applied.txt seeding claim is verified: C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip carries applied.txt at its root with 187 pack ids in it (Package-Thornswood.ps1:462-490 writes it), so a fresh install replays nothing.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 890-930, where version.json is rewritten each publish and where the pack list is appended. 2) tools\app\Updater.cs lines 141-205 and 365-400, which is the launcher end: how the feed is fetched and the AllowedHosts check that governs which hosts a pack may come from. 3) tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 lines 455-500, the applied.txt seeding that is the only reason this has not bitten yet.

**How:** First establish the truth: Patrick fetches the live feed with `curl -s https://raw.githubusercontent.com/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood-Releases/main/version.json | python -c "import json,sys,collections,re;p=json.load(sys.stdin)['packs'];print(len(p));print(collections.Counter(re.search(r'/repos/([^/]+/[^/]+)/',e['url']).group(1) for e in p if 'url' in e))"`. Then choose one of two fixes. Rewriting: map each old asset id to its public equivalent, which needs the assets to exist on Thornswood-Releases and is only worth it if they do. Truncating, which is smaller: drop every entry older than the baseline the current installer carries, since applied.txt already tells the launcher those are done, and keep the tail. Either way, add the check to Publish-Update so a pack url whose host is not the public repo cannot be appended.

**Done when:** The same curl and python one-liner against the published feed reports one repository, and it is the public one. A stronger check, read-only: delete applied.txt from a scratch copy of a game folder and run the launcher's update logic against the feed in a dry mode if one exists, or simply confirm with `curl -sI <each url>` that a sample of ten pack urls returns 200 without a token. Do not actually delete applied.txt from C:\Thornswood\Profile.

**Traps:** Package-Thornswood seeding applied.txt is what is hiding this, so any change that stops seeding it (a rebuild with a different flag, a repair path) exposes 85 percent of the feed at once. The AllowedHosts list in Updater.cs:375-382 already permits both api.github.com and github.com, so a rewritten url will not be refused by the launcher, but a private-repo asset url needs a token the launcher does not have. The manifest-per-release entry further down the list removes this problem permanently, so do not build something elaborate here that will be thrown away. Publish-Update.ps1 is on the CLAUDE.md list of files needing a line in #dev first. And the launcher half is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/assets , https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_release_create

## 12. Nothing backs up the world, and the only two backups are from the same sixteen minutes

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding stands and the measurement is right, with one correction to a sub-claim. Both backups exist at C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Backups: 20260915-0940.zip (194,982 bytes, 46 entries) and 20260915-0956.zip (195,265 bytes, 47 entries), sixteen minutes apart, so "195 KB, 47 entries" is verified for the later one. There is nothing under a world folder in either: the only path matching world is site/data/site/world.md, a markdown page. tools\Backup-Server.ps1:132-139 grabs server-settings.json, gamemode.js and the whole data folder for each server root it finds, and never the world folder, verified. Lines 245-247 are the warning that the zip holds the bot token, the Discord client secret and the server password, verified. But the report's own prose that neither backup "contains a single character" is wrong: game/data/characters.json is in both, 3,219 bytes, and so is voice/owners.json. What is genuinely missing is the SkyMP world database at C:\Thornswood-Server\world, which is where anything a person built or carries actually lives. Say it that way or somebody will open a backup, find characters.json, and conclude the finding was overstated.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Backup-Server.ps1 lines 100-150 and 230-250, the grab list and the warning about what the zip holds. 2) tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1 lines 70-95, which shows the world folder is set aside and restored on install, so the server side already treats it as the precious thing. 3) tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1 lines 27-47, which lists exactly what is and is not copied when a second instance is made, and is the clearest statement in the repo of which folders hold state.

**How:** Add the world folder to the Grab list in tools\Backup-Server.ps1 next to the data grab at line 136, guarded by a Test-Path. Then make it run without a person: register a daily scheduled task on the VPS that runs the same collection locally, encrypts the result, and pushes it off the box to storage that is not the VPS. Keep seven daily and four weekly, which is a retention loop of about fifteen lines. Rehearse one restore onto a throwaway box using tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1, which already restores a world folder it finds set aside, and write down what you actually typed.

**Done when:** A fresh zip, listed with `python -c "import zipfile;z=zipfile.ZipFile(r'<path>');print(len(z.namelist()));print([n for n in z.namelist() if n.startswith('game/world')][:5])"`, shows world entries and is much larger than 195 KB. The restore rehearsal is done when a throwaway server starts, the load order resolves (Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:128-138 refuses otherwise and prints which plugins are missing), and a character from the backup is present. Off-box is done when the newest copy can be fetched from a machine that is not the VPS.

**Traps:** The zip holds the bot token, the Discord client secret and the register secret, which is why the script's own warning at 245-247 exists. Encrypt it and keep it off shared drives and out of the repo, and never paste a path plus a password in the same message. The world folder is live while the server runs, so a copy taken without stopping may be torn; take it from a stopped window or accept that and test the restore. Backup-Server.ps1 uses StrictHostKeyChecking=no like the other twenty-one scripts, which is entry 22, so do not add a twenty-third copy of that pattern here. Everything in this script reaches the VPS, so a person runs it, never a Claude.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/register-scheduledtask , https://restic.readthedocs.io/en/stable/

## 13. One malformed request kills the site, and the game's login dies with it

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The crash is verified exactly; the restart description is not. tools\docs-app\server.js:740-742 is `http.createServer(async (req, res) => {`, `const url = new URL(req.url, 'http://x');`, `const p = decodeURIComponent(url.pathname);`, so a path like /% throws a URIError inside an async handler, which becomes an unhandled promise rejection. Grep confirms there is no `process.on('unhandledRejection')` and no `process.on('uncaughtException')` anywhere in the 1,510 line file, so on a modern Node the process exits. Where the entry is wrong: tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:267-269 sets `-RestartCount 3 -RestartInterval (New-TimeSpan -Minutes 1)`, which is three restarts one minute apart, not three an hour. Worse, and not in the entry: those settings are applied only in the branch that creates the task; the else branch at 274-275 calls Set-ScheduledTask with -Action only, so an existing task keeps whatever settings it already had. And -ExecutionTimeLimit is never set, so the site task inherits the Task Scheduler default of three days and is killed on a timer exactly like Caddy in entry 21.

**Read first:** 1) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 736-760, the top of the request handler, so you see everything after the decode runs inside one async function with no try. 2) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 1470-1510, the listen callback and the 404 fall-through, which is where the process-level handlers belong. 3) tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1 lines 260-280, the scheduled task registration, because the restart behaviour is there and not in the node code.

**How:** Wrap the decode: replace line 742 with a try that falls back to answering 400 and returning, for example `let p; try { p = decodeURIComponent(url.pathname); } catch (e) { return send(res, 400, 'bad request', 'text/plain'); }` using the send helper already in the file. Then wrap the whole handler body in a try/catch that answers 500 with the site's own error page. Add `process.on('unhandledRejection', ...)` and `process.on('uncaughtException', ...)` near the listen call at 1477 that log and carry on rather than exit. Separately, in tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1, set -ExecutionTimeLimit ([TimeSpan]::Zero) and a higher RestartCount the way tools\Install-Voice.ps1:208 already does, and make the else branch pass -Settings too so an existing task is actually updated.

**Done when:** Run the site locally: `node tools/docs-app/server.js` in a scratch copy with a minimal config, then `curl -si http://127.0.0.1:3001/%` must return 400 and the process must still be alive for the next `curl -si http://127.0.0.1:3001/`. `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` must pass. For the task settings, after a deploy Patrick can read them back with `Get-ScheduledTask 'Thornswood Docs' | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Settings`.

**Traps:** uncaughtException is explicitly described in the Node docs as a crude last resort that leaves the process in an undefined state; use it to log and to keep the site answering, not as an excuse to skip the per-request try. The site is a scheduled task running as SYSTEM with no log anywhere, which is entry 25, so today a crash leaves nothing to read and you will not know whether your fix worked unless you add the log at the same time. The else branch in Deploy-Docs means task settings changes do not reach an existing task; the safe path is to unregister and re-register, which Patrick must do because it reaches the VPS. The launcher's login flow depends on this site being up, so a fix here is user-visible and belongs in one complete release with whatever else is going out.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/process.html , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/decodeURIComponent , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/new-scheduledtasksettingsset

## 14. Nothing is code signed: SmartScreen warns forever, and one GitHub login is all that stands between a stranger and every installed machine

**Category:** Packaging  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Nothing is signed anywhere: a grep for signtool, Set-AuthenticodeSignature, Authenticode and codesign across tools\*.ps1, tools\app\*.cs and tools\setup\*.cs finds exactly one hit, a comment at tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:658 about the shape of an Authenticode blob. tools\Build-Launcher.ps1:222-330 is the compile section and it ends with csc and no signing step, verified. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:79-90 is the comment that spells out the reputation problem in its own words, verified word for word: a new binary every release is a new hash every release, so SmartScreen never accumulates reputation and warns forever. tools\app\Updater.cs:375-382 is the AllowedHosts array and 369-374 its comment (the entry cites 369-381, which spans both), and 405-418 is the Download function refusing an update with no checksum. Verified. Nothing in SelfUpdate checks a signer, which is the gap the entry names.

**Read first:** 1) tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs lines 75-95, which already states the reputation argument better than any summary would. 2) tools\app\Updater.cs lines 365-430 and 815-873, the two halves that would host a signer check: the host and checksum layer that exists, and the swap that has no verification at all. 3) tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 218-300, the compile, to see where a signing step slots in and that the exe is produced by the .NET Framework csc, not MSBuild.

**How:** Start the identity validation today, because it is the long pole: it is a manual review, Microsoft has renamed the product to Azure Artifact Signing, and an individual applicant has to submit a government photo ID and a selfie. Nothing else about signing can begin until it clears. While it is pending: add a signing step at the end of tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 that is a no-op when no certificate is configured, so the script does not break on Djinn's machine, and do the same for tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 where the installer is produced. Then in tools\app\Updater.cs SelfUpdate, before File.Copy at line 821, read the downloaded file's signer with X509Certificate.CreateFromSignedFile and compare the subject against a constant, throwing a plain sentence on a mismatch. Keep the host pinning and the checksum; they stay as the second layer.

**Done when:** On a built exe: `Get-AuthenticodeSignature C:\Thornswood\Launcher\Thornswood.exe` reports Valid and names the expected subject, instead of NotSigned. The launcher still compiles under the .NET Framework csc, which tools\Check-Build.ps1 verifies read-only and which the GitHub build workflow at .github\workflows\build.yml runs on every pull request. The signer check itself is testable without signing anything: point it at an already-signed Windows binary and confirm it accepts, and at the unsigned launcher and confirm it throws.

**Traps:** Updater.cs is on the CLAUDE.md list of files that need a line in #dev before anybody edits them, because both machines touch it. The launcher is C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, and X509Certificate.CreateFromSignedFile throws rather than returning null, so wrap it. Signing changes the file hash, so the first signed release invalidates every checksum published for the previous one; publish it as one complete release, launcher and installer together, as CLAUDE.md requires. Entry 26 is the other half: an unsigned or newly-signed exe that Defender flags is exactly when a rollback is needed, and today there is none. And the 527 MB payload has no hash check at all (entry 32), so signing the small binaries while the big one is unverified only moves the problem.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/azure/artifact-signing/faq , https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/products/artifact-signing , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.cryptography.x509certificates.x509certificate.createfromsignedfile , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/seccrypto/signtool

## 15. Identity on the game server is a number the client picks, so anyone can be anyone, including the admin

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every cite lands. tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:185 is `$s | Add-Member -NotePropertyName offlineMode -NotePropertyValue $true -Force`, verified exactly, and it is the only place offlineMode is set. tools\gamemode.js:2274-2296 is the door's opening comment, which says in its own words that the server has had no access control of any kind. 2362 is `pid = Number(mp.get(actorId, 'profileId')) || 0;`. 3097-3108 is the admins() function returning the hardcoded id at 3103 and isAdmin at 3106-3108, with one nuance the entry omits: admins() first reads cfg.thornswoodAdmins out of the server settings at 3099-3101 and only falls back to the hardcoded id, so the number in the source is a fallback rather than the sole list. tools\app\AutoJoin.cs:247-260 is SetProfileId, which writes the profile number into the client's settings file, verified. And the quoted phrase in the report's verdict is real: gamemode.js:2917-2918 reads "the profile id this arrives under is a claim and not a fact", inside the /tp handler, with a paragraph explaining that anybody opening a socket to the voice port with somebody else's id could move that character across Skyrim.

**Read first:** 1) tools\gamemode.js lines 2910-2930, the comment that already describes the whole attack in four sentences, written by whoever built /tp. 2) tools\app\AutoJoin.cs lines 230-300, so you see the number is written into a plain settings file on the person's own machine, which is why it is a claim. 3) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 404-430 and 1240-1300, the site's existing Discord sign-in and ticket reply, because that is the only component in the system that already knows who somebody is and is therefore where a token has to be minted.

**How:** Do it as a token, not as a rewrite of SkyMP's auth. The site already signs a Discord identity cookie and already answers /api/ticket. Add a route there that, for a signed-in person, returns a short-lived token carrying profileId, an expiry and an HMAC over both, keyed on a secret the game server also holds. On the gamemode side, verify that token before an actor is bound to a profile, in the same place the door sweep runs today (gamemode.js:2358-2392), and refuse rather than kick. Change admins() at 3097 to read a role out of the verified identity instead of matching a number, keeping cfg.thornswoodAdmins as the transition. The launcher passes the token through the same path AutoJoin already uses for the profile number.

**Done when:** Checkable without the game: write the token mint and verify as two small functions and unit test them in node, proving that an expired token fails, a token whose profileId is edited fails, and a valid one passes. `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. In the world, for Patrick: a client with a hand-edited profileId in its settings file and no token must be refused with a named reason in thornswood.log, and /tp from that identity must not run.

**Traps:** This is the entry every other control depends on: entry 5 (the door), entry 16 (moderator powers), entry 64 and 65 (moderation), the character register in entry 3, and voice identity in entry 4 are all keyed on the same number, so fixing it makes them real and leaving it makes them decoration. Say that when you hand it over. gamemode.js is hot reloaded with mp.clear() first, so do not keep the verified set in a module-level cache that survives a reload without being rebuilt. The alternative path, turning offlineMode off and using SkyMP's own flow, changes how the client connects and therefore needs a matching client change, and CLAUDE.md settles that the client bundle is not built here. This is a large, coordinated release: launcher, gamemode and site all move together, and per CLAUDE.md it needs the problem, the approach and what it touches in #dev before any code.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/oauth2 , https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc7519 , https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html#cryptocreatehmacalgorithm-key-options

## 16. A moderator has no powers in the game at all

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The conclusion holds, the inventory is incomplete. handle() begins at tools\gamemode.js:2863 and runs to about 3060, so the cited range is right. But the command set is larger than the entry says: /tspicks (2893, a hidden plugin-to-server message for a broken lockpick, deliberately absent from help), /where (2901), /tp or /goto (2916, admin-gated at 2928 by isAdmin), /restart or /shutdown (2972, which only replies "Restarts are not run from chat. Use Request-Restart.ps1."), /ooc or /o (2986, deliberately local), and /help or /commands (3042). /roll is not in this file at all; the help text at 3046 says it is in the chat itself, so it is client side. So there are two admin-visible commands rather than one, and neither does anything a moderator needs. tools\discord\bot.py:676 is `@tree.command(name='close', description='Close this ticket. Staff only.')`, verified, and it is the only staff command in the bot. The entry's point, that a moderator has no in-world powers, is correct.

**Read first:** 1) tools\gamemode.js lines 2860-3066, the whole command handler, so you see the shape every new command must follow (it is dispatched from a queue, not from a chat packet). 2) tools\voice\voice-server.js lines 495-524, because slash commands do not reach the game server directly: the chat page queues them on the voice socket and the gamemode polls GET /commands at 3333. 3) tools\gamemode.js lines 3090-3110, admins() and isAdmin(), the only gate that exists.

**How:** Add commands to handle() in tools\gamemode.js beside the existing ones, each guarded by a role test rather than by isAdmin's number list. The minimum set a roleplay server opens with: goto and bring (the /tp machinery at 2916-2970 already does the teleport half), freeze, mute, kick, a timed ban written into allowed.json so the door at 2307 picks it up, an observe mode, and a note written against a person's record. The note needs somewhere to live: the characters roster at gamemode.js:2067 is the obvious file, but see the trap below about where private files belong. Mirror each action into a staff channel through the bot so there is a record outside the game.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and, without a server, a small harness that calls handle() with a fabricated command object exercises each new branch: a non-staff profile gets "That one is not yours to run.", a staff profile reaches the action. In the world, for Patrick, each command answers through toProfile and writes a line to thornswood.log.

**Traps:** Every one of these is keyed on profileId, which gamemode.js:2917-2918 says is a claim and not a fact, so shipping moderator powers before entry 15 hands a stranger the moderator's kit by editing one number. That is the single most important thing to tell whoever picks this up. Commands arrive through the voice service, so a moderator command is unavailable whenever voice is down, and the deployed voice service is not the file in this repo (entry 7). A ban written to allowed.json only bites when enforce is true (entry 5) and only refers to a Discord role that Build-Discord.py never creates (entry 67). gamemode.js is hot reloaded, so re-registering handlers at load is correct and expected. This is a large piece of work and CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule means it should arrive as several branches, not one.

**Links:** https://docs.discord.com/developers/topics/rate-limits

## 17. The Discord ticket, a thirty day credential as good as identity, is sent in clear HTTP every time a character is made

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact. tools\app\Creation.cs: the Send overload begins at line 670, `string url = "http://" + cfg.ServerAddress + ":" + port + "/choose";` is line 679, the comment explaining that the ticket proves the profile belongs to the Discord account is 681-684, and the ticket is read at 686 and placed in the body at 689. A grep for https:// in Creation.cs returns nothing, so 679 is the only outbound address in the file and it is plain HTTP. The /choose route it posts to is tools\voice\voice-server.js:342, and the file's own comment at line 34 records that /choose cannot be locked to loopback because every launcher calls it. The launcher does protect the ticket with DPAPI on disk, via the Secrets.Protect and Unprotect pair used in Config.cs, so the entry's framing that it is protected at rest and clear on the wire is right.

**Read first:** 1) tools\app\Creation.cs lines 660-710, the whole Send path including what else is in the body (trade, class, town, name). 2) tools\voice\voice-server.js lines 30-40 and 342-395, the /choose route and the comment about why it cannot be loopback-only. 3) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 1240-1300, the site's owns route, which is the same shape in the other direction and is the model the fix should copy.

**How:** Add a /api/choose route to tools\docs-app\server.js that accepts the choice over HTTPS, verifies the ticket the way the existing ticket routes do, and then relays to the voice service over 127.0.0.1 without the ticket in the body. In tools\app\Creation.cs, change line 679 to build an https URL against the site host rather than ServerAddress plus the voice port, and drop the ticket from the body at 689 in favour of whatever header or cookie the site route expects. Remember the launcher already forces TLS 1.2 elsewhere with `ServicePointManager.SecurityProtocol = (SecurityProtocolType)3072;` (Updater.cs:409), so do the same here.

**Done when:** `grep -n "http://" tools/app/Creation.cs` returns nothing. The launcher still compiles: tools\Check-Build.ps1 or the GitHub build workflow. End to end, for Patrick: making a character still sets the trade and town, and a packet capture or simply the absence of the ticket string in the request body confirms it is no longer sent in the clear. Never print the ticket while testing.

**Traps:** This must not ship before entry 7: the deployed voice service is not the file in this repo, so its /choose may not behave as voice-server.js:342 does, and changing the caller against unread code will break character creation for everyone. The launcher is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, which matters because the natural way to write a new URL is interpolation. Creation.cs is not on the CLAUDE.md do-not-touch list, but the site's server.js and the voice service both are effectively shared, so put a line in #dev. This is a coordinated release: launcher, site and voice move together, and a launcher that posts to a route the site does not have yet fails silently at the worst moment, which is somebody's first character.

**Links:** https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/reverse_proxy , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.net.servicepointmanager.securityprotocol

## 18. A failed publish deletes the live server before it checks the new one, and there is no rollback for the server, the site or the bot

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The destructive ordering is exact. tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:84 is `if (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Root) { Remove-Item -LiteralPath $Root -Recurse -Force }`, 85 creates the directory and 86 expands the archive into it. The validation the entry says comes too late really does: the byte order mark fix is at 120-125 and the load order resolution check, which throws on a missing plugin, is at 128-138, all after the live folder has already been deleted. The world and data folders are set aside first at 74-82 and restored at 89-95, so those survive, but the server itself is gone from line 84 until the expand finishes. tools\Publish-Server.ps1:148 invokes that installer and 149 is `if ($LASTEXITCODE -ne 0) { Die "the installer on the VPS exited with $LASTEXITCODE. Nothing local was changed." }`, which is the misleading message the entry describes: nothing local changed, but the remote server is in pieces. tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:135-179 and tools\Install-Bot.ps1:156-198 were not read line by line in this pass; the pattern in Publish-Server and Install-ThornswoodServer is confirmed.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1 lines 70-140 in order, because the whole finding is the order of those lines. 2) tools\Publish-Server.ps1 lines 140-175, the caller, including the verify step that only starts after the install has already replaced everything. 3) tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md, which documents what lives where on the VPS and is what a rollback plan has to preserve.

**How:** Turn C:\Thornswood-Server into a directory junction pointing at C:\Thornswood-Server-releases\<stamp>. In tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1, unpack to a new stamped folder, run the existing validation at 120-138 there, and only then stop the task, repoint the junction with `New-Item -ItemType Junction -Path C:\Thornswood-Server -Target ...` after removing the old link, start, and health-check the resources port the way Publish-Server.ps1:152-165 already does.
