On a failed health check, repoint the junction back. Keep the last three stamped folders. The world and data folders stay outside the junction so nothing has to be copied on a swap.

**Done when:** Rehearsable on a throwaway box without touching production: install twice, confirm two stamped folders exist, break the second deliberately (remove a plugin from the load order so the check at 128-138 throws) and confirm the junction never moved and the old server is still answering on the resources port. `powershell -NoProfile -Command "Get-Item C:\Thornswood-Server | Select-Object LinkTarget"` should name the current stamp.

**Traps:** Moving the world folder by accident loses everything, so make the world and data paths explicit and outside the junction before anything else. server-settings.json holds absolute plugin paths under the server root (vps-dev-instance.ps1:53 rewrites them for the dev copy), so a junction that changes the real path may break the load order resolution; test that specifically. Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1 and Publish-Server.ps1 both reach the VPS, so a person runs them, never a Claude. Publish-Update.ps1 is named in CLAUDE.md as needing a #dev line first and these scripts are the same shared ground. Entry 19 is the same problem in the script that runs most often, and the two should be designed together so there is one staging-and-swap shape rather than two.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/mklink , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/new-item

## 19. Deploy-Server stops the live server first, copies over it, and checks less than the dev path does

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact, including the note about 17 September. tools\Deploy-Server.ps1:183 is `Write-Host 'Stopping the server...'`, the Remote block runs 184-206 and stops whatever holds the UDP port, then 209 is `Write-Host 'Copying...'` and the scp loop follows at 210-220. So it does stop first and copy over the running folder. The narrowing the entry mentions is in the file's own comment at 185-189 and 196-201: the stop was limited to the process owning that root's UDP port, because matching every node.exe running skymp5-server took the dev server down with the live one, and the TCP port one above was deliberately left alone because on the live root that is 7778, the voice service. Both comments are dated to 17 September in the file. There is no staging path, no rename and no health check in this script; Publish-Server.ps1:152-165 has the health check and Deploy-Server does not.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Deploy-Server.ps1 lines 175-230, the stop, the copy and what comes after, which is the whole of it. 2) tools\Publish-Server.ps1 lines 140-175, which already has the health-check shape this script is missing, so you copy rather than invent. 3) tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1 lines 118-140, the validation that the dev path does and this one does not, which is the "checks less than the dev path" half of the finding.

**How:** Reshape Deploy-Server.ps1 the same way as entry 18, so there is one pattern: scp into a staging folder under the server root, validate there on the remote (the load order resolution check from Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:128-138 is the one that matters, since a shortened load order shifts every index after the gap), then stop, move the staged files into place, start, and poll the resources port until it answers or a deadline passes, restoring the previous files on failure. Keep the UDP-only stop exactly as it is; that narrowing was a fix and must not be widened.

**Done when:** Rehearse on the dev root, which is what it is for: run with a deliberately broken plugin path and confirm the live files are untouched and the server is still up. The health check is done when the script itself prints a pass or fail rather than leaving the person to check, matching Publish-Server.ps1's output. Read-only on this machine: `powershell -NoProfile -Command "$null = [scriptblock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw tools/Deploy-Server.ps1))"` proves it still parses, which is also what the GitHub build workflow checks.

**Traps:** Do not widen the stop. The comment at Deploy-Server.ps1:196-201 records that stopping the TCP port one above the game port would kill the voice service, which carries voice chat, the launcher's character choices and the chat command queue the lockpick fix rides on. Do not reach for a script that stops every node.exe; that incident is recorded at 185-189. This script reaches the VPS, so a person runs it. The dev and live roots share the same script with different parameters, and the dev deploy has its own guard (`throw 'Refused: that root is the live server.'` in Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1:78), so any refactor must keep both guards working. Entries 18 and 19 should land as one designed shape but two pull requests, per the one-fix-per-pull-request rule.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/start-scheduledtask , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/nettcpip/get-netudpendpoint

## 20. Nothing tells a person when anything dies, and every check runs on the box being checked

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance is verified and one cite is misplaced. tools\Watch-Services.ps1:72-75 is Note(), which does `Add-Content -LiteralPath $log`, where $log is watch.log under a folder on the same box (line 68). Answers() at 77-85 opens a TcpClient to 127.0.0.1, so every check is a loopback check. 124-141 is the restart loop, and 126-129 is the give-up branch: after three restarts in an hour it writes "is still down after N restarts this hour. Leaving it. This one needs a look." to that same local file and nothing else. All verified. The misplaced cite is tools\Check-Site.ps1:29-31, which is help text inside the script's comment block ("Run it when somebody says they cannot get in"), not a check; Check-Site does run from a developer's machine, so it is an outside check, but it is manual and nothing runs it on a schedule. The watched list is website 3001, game 3000, voice 7778 and the bot by process name (Watch-Services.ps1:109-114), so a missing alert is the whole failure mode.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 60-145, the entire watchdog, which is short and whose three-strikes logic is worth keeping. 2) tools\Check-Site.ps1, which is read-only and safe to run, and shows what an outside check already looks like in this repo. 3) tools\Publish-News.ps1 or tools\Publish-Discord.ps1, whichever posts through a webhook, because the machinery for posting to Discord already exists here and should be reused rather than rewritten.

**How:** Two changes, both small. In tools\Watch-Services.ps1, add a Post function that sends the same sentence Note writes to a webhook whose URL is read from a file on the VPS (name the path, never the value), and call it from the give-up branch at 126-129 and from the "did not come back" branch at 140. Then add an outside monitor: a free uptime service polling https://thornswood.net and the game's resources endpoint from somewhere that is not the VPS, so the box being down is itself the alert. Keep the three-strikes rule exactly as it is.

**Done when:** Locally, with a fake webhook URL pointing at a throwaway endpoint, run the Post function alone and confirm it posts. On the VPS, for Patrick: stop one service deliberately on the dev instance and confirm a message arrives in the private alerts channel within a minute, and that watch.log still records it too. The outside monitor is done when unplugging the site produces an alert that did not originate on the VPS.

**Traps:** The webhook URL is a secret: it lives in a file on the VPS and is named by path, never printed, pasted, committed or logged, which is CLAUDE.md's rule and also just prudence, since anyone holding it can post as the server. Do not alert on every transient failure; the three-strikes rule is what stops a restart wave after a release becoming fifty messages, and the comment at 95-96 explains why it is there. Watch-Services.ps1 uses StrictHostKeyChecking=no like twenty-one other scripts (entry 22), so do not add another copy of that pattern. The watchdog runs on the VPS, so deploying a change to it is a person's job. Caddy is not in the watched list at all, which is entry 21 and is one line in the same array at 109-114, so the two are best done in one sitting even though they are separate rows.

**Links:** https://docs.discord.com/developers/docs/resources/webhook , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/invoke-restmethod

## 21. Caddy is unwatched, and its scheduled task is set to be killed every three days

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both halves check out, and the three-day claim is real though it is a default rather than something written down. tools\Watch-Services.ps1:109-114 is the $checks array, exactly four entries, website 3001, game 3000, voice 7778 and bot, with no Caddy, verified line for line. tools\Setup-Domain.ps1:142-150 registers 'Thornswood Web' with `New-ScheduledTaskSettingsSet -AllowStartIfOnBatteries -DontStopIfGoingOnBatteries -RestartCount 3 -RestartInterval (New-TimeSpan -Minutes 1)` and no -ExecutionTimeLimit. Microsoft documents the default execution time limit as three days, so the task is stopped seventy-two hours after it starts, which is exactly what the entry claims. tools\Install-Voice.ps1:208 shows the project already knows the fix: that task passes `-ExecutionTimeLimit ([TimeSpan]::Zero)` and RestartCount 999. The same defect applies to the site's task in tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:268-269, which the entry does not mention and should.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Setup-Domain.ps1 lines 130-160, the Caddy task registration and the certificate wait after it. 2) tools\Install-Voice.ps1 lines 205-212, the one task in this repo registered correctly, which is the template. 3) tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 105-145, so you see adding Caddy is one row in an array plus a decision about what counts as alive.

**How:** In tools\Setup-Domain.ps1:147-148, add `-ExecutionTimeLimit ([TimeSpan]::Zero)` and raise RestartCount, copying Install-Voice.ps1:208 verbatim so there is one shape. Do the same in tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:268-269. Then add Caddy to the $checks array in tools\Watch-Services.ps1:109-114, but make its liveness test an HTTPS GET of the real hostname rather than a loopback TCP connect, because a loopback port behind the terminator proves nothing about whether TLS is being served; the array currently only supports a port or a process name, so this needs a third check kind. Better still, run Caddy as a real Windows service rather than a task.

**Done when:** Read-only here: `grep -n "ExecutionTimeLimit" tools/*.ps1` should list Setup-Domain, Deploy-Docs and Install-Voice rather than Install-Voice alone, and `grep -n "Caddy\|Thornswood Web" tools/Watch-Services.ps1` should find the new entry. On the VPS, for Patrick: `Get-ScheduledTask 'Thornswood Web' | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Settings` reports ExecutionTimeLimit PT0S, and stopping Caddy on the dev box produces an alert.

**Traps:** Changing task settings in these scripts only reaches a task that does not exist yet: Setup-Domain.ps1:142 and Deploy-Docs.ps1:266 both skip registration when the task is already there, and Deploy-Docs's else branch calls Set-ScheduledTask with -Action only. So the settings fix does nothing on the live box until the task is unregistered and re-registered, which is a person's job and a brief outage of the thing the public touches. Restarting Caddy asks Let's Encrypt for a certificate on first start (Setup-Domain.ps1:153-155 sleeps twelve seconds for it), and Let's Encrypt rate limits repeated issuance, so do not loop on restarts while testing. Watch-Services.ps1's three-strikes rule is correct and must stay. This and entry 20 are nearly the same change and are best done together despite being two rows.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/new-scheduledtasksettingsset , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/taskschd/tasksettings-executiontimelimit , https://caddyserver.com/docs/running

## 22. The VPS is driven as Administrator with host key checking off in eleven scripts, password login never disabled, every service as SYSTEM, RDP open to the internet

**Category:** Security  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The pattern is real and worse than stated; the count is wrong. `grep -rln "StrictHostKeyChecking=no" tools/*.ps1` returns 22 files, not eleven: Backup-Server, Chargen-Log, Check-Load, Check-Manifest, Clean-Everything, Deploy-DevGamemode, Deploy-Docs, Deploy-Server, Fix-Voice, Get-Logs, Go, Install-Bot, Publish-Installer, Publish-News, Request-Restart, Rotate-ServerPassword, Set-DiscordLogin, Set-Door, Setup-Domain, Show-BotLog, Watch-Services and Wipe-Saves. Fix-Voice and Go each contain three occurrences, so it is 26 occurrences across 22 files. The entry's own list names ten scripts plus Check-*, which is twelve at most. Services as SYSTEM is verified in six scripts: Deploy-Docs.ps1:271, Fix-Voice.ps1:94, Install-Bot.ps1:388, Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:163, Install-Voice.ps1:209 and Setup-Domain.ps1:149. The good example the entry cites is real: tools\Setup-SshKey.ps1 uses accept-new at lines 60 and 180. Password login never being disabled and RDP being open are VPS facts and were not checked from here.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Setup-SshKey.ps1 lines 50-70 and 170-185, the one script that does it correctly, which is the shape to extract. 2) tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1 lines 57-100, which holds both the $ssh option array and the Remote helper, and is the most complete example of the pattern being copied. 3) CLAUDE.md, the section on what a Claude does and what the person does, because every one of these scripts reaches the VPS and is therefore a person's to run.

**How:** Extract one helper, for example tools\Ssh-Common.ps1, exporting the option array and a Remote function, and dot-source it from all 22 scripts the way tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 is already dot-sourced by tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:215. Change the option to `StrictHostKeyChecking=accept-new`, which records the key on first use and refuses a changed one, and is the documented middle ground. Then, once both machines' keys are proven with `tools\Setup-SshKey.ps1 -Test` (read-only and safe), Patrick sets PasswordAuthentication no in the VPS sshd_config. One low-privilege account per service and putting RDP behind an allowlist are separate pieces of work and should be separate pull requests.

**Done when:** `grep -rc "StrictHostKeyChecking=no" tools/*.ps1 | grep -v ":0"` returns nothing, and `grep -rn "Ssh-Common" tools/*.ps1 | wc -l` accounts for every script that used to have it. Every script still parses, which the GitHub build workflow at .github\workflows\build.yml checks on the pull request. `tools\Setup-SshKey.ps1 -Test` still reports a working key from both machines.

**Traps:** accept-new still trusts the first connection, so record the key from a trusted network once and then it protects you; do not present this as full host verification. Flipping all 22 scripts at once means one typo breaks every VPS operation, which is why CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule matters here: extract the helper in one branch, migrate scripts in another. On a hostile network today, a machine answering as the VPS receives whatever the script was going to send, and Install-Bot and Set-DiscordLogin send the bot token and the Discord secret, so those two are the ones to migrate first. Entry 23 is the same helper's other half; design them together. None of these scripts may be run by a Claude.

**Links:** https://man.openbsd.org/ssh_config.5 , https://man.openbsd.org/ssh.1 , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/openssh/openssh-server-configuration

## 23. Secrets travel inside ssh command lines

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is correct and the code even carries a comment claiming the opposite, which is worth quoting when you hand it over. tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1:91 base64s the password, 97 opens `Remote @"` and 99 reconstitutes it on the remote. tools\Install-Bot.ps1:241-242 builds a JSON payload holding the bot token and the new key and base64s it, 246 passes it to Remote. tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:216-224 is the -Password branch and 221-224 does the same. Where the entry undersells it: Deploy-Docs.ps1:219-220 comments "Through base64 so quoting survives ssh, cmd and PowerShell, and so it is not sitting in a command line on either machine", and that second claim is false. Remote() at Deploy-Docs.ps1:84-86 builds `powershell -NoProfile -EncodedCommand $e` and hands it to ssh as the command, so the secret is in the remote process's command line, visible to anything that can read Win32_Process, and in whatever sshd logs. Base64 is transport encoding, not protection. So: verified as a finding, with a code comment that needs correcting alongside the code.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1 lines 84-110, the Remote helper and its long comment about the 8,191 character cmd limit, which is the constraint any replacement has to respect. 2) tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 lines 85-115, the clearest single example of a secret making the trip. 3) tools\Setup-SshKey.ps1 lines 170-185, which shows the same EncodedCommand pattern being used for something that is not a secret, so you can see which calls actually need changing.

**How:** Put the secret on stdin instead of on the command line. The remote script reads `$pw = [Console]::In.ReadLine()` (or ReadToEnd for a JSON payload) and the local side pipes it: `$payload | & ssh @ssh $target "powershell -NoProfile -EncodedCommand $e"`, where $e now contains only the script, with no value embedded. The alternative, when a script is too long for stdin to be convenient, is scp into a file whose ACL is locked to the service account, have the remote script read and delete it, and never put the value in an argument. Do it in the one shared helper from entry 22 so there is a single place to get it right.

**Done when:** Locally, against a throwaway remote or even against localhost, run the changed call and check the remote process list during it: the secret must not appear in any command line. A cheap proxy check on the local side: `grep -n "b64" tools/Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 tools/Install-Bot.ps1 tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1` should no longer show a base64 value being interpolated into a Remote block that becomes an argument. Every script must still parse, which the GitHub build workflow verifies.

**Traps:** The 8,191 character cmd limit is real and is documented in the comment at Deploy-Docs.ps1:88-95, with the date it was measured; a naive rewrite that makes the script longer will reintroduce the truncation that produced "Missing closing '}' in statement block" on every deploy. Fix the misleading comment at Deploy-Docs.ps1:219-220 in the same change, or the next reader will trust it. Never print the value while testing, including in an error message you add. These scripts all reach the VPS, so a person runs them. This shares a helper with entry 22, so agree the helper's shape in #dev once rather than twice.

**Links:** https://man.openbsd.org/ssh.1 , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_powershell_exe , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/openssh/openssh-server-configuration

## 24. Deploy-Docs -Password wipes the whole site config, and a wiped config turns the password door back on

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The destruction and the consequence are both verified exactly; one supporting claim is not. tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:216 is `if ($Password) {` and 224 is `[IO.File]::WriteAllBytes('$rootWin\config.json', [Convert]::FromBase64String('$b64'))`, where $b64 encodes only `{"password":"..."}`. So it overwrites the entire file. What is lost is everything else in the default config at tools\docs-app\server.js:46-80: discord.clientId, clientSecret, botToken, guildId and invite, patreon, gamePort, gameCheckPort, onlineFile, the whole access block, botKey, admins, serverPassword and secret. The door reopening is verified: server.js:404-405 defines discordReady as clientId and clientSecret both set, and 411-412 defines isMember as `!discordReady() && sameSecret(...)`, so with Discord gone the password cookie becomes a valid identity again, and /api/login at 769-777 starts accepting. Where the entry is wrong: server.js does not record "the day the Discord configuration vanished this way". The nearest comment, at 81-87, records the site running for days looking configured and holding nothing, but blames a byte order mark and a bare catch, not this switch. tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1:98-107 is the read-modify-write pattern the fix should copy, verified.

**Read first:** 1) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 44-90, the full default config, so you know exactly what is destroyed. 2) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 400-415 and 765-785, discordReady, isMember and /api/login, which is why a wiped config is not merely inconvenient. 3) tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 lines 97-108, the correct read-modify-write, which is already in this repo and should simply be copied.

**How:** Replace the body of the -Password branch in tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:216-226 with the block from Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1:98-107: read config.json if it exists, convert from JSON into a hashtable, set the one key, and write back with `(New-Object Text.UTF8Encoding($false))` so no byte order mark is added, because server.js:81-87 records that node refuses to parse one. If entry 77 removes the hub, delete the -Password parameter entirely instead; then this becomes only about not destroying the Discord secrets and the bot key, which no longer have a writer here either.

**Done when:** Testable locally without the VPS: make a scratch config.json with several keys, run the new block as a plain PowerShell snippet against it, and confirm every other key survives and no byte order mark is written (`(Get-Content -Encoding Byte -TotalCount 3 config.json)` must not be 239 187 191). `grep -n "WriteAllBytes" tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1` should no longer point at a whole-file overwrite.

**Traps:** A byte order mark breaks the site silently: server.js:81-87 says the read used to be a bare catch that swallowed the error and ran on defaults, so the site looked configured from outside while holding nothing. Always write UTF8 with no mark. Recovering from a wipe means re-running Install-Bot and Set-DiscordLogin to put the secrets back, so if this has already happened, fix the cause before the symptom. Do not repeat the entry's claim that server.js records this incident; it does not, and a stranger will look for a comment that is not there. Deploy-Docs.ps1 reaches the VPS, so a person runs it. If entry 77 lands first, this row shrinks to a deletion.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html#fsreadfilesyncpath-options , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/convertfrom-json

## 25. The site runs as SYSTEM under a scheduled task, writes no log anywhere, and every deploy deletes the log that would explain the last crash

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance is verified; two of the five cites are off by a line or point at the caller rather than the act. The site runs as SYSTEM: tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1:270-271 registers the task with `-RunLevel Highest -User 'SYSTEM'`. It writes no log: the task action at 265 is `New-ScheduledTaskAction -Execute $node -Argument '$rootWin\server.js'` with no redirection, and every diagnostic in server.js:1478-1505 goes to console.log or console.error, so it goes nowhere. Verified. The deploy deleting the log: tools\Install-Voice.ps1:212 (not 211) is `if (Test-Path $log) { Remove-Item $log -Force }`, and tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:171-172 is the same for server.log, verified exactly. tools\Publish-Server.ps1:143 is `$instArgs = ...`, the line that invokes the installer that deletes the log, not a deletion itself. One thing the entry misses and should say: the voice service does write voice.log and voice.err (both are in the 15 September backup), so the deletion is destroying evidence that exists, whereas the site never had any.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1 lines 260-280, the task registration, which is where the account and the output redirection both live. 2) tools\docs-app\server.js lines 1476-1510, every diagnostic the site produces, so you can see how little would need to change to write them to a file. 3) tools\Install-Voice.ps1 lines 195-215, the one service in this repo registered with a sensible settings set, which is also where the log deletion is.

**How:** Three separable changes. First, give the site a log: add a small write-line helper in tools\docs-app\server.js beside the listen callback at 1477 that appends the request line, the status and any error to a file beside server.js, and call it from the handler and from the new unhandledRejection handler entry 13 adds. Second, roll rather than delete: in tools\Install-Voice.ps1:212 and tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:172, rename to .1 and shift, keeping five, instead of Remove-Item. Third, create a low-privilege local account on the VPS, grant it the service folders, and register the tasks with `New-ScheduledTaskPrincipal -UserId <account> -LogonType ServiceAccount` instead of SYSTEM. Add the site and the bot to tools\Get-Logs.ps1 so there is one script that fetches everything.

**Done when:** Locally: run `node tools/docs-app/server.js` in a scratch copy, make a few requests including a bad one, and confirm a log file appears beside it holding the request lines and the error. For rolling, run the renamed block against scratch files and confirm five generations survive and the sixth falls off. On the VPS, for Patrick, `Get-ScheduledTask 'Thornswood Docs' | Select-Object -ExpandProperty Principal` should no longer say SYSTEM, and `tools\Get-Logs.ps1` should return the site's log without a second command.

**Traps:** Dropping from SYSTEM to a normal account will break anything the services do that needed privilege: binding a low port, writing outside their own folder, reading another service's files. Test on the dev instance first, which is what tools\vps-dev-instance.ps1 exists for. A log that throws every tick is worse than no log, and gamemode.js:72-76 already solves that by giving up for good on the first failed write; copy that approach rather than inventing one. Logs will contain Discord ids and possibly addresses, so the retention promise in entry 83's privacy page has to cover them. All of these scripts reach the VPS and a person runs them. This is the other half of entry 13: fixing the crash without adding the log leaves you unable to confirm the fix worked.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/console.html , https://github.com/winswldocs , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/new-scheduledtaskprincipal

## 26. The launcher's self update destroys the old exe and has no way back

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact. SelfUpdate is tools\app\Updater.cs:816-873, reached through a cmd written to %TEMP%\st-update.cmd at 825. The swap is `move /y "<exe>.new" "<exe>"` at 856, with no copy of the old exe kept anywhere, so once that move succeeds the previous launcher is gone. The fallback at 866 is `start "" "<exe>"`, which starts whatever is now at that path, meaning a broken new build is started rather than the old one restored. The comment at 828-836 records that this used to sleep two seconds and move once, and that the swap could hit a still-running exe, fail silently, and offer the same update forever; the tasklist wait at 848-851 is the fix for that, and it is good. The chcp 65001 line at 843 exists because cmd reads a .cmd in the console OEM code page, which is also recorded. None of that provides a way back.

**Read first:** 1) tools\app\Updater.cs lines 815-873, the whole method, including the two comments that record what already went wrong here. 2) tools\app\Updater.cs lines 398-430, Download, so you see the checksum and host checks that happen before the swap and which stay whatever else changes. 3) HANDOFF.md, for what is live and what is already in flight, since replacing the updater touches every installed machine at once.

**How:** The small fix, in the existing cmd: before the move at 856, rename the current exe to `<exe>.old` (`move /y "<exe>" "<exe>.old"`), then move the new one in, then start it; on a non-zero errorlevel from start, move the .old back and start that instead; delete .old on the next clean start from inside the launcher rather than from the script, so a crash-on-launch still leaves the old build recoverable. That is about eight more lines in the same StringBuilder. The larger fix is to adopt Velopack, which handles the swap, rollback, delta packages and the shortcut and icon-cache work that Updater.cs does by hand, and removes the one-asset constraint; that is a rewrite of this method and of the publish side, so it is a #dev conversation first.

**Done when:** Testable without shipping: build two launchers, one deliberately broken so it exits non-zero immediately, and drive SelfUpdate against the broken one in a copy of the install folder. The old exe must be back in place and running afterwards, and `<exe>.old` must be gone only after a clean start. Also confirm the generated cmd still starts hidden and still deletes itself (the `del "%~f0"` at 867). The launcher must still compile under the .NET Framework csc: tools\Check-Build.ps1 or the GitHub build workflow.

**Traps:** Updater.cs is on the CLAUDE.md list of files that need a line in #dev before an edit, because both machines touch it. The launcher is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, and this method builds the script with a StringBuilder for exactly that reason. Do not remove the chcp 65001 line or the tasklist wait; both are recorded fixes for real failures and removing either reintroduces a bug the file names. Closing runs Swap.Restore, Hold.Release, Keep.Unpark and SkyMp.Stop, which is why the wait exists, so any change to the timing has to keep waiting for the process to be genuinely gone. An update that half-applies leaves a machine with no launcher, which is the worst outcome in this list for somebody who cannot be talked through a repair, so rehearse the failure path, not just the success path.

**Links:** https://docs.velopack.io/ , https://github.com/velopack/velopack , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-movefileexa

## 27. Three unrelated identifiers per release, no tags, and dev builds published as public releases with no prerelease flag

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All four claims check out. Three identifiers: tools\app\Updater.cs:13 is `public const string Version = "1.1.47";`, tools\Ship.ps1:169-216 reads that constant, bumps the patch number and writes it back, and version.json's own launcherVersion is currently 1.0.196, so the two version numbers in the system already disagree by a major version. The third is the pack id, tools\Publish-Update.ps1:516, `if (-not $Id) { $Id = Get-Date -Format 'yyyyMMdd-HHmm'; if ($Dev) { $Id += '-dev' } }`, verified exactly. No tags: `git tag` in this clone returns nothing at all, zero tags. No prerelease flag: tools\Publish-Update.ps1:710 is `& gh release create $Id @assets @repoArg --title "Update $Id" --notes ...` with no --prerelease anywhere; the only prerelease in the repo is tools\Publish-Pack.ps1:177, which hardcodes prerelease = $false. The GitHub-side counts (62 releases, none marked prerelease, Latest being a dev build) need the public repo and were not re-checked here.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Ship.ps1 lines 165-216, the version block, including the dev-build fourth-number scheme and its comment, which explains how 1.1.35.1 relates to 1.1.36 and why the launcher's part-by-part comparison makes that work. 2) tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 505-525 and 700-720, where the pack id is minted and where the GitHub release is created. 3) tools\app\Updater.cs lines 10-20 and 141-205, the launcher's own constant and how it compares itself against the feed.

**How:** The one-line win first: at tools\Publish-Update.ps1:710, append `--prerelease` to the gh release create call when $Dev is set, and do the same to the gh release upload fallback path just below. That alone stops a dev build showing as Latest on the public page. Then unify the identifier: use the launcher version as the release id rather than a timestamp, tag the commit (`git tag -a v$target -m "..."` in Ship.ps1 after the version is written, pushed with the source commit), and name the assets with it. Finally add a minimumVersion field to version.json and have tools\app\Updater.cs refuse to run below it, so a bad launcher can be withdrawn.

**Done when:** `grep -n "prerelease" tools/Publish-Update.ps1` finds the new flag on both the create and the upload paths, and the scripts still parse, which the GitHub build workflow checks. After the next dev Ship, for Patrick, the Thornswood-Releases releases page shows that release marked Pre-release and Latest pointing at the newest stable one. `git tag` should stop returning nothing after the first tagged release.

**Traps:** Ship.ps1 rewrites Updater.cs in place at line 212 and then commits everything on disk to main (entry 29), so a change to the version scheme lands in the same commit as whatever else is dirty; fix 29 first or at least run with a clean tree. The dev fourth-number scheme is deliberate and documented at Ship.ps1:187-192: the launcher compares part by part so 1.1.36 beats every 1.1.35.x, and collapsing to one identifier must not break that. Ship.ps1 refuses a main release that is not on main or is behind origin, which is a guard worth keeping. Only Patrick's machine ships main, and a person runs Ship.ps1, never a Claude. Changing the release id changes the asset urls, which interacts with entry 11's feed rewrite, so decide those two together.

**Links:** https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_release_create , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases , https://semver.org/

## 28. A failed push still records the release as published, so the next Ship says there is nothing to do

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact, and slightly worse than the entry states. tools\Publish-Update.ps1: the push block opens at 1170 with `$pushFailed = $null` at 1171, runs to 1211, and `Save-State` is at 1213, outside any conditional. So the release is recorded as published whether or not the push succeeded, and the only consequence of failure is the red text at 1215-1219. There are two distinct failure paths that both reach Save-State: no git identity in the clone (1186-1200, which sets $pushFailed = 'no git identity' after printing the commands to run by hand) and a non-zero push exit (1208). The message at 1216-1218 is honest about what happened, saying the assets are on GitHub but version.json is not, so a person who reads it knows; the defect is that the next Ship does not, because the state file already says done.

**Read first:** 1) tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 1165-1226, the push block and Save-State, which is the whole finding in sixty lines. 2) The definition of Save-State and its matching load, earlier in the same file (search for `function Save-State`), so you know what the state file records and what a "failed" state would have to look like. 3) tools\Ship.ps1 lines 500-600, the caller, to see how Ship decides there is nothing to do and what it would need to read to retry.

**How:** Move `Save-State` at line 1213 inside an `if (-not $pushFailed) { }`, and in the else branch write a state entry marked failed, carrying the id and the assets already uploaded, so the next run can finish rather than start again. Then make the retry real: at the top of the publish, if the last state is failed, skip straight to the push for that id instead of minting a new one at line 516. The upload side is already idempotent enough for this, since 710-716 falls back from gh release create to gh release upload --clobber when the release exists.

**Done when:** Reproduce it locally without publishing anything: run the publish path against a scratch clone with no git identity configured, which takes the 1186-1200 branch deterministically, and confirm the state file records a failure rather than a success and that a second run resumes the same id. Then configure an identity and confirm the second run completes and the state flips to published. `powershell -NoProfile -Command "$null = [scriptblock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw tools/Publish-Update.ps1))"` proves it still parses.

**Traps:** Publish-Update.ps1 is named in CLAUDE.md as a file that needs a line in #dev before anybody edits it, because both machines touch it and seven incidents have already happened inside its rules file. Do not make the retry mint a new id: the whole point is that the assets for the old id are already on GitHub, and a new id orphans them and grows the feed further, which is entry 59. The no-identity branch already prints the exact commands to finish by hand; keep that, because it is what somebody will use the first time the automatic retry misbehaves. Ship.ps1 calls this, and a main Ship commits and pushes the source afterwards, so a half-published release leaves main and the feed disagreeing about what shipped, which is exactly the state CLAUDE.md's one-complete-release rule exists to prevent. A person runs the publish; a Claude never does.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases

## 29. Ship commits everything on disk straight to main after publishing, bypassing the pull request rule

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The behaviour is verified exactly. tools\Ship.ps1:646-661 is `if (-not $DryRun -and -not $Dev ...)` then `git -C $RepoDir add -A`, `git commit -q -m "$target`: $Notes"`, `git push -q origin HEAD`. There is no dirty-tree refusal anywhere in the script. The two counts are stale: local history today holds 11 commits matching `N.N.N:` (newest ddfd2da 1.1.47) and 59 merge commits, not "ten version commits beside 53 merges". One more precision: Ship pushes `HEAD`, not literally main. The preflight at Ship.ps1:153-166 computes `$dirty` and `$branch`, prints them at :159, and only Dies when main is behind origin (:162); a dirty tree is never a reason to stop.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** doneWhen: "git -C C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo grep -n 'add -A' -- tools/Ship.ps1 returns nothing"  ->  This test proves the opposite of the entry's own fix and would break a settled rule. The howTo only adds a dirty-tree Die; it never proposes touching git add -A. Making that grep return nothing means deleting tools\Ship.ps1:650, which is the line that stages the release so :653 can commit it and :656 can push it. CLAUDE.md states the requirement plainly: 'A main Ship commits and pushes the source when the release is out, so the other machine can git pull exactly what shipped.' Passing this doneWhen would silently end the two-machine sync between Patrick's machine and Djinn's.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** 'There is no dirty-tree refusal anywhere in the script' and 'a dirty tree is never a reason to stop'  ->  True as stated but it omits the line that matters. tools\Ship.ps1:163 is a deliberate branch: 'if ($dirty.Count) { Say "  the uncommitted changes are what this release is built from; they are committed and pushed when it is out" }'. Building the release from the working tree and committing afterwards is the designed behaviour, backed by CLAUDE.md's rule about a main Ship pushing the source. Note also that the file's own header at :143-146 says a main release 'comes off main, with nothing uncommitted and nothing unpulled', so Ship.ps1 contradicts itself, which is a more useful thing to hand a reader than 'nobody ever wrote one'. Either way this is a workflow change for a line in #dev first, not the small defensive add the entry presents.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo: 'In the if ($branch -eq 'main') arm at :161-163, beside the existing $behind Die, add a dirty-tree Die'  ->  No such arm exists. tools\Ship.ps1:160 is 'if (-not $Dev) {' and :161 is 'if ($branch -ne 'main') { Die "A main release ships from main..." }'. Relatedly, the claim earlier in the same entry that the preflight 'only Dies when main is behind origin (:162)' skips that Die at :161 for a non-main branch.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md, section "How work moves between the two machines" - it is the rule this row says is being bypassed, and it also says only Patrick's machine ships main. 2. tools\Ship.ps1:145-170 - the preflight that already computes $dirty and $branch, so the fix goes in an existing block rather than a new one. 3. tools\Ship.ps1:640-665 - the Source block itself.

**How:** Two edits, both in tools\Ship.ps1. (a) In the `if ($branch -eq 'main')` arm at :161-163, beside the existing `$behind` Die, add a dirty-tree Die, for example `if ($dirty.Count) { Die ("the working tree has {0} uncommitted change(s). Commit them on a branch and merge, then ship." -f $dirty.Count) }`. Keep it inside the main arm so a dev Ship from a branch still works, because CLAUDE.md explicitly allows a dev Ship from any branch. (b) Delete the Source block at :646-661 (or reduce it to `git -C $RepoDir tag` plus `git push origin <tag>`, which records the release without committing anything). The version bump itself then travels as a normal pull request. Note the Say helper is used for all output; do not introduce Write-Host.

**Done when:** Without running Ship: `git -C C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo grep -n 'add -A' -- tools/Ship.ps1` returns nothing, and `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[void][System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile('C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship.ps1',[ref]$null,[ref]$e); $e"` prints no errors (this is the same parse gate .github/workflows/build.yml runs). With Ship: show Patrick the command `.\tools\Ship.ps1 -DryRun "guard test"` with a scratch file left in the tree, and it must stop at the preflight naming the uncommitted count instead of reaching the Source head. Do not run it yourself - CLAUDE.md puts Ship.ps1 in the person's hands.

**Traps:** Ship.ps1 is not on the #dev-first list in CLAUDE.md (that list is Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs and Publish-Update.ps1), but this changes how releases leave the machine on both machines, so it is still worth a line in #dev. HANDOFF.md:12-15 warns that `git status` shows every .ps1 and .md as modified because the working copies are CRLF and the commits are LF; `git diff --ignore-cr-at-eol` is empty. A naive `$dirty.Count` taken from plain `git status --porcelain` would therefore Die on every single Ship on this machine. Check what Ship.ps1:154-155 actually computes before you trust it, and if it is a plain porcelain count, the guard needs the same line-ending tolerance. Do not "fix" the line endings and do not commit them.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-status , https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_pr_create , https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/releasing-projects-on-github/managing-releases-in-a-repository

## 30. No check would have caught any of the five incidents of 17 September, and the cheapest one is the highest value check in the repository

**Category:** GitHub & CI  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: 'Invoke-Pester tools\tests passes locally and the new job shows green on a pull request.'  ->  There is no tools\tests directory and no Pester test anywhere in the repository. A recursive grep for 'pester' or a Describe block across tools\*.ps1 returns nothing, and .github\workflows\build.yml has only two steps, a PowerShell parse pass and a csc build. Invoke-Pester tools\tests errors on a missing path; it does not pass. There is also no 'new job' to be green until one is written.

**Challenged (minor):** '$ChunkCap only splits at 2 GB (see the Say at :552'  ->  $ChunkCap = 1800MB at tools\Publish-Update.ps1:529, used at :542. The 2 GB figure is only the wording of the Say at :552 explaining why GitHub refuses a larger asset. Anyone reusing $ChunkCap as a size gate on the strength of this sentence would be off by 248 MB. Worth adding that :628 already Dies on an oversized single pack, so the gap the entry is right about is total release size, not per-pack size.

**Read first:** 1. .github/workflows/build.yml - all 47 lines; it is two steps (parse every tools\**\*.ps1, then run Build-Launcher.ps1 and assert Thornswood.exe exists) plus an artifact upload. Nothing else runs on a pull request. 2. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1:1-130 - the header names the incident that forced it into one file (the installer carried Data\Platform\PluginsNoLoad\auth-data-no-load.js, the SkyMP saved sign-in token from the build machine) and each list entry carries the incident that added it. 3. tools\Publish-Update.ps1:250-260 - the recorded 11.3 GB near-miss.

**How:** Add one job to .github/workflows/build.yml. Start with the size gate because it is the cheapest: a step that reads the built _dist artifact (or, in CI, a synthetic file list) and fails when the total exceeds a cap. In the shipping path the same guard belongs in tools\Publish-Update.ps1 near line 541 where $currentBytes and $ChunkCap already exist: $ChunkCap only splits at 2 GB (see the Say at :552), it never refuses a release, so add a total-bytes Die before the split loop. For the regression tests, dot-source tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 in a Pester file under tools\tests\ and assert Test-ShipRuntime, Test-ShipMachine and Test-ShipOtherServer against the exact relative paths named in its own comments: 'Claude outputs\report.md', 'Data\SKSE\Plugins\SSEDisplayTweaks.ini', 'Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client-settings.txt', 'Data\SKSE\Plugins\OpenAnimationReplacer.ini'. Every one of those is a real incident already written into the file, so the test is transcription rather than invention.

**Done when:** `Invoke-Pester tools\tests` passes locally and the new job shows green on a pull request. For the size gate specifically, a unit test that feeds 11,368 MB into the guard function and asserts it throws - that is the exact number the incident recorded, so the test is the incident. No game or server needed for any of it.

**Traps:** Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list; both machines touch it, so post the problem, the approach and what it touches in #dev before editing it. The CI runner is windows-latest and the build uses the .NET Framework 4 csc, so anything the test does must work in Windows PowerShell semantics, not just pwsh 7. The parse step already recurses tools with -Recurse, so a Pester file placed under tools\ will itself be parsed by the existing gate - keep it syntactically clean or you break the check you are adding. Five separate 17 September incidents are recorded in comments at Publish-Update.ps1:253, Ship.ps1:275, Ship.ps1:359, Ship-Rules.ps1:80 and Ship-Rules.ps1:123; read all five before deciding what a regression test should assert, because each one names the exact file that leaked.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/actions/writing-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions , https://pester.dev/docs/quick-start , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/invoke-pester

## 31. Every launcher spends half the GitHub hourly quota checking for updates, and two people on one connection spend all of it

**Category:** Packaging  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, with one scope nuance the row does not state. The timer is real: tools\app\Shell.cs:1199-1200 sets a DispatcherTimer at 2 seconds and :1210 is `if (++inPlayTicks >= 60) { inPlayTicks = 0; CheckUpdatesInPlay(); }`, which is every two minutes, 30 calls an hour. But that line is reached only after `if (!playing) return;` (:1206) and `if (Checks.GameRunning())` (:1207), so the polling happens while somebody is in a session, not while the launcher sits idle. The two other callers of Updater.Check are Shell.cs:3993 (on demand/background) and CheckUpdatesInPlay at Shell.cs:4163-4180. The endpoint is confirmed at Updater.cs:154-155: `https://api.github.com/repos/<repo>/contents/version.json?ref=<branch>`, the REST Contents API, which is 60 requests an hour per address unauthenticated. Updater.cs:184-196 already handles the 403/429 case and its own comment names the shared-address problem in so many words.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo (a): 'Raise the interval: Shell.cs:1210, change 60 to 450 (fifteen minutes at a two-second tick) or 900 (thirty). One digit.'  ->  This is not one digit, it defeats the update countdown. tools\app\Shell.cs:4203-4209: the first in-play check only records updateSeenAt and logs 'In five minutes the game closes so the launcher can install it'; a SECOND, later check is what actually closes the game, gated on (DateTime.Now - updateSeenAt).TotalMinutes >= 5. At 60 ticks that second check lands 2 to 4 minutes later, inside the server's window (tools\Ship.ps1:44, $AnnounceMinutes = 5; :667 'Everyone in the world has 5 minutes, then they are put back to the launcher'). At 450 ticks the person is not told for up to 15 minutes and the game does not close for up to 30, long after the server has already put them back. Shell.cs:4169-4175 is a written comment about this exact failure class: a check that did not come back 'wiped the five minute clock every two minutes and the countdown the server was already running never ran out on this machine'. Only howTo (b), moving the feed off api.github.com, is safe here, and it needs no parse change because Updater.cs:42 already sends Accept: application/vnd.github.raw and Config.cs:424 parses raw JSON.

**Challenged (minor):** Line citations across the four 'I opened the file and read the line' entries (#31, #32, #36, #37)  ->  A run of off-by-one to off-by-three citations, which matters in entries whose whole claim is that the line was read. #31: the DispatcherTimer is tools\app\Shell.cs:1198-1199, not 1199-1200, and the Contents API URL is tools\app\Updater.cs:152-153, not 154-155. #32: 'have == size' is tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:752 and 'have > size' is :753, not :749 and :750; the length throw is :807-809, not :806-808; Process.Start is :1026 so the unpack block is :979-1026. #36: 'while (true)' is tools\app\Downgrade.cs:271, not :272; the ten-minute warning is :278-282; the twenty-minute condition is :293 and its break is :299, not ':297'; 'if (!Directory.Exists(folder)) return 0;' is :704, not :703. #37: Downgrade.Start is tools\app\Shell.cs:1626, not :1624, and the same entry gives :1619 for RunDowngrade, which is right, so its two numbers disagree with each other.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Updater.cs:141-205 - Check(), the request itself, the repo-candidate fallback that can double the call, and the existing rate-limit handling. 2. tools\app\Shell.cs:1196-1215 - the timer, and the guards above line 1210 that scope it to a live session. 3. tools\app\Updater.cs:369-418 - the host pinning, so a new feed host is added to the allow list rather than around it.

**How:** Three separable changes. (a) Raise the interval: Shell.cs:1210, change 60 to 450 (fifteen minutes at a two-second tick) or 900 (thirty). One digit. (b) Move the feed off the quota: serve version.json from the site (tools\docs-app) or from the public Thornswood-Releases raw host, and point Updater.Check at it instead of api.github.com. Whatever host you pick has to be added to the pinned host list in Updater.cs:369-418 or the request is refused by the launcher's own check. (c) Cache with an ETag: store the last ETag in Config, send If-None-Match, and treat 304 as no change. A 304 still counts against the quota on the REST API, which is the other reason to move off it.

**Done when:** Compile check only, no game needed: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <a scratch folder> -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe, which is the same gate .github/workflows/build.yml runs. For the behaviour, read Profile\launcher.log after a session and count the update-check lines over a known wall-clock span - at 450 ticks there should be about four an hour, not thirty. C:\Thornswood\Profile\launcher.log is readable without starting anything.

**Traps:** Shell.cs and Updater.cs are both on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list; a line in #dev before you touch either. C# 5 only in tools\app: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var - Updater.cs is already written that way, keep it that way. Do not raise the interval so far that a release announcement is missed: the Ship flow counts people down and puts them back to the launcher to collect an update, so the in-play check has a job. And the download path is separate from the check path: Updater already takes assets through browser_download_url, which has no quota, so this row is only about how often the feed is polled and from where. Entry 59 shrinks what each check downloads; the two changes are complementary and neither replaces the other.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/using-the-rest-api/rate-limits-for-the-rest-api , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/contents , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/ETag , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/If-None-Match

## 32. The 527 MB payload is downloaded and executed without any integrity check

**Category:** Packaging  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs Download() runs 720-810; the only integrity test in it is length - `if (size > 0 && have == size)` at :749 to skip, `if (size > 0 && have > size)` at :750 to restart, and `if (size > 0 && got != size) throw` at :806-808. The unpack-and-run block at :979-1026 opens the zip, extracts with a path-traversal fence at :991-992, and then `Process.Start` on Setup.bat from inside the unpacked payload at :1019-1025. A case-insensitive grep for sha256 or hash across the whole file returns one hit, a comment at :85 about SmartScreen reputation. No hash is fetched, computed or compared anywhere.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo (2): 'tools\docs-app\server.js:1006-1010, add sha256: best.sha to the sendJson payload (read it from files.json rather than hashing 527 MB per request)'  ->  best.sha is undefined, so the field would vanish and the new Bootstrap check would silently never fire. In tools\docs-app\server.js the /api/payload route at :992 builds best at :999 as { name, at: st.mtimeMs, size: st.size } from fs.statSync, and nowhere in that route is files.json read (the only files.json read is at :312, in an unrelated listing path). JSON.stringify drops undefined properties, so the route keeps returning the same three fields and Bootstrap's mismatch branch is dead code. The route has to load files.json and look the name up. Two smaller problems in the doneWhen: the route only matches /^Thornswood-Setup-.*\.zip$/i at :996, which the local C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip filename does not match, and Get-FileHash returns uppercase hex while node's crypto digest is lowercase, so 'must be the same string' fails on case even when the bytes agree.

**Read first:** 1. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:716-812 - the download, so you see exactly which checks exist. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:992-1010 - the /api/payload route that hands Bootstrap {name, url, size}; the hash has to be added to this JSON. 3. tools\app\Updater.cs:405-418 - how the launcher already verifies a pack's checksum, because the installer should match that standard rather than invent a second one.

**How:** Three coordinated edits. (1) Publisher: in the script that writes the payload into the site's files folder, compute `(Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 <zip>).Hash` and write it into files.json beside name and size. (2) Site: tools\docs-app\server.js:1006-1010, add `sha256: best.sha` to the sendJson payload (read it from files.json rather than hashing 527 MB per request). (3) Bootstrap: in Download(), parse it with the existing `Between(doc, "\"sha256\":\"", "\"")` helper at :814, and after the length check at :806 stream the file through `System.Security.Cryptography.SHA256.Create()` and compare case-insensitively; on mismatch `File.Delete(target)` and throw a sentence a person can act on, in the style of the messages already there.

**Done when:** No game needed. `(Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip).Hash` and the value the site returns from GET /api/payload must be the same string. Then flip one byte in a copy of the zip and confirm Bootstrap deletes it and says so. The build gate is `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1` compiling Bootstrap.cs clean.

**Traps:** Bootstrap.cs is C# 5 built with the .NET Framework 4 csc, same as tools\app - no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. SHA256.Create() exists in .NET 4, but hash the file with a streaming read; do not ReadAllBytes 527 MB. This has to ship as one complete release: an old Bootstrap meeting a site that now returns sha256 must still work (it will, because Between returns "" for a missing key - keep the check conditional on a non-empty value), and a new Bootstrap meeting a site that does not yet return it must not hard-fail. Do not sneak this in alongside entry 54 (Range support) as one change; they touch the same route and CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule applies. Deploy-Docs and Publish-Installer are scripts the person runs, not you.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.security.cryptography.sha256 , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/get-filehash , https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html#crypto_class_hash

## 33. The installer is a snapshot of your game folder with your home folder path baked into the script, and it is stale by design

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and the "five releases behind" figure is exactly right as of now. The hard-coded path is tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:491, `$vLive = Join-Path 'C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Releases' 'version.json'`, with a fallback at :492 to the repo parent's version.json if that path is missing. Measured: C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip was last written 16 Sep 22:07 and is 527,227,436 bytes; version.json carries 192 pack entries over 188 distinct release stamps, of which exactly five are newer than the zip - 20260917-0024, 20260917-0102, 20260917-0230, 20260917-0925 and 20260917-1647. The Ship.ps1:453-499 block computes that same count and Show-Installer prints it in yellow; it never refuses.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** 'with a fallback at :492 to the repo parent's version.json if that path is missing'  ->  It is the repo ROOT, not the repo parent, and that distinction is the dangerous part. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:450 sets $here = Split-Path -Parent $MyInvocation.MyCommand.Path, which is ...\Thornswood-Repo\tools, so :492's Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent $here) 'version.json' resolves to C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\version.json. That file exists: 59,496 bytes, last written 15 Sep, 164 packs, and zero of its stamps are newer than the zip. So if the hard-coded Releases path is ever absent, the fallback silently seeds applied.txt from a feed 28 packs stale and Ship reports 'behind 0'. The proposed derived path is correct arithmetic, but the fix has to drop or repoint that fallback too, which the write-up does not say. The rest of #33 is exact: the literal at :491, the zip at 527,227,436 bytes and 16 Sep 22:07, and 192 packs over 188 stamps with exactly those five newer.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:485-515 - the applied.txt seeding, which is why a fresh install survives the 164 dead pack urls in entry 11; changing this changes that too. 2. tools\Ship.ps1:445-500 - the behind-count and Show-Installer, including the comment explaining why the count was moved above the early return. 3. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 - Package-Thornswood dot-sources it, so what the installer may carry is decided there, not here.

**How:** Smallest useful change first: at Package-Thornswood.ps1:491, swap the literal for a derived path. The script already has $here; use `$vLive = Join-Path (Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent (Split-Path -Parent $here)) 'Thornswood-Releases') 'version.json'` and keep the existing fallback at :492, or take a -ReleasesDir parameter defaulting to that. Second change: in Ship.ps1, turn the yellow Show-Installer warning into a Die on the main channel when $behind is greater than zero, leaving dev alone - the $behind variable and $haveZip are already in scope at :454-478, so it is a couple of lines beside the existing Head 'The installer' block. The long-term answer named in the row (a bootstrap that fetches the current manifest and syncs) is a separate, larger change and should not ride along.

**Done when:** Run the derived-path expression on its own and confirm it resolves to the same file: `Test-Path (Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent (Split-Path -Parent 'C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools')) 'Thornswood-Releases\version.json')` returns True. Then confirm the behind-count still reads 5 by comparing `(Get-Item C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip).LastWriteTime` against the pack ids in version.json. Both are read-only and need nothing started.

**Traps:** Package-Thornswood.ps1 is the installer builder and takes about twenty minutes to run (Ship.ps1:499 says so); it is Patrick's to run, not yours. It also writes applied.txt from the live version.json - if you break that path, a fresh install stops being seeded and every one of the 192 packs becomes a pending update on first launch, 164 of which 404 because that repo is now private (entry 11). The fallback at :492 hides a broken primary path silently, so test the primary explicitly rather than trusting that packaging succeeded. Nothing here may be mixed with the own-game-folder branch shape: CLAUDE.md says the two shapes must not be mixed in one release, and what ships today is the Steam copy with Vortex.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/split-path , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/join-path

## 34. The server half is not part of the release, and nothing ever compares the client build against the server build

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. A grep of tools\gamemode.js for build, protocol or version turns up nothing that stamps a release: the only `versionMajor` (line 2556) is the SkyMP manifest document's own field being copied through when the gamemode republishes manifest.json. There is no handshake field, no refusal and no mismatch message anywhere in the file. One qualifier worth carrying: the launcher does now compare content - Verify.cs:240-283 checks every non-storefront plugin's size and CRC-32 against the server's manifest and Fix downloads the server's copy (HANDOFF.md:22-27 records this confirmed against real bytes). So "nothing ever compares the client build against the server build" is true of the release version, not of the game files.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: 'after a deploy, curl https://<site>/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json (read-only GET, safe) returns a build string'  ->  That URL will not return the manifest, so the test proves nothing. tools\gamemode.js:2528 sets KEY = 'thornswood' and :2537-2538 write it to path.join(dataDir, 'api', 'servers', KEY, 'manifest.json'), which the GAME SERVER serves from its own data folder, not the site. tools\app\AutoJoin.cs:163 sets the client's master to 'http://' + host + ':' + ResourcePort(port), and LIST entry 2 in this same document says the data folder answers on TCP 3000 (dev 7788). The read-only check is http://<game server host>:<resource port>/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json over plain HTTP. As written the write-up contradicts entry 2 of its own list.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo (1): 'expose it in the manifest document the gamemode already publishes at gamemode.js:2520-2560 (add a thornswoodBuild field next to versionMajor)'  ->  The line numbers are right (versionMajor is tools\gamemode.js:2556, inside the out object at :2555-2558) but the write-up never says what that document is. It is the load order manifest the client compares against, served at /api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json per the comment at :2495 and read by loadOrderVerificationService, which is the same file LIST entry 40 is about. Adding an unknown key is almost certainly harmless to that parser, but a reader coming to this cold should be told they are editing the load order document and that its shape is what every client checks, especially since the doneWhen's wrong URL means a broken shape would not be caught by the test offered.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:2490-2560 - the manifest publishing block, which is the existing place where the server tells the client about itself and therefore the natural place to add a build field. 2. tools\Ship.ps1:145-220 - where $target (the release version) is computed, because that is the string that has to reach the server. 3. tools\app\Shell.cs:1605-1610 - the status line that already renders `plugins ... game <build> ... launcher <version>` and is where a server build would be shown.

**How:** Three pieces, and they must go out together. (1) Stamp the server: have tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 write the release version into the gamemode at deploy time, or read it from server-settings.json, and expose it in the manifest document the gamemode already publishes at gamemode.js:2520-2560 (add a `thornswoodBuild` field next to versionMajor). (2) Read it client-side: the launcher already fetches that manifest in Verify.Manifest (tools\app\Verify.cs:220-237), so surface the build alongside the plugin data and show it on the status line built at Shell.cs:1605-1609. (3) Refuse on mismatch, with a named reason, in the gamemode's connect path rather than silently. Make Ship refuse a main release when the deployed server build is behind, using the same shape as the installer behind-count at Ship.ps1:454-499.

**Done when:** Without starting anything: after a deploy, `curl https://<site>/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json` (read-only GET, safe) returns a build string that equals the version in the launcher's own Updater.Version and in version.json. And the launcher compiles: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause`.

**Traps:** Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 and Deploy-Server.ps1 talk to the VPS - CLAUDE.md puts those in the person's hands, you show the command. The failure this row exists to prevent is real and named: the lockpick fix went out client-first. So a build check that refuses is only safe if client and server ship together, which is CLAUDE.md's "every change goes out as one complete release: launcher, game files and server together, never a half". Be careful adding a hard refusal before the deploy path is atomic (entries 18 and 19): a stricter handshake on top of a deploy that stops the live server first and checks less than the dev path turns a bad deploy into a locked-out evening. Note also entry 1 - the dev gamemode currently talks to the live voice service on 7778, so anything you test on dev can touch live state until that is fixed.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://semver.org/

## 35. Nothing stops Steam putting Skyrim back on the wrong build

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:371-372 is exactly two advisory log lines: "Skyrim is on 1.6.1170. N MB of downloaded copies removed" and "Do not start Skyrim through Steam from here on: Steam would update it back, and you would be here again. Play from this launcher." A recursive grep for appmanifest across .cs, .ps1 and .js in the whole repo returns nothing. One thing the row's proposed fix understates: AutoUpdateBehavior 1 means "only update this game when I launch it", so it stops background updates but Steam still updates on a launch from Steam - which is the exact thing line 372 already asks people not to do. It is a strong mitigation, not a lock.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: 'Select-String -Path "<steam>\steamapps\appmanifest_489830.acf" -Pattern AutoUpdateBehavior shows "1" after the launcher has run the downgrade'  ->  That check can pass and the setting still not stick, so it does not prove the thing. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:413 opens steam://open/console and the whole downgrade only proceeds while Steam is the foreground window (:441, :452, :454), so Steam is running throughout. Steam holds app state in memory and rewrites appmanifest_489830.acf on exit and on state changes, which can revert an external edit with no sign. The test has to be re-run after Steam has been fully closed and reopened, and the writer at Downgrade.cs:370-374 should either wait for Steam to close or re-verify afterwards. The rest of #35 is right, including that AutoUpdateBehavior 1 is 'only update when launched' and therefore a mitigation rather than a lock.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:36-50 - AppId 489830 and the three depot/manifest pairs, so you know which file to write into and that the same three ids live in Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and Build-Thornswood.ps1. 2. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:315-375 - the apply-and-clean-up stage, the natural place to write the manifest after a successful downgrade. 3. tools\app\Steam.cs - how the launcher already locates the Steam install, so the path to steamapps is derived rather than guessed.

**How:** In Downgrade.cs, after the success path at :370-374, add a small writer: find `<steam>\steamapps\appmanifest_489830.acf`, read it as text, and replace the `"AutoUpdateBehavior"\s+"0"` line with `"AutoUpdateBehavior"		"1"`, preserving the file's tabs; if the key is absent, insert it inside the top-level AppState block. Write via a temp file and File.Replace so a half-written acf cannot be left behind. Steam must not be running when you write it or it will rewrite the file from memory on exit, so gate on Downgrade.SteamProcess() being absent and say so plainly if it is not. Also add a read-back on launcher start that reports the current value on the status line, so somebody who changed it in Steam's Properties can see it.

**Done when:** Fully checkable without starting the game: `Select-String -Path "<steam>\steamapps\appmanifest_489830.acf" -Pattern AutoUpdateBehavior` shows "1" after the launcher has run the downgrade, and Steam's own Properties then Updates dropdown reads "Only update this game when I launch it". Build gate: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause`.

**Traps:** Downgrade.cs is in tools\app: C# 5 only, no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. The acf is Valve's KeyValues format, tab-delimited, not JSON or INI - a regex replacement that normalises whitespace can leave Steam unable to parse it, and a broken appmanifest is a much worse failure than an unwanted update. Never rewrite the file while Steam is running. This is the Steam copy, which under CLAUDE.md's settled design is what the own-game-folder shape is built from, so it matters for both shapes and must not be made conditional on one of them. And Downgrade.cs is closely tied to entries 36 and 37; all three touch the same file, and CLAUDE.md wants one fix per pull request, so split them.

**Links:** https://developer.valvesoftware.com/wiki/KeyValues , https://steamdb.info/app/489830/depots/

## 36. The Downgrade watch loop has no timeout and spins forever when Steam refuses

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. The `while (true)` is at tools\app\Downgrade.cs:272, inside the cited 262-305 range. The only clock in it is `double quiet = (DateTime.Now - lastGrowth).TotalMinutes` at :277 - the sole TotalMinutes hit in the whole file - which drives a one-shot ten-minute warning at :278-283 and a twenty-minute "taken as done" break at :297. That break is gated on `size > 0`, so if Steam never writes a byte the folder stays at zero, the gate never opens, and the loop runs forever on the two-second sleep at :304. There is no wall-clock deadline and no named failure. FolderSize at :699-712 does return 0 for a folder that does not exist (`if (!Directory.Exists(folder)) return 0;` at :703), which is what keeps size at zero in that case.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: 'before the while (true) at :272 add DateTime deadline = DateTime.Now.AddMinutes(15); and inside the loop, before the sleep, add a check like if (DateTime.Now > deadline && size == lastSize)'  ->  size == lastSize is unconditionally true at that point, so the guard collapses to a flat wall-clock abort. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:275 is 'if (size != lastSize) { lastSize = size; lastGrowth = DateTime.Now; warned = false; }' at the TOP of the loop, so by the time control reaches the sleep at :304, lastSize has already been set to size. deadline is computed once before the loop and never reset, so the suggested code kills any downgrade at 15 minutes regardless of progress. The three depots total roughly 12 GB; on a normal home connection that is well past 15 minutes, so this aborts healthy downloads. The file already holds the correct signal: lastGrowth at :268 and double quiet at :277, which is what a no-progress deadline must use. The row's own wording ('Ten or fifteen minutes with no progress') is right and the howTo dropped the 'with no progress' part.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:255-310 - the loop, the ten and twenty minute branches, and the comment explaining why a full-and-still folder from an aborted run must not be taken as done. 2. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:690-712 - IsComplete and FolderSize, the two functions the loop's conditions depend on. 3. tools\app\Shell.cs:1619-1640 - RunDowngrade, where the status callback lands, so the named failure has somewhere to be shown.

**How:** In Downgrade.cs, before the `while (true)` at :272 add `DateTime deadline = DateTime.Now.AddMinutes(15);` and inside the loop, before the sleep, add a check like `if (DateTime.Now > deadline && size == lastSize) { log("Steam has not downloaded anything for fifteen minutes. The command was typed and Steam has not answered. Depot " + depot + " is still at " + (size / 1048576) + " MB. Try the manual route: <the command>, or press Downgrade again."); return false; }`. Reset the deadline whenever size grows, beside the existing `lastGrowth = DateTime.Now` at :275, so a slow but live download is never cut off. Keep the existing ten and twenty minute branches; they are useful and this only adds the floor under them.

**Done when:** Compile gate: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe. Behaviour gate without starting Steam: temporarily point AppId or the depot folder at a path that will never fill, confirm the status line reports the named failure and the worker thread ends, then read Profile\launcher.log for the sentence. C:\Thornswood\Profile\launcher.log is readable at any time.

**Traps:** C# 5 in tools\app - no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var; build the message with + concatenation, which is what the file already does. Do not tighten the twenty-minute `size > 0 && !Directory.Exists(downloading)` branch at :291-302 while you are in here: the comment above it records that treating a full-and-still folder as done once wrote the completion marker over truncated files, after which every later attempt skipped the depot as already downloaded. That is a bug that was found and fixed; do not reintroduce it. Downgrade.cs is in the launcher, which under CLAUDE.md ships as one complete release with the game files and the server. This is one of three rows on the same file (35, 36, 37) - one fix per pull request.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.diagnostics.stopwatch , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.datetime.now

## 37. Downgrade drives Valve's own window by synthesised keystrokes at a measured pixel offset, with no consent screen

**Category:** Trust  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in every particular. Three depot/manifest pairs are hardcoded at tools\app\Downgrade.cs:41-45 (489831/8442952117333549665, 489832/8042843504692938467, 489833/1914580699073641964), and the comment says the same three live in Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and Build-Thornswood.ps1. TypeIntoConsole at :411-471 launches steam://open/console, brings Steam to the front (retrying up to twenty seconds and reopening the console once), confirms the foreground window is Steam, clicks the command line, sends Ctrl+A, Backspace, types the command and presses Enter twice. The 72-pixel offset is documented in the ClickCommandLine doc comment at :532-541 and scaled by the DPI ratio. Shell.cs:1619 RunDowngrade goes from the button straight to Downgrade.Start with no confirmation dialog of any kind - confirmed, there is no consent screen.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** 'Verified in every particular', endorsing the row's 'DepotDownloader, Valve's own SteamKit-based tool, which takes app, depot and manifest as arguments and needs no window at all'  ->  DepotDownloader is not Valve's. It is a community project by SteamRE under GPL-2.0, which the entry's own first link (github.com/SteamRE/DepotDownloader) states on its front page. Two things follow that the write-up never mentions. First, its README says it uses an anonymous account by default and needs -username plus a password for restricted content; Skyrim Special Edition depots 489831, 489832 and 489833 (tools\app\Downgrade.cs:41-45) are owned-game depots, so the 'better answer' asks every customer to type their Steam username, password and Steam Guard code into a bundled third-party binary. That is a worse trust posture than the thing entry 37 objects to, not a better one. Second, shipping a GPL-2.0 binary lands straight in entries 10 and 87, which say there is no source offer and no notices file anywhere in either repository. Everything else in #37 does check out: Depots at :41-45, TypeIntoConsole at :411, the 72-pixel offset at :536 and :557, the Ctrl+A, Backspace, type, Enter twice sequence, and no consent screen on the path from Shell.cs:869 to RunDowngrade at :1619.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Downgrade.cs:405-475 - the whole typing routine, including the two comments recording the 17 Sep failures it was built to survive (the command typed into the page instead of the input, and the command typed twice onto itself producing "...8442952117333549665download_depot..."). 2. tools\app\Shell.cs:1611-1650 - RunDowngrade and the callbacks, where a dialog would go. 3. tools\ui.xaml - the Downgrade button and the existing card and dialog styles, so a consent screen looks like the rest of the launcher.
