**How:** Minimum viable, and it is genuinely small: in Shell.cs RunDowngrade, before `Downgrade.Start(...)` at :1624, put a MessageBox or a styled in-app card that names exactly what will happen - that Steam's window will be brought to the front, that three commands will be typed into Steam's developer console, the literal text of the first one, and that about 12 GB will be downloaded - with Continue and Cancel, and return on Cancel. The better answer the row names is DepotDownloader: it takes `-app 489830 -depot 489831 -manifest 8442952117333549665` on the command line and needs no window at all, so Downgrade.cs's TypeIntoConsole, ClickCommandLine, BringToFront, Chord, Key and Type all disappear and the depot ids at :41-45 become arguments instead of keystrokes. That is a real project, not an afternoon, and it changes what the installer has to carry.

**Done when:** For the consent screen: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause` compiles, and a code read confirms every path into Downgrade.Start passes through the dialog - `git grep -n 'Downgrade.Start' -- tools/app` should show one call site. You cannot press the button here; CLAUDE.md forbids starting the launcher. For DepotDownloader: run it yourself outside the launcher with those exact arguments and confirm the depot lands, before any launcher code changes.

**Traps:** C# 5 only in tools\app. Downgrade.cs is not on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list, but automating another company's user interface in a shipping product is exactly the kind of thing the rules say goes to #dev as a few lines first: the problem, the approach, what it touches. DepotDownloader is GPL-adjacent tooling with its own licence and its own Steam login flow - read its README before assuming it can be bundled, and note entry 10 already flags that this project ships GPLv3 code with no source offer, so adding another redistributed binary without checking terms makes that row worse. Do not remove the keystroke path until DepotDownloader is proven on both machines; the comments at :445-465 record two separate ways the console path failed in one day, and losing the fallback before the replacement works means nobody can downgrade at all.

**Links:** https://github.com/SteamRE/DepotDownloader , https://github.com/SteamRE/DepotDownloader/blob/master/README.md , https://steamdb.info/app/489830/depots/

## 38. The Nexus collection carries no plugin order, so a fresh install lands on whatever LOOT produces

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** unverifiable

**Correction:** The Nexus half could not be checked from here and should not be marked verified. There is no collection.json anywhere in the repo or on this machine (a filesystem search for collection*.json returns nothing), so "plugin rules empty", "97 plugins with one listed twice" and "differs from the server at 48 of 55 positions" are all statements about a file only Nexus and Vortex hold. What I can confirm is the comparison target: C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt is 98 lines, 96 of them enabled (leading asterisk), and it contains no duplicate entries. So whatever duplicate exists is in the collection, not in the reference list. Whoever picks this up should export the collection from Vortex and diff it before quoting either number.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt - the reference list the server expects; 98 lines, 96 enabled. 2. tools\app\Verify.cs:84-133 - what the launcher does when the two disagree, including the positional comparison at :122-133 and the MCM Helper light-flag special case at :115-121 that the collection is known to get wrong. 3. CLAUDE.md, the "Settled design" section - the own-game-folder branch (djinn/own-game-folder) is the alternative this row names, and it is explicitly not on main yet.

**How:** First, get the facts: in Vortex, open the Thornswood profile, export or open the collection, and diff its plugin order against C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt position by position. Only then decide. The cheap fix the row proposes is to re-upload the collection with its load order included, which Vortex supports through the collection's own load-order rules, and which is Patrick's action on Nexus, not a code change. If you take the second option instead - ship only the own-folder shape so the reference list is authoritative everywhere - that is the djinn/own-game-folder branch and a much larger decision.

**Done when:** Checkable without the game: after re-uploading, install the collection into a clean Vortex profile and confirm the resulting plugins.txt matches C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt line for line, ignoring the leading asterisk - `Compare-Object (Get-Content a) (Get-Content b)` returns nothing. Second gate: the launcher's Scan reports no load-order issue, which you can reason about from Verify.cs:84-133 without pressing Play.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md is explicit: the own-game-folder shape and the Steam-copy-with-Vortex shape must not be mixed in one release, and until djinn/own-game-folder is on main, what ships is the Steam copy with Vortex. So do not reach for the second option as a shortcut. Note also that Verify.cs:115-121 records that the collection installs MCM Helper's plugin without its light flag, which shifts every index after it by one - a collection re-upload that does not also fix that leaves the symptom in place. Anything touching Nexus is Patrick's account and Patrick's action; nothing here is yours to upload. Entry 39 is the other half of this row and entry 40 is what happens when the two lists disagree; read all three before proposing a fix to any one.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/about/vortex/ , https://loot.github.io/

## 39. Seven copies of the list on your machine, written by five tools, and nothing compares any two at Ship time

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and I can name them. On this machine right now: (1) C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, the reference; (2) C:\Thornswood\Profile\OwnGame\plugins.txt; (3) C:\Thornswood\Profile\LocalAppData\plugins.txt; (4) C:\Users\evilp\AppData\Local\Skyrim Special Edition\plugins.txt, the one Skyrim actually reads; (5) C:\Thornswood\_dist\stage\profile\Plugins.txt, what the installer ships; and (6, 7, 8) three Vortex profiles under C:\Users\evilp\AppData\Roaming\Vortex\skyrimse\profiles\*\plugins.txt. So seven is if anything conservative. The Ship half is exact: `grep -n "manifest\|plugins.txt" tools\Ship.ps1` returns nothing at all. Ship.ps1 never mentions either.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\OwnGame.cs:404-428 - SettleSettings, the function that already copies the reference plugins.txt and loadorder.txt into the live location and separately captures Vortex's own copy for the shipping machine; it is the existing map of which copy is which. 2. tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 - the header logic the row says to reuse for deriving the server list. 3. tools\Ship.ps1:290-330 - the existing plugin-freshness gate, which is the precedent for a Ship-time refusal that names the first thing that differs.

**How:** Add a step to tools\Ship.ps1 beside the existing plugin hash check at :298-320. Read the reference list from Get-ShipLoadOrderDir (tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 already exposes exactly this, which is the point of that helper), derive the server's expected order from it using the same header logic Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 has, fetch the live manifest, and compare position by position. On the first difference, Unbump and Die naming the index and both names - the file already has Unbump and Die and the Say colours, so this is roughly ten lines in the house style.

**Done when:** Without shipping anything: `Compare-Object (Get-Content 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt') (Get-Content 'C:\Users\evilp\AppData\Local\Skyrim Special Edition\plugins.txt')` returns nothing today, and returns a named difference when you deliberately reorder one line in a copy. Parse gate: the PowerShell parse step in .github/workflows/build.yml must stay green, which you can run locally with [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile.

**Traps:** Ship-Rules.ps1 already owns the question of where the reference list is read from (Get-ShipLoadOrderDir); do not add a second answer beside it, because the whole reason that file exists is that Publish-Update and Package-Thornswood each had their own list and a sign-in token fell through the gap. Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list. The case of the filename varies (Plugins.txt in _dist\stage\profile, plugins.txt everywhere else) - compare case-insensitively on Windows but do not assume the string matches. And the reason the seven agree today is that the steps happened to run in the right order on one machine; the test you write must fail when they do not, so build it against a deliberately stale copy before trusting a green run.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/compare-object , https://loot.github.io/

## 40. A mismatched session carries on behind a red caption, and the launcher never compares positions at all

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Wrong on the launcher half, and wrong about what the red caption means. (1) Verify.cs is not a name check only. tools\app\Verify.cs:122-133 walks want[i] against have[i] and raises "The load order does not match the server's from X onwards." And Verify.cs:240-283 (Bytes) compares every non-storefront plugin's size and CRC-32 against the server's manifest and records WantIndex, the position, on each Issue. HANDOFF.md:22-27 records this live and confirmed against real bytes (10.bin answered with all 3,157,818 bytes of JKs Skyrim.esp). (2) The caption does not mean a mismatched session. tools\gamemode.js:2493-2512 records that loadOrderVerificationService was asking gateway.skymp.net for a manifest this server was never registered with, getting five 404s and an empty list, and that an empty list is not treated as an error: the count check printed the yellow "more mods than server" line for five seconds and then compared zero entries and passed. That caption is the signature of a failed manifest lookup, not of a mismatch, so the escalation in this row - "he was playing a mismatched session", "treat any report from a session showing that caption as unusable" - does not follow and should be struck. gamemode.js:2520-2560 already republishes data/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json at the path the client asks for, filtered to plugin extensions, which is the fix for that lookup. (3) Still true and still worth doing: nothing disconnects. There is no session refusal on load-order mismatch anywhere in tools\gamemode.js, and the client-side service only captions. (4) Unverifiable: skymp5-client's loadOrderVerificationService.ts is not in this repo or on this machine - the client ships as a patched bundle per CLAUDE.md - so :33-41 and :60-75 could not be checked.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:2490-2560 - the whole manifest block; the comment is the best account anywhere of why the client-side check was doing nothing, and the code below it is the fix. 2. tools\app\Verify.cs:84-133 and :220-283 - what the launcher already checks, so you do not rebuild it. 3. HANDOFF.md:20-27 - the confirmation that the byte check is live, which dates the rest of this row.

**How:** Rewrite the row first; most of its work is already done. What remains is the refusal. Server side: in tools\gamemode.js, have the front plugin send its plugin names with size and CRC on first update, compare against the manifest the gamemode already generates, and refuse the session with one named sentence on a mismatch instead of letting it continue. Client side: tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js already talks to the server (tellServer at :866) and already has the note channel from entry 42, so the refusal message has somewhere to be shown the moment entry 42 lands. Before any of that, confirm on the live server that /api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json now answers with a non-empty mods array - if it does, the caption people have been ignoring should already be gone, and that changes what this row is for.

**Done when:** Read-only and no game needed: `curl -s https://<server>/api/servers/thornswood/manifest.json | head -c 400` returns a mods array with filename, size and crc32 for every plugin, not an empty list or a 404. Then, for the refusal, a unit check that feeds a deliberately altered plugin list into the comparison and asserts a refusal rather than a caption.

**Traps:** This row's premise is stale, so anyone who acts on it as written will spend their time reimplementing Verify.cs. Read the correction above first. The manifest is compared POSITIONALLY by the SkyMP client against Game.getModName, which is plugins only - gamemode.js:2515-2519 warns that a single bsa entry in the manifest shifts everything after it and turns a healthy install into a red load order error, which is why the republish filters on .esp/.esm/.esl. Do not remove that filter. Verify.cs's positional check only runs when the counts are equal and no other issue was found (:122), so a missing plugin masks a reorder; that is a real gap and a better target than "name check only". Any change that moves the load order needs a coordinated client and server release - CLAUDE.md, every change goes out as one complete release. And skymp5-client.js is not built here: CLAUDE.md says the copy in the game folder is patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is, so a fix inside loadOrderVerificationService means a patch in that script, not an edit to a source file you will not find.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Status/404

## 41. An updated reference list only reaches the game folder when a person presses Fix

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** True for the Steam-copy shape, and the row cites two lines that are the half that already works. Verified: tools\app\Updater.cs:517-518 lands any pack entry under `profile\` in Paths.Golden (`dest = Path.Combine(Paths.Golden, Path.GetFileName(rel))`), and the only thing that copies Golden's plugins.txt and loadorder.txt into Paths.Live is the first loop of Verify.Fix at tools\app\Verify.cs:434-441. So an ordinary pack carrying a new reference list does not reach the live location by itself. But OwnGame.cs:297 and :389 are calls to SettleSettings, and SettleSettings at OwnGame.cs:404-416 does copy Golden's plugins.txt and loadorder.txt into Paths.LocalAD every time the own game folder is built or brought up to date. Those two lines are not part of the gap; they are the existing mechanism the fix should mirror.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Verify.cs:428-450 - Fix(), so you can see the first loop is two File.Copy calls and nothing more. 2. tools\app\OwnGame.cs:404-428 - SettleSettings, the working precedent for pushing Golden into the live location. 3. tools\app\Updater.cs:505-525 - where a pack entry is routed, and the fence comment explaining why a game entry must land under the game.

**How:** In the launcher, after a pack finishes applying, if any entry in it started with `profile\`, call the first loop of Verify.Fix. Cleanest shape: extract those six lines from Verify.cs:434-441 into a small `public static int SettleLoadOrder()` in Verify.cs and call it both from Fix() and from the post-pack path in Updater.cs. That way there is one copy of the logic and Fix keeps working exactly as it does now. Set a flag while iterating pack entries at Updater.cs:517-518 so you only do the work when a profile file actually arrived.

**Done when:** Compile gate: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause`. Behaviour gate without starting the game: after an update that carried a profile file, `Compare-Object (Get-Content 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt') (Get-Content 'C:\Users\evilp\AppData\Local\Skyrim Special Edition\plugins.txt')` returns nothing, with nobody having pressed Fix. Read C:\Thornswood\Profile\launcher.log to confirm the step ran and said so.

**Traps:** Updater.cs is on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list - a line in #dev before touching it. C# 5 in tools\app: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Verify.Fix does much more than the first loop (IsolateForeign at :447, FlagLight at :448, then fetching the server's copy of mismatched plugins) - do not call the whole of Fix() after every pack or you will move other servers' clients aside and start downloads without anybody asking. Extract the first loop only. Watch the two shapes: Paths.Live and Paths.LocalAD are not the same thing in the own-game-folder branch, and CLAUDE.md forbids mixing the shapes in one release, so make sure the destination you write to is the one the current shape actually reads. Whatever you change, the reference list moving is a load-order change, which means client and server ship together.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.file.copy

## 42. The on-screen notification channel is written, exported, and never called once

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly. In tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js, `note` is defined at :138-144 (`sp.Debug.notification('ThornsWood: ' + text)` inside a try, then log) and exported at :145 as T.note. `say` is defined at :132-134 as nothing but `log(text)` and exported at :135, and the comment above it at :120-131 says in its own words that the log takes everything and the screen takes nothing "unless somebody deliberately calls the one below". A grep for `.note(` across tools\ts-plugins and tools\ts-ui returns exactly one hit: the export itself. Zero callers.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:118-150 - the whole log/say/note block including the reasoning for why say is log-only, so you route the right things rather than firehosing the corner of the screen. 2. tools\gamemode.js:117-160 - the server's two equivalents, notify (may do nothing outside a Papyrus stack) and say (POSTs to the voice service over loopback and actually works); entries 43, 44 and 49 all need to pick one. 3. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js - the in-game chat surface, which is the other place a line can be put in front of a person.

**How:** Do not change note(). Add callers. The pattern is already there: pick the handful of moments a person needs to be told rather than a log needs to record, and change `say(...)` to `note(...)` at exactly those sites. The shortlist is the other rows: the server-down message at thornswood-front.js:1727 (entry 44), the console-disabled message at :873 (entry 49), and the character-creation state changes on the server side (entry 43). Everything else stays on say(). Do this row first and on its own, because the other three depend on it and because the diff is small enough to review.

**Done when:** No game needed: `grep -rn "T.note\|\.note(" tools/ts-plugins tools/ts-ui` returns more than one hit, and every new hit is at a moment you can justify as something a person must see. Node can at least parse the file: `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js`. Then the Ship gate does the rest - tools\Ship.ps1:298-320 hashes every .js in tools\ts-plugins against the game folder's copy and refuses the release if any differs, so an edited plugin must be redeployed before a release will go out.

**Traps:** Read the comment at :120-131 before you route anything: it exists because a pulse every five seconds counting keystrokes was ending up on screen next to people's real mod notifications, and routing everything to the screen is how you get that back. Debug.notification has a queue and a rate limit in Skyrim; a burst of them will drop lines silently, so do not use it for anything that must be read. Ship.ps1 will refuse the whole release if tools\ts-plugins and the game folder disagree by a single byte, so the person has to deploy the plugins before shipping. Client plugin changes are game files and ship with the launcher and the server as one complete release (CLAUDE.md). Entries 43, 44 and 49 are downstream of this one, so land this first rather than doing all four in one branch - CLAUDE.md, one fix per pull request.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Debug_Script , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 43. The whole of character creation is narrated to a log file on the VPS and to nothing the person can see

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance holds; two details are off. The log count is 70 call sites in tools\gamemode.js, not 72 (71 matches for a log( call, minus the definition at :77). "Exactly two that can put words on a screen" is correct as call sites: notify at :1806 and say at :1807, both inside the craft/harvest result block. But the reader needs to know what those two are: notify (gamemode.js:122-130) goes through Debug.Notification and its own comment at :117-121 says it "may well do nothing" when the call starts here rather than in a game event, while say (gamemode.js:140-160) is the one that works, posting to the voice service's /say on 127.0.0.1:7778 over loopback. The wrong citation is ":1231-1240 the kit arriving". gamemode.js:1231 begins the `state.stage === 'appearance'` branch. The kit block is :1173-1229, `if (state.stage === 'arriving')`, with the silent "put N missing items into the kit" log at :1205-1208 and "kit confirmed" at :1222. The other three cited sites are right: sendToHall returns silently at :893-903, beginAppearance logs only at :950-951, applyLauncherChoice logs only at :771-774.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:117-160 - notify and say, and why one of them does not work; every line you add has to go through say() or through the client's note channel from entry 42. 2. tools\gamemode.js:1173-1229 - the arriving/kit state machine, the largest silent stretch. 3. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:132-145 - the client's say and note, the other end of the same problem.

**How:** Work state change by state change and add a say() beside each existing log(), not instead of it - the log lines are the debugging record and should stay. The list: sendToHall (:893-903) "You are in the creation hall."; beginAppearance (:950-951) "Choose how you look."; beginClass (:954-959) "Choose a trade."; chooseClass (:962-971) and chooseDestination (:973-998); applyLauncherChoice (:771-774) "Creating your character..."; the arrival teleport "Welcome to <town>."; and the kit block at :1205-1222 "Your starting kit is in your pack." Use say(actorId, text), which takes the actor and resolves the profile at :141-142. Do not use notify for these; its own comment says it will not fire from here.

**Done when:** Without starting the game: `node --check tools/gamemode.js` parses, and `grep -c 'say(' tools/gamemode.js` has risen from 2 call sites to roughly a dozen. Then, on the dev server only, tail the voice service's log for POSTs to /say during a creation run - the voice service is what actually delivers the line, so its log is the proof the message left the gamemode.

**Traps:** say() hardcodes port 7778 at gamemode.js:148. Entry 1 is exactly this: the dev gamemode ships that file unchanged and 7778 is the LIVE voice service, so every message you add is, today, delivered by the live voice service even when you are testing on dev. Land entry 1 or at least know that before you test. gamemode.js is deployed by tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 and tools\Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1, both of which talk to the VPS and are the person's to run. Do not restructure the chargen state machine while you are adding messages - it has an arrival window, face-change detection and reconnect handling with comments recording specific bugs at :1176-1183 and :1232-1245, and this row is only about narration. Server changes ship with the client and the launcher as one complete release.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Debug_Script , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 44. When the server restarts you are thrown to the main menu with no message at all

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:1727 is `say('the server has gone down, going back to the menu');`, and say at :132-134 is `log(text)` and nothing else, so that sentence goes only to the log file. The quit is at :1581-1590: `if (leaving && now - leaving > 5000)` then `sp.Game.quitToMainMenu()` at :1587, with a fallback say at :1590. One thing to be clear about when fixing it: there is no countdown drawn at all. The five seconds at :1581 is a bare delay with nothing on screen during it, so the row's "with the countdown" means building one, not routing an existing one.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:1700-1735 - checkServer, the strike counter (PROBE_STRIKES) and the message, so you know how many failed probes precede the quit. 2. thornswood-front.js:1575-1595 - the five-second delay and the quit, and the comment explaining why the line is said before the quit rather than after. 3. thornswood-front.js:132-145 - say and note, because this row is a direct dependant of entry 42.

**How:** Two small edits. At :1727 change `say(...)` to `note(...)` so the sentence reaches the corner of the screen. For the countdown, inside the block at :1581-1590, before the quit, tick a note once a second for the five seconds - keep a `lastCount` variable and call note('Connection lost. Returning to the main menu in ' + n + '...') when the whole-second value changes, so you emit five lines rather than one per frame. Then make the fallback at :1590 a note as well, since that path is the one where the quit itself failed and the person is left sitting in a dead world.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` parses. `grep -n 'note(' tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` shows the new call sites. Ship then enforces the rest: tools\Ship.ps1:298-320 refuses the release unless the game folder's copy of every ts-plugins .js hashes identical to the source, so the plugin must be deployed first.

**Traps:** Entry 42 has to land first or note() still has zero callers and this change does nothing visible. Debug.notification is rate limited and queued in Skyrim - five notes a second is close to the practical ceiling, so do not emit one per frame; gate on the whole-second value changing. Do not shorten the five seconds: the comment at :1579-1580 says it exists so the line is readable and so a server that comes straight back up is not turned into an outage. thornswood-front.js is a game file and ships with the launcher and the server as one complete release (CLAUDE.md). Note that PROBE_STRIKES failures have already happened before this point, so by the time the message shows, the person has usually been standing still for a while - consider saying something at the first missed probe too, but that is a separate change and a separate pull request.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Debug_Script , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 45. Stand still in the creation hall and you are stuck there forever

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and the defect is exactly as described. tools\gamemode.js:1480 is `if (!state.liveSince) { continue; }`, and both rescues are below it: the nudge that reopens the race menu at :1482-1494 (gated on NUDGE_MS) and the give-up that applies the launcher choice anyway at :1496-1512 (gated on GIVEUP_MS). liveSince is set in exactly one place, :1318-1319, gated on `live`, which is set only at :1296 when `moved >= MOVED_UNITS || turned >= MOVED_DEGREES`. So a person who stands perfectly still never trips it and neither rescue can fire. The row's note that a proposed fix was rejected is well founded: the comment at :1461-1475 records that asking for the race menu unconditionally produced a second menu and an access violation inside NativeFunction1<TESNPC, BGSHeadPart*, unsigned long>, a head-part call on a character whose last race menu had already been torn down, and says "the second menu and the crash were the same thing".

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:1446-1512 - the whole rescue block; the comment above the gate is the design rationale and the crash report in one, and it tells you what you may not do. 2. tools\gamemode.js:1280-1325 - where live and liveSince are computed, including the comment at :1300-1314 recording that the entire gate was dead once already because state.where was assigned to the local copy and never passed to setChargen. 3. tools\gamemode.js:1231-1260 - the reconnect handling, because any new clock you add has the same reconnect problem liveSince had.

**How:** Do not just delete the gate; that is the rejected fix. The gate exists to prove somebody is not standing in a menu already, because asking for a second race menu crashes the game. So find a different proof. Options worth designing around, in rough order of safety: (a) ask the client - thornswood-front.js can report whether a menu is open via sp.Ui.isMenuOpen, the same call it already uses for the console at :886-888, and the server can then treat "no menu open and no appearance after N seconds" as the rescue condition without guessing from movement; (b) use the arrival of any client update at all, rather than movement, as the proof of life, since a client mid save-load sends nothing; (c) leave the nudge gated as it is but move only the GIVEUP_MS give-up above the gate, since applying the launcher choice does not open a menu and so cannot cause the crash. Option (c) is the smallest and gets a stuck person out of the room; option (a) is the right long-term answer and needs a client change.

**Done when:** Reason it out in code before anything runs: for a state object with liveSince unset and state.at five minutes in the past, the give-up branch must be reached. That is testable as a plain Node harness over the state machine - tools\thornswood-src\test-chargen.js already exists as a precedent for testing chargen without a game. `node --check tools/gamemode.js` parses. Do not test this by standing in the hall; you are not allowed to start the game here.

**Traps:** This is the one row in this slice where the obvious fix is explicitly recorded as having been tried and caused a crash to desktop. Read gamemode.js:1461-1475 before writing a line. Second trap in the same block: :1232-1245 records that a reconnect returns with liveSince and askedAt already set, and that the first settled tick then read that as somebody who had been on their feet for as long as they were away and teleported them to the inn while the client was still loading - so any clock you add has to be able to tell a reconnect from the session that set it, which is what firstSeen is for. Third: state fields must go through setChargen or they vanish on the next tick (:1300-1314). Option (a) needs a client plugin change as well as a server change, which means a coordinated release under CLAUDE.md. And CLAUDE.md is explicit that a Claude does not settle a design question for Patrick and Djinn - this one is a design change and belongs in #dev first.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/ShowRaceMenu_-_Game

## 46. There is no way to ask anyone for help from inside the game, and no way to report a person from anywhere

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The in-game half is verified; the "nowhere at all" half is wrong and the row contradicts itself. Verified: the complete command set in tools\gamemode.js is /tspicks (:2893), /where (:2901), /tp or /goto (:2916), /restart or /shutdown (:2972), /ooc or /o (:2986), /help or /commands (:3042), with the unknown-command fallback at :3065-3066 naming "/ooc, /where, /tp <place> and /help". There is no page-a-moderator command and no unstick command. Wrong: "no way to report a person from anywhere". tools\discord\bot.py:284 is the ticket kind `('report', 'Report somebody', 'Something happened and it should not have.')`, and :285 is `('help', 'I need help', ...)`. Both exist and both are on the public panel. The row's own What-to-do says the Discord ticket is the only route, so the Finding and the What-to-do disagree with each other - the Finding should read "no route from inside the game, and none from the site". The site half is verified: tools\docs-app\server.js:1017-1037 accepts only `kind: o.kind === 'idea' ? 'idea' : 'bug'` at :1026. One thing for whoever reads the neighbouring rows: entry 16 says the command set includes /roll. There is no /roll anywhere in tools\gamemode.js.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:2863-3066 - handle(), the whole command surface, so a new command follows the same shape (profileId resolution at :2864-2876, actorOf, toProfile for the reply). 2. tools\discord\bot.py:272-300 - the ticket kinds and what already exists, so an in-game /report opens the same kind of ticket rather than a parallel system. 3. tools\docs-app\server.js:1013-1037 - the /api/report route the in-game command would most naturally post through.

**How:** Two commands in tools\gamemode.js, both modelled on the /ooc handler at :2986. (1) `/report <who> <what>`: take the text, attach the reporter's profileId, the target if resolvable, the cell and the time, POST it to the site's report route or straight to the bot, and reply through toProfile with "That has reached staff" so the person knows it landed. (2) `/stuck`: call the existing arrive() helper at gamemode.js:219 with the person's current hold, which is the same code the temple respawn already uses, so it is a reuse rather than a new mechanism. Add both to the /help text at :3042-3048 and to the fallback sentence at :3065-3066, which currently advertises commands and then refuses some of them. On the site side, widen server.js:1026 from the two-value ternary to include 'report' and route those to a staff channel through the bot rather than the shared inbox.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` parses and `python -m py_compile tools/discord/bot.py` succeeds. Then, against the dev server only, POST a test report to the dev site's /api/report and confirm it appears where staff read it. Checking the in-game command itself needs a session, which is not allowed here, so verify the handler logic with a small harness over handle() instead.

**Traps:** A report route that carries chat context depends on entry 64 (nothing anybody says is written down) and has to be declared in the privacy page from entry 83 - do not start retaining messages as a side effect of this row. A /report that names a person is only as good as the identity behind it, and entry 15 is that the server takes the profile id from the client and the gamemode's own comment calls it "a claim and not a fact", so a report is currently attributable to a number anyone can pick. Say that plainly rather than shipping a report system that looks authoritative. bot.py:531-551 (entry 63) falls back to posting into the public channel when a private thread cannot open, so an in-game report could surface in front of the person being reported - fix that before routing more sensitive content through it. Server and client ship together as one complete release.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/ , https://discord.com/developers/docs/resources/channel

## 47. A developer coordinate-marking tool ships to every customer, bound to F8

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in every particular. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-mark.js is exactly 154 lines. The key is at tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini:101, `MarkKey = 66`, which is the last line of that file; 66 is the DirectInput scan code for F8, matching the "F8." in the comment at :100. And tools\Ship.ps1:298-320 does ship it - that block hashes every .js in tools\ts-plugins against the game folder's copy and Dies if any differs, so mark.js is not merely permitted to ship, its presence is required for a release to go out at all.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-mark.js:1-20 - the header, which explains what it is for and names the arrival-point problem it was written to solve (that is entry 51, and it is not finished, so the tool is still needed by somebody). 2. tools\Ship.ps1:295-322 - the plugin hash gate, because removing a file from tools\ts-plugins without removing it from the game folder stops the release. 3. tools\Mark-Spot.ps1 - the other half of the tool, which reads the file the plugin writes.

**How:** Cheapest and safest: gate it at runtime rather than removing the file. The launcher already writes a developer flag - C:\Thornswood\Profile\developer.flag exists on this machine - so have mark.js read for that marker (or for a `Developer = 1` key in Thornswood.ini) at load and return immediately when it is absent, the same early-return shape the other plugins use. That keeps Ship.ps1's hash gate happy because the file still exists in both places. If you want it out of the package entirely, move it to a tools\ts-plugins-dev\ folder and add that folder to the Ship step at :299 as a separate, dev-only deployment, then confirm Ship.ps1:316-319 still sees a non-zero $seen for the retail folder.

**Done when:** No game needed: `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-mark.js` parses, and a read of the file confirms every path after the guard is unreachable without the flag. Then `.\tools\Ship.ps1 -DryRun` (Patrick runs it) must still report "N plugin(s) in the game folder match this source" rather than dying at that step. Also confirm C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\Thornswood.ini still carries MarkKey so a developer machine keeps the binding.

**Traps:** Do not just delete the file: tools\Ship.ps1:316-319 Dies with "tools\ts-plugins has no .js in it" if the folder empties, and Dies with "the in game plugins are not this source" if the folder and the game copy disagree by a byte - so a half-done removal stops all releases. The ini is not shipped in packs (Build-Launcher.ps1:256-259 and Ship-Rules.ps1 both say so, after one machine's binds and field of view landed on everybody on 16 Sep), so removing MarkKey from tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini only affects machines that have no ini yet - existing installs keep F8 bound whatever you do, which is an argument for the runtime guard over the ini edit. 66 is a scan code, not a virtual key code; do not "correct" it to 119. Game files ship with the launcher and the server as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://www.creationkit.com/index.php?title=Input_Script

## 48. The shipped config carries a debugging state that disables the in-game microphone

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified for a fresh install, and not true of a machine that already has the file - which matters for how you test it. tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini:88 is `Microphone = 0`, and the comment at :80-87 says outright it is at 0 "while we work out whether a live capture is what leaves the keyboard dead in character creation", which is a debugging state in the file's own words. It is read at tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js:226, `MIC_ON = grab('Microphone', 1) !== 0`. The embed line is Build-Launcher.ps1:262 (`$cscArgs += "/resource:...,Thornswood.ini"`), not :260, which is the $iniTemplate path. The nuance: this ini never travels in a pack (Build-Launcher.ps1:256-259 and Ship-Rules.ps1 both record that, after one machine's binds and field of view landed on everybody on 16 Sep) and the launcher writes it only when it is missing, via ThornIni.Ensure. This machine's live copy, C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\Thornswood.ini:114, already reads Microphone = 1. So the defect reaches first-time installs only.

**Read first:** 1. tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini:80-88 - the comment and the value; it names the experiment the 0 belongs to, so somebody has to say whether that experiment concluded before flipping it. 2. tools\Build-Launcher.ps1:255-266 - how the template is embedded and why it is not shipped in packs. 3. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js:215-235 - how the value is read, including the MicrophoneName parsing bug the comment at :227-231 records.

**How:** One character: tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini:88, `Microphone = 0` becomes `Microphone = 1`, and rewrite the comment at :80-87 so it no longer describes a live experiment. Then the durable half of the row: add a check that compares the shipping tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini against a known-good defaults file and refuses a main release on a difference, in tools\Ship.ps1 beside the plugin hash gate at :298-320. Ten lines and it catches the next one of these automatically.

**Done when:** Fully checkable without starting anything: `Select-String -Path tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini -Pattern '^Microphone'` reads 1. Build the launcher into a scratch root with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause`, then extract the embedded resource from the produced Thornswood.exe and confirm the embedded copy also reads 1 - that is the copy a fresh install actually gets, and reading the source file alone does not prove it.

**Traps:** Before flipping it, find out whether the experiment it belongs to ever concluded. The comment says the 0 is there to test whether a live capture is what leaves the keyboard dead in character creation - if that was never settled, flipping it may bring back a bug that is currently masked, and that is a #dev question, not a Claude's call. Existing machines are unaffected because the ini is never packed, so you cannot prove the fix by looking at C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\Thornswood.ini (it already says 1); you have to check the embedded resource. Do not add Thornswood.ini to a pack to "push" the fix - Ship-Rules.ps1 deliberately keeps machine settings out of packs and doing it once already stamped one person's monitor and field of view onto everybody. Related but separate: thornswood-voice.js:227-231 records a parsing bug where an empty MicrophoneName value made \s* run over the line break and take the next comment line as the microphone's name; the shipped ini leaves that value empty on purpose.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MediaDevices/getUserMedia , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 49. Pressing tilde makes the console flash and vanish, silently, forever, and nothing says why

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Verified in substance; two of the three line numbers are off by two. The messages are at tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:873, :877 and :880, not :875/:877/:880 - shutConsole runs :871-883 and the update handler that calls it runs :885-900, both inside the cited 868-901. All three go through say(), which at :132-134 is `log(text)` and nothing else, so all three land in the log file on the machine and nothing reaches the screen. The behaviour is as described: sp.Ui.isMenuOpen is polled every update for 'Console Native UI Menu' and 'Console' (:886-891), a 400 ms grace at :897-898 lets a console opened and closed in one frame pass, and then sp.TESModPlatform.closeMenu shuts it.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-front.js:868-901 - the whole block, including the 400 ms grace and the recentKeysText() capture, so you understand what it already records. 2. thornswood-front.js:132-145 - say and note; this row is a dependant of entry 42. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md - where the disclosure belongs on the public side.

**How:** Two parts, and they should go together. In the code: at thornswood-front.js:877, change that `say(...)` to `note('The console is disabled on Thornswood.')` so the person gets one plain sentence the moment it closes. Leave :873 and :880 on say() - the key capture and the closeMenu exception are debugging detail nobody needs on screen. Add a simple cooldown so somebody leaning on tilde gets one note rather than one per 400 ms: keep a `lastToldConsole` timestamp and only note when more than, say, thirty seconds have passed. On the site: add a line to tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md saying the console is disabled and why, so the behaviour is disclosed rather than discovered.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` parses and `grep -n 'note(' tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` shows the new call. For the site, `grep -n -i console tools/docs-app/data/site/rules.md` finds the disclosure. Ship.ps1:298-320 then enforces that the game folder's copy matches the source before a release goes out. None of this needs the game.

**Traps:** Entry 42 must land first or note() still has no callers and the change is invisible. Anti-cheat behaviour that is undisclosed is the part that makes people angry, so the rules.md line is not optional garnish - it is the fix, and the code change is the reminder. Do not weaken the 400 ms grace or the closeMenu call while you are in here; this is anti-cheat and loosening it is a design question for #dev. Debug.notification is rate limited, which is the other reason for the cooldown. thornswood-front.js is a game file, so it ships with the launcher and the server as one complete release (CLAUDE.md). Entries 43, 44 and 49 all touch the same file and all depend on 42; that is four pull requests, not one.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Debug_Script , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 50. Two of the three choices the launcher makes you agonise over do nothing in the game

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly. tools\gamemode.js:760-761 write `state.half = String(choice.half || '')` and `state.gathering = String(choice.cls || '')`, and the only other occurrences of either name in the entire file are :772-773, inside the log line that renders them. Nothing reads them back. The comment directly above, at :747-759, states the intent in detail - half "decides what they are able to MAKE, which is server side", gathering "owns a verb nobody else has", and both are "stored on the character here so the crafting and gathering checks have them the moment they are written" - so the design was written down and the consumer was never built. classes.md:22-44 is the public page describing them as systems.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:736-775 - applyLauncherChoice and the comment block that is the specification for what half and gathering were meant to do. 2. tools\gamemode.js:1780-1812 - the crafting and harvest handler, which is where a half check would have to go; it is the one place in the file that already produces a message a person sees. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\classes.md:22-44 and :126-130 - what the public has been told, which is the other half of the row.

**How:** Decide which way first, and that is Patrick and Djinn's call, not yours. If the page is corrected: edit tools\docs-app\data\site\classes.md so the half and the gathering specialisation are described as planned rather than as running systems, and move them into whatever "being built" treatment entry 76 settles on for the feature grid. That is markdown and the site deploy, no game change. If the systems are built: the hook is the craft/harvest branch at gamemode.js:1780-1804, where baseDesc is already matched against STATIONS, SHRINES and HARVESTS - read state.half there via the same getChargen the file uses elsewhere and refuse or allow the recipe, returning a message through the existing `if (message) { notify(...); say(...) }` at :1805-1808. The gathering verb is the same shape against HARVESTS.

**Done when:** For the page fix: `grep -n -i 'half\|gathering' tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md` shows the planned framing, and the rendered page says so. For the code fix, no game needed: `node --check tools/gamemode.js` parses, and `grep -c 'state.half' tools/gamemode.js` has risen above the current three occurrences, with at least one of them a read rather than a write.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md is explicit that a design decision like this goes to #dev as a few lines first - the problem, the approach, what it touches - and is agreed between Patrick and Djinn before code. A Claude does not settle which way this goes. The state object is read back off the actor at the top of every tick, and gamemode.js:1300-1314 records that a field written to the local copy without passing it to setChargen is gone three seconds later - so if you add anything to state while building this, it goes through setChargen. The public page and the server have to change together or the row just moves: if you build the system, update classes.md in the same release; if you correct the page, say when the system is expected. And anything in gamemode.js ships with the launcher and the game files as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Crafting

## 51. Four of the nine starting towns use hand-written arrival points, and the shipped config says one of them puts you in a wall

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The core is verified and I can name the four; the supporting evidence is stale and will send the next person after a bug that is already fixed. The towns table is the CHARGEN constant at tools\gamemode.js:616 (not :617). Five towns carry measured-looking values - Whiterun rot 3.7, Riften 36.3, Windhelm 91.9, Morthal 270.9, Winterhold 88 - which matches the 1.1.41 release note "arrival points fixed in Whiterun, Riften, Windhelm, Morthal and Winterhold". The four still on round hand-written values are Solitude (-178.0, -1504.0, 128.0, rot 90.0), Markarth (-5588.5, -1925.8, -16.0, rot 180.0), Falkreath (673.1, -620.3, 0.0, rot 180.0) and Dawnstar (1824.3, -683.7, 0.0, rot 180.0). But "Your own config already says it: Morthal and Dawnstar are two units apart" is no longer true of the code. Morthal is now at 1970.6, -160.2; only Dawnstar is still at 1824.3, -683.7. The two-units line survives only in stale comments at tools\emotes\Thornswood.ini:97-101 and tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-mark.js:4-8, written before 1.1.41 and never updated. Those two comments should be corrected as part of this row.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js:610-620 - HALL and the CHARGEN table; the towns entries are near the end of the CHARGEN line and each carries cell, desc, pos and rot. 2. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-mark.js:1-20 - the measuring tool's header, which is also the stale comment that needs correcting. 3. tools\Mark-Spot.ps1 - what turns the measured file into the line the table wants, so you paste a generated line rather than hand-typing coordinates again.

**How:** The four to walk are Solitude (Winking Skeever), Markarth (Silver-Blood Inn), Falkreath (Dead Man's Drink) and Dawnstar (Windpeak Inn). In game, stand where somebody should arrive, face the way they should face, press F8 (MarkKey = 66, Thornswood.ini:101). That writes Data\Platform\thornswood-spots.txt; run tools\Mark-Spot.ps1 to turn it into the table line, and paste the four results into the CHARGEN constant at gamemode.js:616. While you are there, fix the two stale comments: Thornswood.ini:97-101 and thornswood-mark.js:4-8 both still say Morthal and Dawnstar are two units apart. Note that the CHARGEN constant is generated - the comment at gamemode.js:625-626 says it comes from the same table the plugin and the message box records came from - so check whether there is a generator to edit rather than editing the generated line by hand.

**Done when:** Before the walk, a pure-data check anyone can run now: parse the CHARGEN towns table and assert that no two arrival positions are within, say, 100 units of each other, and that no rot[2] is exactly 0.0, 90.0, 180.0 or 270.0 - the four hand-written ones fail that today and the five measured ones pass. After the walk, the same check passes for all nine. Also `node --check tools/gamemode.js`.

**Traps:** You cannot do the measuring part yourself - CLAUDE.md forbids a Claude starting the launcher or the game, so the walk is Patrick or Djinn's. What you can do is the data check, the comment corrections and the paste. The stale comments are the trap: anyone reading Thornswood.ini:97-101 today will go looking for a Morthal-Dawnstar collision that 1.1.41 already fixed. Do not edit the CHARGEN line by hand without checking for the generator the comment at :625-626 implies, or the next regeneration reverts you. gamemode.js ships with the launcher and the game files as one complete release. And thornswood-mark.js is the tool that does the measuring, which is exactly the file entry 47 wants gated behind a developer flag - do the gating in a way that still lets a developer machine measure, or these four never get walked.

## 52. Four mods your own documentation calls broken are in the live load order right now

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, with one addition and one path correction. All four are enabled in C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt with a leading asterisk: Respawn - Death Overhaul.esp (line 48), SunHelmSurvival.esp (51), SunHelmDiseases.esp (64), TavernGames.esp (86). All four are listed at tools\docs-app\data\known.md:32-35, each with the reason it does not work, and Immersive Armors standard is at :36 and is indeed not in the live list. The addition the row misses: line 78 is `*Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp`, a patch that exists only for SunHelm, so removing SunHelm is three plugins out of the order rather than two. The path correction: it is tools\docs-app\data\known.md, not data\site\known.md - the site folder holds classes, credits, join, rules, support and world.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\data\known.md:28-42 - the reasons, which are specific and technical (SunHelm polls on a timer and writes values the server crops and corrects; Respawn sets you essential and runs its own death handler against a server that owns respawn; Tavern Games creates gold on your own machine that the server does not accept) - read them before assuming any of the four can simply be re-enabled. 2. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt - the live reference list, 98 lines. 3. CLAUDE.md, the "Settled design" section, because the collection and the reference list are the same question and changing one without the other is entry 38.

**How:** This is a collection change and a reference-list change and they must match. Remove SunHelmSurvival.esp, SunHelmDiseases.esp, Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp, Respawn - Death Overhaul.esp and TavernGames.esp from the Nexus collection, let Vortex redeploy, then let the launcher regenerate the reference list so C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt matches. Then update tools\docs-app\data\known.md so it no longer lists as broken things that are no longer shipped. Do not hand-edit plugins.txt as the primary action: it is derived from what Vortex deploys, and a hand edit will be overwritten and will leave the collection and the list disagreeing, which is entry 38's problem.

**Done when:** `Select-String -Path 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt' -Pattern 'SunHelm|Respawn - Death|TavernGames'` returns nothing. `Compare-Object` between the reference list and the manifest the server publishes shows no difference. And a clean Vortex install of the collection produces a plugins.txt matching the reference list line for line. All read-only, no game needed.

**Traps:** This moves the load order. Every plugin after the removed ones shifts index, which means the server's manifest, the reference list, the collection and every installed machine have to change together - CLAUDE.md, every change goes out as one complete release, launcher, game files and server, never a half. Doing this without a coordinated release is exactly how you generate the LOAD ORDER ERROR the launcher's Fix exists to clean up. The Nexus collection is Patrick's to re-upload; nothing here is yours to publish. There are five plugins to remove, not four - do not miss the Embers XD patch, which will fail to load with its master gone. And if the decision goes the other way (they now work and should come off the known page instead), that is a claim that has to be tested, not assumed, and the reasons at known.md:32-35 say why each one conflicts structurally with a server that owns actor values, death and inventory.

## 53. Nothing anywhere limits how many people can connect, and the only number that looks like a limit is decoration

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Verified where I could check, unverifiable where the file is on the VPS. The launcher half is exact: tools\app\Config.cs:70 is `[DataMember] public int MaxOnline = 25;` with a comment calling it what the Play page "can say something useful" with; Online.cs:42 copies it into got.Max; Online.cs:100-106 renders "N of 25 awake" or "room for 25". It is also overwritten from the feed at Shell.cs:4031 and published by Publish-Update.ps1:895-897, so it is a shipped display string end to end and nothing enforces it. The gamemode half is verified: nothing in tools\gamemode.js caps connections or refuses one - the only `slot` hits in the file are respawn slots at :28 and :201-228. The one thing I could not check is "server-settings.json has no such key": that file lives on the VPS, not in this repo, so take that clause as unconfirmed until somebody reads it there.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Config.cs:68-70 and tools\app\Online.cs:38-106 - the whole of what MaxOnline currently is, so nobody mistakes it for a limit. 2. tools\gamemode.js:2274-2400 - the connect and profile-binding path, which is where a slot count has to be enforced and is also where entry 15 lives. 3. tools\voice\voice-server.js:150-210 - the other service people connect to, which needs the same cap or the game cap is half a fix.

**How:** Add a real cap on the server side, in tools\gamemode.js's connect path, refusing past the count with a named reason rather than a silent drop; read the number from server-settings.json so it can be changed without a code release. Then keep the launcher's display honest by having the server announce its actual cap in the status the launcher already polls - Shell.cs:4031 already adopts maxOnline from the feed, so the wiring exists. Before picking the number, run a load test: the honest answer to "how many does it hold" comes from measurement, and entry 55's position broadcast is the thing that will fall over first, so test the voice service under the same load or the number will be wrong.

**Done when:** Readable without a game: server-settings.json on the VPS carries the key, and the gamemode refuses connection N+1 with a named message that appears in the server log. The launcher side is a compile check, `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root <scratch> -NoPause`, plus confirming the Play page string is derived from the announced value rather than the baked 25.

**Traps:** A cap enforced on a profile id the client picks is not a cap - entry 15 is that the server runs in offline mode and takes the id from the client, and the gamemode's own comment calls it "a claim and not a fact", so one person can hold several slots and a refused person can pick a different number. Order this after entry 15 or say plainly that it is a soft limit. tools\gamemode.js and server-settings.json are deployed by Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 and Deploy-Server.ps1, which talk to the VPS and are the person's to run, not yours. Config.cs is in tools\app: C# 5, no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Shell.cs is on CLAUDE.md's #dev-first list. And the voice service is the load bottleneck, not the game server - capping one without the other just moves where it breaks.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html

## 54. The 527 MB installer is served off the VPS by hand-written node with no resume, while the update packs already come from a CDN

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Verified in substance; both citations are slightly off and the client side is worth knowing. tools\docs-app\server.js:1142-1156 is the /file/ route and does exactly what the row says: writeHead 200 with Content-Type, Content-Length and Content-Disposition, then pipes the whole stream. There is no Accept-Ranges header, no Range parsing and no 206 anywhere in the file. The payload url comes from /api/payload at server.js:992-1010, which points at that same /file/ route, so the 527 MB download goes through it. The promise is real but is not at home.html:870-874 and never uses the word resume: it is public\home.html:864, "a connection that drops does not mean starting again", inside the "Take the installer" step. And the client already tries: tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:762 sends `req.AddRange(have)` and :767-769 silently starts over when the answer is not 206 ("A server that ignores the range answers 200 with the whole file"). So resume is implemented on both ends of the wire and only the middle is missing.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:1139-1156 - the /file/ route as it stands, and :992-1010 for how the payload url is chosen. 2. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:749-812 - the client's resume attempt and its silent fallback, so you know what a correct server answer has to look like. 3. tools\Setup-Domain.ps1:120-160 - how Caddy is configured, if you take the Caddy route rather than writing Range support in node.

**How:** Two routes and the second is better. (a) In node: in server.js's /file/ handler, always send `Accept-Ranges: bytes`; when req.headers.range matches `bytes=(\d+)-(\d*)`, answer 206 with `Content-Range: bytes start-end/total` and `Content-Length: end-start+1`, and pipe `fs.createReadStream(file, { start, end })`. Reject an unsatisfiable range with 416. That is about fifteen lines. (b) Better: let Caddy serve the files directory with its `file_server` directive, which does Range, ETag and conditional requests for free and takes the 527 MB stream off the node process entirely, then have /api/payload return the Caddy url. Either way, publish the SHA-256 beside the link on the download page - that is the same value entry 32 needs, so do the hash once and use it in both places.

**Done when:** One command, no game: `curl -s -D - -o /dev/null -H "Range: bytes=0-1023" https://<site>/file/<payload name>` must answer `206 Partial Content` with `Content-Range: bytes 0-1023/527227436` and `Content-Length: 1024`. Today it answers 200 with the full length. A second check: `curl -s -I` on the same url shows `Accept-Ranges: bytes`.

**Traps:** server.js:464-471 currently sets no-store on every response (entry 82); a Range implementation that inherits that header will still work but throws away the conditional-request half of the benefit, so the two rows are related. Do not mix this with entry 32's SHA-256 in one pull request even though both touch /api/payload - CLAUDE.md wants one fix per pull request. Deploy-Docs.ps1 is the person's to run and it has its own hazards (entry 24: -Password wipes the whole site config; entry 25: every deploy deletes the log). If you take the Caddy route, note entry 21: Caddy is not in Watch-Services.ps1's watched list and its scheduled task has an execution time limit, so moving the biggest download behind it without fixing that puts the installer behind an unwatched process. And the site currently dies on one malformed request (entry 13), so a scanner probing Range syntax is a live risk until that is fixed.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Guides/Range_requests , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Accept-Ranges , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Range , https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/file_server

## 55. What the unculled position broadcast costs: 369 GB an evening at two hundred people, against an 8 TB monthly cap

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The mechanism is verified exactly; the traffic figures are VPS measurements I cannot re-take. tools\voice\voice-server.js:202-206 is `setInterval(() => { if (peers.size === 0) return; const snap = snapshot(); for (const [, p] of peers) p.ws.send({ t: 'pos', at: Date.now(), who: snap }); }, 200)` - the whole snapshot to every peer, five times a second. snapshot() at :192-200 filters on staleness (STALE_MS) and nothing else; it never looks at distance or cell. Each row is `{ pos, angleZ, cell, name }`, so every person is told every other person's coordinates, facing, cell and name. Verified. The arithmetic is the right order: at 128 bytes a row, 200 people is 128 x 200 x 200 x 5 = 25.6 MB/s against the row's 24.5. The 8 TB monthly cap and the 93 Mbit/s line measurement are VPS facts not readable from this machine.

**Read first:** 1. tools\voice\voice-server.js:150-210 - join, positions, snapshot and the 200 ms interval, the whole mechanism in sixty lines. 2. tools\gamemode.js:617-620 and the towns table at :616 - cell descriptors, so you know what a cell comparison actually compares. 3. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:837-849 - how the client already computes gain by cell and distance, which is the culling rule the server should be applying instead.

**How:** Cull in snapshot(), or rather make snapshot per-recipient. Replace the single `const snap = snapshot()` with a loop that builds each peer's view: for each recipient, include a row only when the other person is in the same cell, or within the largest voice range from them. The client already has the rule - thornswood-voice.js:837-849 sets gain from cell and distance - so lift that same test to the server rather than inventing a second one. Bucket by cell first so the loop is not O(n squared) over raw distance: keep a Map from cell descriptor to the peers in it, and only do distance maths within a cell. Keep sending a tiny "this person left your range" so the client can tear the peer connection down rather than leaving a stale panner running.

**Done when:** Measurable without a game, from the service's own numbers: instrument the interval to log total bytes written per tick, drive it with a synthetic set of 200 positions spread across ten cells, and confirm the per-tick total falls by roughly the ratio of cell occupancy - from 200 rows per recipient to a handful. A unit test over snapshot() asserting that a peer in a different cell does not appear in another peer's view is the cheap version and needs no network at all.

**Traps:** This is the same change as entry 4's privacy problem and entry 56's mesh problem, and doing it wrong breaks voice for people who should hear each other. Do not cull tighter than the client's own range table or people will cut out mid-conversation - read thornswood-voice.js:837-849 and use the same constants. Entry 7 is the hard blocker: the voice service running on the VPS is not the one in this repository (:7778/health and :7789/health answer build owners-4 with routes that do not exist in tools\voice\voice-server.js), so a change written against the repo copy may not match what is deployed. Pull the deployed file and read it before editing. Entry 1: the dev gamemode currently talks to the live voice service on 7778, so testing on dev touches live. Install-Voice.ps1 and anything else that reaches the VPS is the person's to run. And the 200 ms tick is also what carries positions for the audio panner, so slowing it is not a substitute for culling.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/timers.html#setintervalcallback-delay-args , https://github.com/websockets/ws , https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Area_of_interest_management

## 56. Voice is a full peer-to-peer mesh with no relay and no cap, and the code already admits a pair that cannot connect is simply lost

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, including the phrase the row quotes. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js reconcile at :630-650 calls `open(id, p, myPeer < id)` for every other peer in the list, and open at :652 onward builds an RTCPeerConnection per peer with `iceServers: [{ urls: 'stun:stun.l.google.com:19302' }]` and no TURN entry. tools\launcher\voice-host.html:133-134 is the same shape in the overlay. The connection counts are right: 50 x 49 / 2 = 1,225 and 200 x 199 / 2 = 19,900. The "code already admits a pair that cannot connect is simply lost" is at thornswood-voice.js:700-708: after one ICE restart it reports "ice failed twice. STUN is not enough for this pair, which needs a TURN relay" and returns, with the comment "A pair that cannot reach each other over STUN needs a relay, and hammering it does not conjure one." One figure to soften: the row says browsers are usable to "about eight or twelve peers". Published guidance is stricter - roughly four to six for a video mesh - and this is audio only, which goes further, so eight to twelve is defensible here but should not be quoted as a general number.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:620-720 - reconcile, open, and the ICE failure handler that already names the missing relay; it is the clearest statement of the problem in the repo. 2. tools\launcher\voice-host.html:95-140 - the overlay's copy of the same logic, which has to change with it or the two implementations diverge further (entry 91 is already about them having separate constant tables). 3. tools\voice\voice-server.js:150-210 - the signalling and position service, which is where an SFU would sit or be fronted.

**How:** Two steps, and the first is much smaller than the second. (1) Add a TURN relay so no pair is lost: stand up coturn on the VPS, add its url and short-lived credentials to the iceServers array at thornswood-voice.js:653 and voice-host.html:134, and the ICE-failed branch at :700-708 stops being a dead end. That alone fixes the "simply lost" half and, because a relayed connection hides each person's address from the other, it fixes most of the IP exposure too. (2) Replace the mesh with an SFU - mediasoup if you want to run it in-process with the existing node service, LiveKit if you want it as a separate server - so each person holds one upstream and one downstream regardless of how many people are present. That is a rewrite of the client audio path, not an edit, and it changes what the launcher overlay does.

**Done when:** Step 1 is checkable without a game: `turnutils_uclient` against the coturn host authenticates and relays, and a plain browser page using the same iceServers array gathers a candidate with `typ relay` in chrome://webrtc-internals. Step 2: a load harness with fifty synthetic participants shows one upstream per client rather than forty-nine, measured from the SFU's own stats endpoint.

**Traps:** Entry 7 blocks anything here: the voice service actually running on the VPS is not the one in tools\voice\voice-server.js - both instances answer /health with build owners-4 and routes that do not exist in the repo copy - so pull the deployed file and read it before writing a line against the repo version. Entry 4 is the other blocker: the voice port accepts any WebSocket and believes whatever it is told about identity, so adding a relay without fixing that gives a stranger a relay too. TURN credentials are a secret and belong on the VPS, named by location only, never in the client bundle as a static password - use short-lived credentials minted at join, which is the same token entry 4 needs. There are two client implementations (tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js and tools\launcher\voice-host.html) and entry 91 already records that they keep separate constant tables and that two of the three voice ranges have never worked because of it - change both or the divergence gets worse. A relay is bandwidth on the VPS, which is the same 8 TB budget entry 55 is about, so do entry 55's culling first or the relay inherits the problem. Nothing that touches the VPS is yours to run.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/WebRTC_API , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/RTCPeerConnection/RTCPeerConnection , https://github.com/coturn/coturn , https://mediasoup.org/ , https://github.com/livekit/livekit , https://www.freecodecamp.org/news/how-webrtc-scales-signaling-nat-traversal-and-the-mesh-sfu-mcu-tradeoff

## 57. The door's allow list stops refreshing somewhere past a hundred linked accounts, and one rate-limited lookup throws the whole list away

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The sequential await loop is real, at tools\docs-app\server.js:1317-1329 (the entry says 1316-1329; 1316 is the `seen` Set). Two claims need correcting. (1) "no 429 handling" at :522-551 is imprecise: discordGet does recognise 429 and returns {ok:false, status:429, why:'rate limited by Discord'} at server.js:545. What it never does is read Retry-After or x-ratelimit-reset-after, retry, or back off, so a rate limit is silently turned into a failed lookup. (2) "one rate-limited lookup throws the whole list away" is not what the site does. server.js:1324 sets out.lookupFailed = true and skips only that one account; the 200 still carries every good profile. It is the consumer, tools\gamemode.js:2435-2438, that then discards the whole answer and keeps the previous allowed.json, and its comment says that is deliberate ("Keeping the old list is the safe failure"). The real damage is the one described: while any single lookup keeps failing, the allow list never updates again, so a newly accepted tester is never added and a newly banned one is never removed. (3) "past a hundred linked accounts" is arithmetic, not a measurement. The gamemode's request times out at 10 seconds (tools\gamemode.js:2462) and refreshes every 300 seconds (gamemode.js:2468), while standingCached's cache is 60 seconds (server.js:428-435), so the cache is always cold at refresh time and every linked account costs at least one uncached Discord round trip (two when roleNames() is also cold, server.js:558-561). At roughly 100ms each, about a hundred accounts crosses the 10 second timeout. Nothing has been measured: entry 5 reports the live list at 20 profiles.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo (a): "in discordGet (server.js:522-551), on statusCode 429 read res.headers['retry-after'] and res.headers['x-ratelimit-reset-after'], wait that many seconds and retry once before resolving not-ok."  ->  Put inside discordGet, this makes the timeout the entry is about worse. /api/allowed calls standingCached once per linked account in a sequential await loop (server.js:1317-1329), and by the correction's own reasoning the 60 second standCache (server.js:428-435) is always cold at the 300 second refresh, so a rate limit hits every account in the run and the sleep multiplies by the number of accounts. That whole run sits inside the gamemode's 10 second idle timeout at gamemode.js:2462, and each account may pay it twice (standing() calls discordGet at server.js:644 and roleNames() at :655). The back-off belongs once per refresh or as a shared cooldown, not per lookup.

**Challenged (minor):** "discordGet does recognise 429 and returns {ok:false, status:429, why:'rate limited by Discord'} at server.js:545" and "refreshes every 300 seconds (gamemode.js:2468)"  ->  server.js:545 is the console.error; the resolve carrying status and why is :546 (:544 sets the message). gamemode.js:2468 is the clearInterval guard; the 300000 ms setInterval is :2469-2471. Also "every linked account costs at least one uncached Discord round trip (two when roleNames() is also cold)" reads as per account, but roleNames caches globally for 300 s (server.js:556-566), so only the first account in a refresh pays the second call.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:1292-1331, the /api/allowed route and the block comment above it, which already states the intended failure behaviour in the author's own words. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:613-664 (standing()), 428-435 (standingCached) and 522-551 (discordGet), which together tell you how many Discord calls one account costs and what happens to a non-200. 3. tools\gamemode.js:2400-2470, the refresh that consumes the answer, its 10 second timeout and its 5 minute interval, because the timeout is the actual ceiling.

**How:** Three separable changes, smallest first. (a) Honour the rate limit: in discordGet (server.js:522-551), on statusCode 429 read res.headers['retry-after'] and res.headers['x-ratelimit-reset-after'], wait that many seconds and retry once before resolving not-ok. (b) Make partial failure partial: in /api/allowed change out.lookupFailed from a single boolean into a count plus a list of ids, return both, and in tools\gamemode.js:2435 accept the answer when the failures are a small fraction (say under 10 percent) and merge rather than replace, only refusing the write when the site says it could reach Discord for nobody. (c) Stop re-deriving membership per account: the bot already runs with members=True (tools\discord\bot.py:102), so have bot.py keep a dict of member id to role names updated from on_member_update / on_member_join / on_member_remove and POST it to a new site route with cfg.botKey, and have /api/allowed read that cache instead of calling standingCached in a loop. That turns the loop from N Discord calls into zero.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes, and a harness proves the partial case without Discord: stub discordGet to return {ok:false,status:429} for one id out of five and {ok:true,...} for the rest, call the /api/allowed handler, and assert the response still lists the four good profiles and that a copy of the gamemode's refresh() logic writes them rather than returning early. For the timing ceiling, time the loop with a stub that sleeps 100ms per call and confirm 100 accounts exceeds 10 seconds before the fix and does not after.

**Traps:** Do not simply drop the gamemode's early return at 2435-2438. The comment there records why it exists: a short list written while Discord was unreachable turns away everybody missing from it, and with entry 5's enforce flag set true that is a mass lockout, not a logged warning. Any change here touches tools\gamemode.js, which is deployed by Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 and is not part of the launcher release, so a site change and a gamemode change land through two different paths and can be live at different times: make the site's new field additive so an old gamemode still works. Do not run Deploy-* or Ship.ps1 yourself. Adding a site route that the bot POSTs to means a new shared secret path; the bot key lives in the site config on the VPS and in door.json, and entry 2 is already about rotating it, so coordinate rather than minting a second one.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/gateway , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/intents.html , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 58. The voice service writes to sockets with no backpressure, so one slow connection grows server memory without limit

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Three of the four citations are right and the fourth is not. Verified: tools\voice\voice-server.js:109-112 is send(obj), which calls this.socket.write(...) inside a try and ignores the boolean return; :175-178 is announce(), which sends the roster to every peer; :202-206 is the 200ms setInterval that sends a position snapshot to every peer.
