The fourth citation, :383-390, is the GET /choices HTTP route answering the gamemode over loopback with res.end, which is a one-shot HTTP response, not a per-peer socket write, and is not a backpressure site. The real fourth site is the chat broadcast at :532, `for (const [, p] of peers) p.ws.send(out);`. The underlying finding is correct: Node's socket.write returns false when the kernel buffer is full and nothing in this file ever checks it or listens for 'drain', so a peer on a slow link accumulates unbounded queued buffers in the server process.

**Challenged (minor):** "The real fourth site is the chat broadcast at :532" and "Do it once, in send(), so all four call sites get it."  ->  There are six Sock.send call sites, not four: tools\voice\voice-server.js:177 (announce), :186 (welcome), :205 (the 200ms position fan-out), :436 (the second broadcast, the loopback POST that sends a message to every peer or to one profile), :483 (the signalling relay to one peer) and :532 (chat). The write-up misses :436 and :483. The fix in send() does cover them all, so the howTo still works; only the count and the phrase "the real fourth site" are wrong.

**Read first:** 1. tools\voice\voice-server.js:90-120, the minimal WebSocket wrapper class, so you see that send() is the single choke point every broadcast goes through. 2. tools\voice\voice-server.js:192-206, the snapshot and the 200ms tick, which is the traffic that actually matters and is the same code entry 55 wants to cull by distance. 3. Node's stream backpressure page (link below), because the fix is the standard write-returns-false pattern and not something bespoke.

**How:** Do it once, in send(), so all four call sites get it. In the socket wrapper (voice-server.js around 90-112) add `this.backed = false;` in the constructor and `this.socket.on('drain', () => { this.backed = false; });`. Change send to: if (this.closed) return false; try { const ok = this.socket.write(frame(JSON.stringify(obj))); if (!ok) this.backed = true; return ok; } catch (e) { this.close(); return false; }. Then add a dropping send for superseded state, `sendLossy(obj) { if (this.backed) return false; return this.send(obj); }`, and use it at :205 for the position tick only. Leave chat (:532), the roster (:177) and welcome (:186) on the reliable send, because those are not superseded every tick. Optionally add a hard ceiling: if this.socket.writableLength exceeds, say, 1 MB, call this.close() and let the client reconnect, which is better than growing forever.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` passes, and a local harness proves the drop: start the service on a spare port with PORT set, connect a socket that completes the handshake and then never reads (pause the socket), push positions in through POST /positions, and assert process.memoryUsage().heapUsed and the socket's writableLength stop growing after the ceiling instead of climbing with every tick. Run it against localhost only; do not point it at the VPS.

**Traps:** Entry 55 is the same 200ms tick and is the bigger win: culling by distance shrinks each message before backpressure ever matters, and both changes land in the same lines, so do them together or the second one rewrites the first. This service is the one entry 7 says is NOT the code that is actually running on the VPS (the deployed build answers /health as owners-4 with routes that do not exist in this file), so verify against the deployed copy before assuming your edit lands on the same function. Install-Voice.ps1 is a VPS script: show the command, let Patrick run it. The chat path at :532 is also entries 64, 65 and 71, so three other pieces of work touch the same forty lines.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/net.html , https://nodejs.org/api/stream.html , https://nodejs.org/en/learn/modules/backpressuring-in-streams

## 59. The update feed only ever grows, and every launcher downloads all of it on every check

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The size is exactly right and the counts and one line reference are not. The repository copy of version.json is 59,496 bytes, confirmed by wc -c, but it holds 164 pack entries, not 192 (checked with node: v.packs.length === 164). The entry is internally inconsistent with entry 11, which gives 192 as the published total (164 private plus 28 public); the 59,496 byte figure belongs to the 164 entry local file, so the row states a byte count and a pack count that do not come from the same file. All 164 urls in the local copy point at api.github.com/repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood, and zero at Thornswood-Releases, which supports entry 11. The Publish-Update.ps1 line reference is wrong: line 898 is the MaxOnline block. The append-and-never-prune code is tools\Publish-Update.ps1:799-812 (`$packs = @()` at 799, `$packs += [pscustomobject]@{` at 804, `Set-Prop $doc 'packs' $packs` at 812), with no removal anywhere. Updater.cs:141-160 is correct: Check() begins at line 141 and downloads the whole document with wc.DownloadData(url) at :156. Worth adding: the url is the GitHub Contents API, which returns the file base64 encoded inside a JSON envelope, so each check pulls roughly 80 KB over the wire for a 59 KB file, not 59 KB.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** "it holds 164 pack entries, not 192 (checked with node: v.packs.length === 164)" and "The entry is internally inconsistent with entry 11 ... the row states a byte count and a pack count that do not come from the same file."  ->  It read the wrong file. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\version.json is a stale repo copy: 59,496 bytes, 164 packs, all private urls. The feed every launcher actually downloads (the captured live copy in the scratchpad, live-version.json, launcherVersion 1.1.47 which matches HEAD ddfd2da) is 83,059 bytes with 192 packs: 164 under api.github.com/repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood and 28 under Thornswood-Releases. The row's "192 pack entries" is right and matches entry 11 exactly; only the byte figure is the stale one and should be 83,059. The correction turns a correct number into a wrong one and tells the reader to distrust entry 11, which is correct.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "Worth adding: the url is the GitHub Contents API, which returns the file base64 encoded inside a JSON envelope, so each check pulls roughly 80 KB over the wire for a 59 KB file, not 59 KB."  ->  tools\app\Updater.cs:42 sets wc.Headers["Accept"] = octet ? "application/octet-stream" : "application/vnd.github.raw", and the version.json fetch passes octet:false (Updater.cs:157). GitHub returns the raw file, not a base64 JSON envelope, and VersionDoc.Parse (tools\app\Config.cs:424) parses raw JSON after a BOM strip. There is no 1.33x base64 inflation. The real transfer is the raw live file, about 83 KB.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "write a `current.json` containing only launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, serverPort, voicePort, maxOnline, message and the newest N packs (N small, say the last 5). In tools\app\Updater.cs:Check, fetch curr[ent.json first]"  ->  This silently drops mod files and moves the Skyrim load order, and the write-up never says so. tools\app\Updater.cs:346-354 Pending() is the only selector: it returns every pack in the feed whose id is not in applied.txt. There is no other source and no catch-up path, and applied.txt is seeded per machine at install (comment at Updater.cs:604-609), so any install more than N packs behind never receives the skipped packs. Packs carry plugins.txt and loadorder.txt (tools\Publish-Update.ps1:398 and :614) and ModCheck compares against the server's own loadorder.txt (Publish-Update.ps1:1151), so such a machine gets the newest load order naming plugins it does not have on disk. With 192 packs live and 164 already unreachable (entry 11), N=5 makes this the normal case. It also drops `optional`, which Shell.cs:1861 reads for the Mods page.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Updater.cs:141-205, Check(), because it is the caller that pays the cost and it already contains the rate limit handling that entry 31 is about. 2. tools\Publish-Update.ps1:790-815, where a pack is appended, so you can see there is no prune and no notion of a baseline. 3. version.json itself in the repo root, which is the artefact: open it and look at one pack object to see how much of it the launcher actually needs.

**How:** Interim, which is what this row asks for: publish a second small document beside the big one. In tools\Publish-Update.ps1, after Set-Prop $doc 'packs' $packs at :812, write a `current.json` containing only launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, serverPort, voicePort, maxOnline, message and the newest N packs (N small, say the last 5). In tools\app\Updater.cs:Check, fetch current.json first and fall back to version.json when it 404s, so old launchers keep working. Serve it with an ETag, and store the returned ETag in Config so the next check sends If-None-Match and usually gets a 304. Do not prune version.json itself yet: entry 11 shows 164 of the entries are the only record of where those assets live.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` and confirm the launcher still compiles under the .NET Framework 4 csc, which is the same check .github\workflows\build.yml runs. Then prove the size: `node -e "const v=require('./version.json');console.log(JSON.stringify(v).length)"` against the same one-liner on the new current.json, and assert the new document is under a few kilobytes. A dry run of Publish-Update (its own -DryRun path) should leave version.json byte identical, which is one of the regression tests entry 30 asks for.

**Traps:** Launcher code is C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression bodied members, no out var. Updater.cs is on the do-not-edit-without-a-line-in-#dev list in CLAUDE.md, along with Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs and Publish-Update.ps1, and this change touches two of those. Anything that changes what the feed looks like must ship as one complete release, because an old launcher reading a new feed and a new launcher reading an old feed both have to work. Entry 31 (how often and from where) and entry 27 (one version per release, prerelease flags) are the same file and the same trip to GitHub, so agree the shape of all three before writing any of them.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/repos/contents , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/assets , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Caching

## 60. The rules disagree with themselves on the one rule people get banned over

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The contradiction is real and sharper than the row says. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md:102-110 is quoted exactly right: heading "## 3. Fights start freely. What they cost does not" at :102, and at :107-110 "Combat against another character does not need asking first. Ambush them on the road, rob them, corner them in an alley, start it badly and on purpose. A bandit who has to ask is not a bandit". tools\discord\content.py:172-174 says the opposite: "**4. Ask before you draw on someone.** ... Fighting another character takes one line out of character first." The cited content.py range 163-174 is right but covers rules 2 through 4; the contradicting text is specifically 172-174. The second content.py citation, 190-192, is wrong: that is the paragraph about claiming an empty title ("Claiming an empty one is fine within reason"), nothing to do with drawing on somebody. lore\game-rules.md:22-31 is correctly cited but is rule 2 (you do not decide what happens to other people), not the fight rule; the third copy's version of this rule is lore\game-rules.md:46, "## 4. Fights between people are agreed first", which agrees with Discord and contradicts the site. Add: the three copies also number differently, seven rules in content.py, nine in rules.md, ten in lore\game-rules.md, so "rule 4" means three different things depending on where somebody read it.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "In tools\discord\content.py, replace the literal RULES and GAME_RULES strings with a loader that reads tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md and lore\game-rules.md at build time"  ->  RULES and GAME_RULES are two different documents and only GAME_RULES corresponds to rules.md. RULES (tools\discord\content.py:50-82) is the seven Discord conduct rules: don't be a dick, keep it appropriate, don't wreck somebody else's game, don't pass the installer around, push to talk, bugs in #bugs, do what a moderator says. None of that is in rules.md, which is the nine roleplay rules. Build-Discord.py:147-148 posts C.RULES into #rules with the topic "Seven rules. Read them." Sourcing RULES from rules.md deletes the server conduct rules, including the only written no-slurs and no-sexual-content-involving-minors line, and replaces them with roleplay rules. Only GAME_RULES should be generated.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "the three copies also number differently, seven rules in content.py, nine in rules.md, ten in lore\game-rules.md, so "rule 4" means three different things depending on where somebody read it." (marked verified)  ->  The seven is the wrong block. tools\discord\content.py holds RULES (:50-82, seven conduct rules) and GAME_RULES (:149-212, ten roleplay rules). The contradicting text the entry is about, :172-174, is GAME_RULES rule 4. GAME_RULES numbers identically to lore\game-rules.md: both have ten and rule 4 is the fight rule in both (content.py:172, lore\game-rules.md:46). Only tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md differs, with nine, because it folds "you do not decide what happens to other people" away, so its rule 4 is "Theft and property" (rules.md:140). "Rule 4" means two things, not three.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: a loader "that reads tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md and lore\game-rules.md"  ->  Reading both keeps the contradiction the entry exists to remove. lore\game-rules.md:46-53 says "Combat against another character needs both of you wanting it. One line out of character before you draw is enough"; rules.md:107-110 says the opposite. Generating the Discord copy from both sources means Discord still carries the contradicting line. The row's own instruction is "Make rules.md the source, turn the lore copy into a pointer", which this howTo does not do.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md, whole file, because it is the public one and the one the row proposes as the source. 2. tools\discord\content.py, the RULES and GAME_RULES strings, to see that the Discord copy is a hand written duplicate rather than a render of anything. 3. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:135-215, the PLAN table, where the rules channel is mode='read' and post=C.RULES, which is the mechanism that would have to read from rules.md instead.

**How:** Make rules.md the only copy. In tools\discord\content.py, replace the literal RULES and GAME_RULES strings with a loader that reads tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md and lore\game-rules.md at build time (content.py is imported by Build-Discord.py, so a module level read with pathlib is enough), stripping the front matter and splitting on '## ' so each rule becomes its own message under Discord's 2000 character limit. Replace lore\game-rules.md's body with a pointer to /rules. Then fix the substance, which is a decision not a refactor: pick one of "fights start freely" or "ask before you draw" and make all three copies say it. Finally correct the escalation order in the enforcement section of rules.md so it names powers that exist, which today is a Discord role and nothing in the game (entry 16).

**Done when:** `python -c "import tools.discord.content"` still imports with the files loaded, and a grep proves one copy: `grep -rn "does not need asking first" tools lore` returns exactly one file, and `grep -rn "Ask before you draw" tools lore` returns zero, or the reverse, depending on which way the decision goes. Re-running Build-Discord.py is a person's job, not yours.

**Traps:** Which way this goes is a design decision between Patrick and Djinn, not a Claude's to settle: CLAUDE.md says anything that changes settled design is a few lines in #dev first. Rule numbering changes break every existing link and every moderator's habit of citing "rule 4", and the Discord copy is posted into a mode='read' channel by Build-Discord.py, so re-running the builder rewrites channel content live. content.py is also entry 74 (the welcome and start channels describe an installer that no longer exists), so if you touch this file, look at whether the two changes should go out together.

## 61. There is no public mode: the panel, the read-only posts, the access list and the age question all describe the closed playtest, and nobody ever accepts the rules

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every citation checks out. tools\discord\bot.py:272-286 is the KINDS list with the comment above it saying 'Apply to play' was deliberately removed because everybody is a playtester and "when the doors open there will be no application to play at all"; :320-321 are AGE_Q and AGE_KINDS = ('playtest',); :703-714 is the PANEL text naming "Apply to playtest" as "the one everybody starts with". tools\docs-app\server.js:60-74 is the access block, and the comment at :62-70 says exactly what the row quotes: "When the server opens to everybody, add 'Member' to this list and that is the entire change." tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:471-475 is the ChkAge checkbox, text "I am 18 or over. Thornswood is an adult server." at :475. The row's strongest claim also holds: that checkbox is the only acceptance anywhere in the install path and it is about age, not about rules. Grep finds no rules acceptance, no consent record and no accepted-at field in bot.py, server.js or Bootstrap.cs.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo (1): "server.js:71-72, when cfg.open is true append 'Member' to cfg.access.play"  ->  tools\docs-app\server.js:71 is `access: {` and :72 is the admin list; the play list is :73-74. The instruction names cfg.access.play so it is unambiguous, but the line pair points at the admin roles. Everything else in #61 checks out, including the claim that Bootstrap.cs:471-475 is the only acceptance in the install path: grep for accept, agree and consent across bot.py, server.js and Bootstrap.cs finds nothing about rules.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:60-80, the access role lists, because that comment is the single line the row is about and it tells you what the current design intends. 2. tools\discord\bot.py:270-330, KINDS, ROLE_FOR and the age question, so you see which ticket kinds are playtest-only. 3. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:329-347, the notice bar and the hero button, because entry 75 is the same switch on the site and the row says they should be one flag.

**How:** Add one flag and read it in four places. Put `open: false` in the site config (the same config.json on the VPS that holds cfg.access), expose it on an existing public route so the bot and the pages can read it, then: (1) server.js:71-72, when cfg.open is true append 'Member' to cfg.access.play at load time rather than editing the literal; (2) bot.py, filter KINDS so 'playtest' is dropped and a plain 'Apply to play' or nothing at all appears when open; (3) bot.py PANEL at :703-714, two variants of the text; (4) home.html, the notice bar at 329-335 and the hero button at 347. For the rules acceptance, the cheapest honest version is Discord's own onboarding rules screen plus a recorded role, with the role name added to cfg.access.play, so entry becomes an explicit act with a timestamp on it.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` and `python -m py_compile tools/discord/bot.py` both pass, and with the flag flipped in a local copy of the config a unit check of standing() returns play true for a member carrying only the Member role and false with the flag off. Do not test by pointing anything at the live Discord; Build-Discord.py and Install-Bot.ps1 are Patrick's to run.

**Traps:** This is the launch day switch, so it must be tried on dev first, and CLAUDE.md warns there is one dev feed and the last dev Ship wins, so say in #dev when you take it. The Bootstrap.cs age checkbox is in the installer, which is a separate artefact from the launcher and is already stale by design (entry 33), so a change there does not reach anybody who installed earlier. Entry 75 is the site half of the same flag and entry 77 deletes the hub and the member password, which also touches server.js:60-80: three rows converge on the same twenty lines, so sequence them. Bootstrap.cs is C# compiled by the same .NET Framework csc, so the C# 5 restrictions apply there too.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/oauth2 , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 62. A Discord outage or rate limit tells every launcher they are not in the Discord, and erases their grace period

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The defect is real and the supporting detail is slightly off. tools\docs-app\server.js:1243-1259 is the /api/play/join route and it genuinely never tests st.lookupFailed: standing() leaves inGuild false on any non-404 failure (server.js:645-650), so a Discord outage or a 429 falls into `if (!st.inGuild) return sendJson(res, 403, {ok:false, why:'guild', message:'That Discord account is not in the Thornswood Discord.'})` at :1252-1254. tools\app\Door.cs:157-167 then treats why=='guild' as a real no and calls Remember(false) at :167, erasing the remembered yes. Both halves verified. The wording "as the other three routes already do" overstates it: exactly one route answers 503 on a failed lookup, /api/owns at server.js:1378-1379, and its own comment at :1367-1376 says "Could not ask Discord is not the same answer as not allowed" and points at /api/allowed as the precedent. The other two places that notice lookupFailed do something weaker: server.js:589 records the outcome as 'could not check' in the sign-in log, and :708-714 renders it on the refusal page. So the pattern to copy is one route, not three, and it is already written twenty lines away.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo: "immediately after `const st = await standing(t.d);` at :1251"  ->  That statement is at tools\docs-app\server.js:1250; :1251 is standCache.delete(String(t.d)). Inserting after :1251 still lands before the inGuild test at :1252, so the fix works as written. Citation only. The rest of #62 is fully confirmed: grep finds exactly one 503 in server.js, at :1379.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:1364-1381, the /api/owns route, because the exact fix is already written there with a comment explaining why. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:613-664, standing(), to see that inGuild false and lookupFailed set is the failure shape, not a no. 3. tools\app\Door.cs:110-200, the whole answer handler, so you see what Remember(true), Remember(false) and the 'offline' branch at :120 and :143 already do.

**How:** Site side: in tools\docs-app\server.js, immediately after `const st = await standing(t.d);` at :1251 insert `if (st.lookupFailed) return sendJson(res, 503, { ok:false, why:'outage', message:'Could not reach Discord just now. Try again in a minute.' });` before the inGuild test. Launcher side: in tools\app\Door.cs, extend the branch at :143 so `if (st.Why == "offline" || st.Why == "outage")` takes the same path, which is the one that leaves the remembered answer alone. Do not add 'outage' to the Remember(false) list at :157. Keep it C# 5: a plain string comparison with ||, no pattern matching, no switch expression.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes and `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe. For behaviour without Discord: stub discordGet to resolve {ok:false, status:429} and call the /api/play/join handler, asserting a 503 with why 'outage' rather than a 403 with why 'guild'. For the launcher half, confirm by reading Door.cs that the 'outage' string reaches the same branch as 'offline' and never reaches Remember(false).

**Traps:** Door.cs is not on CLAUDE.md's do-not-touch list but Checks.cs and Shell.cs nearby are, and this is a two-sided change: a launcher that understands 'outage' and a site that does not, or the reverse, must both behave. Ship the pair as one complete release, which CLAUDE.md requires anyway. The old launchers already installed will keep seeing a 503 as an unrecognised answer, so make sure the launcher's default for an unknown Why is the safe one (it currently logs and shouts rather than calling Remember(false), which is correct). C# 5 only: no ?. and no string interpolation in Door.cs. Entry 57 is the other half of the same Discord failure story; fix them in the same week or the allow list and the join door will disagree about what an outage means.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/503

## 63. When a private thread cannot open, reports and applications are posted into the public channel

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and the safety claim is stronger than the row states. tools\discord\bot.py:539-551 is the fallback: `fell_back = False` at :539, `if not posted and ch is not None:` at :540, and `await ch.send(('**%s**\n%s%s' % (title, head, note))[:1900])` at :546 posting into the tickets channel. :571-575 is the followup telling the person it went to the channel. :752-757 is the startup path, which only logs the warning and carries on. The row's cited 531-551 is right if you include the block comment; the code itself is 539-551. The part that makes this a safety problem rather than a formatting one: tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:163-164 defines the tickets channel with mode='open', and the mode legend at Build-Discord.py:136-139 defines open as "anybody talks", so every member of the server can read it. The code comment at bot.py:531-538 claims "That channel is already the one the panel lives in, so it is no more public than the thread would have been", which is false: a private thread is visible only to its members and staff, the channel is visible to everyone. A report about a person, and the reporter's name, lands in front of the reported person.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo (1): "In bot.py:540, change the fallback target from the tickets channel to the staff log channel: log_channel(guild) is already used at :748 and that channel is deny-first in the PLAN."  ->  log_channel() does not resolve to a staff channel in a server built by Build-Discord.py, so this fix changes nothing. TICKET_LOG defaults to 'tickets,ticket-log' (tools\discord\bot.py:80-82; tools\Install-Bot.ps1:38), by_name takes the first name in the list that exists (bot.py:145-160), and Build-Discord.py:163 creates a channel literally named `tickets` with mode='open', which the legend at Build-Discord.py:136 defines as "anybody talks". log_channel only creates the deny-first channel when none of the names exist (bot.py:177-179), and here one does. The PLAN's staff channels are staff-general, dev-notes, deploys and reports (Build-Discord.py:217-225); none is named in TICKET_LOG. Applying this howTo moves a report about a person from one channel everyone can read to another channel everyone can read, while the write-up states it is now staff only. The collision between TICKET_LOG's first entry and the PLAN's public `tickets` is itself an unreported defect, and bot.py:75-79 asserts the opposite in a comment.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:163-164 defines the tickets channel with mode='open' ... so every member of the server can read it", given as the channel the fallback posts into  ->  Wrong channel, right conclusion. The fallback posts into `ch`, the panel channel, which is TICKETS = 'tickets-and-applications,applications' (bot.py:70-71; Install-Bot.ps1:34), and in the PLAN that resolves to `applications` at Build-Discord.py:166-167. The channel at :163-164 named `tickets` is what TICKET_LOG resolves to. Both carry mode='open', so the finding that it is publicly readable stands, but the citation points at a different channel and is what the broken howTo above was built on.

**Read first:** 1. tools\discord\bot.py:490-580, TicketModal.on_submit end to end, so you see the thread attempt, the exception, the fallback and the three different followup messages. 2. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:135-175, the PLAN table and the mode legend, which is what proves the fallback channel is world readable. 3. tools\discord\bot.py:211-230, thread_block(), the function that already knows in advance whether threads will work.

**How:** Fail closed in three steps. (1) In bot.py:540, change the fallback target from the tickets channel to the staff log channel: log_channel(guild) is already used at :748 and that channel is deny-first in the PLAN. (2) Keep the person's confirmation honest: the :571-575 followup should say it reached staff, not that it is in the channel. (3) At startup, bot.py:752-757, when thread_block(guild, ch) returns a reason, refuse to publish the ticket panel at all rather than logging and continuing, so the permission gets fixed before a stranger opens a report. If refusing to start is too blunt, at minimum drop the 'report' kind out of KINDS while threads are broken, since that is the one with a person on the other end of it.

**Done when:** `python -m py_compile tools/discord/bot.py` passes, and a stub test proves the target: monkeypatch the thread creation to raise, call on_submit with a fake interaction, and assert the send lands on the object returned by log_channel and never on the object returned by by_name(guild, TICKETS). Grep is a cheap second check: `grep -n "if not posted and ch is not None" tools/discord/bot.py` should no longer exist.

**Traps:** The comment at bot.py:531-538 argues for the current behaviour, so removing it without replacing the reasoning will read as an unexplained revert to the next person; write down why the channel is not equivalent to a thread. Do not change the tickets channel to mode='staff' as a shortcut: the panel lives there and members have to be able to open it. Build-Discord.py rewrites live channel permissions when run and is Patrick's to run, not yours. Entry 68 (no ticket cap) is the same modal path, and entry 84 (the inbox trimmed to 2000) is where these tickets also land, so a flood in one shows up as lost tickets in the other.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/permissions

## 64. Nothing anybody says is written down, so every report is one person's word against another's

**Category:** Moderation  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both counts check out. tools\voice\voice-server.js contains exactly seven console calls in the whole file (lines 234, 349, 355, 374, 401, 543, 544), and none of them is in the chat branch at :495-533. tools\gamemode.js:3038 is exactly `log('command: /ooc', actorId.toString(16), 'heard by', heard);` with no text argument. Worth adding for whoever picks this up: text chat does not pass through the game server at all. It rides the voice socket (voice-server.js:495-533, and the comment at :490-494 explains that adding a packet type to skymp5-client would mean recompiling it), so the only place chat text can be captured is the voice service, which entry 7 says is not the code that is deployed. The gamemode sees only slash commands, which arrive by the queue at :513-520.

**Read first:** 1. tools\voice\voice-server.js:486-535, the chat branch and the comment above it explaining why chat lives here and not in the game server. 2. tools\gamemode.js:2982-3040, the /ooc handler, which is the only other place words are exchanged and which already counts hearers without keeping the line. 3. Entry 83's target, tools\docs-app\server.js:583-606, because whatever retention you pick has to be declared and that is the file where the other personal data already lives.

**How:** Write chat to a rolling file in the voice service and give the moderator a way to pull a window of it. In voice-server.js, add a small appendLine(obj) that JSON-stringifies one row per line to chat.log with fs.appendFile, and call it in the chat branch just before the broadcast at :532 with {at, profileId, name, text, cell}. Roll the file the way entry 25 asks for the other services (chat.log to chat.log.1, keep five). Then add a loopback-only GET /chatlog?from=&to= beside the existing loopback routes at :383-402 so the gamemode or a staff tool can fetch a window without anyone reading the file by hand. For /ooc, change gamemode.js:3038 to include the text alongside the hearer count. Pick a retention window first, thirty to ninety days is normal, and make the roller enforce it.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` passes; start the service locally on a spare port, connect two sockets, send a chat line from one, and confirm chat.log gained exactly one JSON line containing the text and the profile id. Confirm the roll: write past the size limit and check chat.log.1 appears and the sixth file is gone.

**Traps:** This is personal data and it cannot land before entry 83's privacy page says it exists, how long it is kept and how to ask for deletion; the row says so and it is the correct order. The deployed voice service is not this file (entry 7), so a change here may not reach the running process at all until that is resolved. Everything a moderator would act on also depends on identity being real, which is entry 15: a log keyed on a profile number the client chose proves nothing. Install-Voice.ps1 and every Deploy-* script are the person's to run. Adding disk writes to the 200ms path would be a mistake; only the chat branch should touch the disk.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html , https://gdpr-info.eu/art-5-gdpr/

## 65. Chat is server-wide, unfiltered, has no rate limit anywhere, and no name is ever checked

**Category:** Moderation  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance is verified and one line reference is off. There is no throttle, no duplicate check and no word list anywhere: grep for throttle, ratelimit, rate limit, cooldown and slowmode across tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js and tools\voice\voice-server.js returns nothing, and grep for badword, profanity, slur, wordlist and filter across tools\voice\, tools\ts-ui\ and tools\gamemode.js returns only unrelated CSS and array filters. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js:216-238 is correctly cited: window.thornswoodNet.say begins at :217 and the send is at :233, with only a local /roll rewrite and a 20 message offline queue in between. The voice-server citation is wrong: 527-536 lands in the middle of the `out` object literal. The chat handler is voice-server.js:495-533, the only limit on it is `.slice(0, 400)` at :497, and the broadcast to every peer is the single line at :532. On names: verified by absence, there is no validation of a character name anywhere in tools\app\Creation.cs or tools\gamemode.js.

**Read first:** 1. tools\voice\voice-server.js:486-535, the chat handler, which is where both a rate limit and a filter have to go because it is the only server side the text passes through. 2. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js:216-241, say(), so you understand what the client already does (the /roll rewrite, the 400 character server cap, the offline queue) and do not duplicate it. 3. tools\gamemode.js:2982-3040, the /ooc handler, because it has its own 300 character cap at :2992 and is the second text path.

**How:** All of it goes in the voice service, because a client side limit is advice. In voice-server.js, give each peer a token bucket: in onJoin (:179-188) add `tokens: 5, lastFill: Date.now()` to the peer record; at the top of the chat branch (:495) refill at, say, one token per 1.5 seconds to a ceiling of 5, and when the bucket is empty drop the line and send that one peer a `{t:'chat', name:'', text:'Slow down a moment.'}` rather than broadcasting. Add a duplicate check by keeping the last line per peer and dropping an identical repeat inside ten seconds. For the filter, put a small module beside voice-server.js exporting a function over a short list of the terms that end an account, normalise the text first (lowercase, strip non-letters, collapse repeats) and reject rather than star out, so nothing is silently altered. Run character names through the same function where the name is accepted, which is the /choose path (voice-server.js:340-380) plus tools\app\Creation.cs on the launcher side.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` passes; a local harness connects one socket and sends twenty chat lines in one second, and asserts at most five are broadcast and the rest are dropped with a single notice back to the sender; a second harness sends a term from the list and asserts nothing is broadcast to the other peer. No game needed.

**Traps:** The word list is content nobody wants in a public repository's history in plain form; keep it short, keep it in its own file, and decide with Patrick whether it belongs in the repo or in the service's config on the VPS, since CLAUDE.md's rule is that things like this are named by location. Entry 71 rewrites this same handler to make plain text local, so a rate limit written now has to survive that change: write the bucket into the peer record, not into the broadcast loop. Entry 58 is also in these lines. The deployed voice service is not this file (entry 7). A name filter that rejects after the character already exists is worse than none, so the launcher side has to refuse before the choice is stored.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/net.html , https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Token_bucket

## 66. Voice has no mute and no block

**Category:** Moderation  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is true; the evidence is quoted slightly wrong. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:1015-1017 does say "There is no mute", but read it in context: it is about the local microphone, "Push to talk already means the microphone is shut unless a key is held, so a second control for shut was answering a question nobody asked." That is self-mute, not muting somebody else, so the file does not in fact say in its own words that there is no way to silence another person. The real evidence is absence: :835-851 is the per-connection loop and it sets rec.gain.gain.value to 0 only when the other person is in a different cell or has no position, and to 1 otherwise, with the distance feeding the panner position rather than the gain, and there is no per-profileId mute set, block list or persisted preference anywhere in the file. The cited 837-849 is within the loop and correct. So: no per-person mute, no block, verified by reading the whole loop.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:820-855, the spatialisation loop, because that is the one place a per-person gain decision can be made. 2. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js:1000-1030, the public API object, to see what the rest of the UI can already call and where a setMuted would sit beside setMode. 3. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js:216-241, because the roster and the names people would click on come from the same socket.

**How:** Client side is enough for mute and is the smaller half. In thornswood-voice.js add `var muted = {};` near the other module state, expose `setMuted: function (profileId, on) { muted[String(profileId)] = !!on; }` and `isMuted` on the window.thornswoodVoice object around :1005-1020, and in the loop at :835 add `if (muted[String(rec.profileId)]) { rec.gain.gain.value = 0; rec.dist = null; return; }` as the first test inside the forEach. Persist the set in localStorage so it survives a reload. Then put it in front of people: the voice indicator already lists who is near, so a click on a name toggles it. Block, meaning they cannot hear you either, cannot be done client side and needs the voice service to stop relaying between the pair, which belongs with entry 56's relay work rather than here.

**Done when:** Open tools\ts-ui\thornswood-voice.js in a browser console harness, or simply assert by reading: call setMuted(123, true), run the loop with a fake lastWho entry for profile 123 in the same cell at zero distance, and confirm rec.gain.gain.value is 0. A syntax check is the cheap gate: `node --check tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js`.

**Traps:** This file runs in the launcher's embedded browser through tools\launcher\voice-host.html, not in the game, so nothing here is Skyrim Platform and nothing here can call mp. A mute that only hides audio does not stop the other person's home address being handed over, because the peer connection is already established: that is entry 56 and it is the honest half of "block". Do not add a server side mute list until identity is real (entry 15), or a mute is keyed on a number anybody can claim. Voice is entries 4, 55, 56 and 58 as well, and several of those rewrite this file's connection handling, so agree the order in #dev.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/GainNode , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Web_Audio_API

## 67. The Discord role a ban depends on is never created

**Category:** Moderation  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly, by name and by absence. tools\docs-app\server.js:75 is `banned: ['Banned']` inside the access block, and :658-662 is where it is used: `out.banned = has(cfg.access.banned);` at :659 followed by the early return that clears admin and play. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:100-131 is the ROLES list, running from `ROLES = [` at :100 through the Member append at :131, and a grep for "Banned" across the whole of Build-Discord.py returns zero hits. So the site checks for a role the builder never creates. The consequence chain is worth spelling out: server.js:1252-1257 refuses a join on st.banned, gamemode.js's allow list drops banned profiles, and all of it is keyed on a role name that has to be typed by hand into Discord by somebody who knows the exact spelling, capital B included.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:60-80 and 613-664, so you see that every ban decision in the system is a string match on a role name from config. 2. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:95-133, the ROLES table and the permission constants, which is where a role is created. 3. tools\docs-app\server.js:1292-1331, /api/allowed, because that is what turns the role into the game server's allow list.

**How:** Smallest version, do it first: in tools\discord\Build-Discord.py add `('Banned', 0x4A4A4A, False, PLAIN),` to the ROLES list, near the bottom beside Member at :131, so the role exists with no permissions and is not hoisted. Better version, which is what entry 5 needs anyway: stop depending on a role name. Add a bans.json beside the site's other data (the same folder as linked.json and signins.json), a map of discord id to {at, by, until, reason}, write to it from a bot command, and have standing() at server.js:658-662 set out.banned when either the role is present or the id is in bans.json. The role then becomes a visible marker rather than the mechanism, and a typo stops being a security hole.

**Done when:** `python -c "import ast,sys; ast.parse(open('tools/discord/Build-Discord.py').read())"` passes and `grep -n "'Banned'" tools/discord/Build-Discord.py` returns a line. For the bans.json version, `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` plus a stub test: put an id in a temp bans.json, call standing() with a stubbed discordGet returning an ordinary member, and assert banned is true and play is false.

**Traps:** Entry 5 is the enforcement (the door is in watch-only mode with enforce false, so a ban currently does nothing even when the role exists) and this row is only the fact that the thing being enforced does not exist. Doing this one alone changes no behaviour, which is fine, but do not report it as "bans now work". Build-Discord.py creates and reorders live roles when run and is Patrick's to run. A hand made Banned role that already exists in the server with different capitalisation will not match cfg.access.banned, which is exactly the failure mode this row is about, so check the live server's spelling before adding a second one. Everything here is keyed on the Discord id, which is only as good as entry 15.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/permissions , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 68. No cap on tickets per person and no cooldown

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified by absence, and the absence is complete. tools\discord\bot.py:689-700 is TicketPicker, whose callback at :697-700 does nothing but `await interaction.response.send_modal(TicketModal(k, lab))`: no check of who is asking, no count of what they already have open, no timestamp. :423-580 is TicketModal.on_submit, which creates a thread and posts, again with no cap. A grep of the whole file for cooldown finds nothing, and the only mention of an open ticket limit is a comment at :494 about a member who could not be added to a thread. The Member role is never required: the select is registered with custom_id 'thornswood:ticket' on a persistent view, so anybody who can see the panel channel can open the modal, and Build-Discord.py:163 gives that channel mode='open'.

**Read first:** 1. tools\discord\bot.py:676-715, TicketView, TicketPicker and PANEL, the whole path from a click to a modal. 2. tools\discord\bot.py:423-580, on_submit, so you know what one ticket actually costs: a thread, staff added to it, a post, and a push to the site inbox at :563. 3. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:135-175, to confirm the panel channel is open to everyone.

**How:** In bot.py, add a module level dict `_last_ticket = {}` mapping user id to a timestamp, and in TicketPicker.callback before send_modal: refuse with an ephemeral message when the person opened one inside the cooldown (five minutes is a reasonable start). For the one-open-ticket rule, before send_modal walk the tickets channel's active threads (`ch.threads`, plus `ch.archived_threads()` if you want closed ones to count) and look for one whose name carries the person's id, which on_submit already puts in the thread name; if found, reply ephemerally with its .mention rather than opening a modal. For the role check, add `if not any(r.name == 'Member' for r in interaction.user.roles): return await interaction.response.send_message(...)`, but only when the role actually exists in the guild, since a fresh server may not have it yet.

**Done when:** `python -m py_compile tools/discord/bot.py` passes, and a stub test drives TicketPicker.callback twice with the same fake user inside the cooldown and asserts send_modal was called once and send_message once. Do not test against the live Discord.

**Traps:** The push at bot.py:563 lands in the same site inbox that entry 84 shows is trimmed to a fixed 2000 rows shared with the anonymous public report form, so a ticket flood silently deletes real tickets: fix the two together or the cap here just moves the damage. Entry 63 is the same submit path (a failed thread posts publicly), and entry 61 changes KINDS on launch day, so three rows edit the same two hundred lines. The cooldown dict is in memory, so it resets every time the wrapper loop at Install-Bot.ps1:360-368 restarts the process, which per entry 69 is every fifteen seconds when the bot is unhealthy; that is acceptable for a cooldown and not acceptable for the one-open-ticket rule, which is why that one reads the threads rather than memory.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits

## 69. Nothing tells anyone when the bot is down, and the panel is the first thing a stranger touches

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. tools\Install-Bot.ps1:360-368 is the generated run.ps1 wrapper: `while ($true) { & cmd /c "python bot.py >> bot.log 2>&1"; Start-Sleep -Seconds 15 }`, with no failure counter and no ceiling, and the cited 360-388 also covers the scheduled task registration, which sets RestartCount 999 with a one minute interval and no execution time limit, running as SYSTEM. tools\discord\bot.py:851-862 is the `client.run(TOKEN)` block whose handlers for PrivilegedIntentsRequired and LoginFailure print advice and `raise SystemExit(1)`, which the wrapper treats identically to a crash and retries forever. A grep of Install-Bot.ps1 for webhook, heartbeat and alert returns nothing, so the claim that nothing tells anyone is verified by absence.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Install-Bot.ps1:340-392, the generated run.ps1 and the scheduled task, which is the actual supervision. 2. tools\discord\bot.py:845-862, the exit paths, so you know which failures are permanent (a rejected token) and which are worth retrying. 3. tools\Watch-Services.ps1:72-141, because entry 20's three-strikes rule is the pattern the row says to keep and not to duplicate.

**How:** Three small pieces. (1) Heartbeat: in bot.py's on_ready or a one minute task loop, GET a monitor url read from the bot's config file on the VPS, so a silent bot raises an alarm from outside the box. (2) Give up on permanent failures: in the generated run.ps1 in Install-Bot.ps1:360-368, capture $LASTEXITCODE, count consecutive fast exits (under, say, 30 seconds of runtime), and after three of them post to the alerts webhook and exit the loop rather than spinning. (3) Tell somebody: post from that failure branch to the private alerts channel webhook, which is the same machinery entry 20 wants for the other services. Name the webhook by its location in the VPS config; never paste it into a script in the repo.

**Done when:** `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile('<abs path>\tools\Install-Bot.ps1',[ref]$null,[ref]$null)"` reports no parse errors, which is the same check .github\workflows\build.yml runs over every script. Then test the generated wrapper in isolation: write the run.ps1 body to a temp file with `python bot.py` replaced by a command that exits immediately, run it locally, and confirm it stops after three fast exits instead of looping.

**Traps:** Install-Bot.ps1 writes files on the VPS and registers a scheduled task; it is the person's to run, not yours. The script is a here-string that generates another script, so every `$` that belongs to the generated file is escaped with a backtick: get that wrong and the check will still parse but the wrapper will misbehave at runtime. Entry 20 is the watchdog and its three-strikes rule is explicitly called correct and to be left alone, so do not fold this into Watch-Services.ps1. Entry 22 notes every service runs as SYSTEM with RunLevel Highest, which this task does too; the low-privilege account work belongs there and would change this registration, so sequence them.

**Links:** https://healthchecks.io/docs/ , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/gateway , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 70. The bot is invited with Administrator and keeps it

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly. tools\Setup-Discord.ps1:24-26 reads "4. OAuth2, URL Generator, tick \"bot\" and then \"Administrator\". Open the URL it builds and add the bot to your server." tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:33-34 gives the same instruction in the same words. Nothing anywhere untick it afterwards, and nothing computes a reduced permission integer, so the bot is invited with Administrator and keeps it. Two things sharpen the row. First, the reason it was written that way is real and is in the file: Build-Discord.py:39-40 notes the bot must sit above the roles it creates, and it creates eighteen or more roles plus every channel in PLAN, so a first build genuinely needs a lot. Second, that is an argument for reducing after the build, which is exactly what the row's fix says.

**Read first:** 1. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:20-45, the setup instructions and the note about role ordering, so you know why Administrator was asked for. 2. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:95-215, ROLES and PLAN, which is the full list of what the builder creates and therefore what permissions it genuinely needs. 3. tools\discord\bot.py:100-110 and 239-262, the intents and what the running bot does day to day, which is far less than the builder.

**How:** Work out the real permission set from PLAN and ROLES: manage roles, manage channels, view channels, send messages, create public and private threads, send messages in threads, manage messages, manage threads, embed links, attach files, read message history. Sum those bit values into one integer, and at the end of a successful Build-Discord.py run print the reduced invite url with that integer plus scope=bot, followed by one line telling the person to untick Administrator on the bot's role now the build is done. Keep the Administrator invite in the first-run instructions only if the reduced set genuinely cannot create the community features, and say which step needs it. Never widen the running bot's permissions to make a new feature work without a line in #dev.

**Done when:** `python -c "import ast; ast.parse(open('tools/discord/Build-Discord.py').read())"` passes and running the builder's help or dry path prints an invite url whose permissions parameter is a specific integer rather than 8. Verify the integer by decoding it against the documented bit values before anybody uses it.

**Traps:** Do not run Build-Discord.py or Setup-Discord.ps1: they rewrite a live Discord server's roles, channels and channel content. Reducing permissions on a live bot can break the next build, because the builder needs more than the runtime does, so the honest shape is two permission sets and a note saying which is which. Entry 22 is the same blast-radius argument for the VPS and lists the bot token among what a backup zip would expose, so this row and that one share a threat model. Discord's community features (forum channels, announcement channels, the rules screen) are referenced in PLAN and may need permissions the reduced set drops; check each channel kind in PLAN against the list before publishing the integer.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/permissions , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/oauth2 , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/intents.html

## 71. In-character text goes to everyone on the server, only /ooc is local, and there is no /me

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All three citations are exact. tools\voice\voice-server.js:495-533 is the chat branch, and the broadcast at :532 is `for (const [, p] of peers) p.ws.send(out);`, every typed line to every connected socket with no distance test. tools\gamemode.js:2829 is `var OOC_RANGE = 4000;` with the comment above it, and :2986-3040 is the /ooc handler, which does the position and cell test and counts hearers. No /me and no /do exist: grep for them across gamemode.js and thornswood-chat.js returns only an unrelated comment at gamemode.js:3094. The architectural detail that makes this harder than it looks is in the file: voice-server.js:490-494 explains chat rides the voice socket precisely because adding a packet type to skymp5-client would mean recompiling it, and the voice service is the only component that knows where everybody is (it receives positions at POST /positions from the gamemode and holds them in the same map the 200ms tick uses).

**Read first:** 1. tools\voice\voice-server.js:486-535, the chat branch, plus :192-206 where positions already live, because everything a range test needs is already in that file. 2. tools\gamemode.js:2825-3045, OOC_RANGE and the /ooc handler, which is the working reference implementation of a local line. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md:87-140, rules 2 and 3, because the row's point is that the chat cannot express attempting versus landing and the rules depend on it.

**How:** Put the range test in the voice service, where the positions already are. In voice-server.js, before the broadcast at :532, look up the sender's entry in the positions map by profileId and, for each peer, skip anyone in a different cell or beyond a range constant; mirror gamemode.js's OOC_RANGE (4000 units) as the default. Then add the verbs: parse a leading /me or /do in the chat branch and set a kind field on the broadcast object, wider ranges for /shout and a much shorter one for /whisper, and route /ooc to the existing global-or-local behaviour so out of character stays distinguishable. On the client, tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js renders whatever arrives, so give each kind its own colour and prefix there. Leave the game server's /ooc alone; it already works and is the fallback when the voice socket is down.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` and `node --check tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js` pass; a local harness connects three sockets, feeds positions through POST /positions putting two in one cell close together and one far away, sends a plain line from the first, and asserts exactly two sockets received it. That is a real test of the whole feature without starting the game.

**Traps:** Whoever does this owns the same forty lines as entries 58, 64 and 65, so agree an order in #dev rather than merging three branches into one handler. Do not move chat into skymp5-client: CLAUDE.md is explicit that the client bundle is not built here, only patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is, and the comment at voice-server.js:490-494 records that this was the reason chat lives where it does. The deployed voice service is not this file (entry 7), so confirm what is actually running first. Making chat local changes what moderators can see, which ties to entry 64 (nothing is logged) and entry 16 (a moderator has no observe mode): a local chat with no log is harder to moderate than a global one, so land the log first or at the same time.

## 72. The Play page tells a new person to name the character after themselves, and to save before connecting

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The card is quoted correctly and the claim about Help.cs is half wrong. tools\ui.xaml:575-584 is the FirstRunCard and it says exactly "Create a character and name it after yourself. That name is what other people see above your head, and it cannot be changed later without a new character." at :579 and "Save once before connecting. Joining from an unsaved game drops you back to the menu." at :581. But tools\app\Help.cs:14-15 does not say the opposite, it says the same thing: the first Joining item is titled "1.  Make a character, named after yourself". Only the save line is contradicted, by Help.cs:26-27, "3.  You do not need to save first ... The old mod would not accept a connection from an unsaved game and this one does not care." So the correct statement of the finding is: the save advice is contradicted two pages away and is untrue here, and the name-after-yourself advice is not contradicted at all, it is repeated in both places, which means fixing the card alone leaves the launcher still telling people to walk into Whiterun under their own name. Also note Help.cs:26 is itself one of the leftover old-mod references entry 73 is about.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ui.xaml:575-585, the FirstRunCard, and the code that shows and hides it (grep FirstRunCard in tools\app\Shell.cs) so you know when a person sees it. 2. tools\app\Help.cs:12-30, the Joining items, because the same advice is there and both have to change. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md and tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md, so the launcher's wording matches what the site already tells people.

**How:** In tools\ui.xaml replace the :579 text with a name rule: a first name and a family name or a byname that would not look out of place in Skyrim, not your own name, and it cannot be changed later without a new character. Delete the :581 TextBlock and the Rectangle separator above it, since the save line is untrue here. Then make the same edit in tools\app\Help.cs:14-15 so the two agree, and either delete Help.cs:26-27 or rewrite it without the old mod sentence. If you want the check rather than the advice, the name is accepted on the launcher side in tools\app\Creation.cs and stored through the voice service's /choose route, which is where a server side rule would go, and it is the same hook as the name filter in entry 65.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe, which is the compile plus XAML check the CI runs. Then grep proves the copy: `grep -rn "name it after yourself\|named after yourself" tools/ui.xaml tools/app/Help.cs` returns nothing, and `grep -n "Save once before connecting" tools/ui.xaml` returns nothing.

**Traps:** ui.xaml is embedded into the exe by Build-Launcher.ps1, so a text change is a full launcher release, not a config edit; per CLAUDE.md that means launcher, game files and server go out together. Launcher code is C# 5 only, so if you add any code behind this card, no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression bodied members, no out var. Do not add a name check that rejects after the character exists: the choice is stored through the voice service and the character is created in the game, so a late refusal strands somebody. Entry 73 rebuilds Help.cs wholesale, so coordinate: if that work is happening, make this edit part of it rather than a separate pull request, since CLAUDE.md asks for one fix per pull request and these two are genuinely one fix.

## 73. The launcher Help page is the old project's help page with edits: a dead address, a password box that does not exist, and an explanation of parties

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The headline is verified and three of the eight line references are weak. Verified: tools\app\Help.cs:155-156 contains the dead address, "The address has to be 192.135.119.166:25565 in full", and :158-159 is the password item, "The password you entered is incorrect" with "The build carries the password", both describing a screen the launcher no longer has. :129-130 is the parties item, which opens "The old mod had parties, a leader, and teleporting to each other." :26 mentions the old mod in the save item. :87 mentions "how Together carries sound" in the Bard item. :17-27 is correctly cited as the Joining block. The weak three: :143 is the SunHelm needs display and :146 is what dying costs, neither of which is old-project text (they may have been cited for other reasons, and SunHelm is separately entry 52's broken mod); :199 is the mods-list tip, which mentions the other server plugins but is not the old mod content. So the row is right that the page is the old project's with edits, and a stranger following it would be told to type an address into a screen that does not exist, but three of its citations do not support that claim and should not be used as a checklist.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Help.cs end to end, all 214 lines, because the page is one array of HelpItem and you need to see which items are current and which are inherited. 2. tools\docs-app\data\site\help.md and known.md, which the row names as the current, accurate source and which the site already serves publicly (server.js:164-174 lists /known and /help as public pages). 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md, so the launcher's joining section matches the one story the site tells.

**How:** Do not patch item by item. Rebuild the array from help.md and known.md: keep the HelpItem(category, title, body) shape, write one item per heading, and add the two sections that do not exist anywhere yet, a chat and commands section (what /ooc does, its range, what the console does per entry 49) and a character section (the name rule from entry 72, what the choices do per entry 50). Delete every item that starts from the old engine: :17-27's save item, :87's Together sentence, :129-133's parties and quests items, :155-159's address and password items. If you want the page to stop drifting, go further and have Build-Launcher.ps1 embed help.md as a resource that Help.cs parses at runtime, which is the same trick the site uses and makes the next rewrite a markdown edit.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe, and greps prove the leftovers are gone: `grep -n "OldMod\|192.135.119.166\|password you entered" tools/app/Help.cs` returns nothing.

**Traps:** C# 5 only in the launcher: the HelpItem array is verbatim string literals, so no string interpolation and no expression bodied members if you add helpers. The address in :155 is an old-mod address and is not the current server, which is named in version.json's serverAddress; do not copy the current one into the help text, because entry 27 and entry 59 both want the address to come from the feed so raising or moving it does not need a reinstall. This ships as a launcher release, so launcher, game files and server go together. Entry 72 edits :14-15 and :26-27 of this same file and entry 49 (the console explanation) and entry 50 (the choices that do nothing) both change what this page should say, so read those three rows before writing the new text.

## 74. The read-only channels describe an installer that no longer exists and never mention Vortex

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and the contradiction is direct rather than a matter of tone. tools\discord\content.py:15-16 says "It doesn't touch the Skyrim you already have. It installs its own copy, its own mods, its own launcher, off to one side, and leaves yours alone", while tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md:16-17 says "Thornswood goes into the Skyrim you already have. It does not make a second copy of the game." content.py:34 tells people to "Grab both installer files" and :92-96 is the heading "## 1. Two files, same folder" describing a small exe and a large zip that must sit together, with :100-101 saying "First run copies about twenty gigabytes into place", against join.md:44 "One file" and :46-47 "It is small because the mods are not in it ... about half a gigabyte". content.py:273-274 is the FAQ item "Will this mess up the Skyrim I already play? No. It installs somewhere else entirely and never touches yours." And the row's last claim is exactly right: a grep of the entire content.py for Vortex, collection or Nexus returns zero hits, so the Discord never mentions the mod manager, the collection or the Nexus account a person actually needs.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md, whole file, because it is the current and correct account and the row proposes generating from it. 2. tools\discord\content.py, the WELCOME, START and FAQ strings, to see how much is hand written prose rather than generated. 3. tools\discord\Build-Discord.py:135-175, the PLAN table, where each channel names its post=C.<NAME>, which is the mechanism that would read the markdown instead.

**How:** Same shape as entry 60. In content.py, replace the literal WELCOME, START and FAQ bodies with functions that read tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md and help.md at import time and split them into Discord sized chunks, keeping the <#CHANNEL> placeholder substitution that Build-Discord.py already does. Where the Discord needs something the site page does not have (the invite-specific wording, the channel mentions), keep those as short literals wrapped around the generated body rather than duplicating the instructions. If reading across directories from content.py is awkward, the alternative is a small generator script that rewrites content.py's strings from the markdown and is run before the builder, so the duplication is at least mechanical. Whichever way, the result must mention Vortex, the Nexus account and the collection, because none of those words appear in the Discord today.

**Done when:** `python -c "import sys; sys.path.insert(0,'tools/discord'); import content; print('Vortex' in content.START, 'collection' in content.START)"` prints True True, and `grep -c "twenty gigabytes\|Two files" tools/discord/content.py` returns 0. Re-running Build-Discord.py to publish the new text is Patrick's job.

**Traps:** Build-Discord.py posts into mode='read' channels, so re-running it rewrites live channel content that people have already read and possibly linked to; that is a person's decision, not yours. Discord messages cap at 2000 characters, so a naive dump of a markdown file will fail at post time, not at import time, which is why the splitting has to be explicit. Entry 60 proposes the same generate-from-markdown treatment for the rules copy in the same file, so do them as one change or the second one will conflict. content.py's prose is written in the house voice (no em dashes, customers and people rather than that other word), and generated text inherits whatever join.md says, so check join.md against the wording rules before wiring it up.

## 75. The public copy still describes a closed playtest in nine places, and rule 4 contradicts the download page

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding holds and two of the four line references are off. Verified: tools\docs-app\public\home.html:329-335 is the notice bar, "Closed playtest. Thornswood is not open to everybody yet"; :347 is the hero button, "Apply to playtest"; tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md:26 is "**Thornswood is in closed playtesting.** It is not open to everybody yet"; tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md:44 is "**4. Do not share the installer outside the Discord.** Send people here instead", which does contradict a download page that answers anonymously. Wrong: :379-384 is the "01 Fewer, and worth talking to" block; the playtest string in that section is at :386, "Getting in is an application while we are playtesting". The range :795-884 is also off; the closed playtest paragraph is at :792-795 and the apply-first flow is at :854-860, with a further apply button at :900. The full set of closed-playtest strings in home.html is lines 331, 347, 386, 528, 792, 854, 856 and 900, which is eight, not nine; with join.md:26 and rules.md:44 the total across the site is ten. Whoever does this should re-run the grep rather than trust either count.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:780-905, the downloads page builder, because most of the strings are inside a JavaScript template there rather than in static markup. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:960-1000, where the page's data object is assembled, which is where an open flag would arrive. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\join.md and rules.md, the two markdown copies, since they drift independently of the HTML.

**How:** One flag, read in one place. Add `open: false` to the site config and include it in the data object the page already receives (server.js:960-1000, beside gallery and collection). In home.html, gate the notice bar (329-335) on it, swap the hero button text and href at :347 between "Apply to playtest" pointing at /downloads and "Download" pointing at the same page, swap the :386 sentence, and in the downloads builder swap the :792-795 paragraph and the :854-860 first step. For the markdown, put both versions in join.md behind a marker the renderer can pick (or simply have the page prefer a short generated paragraph over the file's when open is true), and change rules.md:44 to match whatever the download policy actually becomes. Run `grep -n "playtest\|closed\|Apply" tools/docs-app/public/home.html` at the end to prove nothing was missed.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes, and with the flag set true in a local config the grep above returns only strings that are inside the false branch. A second check: `grep -rn "closed playtest\|closed playtesting" tools/docs-app/data/site/` returns nothing unconditional.

**Traps:** This is the same flag as entry 61 on the Discord side and the row says so; two flags with two names is exactly the hunt-through-nine-strings problem restated, so agree one name and one source. Entry 77 deletes the hub and makes Download the single highlighted button, which edits the same nav and footer, and entry 81 edits the download page copy, so three rows touch home.html's download section. The site is served by a scheduled task that Deploy-Docs.ps1 restarts, and Deploy-Docs -Password wipes the config (entry 24), so adding a config key means the wipe would take the open flag with it: set it in a way that defaults safely to closed. Do not run Deploy-Docs.

## 76. The landing page's feature grid promises four things the gamemode does not have

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance is verified and the cited range is wrong at both ends. The feature grid is tools\docs-app\public\home.html:447-497, opened by `<div class="feats">` at :447 inside `<section id="life">` at :440; the cited 462-514 starts mid-grid and runs past the closing tag into the gallery section at :501. The four unimplemented features are at :462-468 ("Notice boards in every hold"), :477-482 ("Property worth owning", with the steward), :483-489 ("Law with people behind it", bounties, cells and courts) and :490-496 ("People with their own work"). The claim that the gamemode implements none of them is verified: a grep of tools\gamemode.js for notice board, noticeboard, steward, bounty, bounties, court, missive, property and plot returns only unrelated hits (SKILL_PROPERTY and CHARGEN_PROPERTY, which are Skyrim Platform property names). The row's own recommendation is consistent with the rest of the list: "People with their own work" is entry 177 on the roadmap and the emote wheel is entry 175, so the being-built strip has somewhere to link to.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:440-500, the whole life section, so you can see which of the eight cards describe things that run. 2. tools\gamemode.js, searched for each promise in turn, which is the only honest way to decide what stays. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\classes.md and the roadmap board, because entry 50 shows the same over-promising problem on the classes page and the roadmap page is where the moved cards should point.

**How:** Split the grid in place. Keep in the top grid only what runs today: proximity voice, locks and lockpicking, professions and classes (with the honest limits from entry 50), the character path, temple respawn and the reduced, kept population. Move the four cards at :462-468, :477-482, :483-489 and :490-496 into a second block below with its own heading, something plain like "Being built", and a single link to /roadmap, which is already a public page (server.js:164-166). Do not delete the cards: the writing is good and the roadmap is where it belongs. While you are in there, check each remaining card against the gamemode the same way, because the grid has eight cards and only four were audited.

**Done when:** `grep -n "Notice boards\|Property worth owning\|bounties\|Law with people behind it" tools/docs-app/public/home.html` shows each one inside the being-built block, and for every card left in the top grid a grep of tools\gamemode.js finds the code that implements it. Open the file in a browser locally to confirm the layout survives; the page is static HTML and needs no server to render the markup.

**Traps:** Missives was removed on 15 September, which the row notes, so notice boards are not merely unbuilt, something adjacent was taken out: check the news entry before writing copy that implies it is coming back. Entry 50 is the same problem on data\site\classes.md:22-44, where two of the three launcher choices are described as systems and do nothing, and entry 80 is the empty gallery band in the section immediately below at :501-509, so the whole middle of this page is being reworked by three rows at once. Deciding what is promised is Patrick's call, not a Claude's. Do not run Deploy-Docs; show the command.

## 77. Delete the hub, and give the Discord bot commands for the three things it still does

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The core is verified and two of the four home.html lines are wrong. Verified: tools\docs-app\server.js:154-159 is the LEVEL map and it lists exactly the fifteen pages the row names, every one of them 'admin': tests, help, known, roadmap, suggestions, files, uploads, report, news, link, linked, active, archive, todo, inbox. The surrounding 130-215 also holds DOCS, BOARDS, PUBLIC_PAGES, BLURB and the PAGES route table, so the cited range is fair. The comment at :149-153 confirms the tester tier was already removed and everything behind /hub is admin only. Wrong: home.html:324 is the "Admin login" nav link (class="enter", and the .enter rule at :76-79 does give it the gold border, so "the only gold button in the bar" is right) and :561 is the footer link, but :319 is the Professions nav link and :500 is a closing tag, neither of which mentions the hub. The correct set is home.html:324 and :561, two links, not four.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:128-215, DOCS, BOARDS, LEVEL, PUBLIC_PAGES and PAGES, which is the whole map of what the hub is. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:40-80, the config and access block, because the member password and cfg.serverPassword go with the hub and entry 24 is about not destroying them. 3. tools\discord\bot.py:676-715, the ticket panel, since the three surviving functions become bot commands and that is where a command would live.

**How:** Build the replacement before deleting anything. Add three slash commands to tools\discord\bot.py: /roadmap, which fetches the public /api/roadmap route the site already serves at server.js:1069 and posts it; /help, which posts help.md; and /linked, which asks the site for the linked list using cfg.botKey and answers ephemerally to staff only. Then delete: the PAGES entries for the hub routes, the LEVEL map, the DOCS and BOARDS tables and every handler they gate, the member cookie and password path (server.js:404-412, 770-777), and the two links at home.html:324 and :561. Promote Download to the highlighted button by moving class="enter" onto the /downloads link at :317. Keep /roadmap, /news, /known, /help and /report as public pages, because PUBLIC_PAGES already serves those from markdown and they are not hub pages.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes; `grep -n "/hub" tools/docs-app/server.js tools/docs-app/public/home.html` returns nothing; and starting the site locally against a throwaway data folder, a request to /hub returns the ordinary 404 rather than a login. `python -m py_compile tools/discord/bot.py` passes for the new commands.

**Traps:** This is a deletion with dependencies in three other rows: it shrinks entry 24 (Deploy-Docs -Password wipes the config) to just the Discord secrets, and the row says it also touches entries 124 and 168, so read those before starting.
