The inbox that the hub displays is the same INBOX the public report form and the Discord bot both write to (entry 84), so deleting the hub removes the only place anybody currently reads reports: the bot command or a staff channel post has to exist first, or reports go into a file nobody opens. server.js is not on CLAUDE.md's do-not-touch list but a change this size to the shipping site deserves the same courtesy of a line in #dev. Do not run Deploy-Docs.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/interactions/api.html , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 78. Errors are bare text, and one of them names a person

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is true and every line reference in the row is wrong. The sentence naming a person is at tools\docs-app\server.js:714, not :706: `'<p>Tell Patrick and try again in a minute.</p>'` inside the refusal page. Line 706 is an esc helper. The other three citations, 956, 1162 and 1499, are not error text: 956 is a comment, 1162 is a path join, 1499 is a promise chain. The actual bare text errors are at :953 ('no home.html', 500), :1113 ('no such picture', 404), :1130 ('no index.html', 500), :1146 and :1164 ('no such file', 404), :1161 ('sign in first', 401), :814 ('no such file', 404), :819 ('Discord login is not set up on this server yet.', 500) and, most visibly, the catch-all at :1476, `send(res, 404, 'not here', 'text/plain')`, which is the bare "not here" the row's fix mentions. One more thing worth saying: the name in :714 is the owner's old name, and the project now uses Patrick, so this line is doubly stale.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:457-464, the send() helper every response goes through, because a themed error page is easiest as one function beside it. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:700-720, the refusal page, which is the one error that already has HTML and is where the name is. 3. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:300-330, the header and nav markup, so the error page can carry the same navigation.

**How:** Add one function, `errorPage(code, headline, detail)`, next to send() at server.js:457, returning a small HTML document that reuses the site's own colours and header markup and links back to / and to the Discord. Replace :1476's `send(res, 404, 'not here', 'text/plain')` with `send(res, 404, errorPage(404, 'Not here', 'That address does not exist. Try the home page.'), TYPES['.html'])`, and do the same for :953, :1130, :1113 and :1146. Leave the API routes answering JSON: only the ones a browser lands on need the page. At :714 replace "Tell Patrick and try again in a minute" with a line pointing at the Discord, which is already in cfg.discord.invite and already used at :796.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes; `grep -rn "Patrick" tools/docs-app/` returns nothing; and starting the site locally against a throwaway data folder, `curl -i http://127.0.0.1:<port>/nonsense` returns a 404 whose body contains the site's header markup rather than the four characters "not here".

**Traps:** Keep the API routes on JSON: tools\app\Door.cs, tools\app\Updater.cs and tools\gamemode.js all parse these answers and a themed HTML body where JSON was expected will be read as an unparseable reply, which Door.cs handles as neither yes nor no. The catch-all at :1476 is reached by scanners constantly, so do not make the page expensive to build or read a file per request; build the string once at startup. Entry 13 is the crash on a malformed request in the same file, and entry 82 is the no-store header in the same send() helper, so three rows edit these sixty lines. Do not run Deploy-Docs.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/404 , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/500

## 79. The public news feed is a developer changelog

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The file reference is wrong and the underlying problem is already half solved, which changes what the work is. tools\news-public.json is 187 lines and 13,579 bytes, so the cited range :614-682 does not exist. More importantly, tools\Publish-News.ps1 exists and is exactly the cut-down the row asks for: its header at :96-101 says in the author's own words "The launcher's feed is a developer's changelog and reads like one ... the public copy is cut down on the way out", and it filters with a $noise list (:106-111) that strips file paths, SKSE ini lines and version lines, a $dull list (:112) that drops "server updated", "fixes", "wip" and "no corner box", a same-release deduplicator (:114-150) and a date cutoff (:155-165). Ship.ps1:551-558 calls it. So the public feed is not the raw launcher changelog. What is true: the dev-facing source, version.json's news array, has 12 entries and the newest is titled "Server updated" with the detail "Load order: 60 plugins server side", which is the load order index the row mentions, and $dull's `^server updated\.?$` would drop that title but not the detail line beneath it. The "403" on the front page could not be confirmed from the repository; it would be in the live data\news.json on the VPS, which is not readable here. Finally, Publish-News.ps1:103 states the opposite of the row's fix: "Anything that needs different words needs them typing into Ship.ps1, because that title is what the world reads now." And Ship.ps1's parameter is $Notes (Ship.ps1:29), not a message.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Publish-News.ps1, whole file, first, because it already does most of what this row asks and its comments record the reasoning. 2. tools\Ship.ps1:28-52 (the parameters, where $Notes is mandatory and positional) and :551-590 (the news step and the dev exclusion). 3. version.json's news array in the repo root, to see what the source actually looks like before filtering.

**How:** Decide first whether this row is still open, because Publish-News.ps1 may already close it. If it is, the smaller change is to strengthen the filters rather than add a parameter: extend $noise in Publish-News.ps1:106-111 with a pattern for load order counts (something like '^\s*Load order\s*:') so a detail line like "Load order: 60 plugins server side" is dropped even when its title survives, and extend $dull with any other title that means nothing to a reader. If a separate public title is genuinely wanted, add `[string]$PublicTitle` to Ship.ps1's param block at :28-52, carry it into the news object Publish-Update writes, and have Publish-News prefer it over $Notes; that contradicts the comment at Publish-News.ps1:103, so it needs agreeing first. Either way, verify against the live feed what a stranger sees before changing anything, because the repository copy is not what is served.

**Done when:** `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile('<abs>\tools\Publish-News.ps1',[ref]$null,[ref]$null)"` reports no errors, then run Publish-News.ps1's filtering logic over a copy of version.json in a scratch folder (it reads and writes JSON, so point it at temp paths) and read the twelve resulting titles as a stranger would. Anything that is a version number, a file path, a plugin count or the words "server updated" should be gone.

**Traps:** Publish-News.ps1:103 records a deliberate decision that the Ship title is the public title, so adding a second title is a design change and belongs in #dev before code, per CLAUDE.md. Ship.ps1 is the person's to run, never yours, and this row's testing has to be done on copies in a scratch folder. The dev channel skips the news step entirely (Ship.ps1:569-570), so a dev Ship will not exercise the change. The claim about a 403 on the public feed could not be checked from the repository; treat it as unverified and look at the live page before acting on it.

## 80. No screenshots or footage anywhere on the site

**Category:** Trust  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly, including the mechanism. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:104-109 is the comment that says it in the project's own words: "The hero is a drawn landscape, not a photograph and not an empty field. Every other server puts a screenshot here; we have none worth using yet and a fake one would be worse than none." :338-352 is the hero itself, using /ridge.svg and /logo.png. :501-509 is `<section id="gallerySection" hidden>` with the empty `<div class="gallery" id="gallery">`, and the unhide is at home.html:1013. The server side is server.js:969, which puts `gallery: jsonFile(path.join(DATA, 'gallery.json'), [])` into the page data, and :1109-1113, which serves a picture only if it is listed in gallery.json. No gallery.json exists anywhere in the repository, so the band has never had anything to show. The row's claim that the machinery is already there waiting for the file is correct.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:1005-1025, the script that reads the gallery array and unhides the band, so you know the exact shape each entry must have. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:960-975 and 1100-1120, where gallery.json is read and where a picture request is checked against it. 3. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:96-112, the hero comment, because whoever wrote it set the bar for what is worth putting up.

**How:** This is content work, not code. Read home.html:1005-1025 to learn the object shape the page expects (at minimum a file name and an alt or caption), take twenty to thirty shots at the next session, pick six that show people rather than scenery, resize the long edge to around 1600 pixels, save as WebP or well-compressed JPEG under about 250 KB each, put them in the site's data pictures folder on the VPS, and write gallery.json listing them. The band unhides itself. While you are there, pick the best one as the share image for entry 82, which needs 1200x630 and under 300 KB.

**Done when:** Locally, put a gallery.json with two entries and two small images into a throwaway data folder, start the site against it, and confirm the section loses its hidden attribute and both images load. Without starting anything: `node -e "const g=require('<path>/gallery.json'); console.log(Array.isArray(g), g.length)"` plus a read of home.html:1005-1025 to confirm the field names match.

**Traps:** Every picture has people in it and those people are testers, so ask before publishing anybody's character or name; the privacy page in entry 83 is where what is public gets stated. The pictures go into the site's data folder on the VPS and are deployed by Deploy-Docs.ps1, which is the person's script to run. Do not put the images in the repository if they are large: Ship-Rules.ps1 governs what leaves a machine in a release and entry 30 describes an 11.3 GB near-miss, so size discipline matters. Entry 82 is about the share image and caching in the same page, and entry 76 reworks the section directly above this one, so coordinate.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTML/Element/img , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Media/Formats/Image_types

## 81. The virus warning is honest, too long, and in the wrong place

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Honest and too long are verified; the wrong place is wrong. The block is tools\docs-app\public\home.html:839-852, guarded by `if (exe) {`, with the comment explaining it at :828-838. It is twelve lines of prose and it does name the detection (Trojan:Win32/Wacatac.B!ml) and explain the ml suffix, so "honest, too long" is right. But it is not at the top of the download page: the string is appended to the `grab` variable and inserted at :867, inside the second of five steps, "Take the installer", directly under the download button. The comment at :797-799 records that placement as a deliberate decision: "The button belongs under the step that tells you to take it, not above the list where it is the first thing you meet." So the position the row objects to is one somebody already moved it away from. Also confirmed: there is no SHA-256 anywhere on the page (grep for sha256 and checksum in home.html returns nothing), which is the part of the fix that is genuinely missing and which depends on entry 32 publishing the hash in the first place.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:790-870, the downloads page builder including the comment at :797-799 that explains the current placement, so you do not undo a considered decision. 2. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:716-812, which entry 32 says checks only the length of the payload, because publishing a hash people can check is pointless if the installer itself does not. 3. tools\Publish-Installer or tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1, whichever writes files.json, since that is where a SHA-256 would be produced.

**How:** Cut, do not move. Reduce :840-851 to one sentence above the button, along the lines of: Windows may warn about this file because it is not signed yet; the SHA-256 is below and you can check it on VirusTotal. Keep the rest of the current text as a short expandable details element for anyone who wants the explanation, since the reasoning is good and deleting it loses something. Then add the two facts that make the sentence useful: print the SHA-256 beside the size and date (it has to come from files.json, which is entry 32's work), and link the VirusTotal report for that hash rather than telling people to upload the file themselves. Do not tell anybody to switch off Defender, and keep the closing line that says not clicking past a malware warning is a reasonable thing to do.

**Done when:** Open tools\docs-app\public\home.html in a browser locally and confirm the downloads step renders with one sentence plus a details element, and that the hash appears beside the size. `grep -c "Wacatac" tools/docs-app/public/home.html` should return 1 at most, inside the collapsed text.

**Traps:** Signing is the actual fix and it is entry 14, which says to start the Trusted Signing identity validation now because it takes days; anything here is interim and should say so. The hash cannot be published until something computes it, which is entry 32, so this row has a hard dependency. Do not shorten the warning into something that reads as dismissive: the current text's honesty is called out as right in the row itself and in the code comment. Entry 54 (no Range support on the installer download) and entry 75 (the closed playtest copy) edit the same builder function, and entry 8 may remove the payload from public download entirely, which would change this page's shape. Do not run Deploy-Docs or Publish-Installer.

**Links:** https://www.virustotal.com/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/security/threat-protection/intelligence/criteria

## 82. Nothing is cached, the favicon is 237 KB, and the share image is a relative URL at 1.4 MB

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The most accurate row in this slice. favicon.ico is 237,344 bytes, which is the 237 KB claimed. presence.png is 1,450,149 bytes, the 1.4 MB claimed. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:16 is `<meta property="og:image" content="/presence.png">`, a relative URL, exactly as stated. tools\docs-app\server.js:1129 and :1135 are both `fs.readFileSync`, so the cited 1129-1136 is right for the synchronous static reads. One line reference is slightly off: the no-store header is at server.js:460, inside the send() helper at :457-464, not at :464-471; since every response in the file goes through send(), the consequence the row describes is if anything understated. Add for the fixer: the page also carries `<meta name="twitter:card" content="summary_large_image">` at :18 and a second icon link to /logo.png at :20, so the head is already set up for a proper card and only the assets and the absolute URL are missing.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:455-470, send() and the header block, because one line there sets the policy for every response on the site. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:1125-1140, the static file branch, so you see which requests are pages and which are assets. 3. tools\docs-app\public\home.html:10-22, the head, where og:image, the twitter card and both icon links live.

**How:** Four separable edits. (1) Stop blanket no-store: in send() at server.js:460 keep no-store as the default but let callers override it, and in the static branch at :1131-1137 send `Cache-Control: public, max-age=31536000, immutable` for fingerprinted assets and a short max-age with an ETag for the rest. Compute the ETag once at startup from the file's size and mtime rather than per request. (2) Replace favicon.ico with a multi-size ico under about 15 KB plus an SVG icon, keeping both link tags at :19-20. (3) Produce a 1200x630 share image under 300 KB, ideally one of entry 80's screenshots, and make :16 absolute: `content="https://thornswood.net/presence.png"`, since Open Graph requires an absolute URL and most unfurlers will not resolve a relative one. (4) Consider handing static files to Caddy entirely, which does Range, ETag and conditional requests for free and is the same answer entry 54 gives for the installer.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes; `ls -l tools/docs-app/public/favicon.ico tools/docs-app/public/presence.png` shows the new sizes under their targets; `grep -n "og:image" tools/docs-app/public/home.html` shows an absolute https URL; and locally, `curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:<port>/logo.png` shows a Cache-Control with a max-age rather than no-store, while `curl -sI http://127.0.0.1:<port>/` still shows no-store.

**Traps:** no-store is doing real work on the pages that carry somebody's signed-in state, so do not lift it globally: the hub pages and the API answers must stay uncached, and entry 83 shows how much personal data those routes carry. Caching fingerprinted assets means the file names have to change when the contents do, or people will hold a year-old logo; if you are not adding fingerprints, use a short max-age with an ETag instead of a year. Entry 54 wants Caddy serving the installer and entry 21 says Caddy is currently unwatched and set to be killed every three days, so moving static serving to Caddy makes that row urgent rather than optional. Do not run Deploy-Docs or Setup-Domain.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Caching , https://ogp.me/ , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Headers/ETag , https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/file_server

## 83. No privacy policy, no terms, no contact and no deletion route, on a site that keeps Discord ids, handles, roles and sign-in addresses

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every claim checks out. tools\docs-app\server.js:583-607 is noteSignIn, which unshifts a row holding the Discord id, username, display name, the page they wanted, admin flag, outcome, up to twelve role names and the address from x-forwarded-for or the socket, then keeps ATTEMPTS_KEEP rows, and ATTEMPTS_KEEP is 500 at :581. So "500 sign-ins with address" is exact. :878-896 is the linked-account write: linked[me.id] gets id, name, handle, first, seen and roles, and putJson writes the whole map with no cap and no expiry anywhere in the file, so "uncapped, kept forever" is exact. :1017-1068 are the report and ticket routes, which store free text, a name and a Discord id. tools\app\Steam.cs:57, :70 and :89-94 read the Steam id out of the registry (HKCU Software\Valve\Steam\ActiveProcess ActiveUser and the Wow6432Node install key). And the absence is verified: tools\docs-app\data\site\ contains only classes.md, credits.md, join.md, rules.md, support.md and world.md, with no privacy or terms page, and PUBLIC_PAGES at server.js:164-173 lists no /privacy and no /terms.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:575-610 and 870-900, the two writes, because you cannot describe what is stored until you have read exactly what goes into each row. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:160-200, PUBLIC_PAGES, SITE and BLURB, which is the mechanism for adding a page: drop the markdown in data\site and add the route and a blurb. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\credits.md, as the model for tone and length of a short public page here.

**How:** Write two markdown files into tools\docs-app\data\site\: privacy.md and terms.md. Add '/privacy' and '/terms' to PUBLIC_PAGES at server.js:164-173, add a BLURB entry for each at :184-199 so a shared link unfurls, and add both to the footer in home.html around :555-564. privacy.md has to name every store the code actually has: the sign-in log (Discord id, handle, display name, roles, outcome, address, last 500 entries), the linked-accounts map (id, name, handle, first seen, last seen, roles, kept indefinitely today), the inbox (report text, a name, a Discord id), the character roster, the Steam id the launcher reads from the registry, and whatever entry 64 decides about chat retention. Say how long each is kept, how to ask for deletion and what is public. terms.md covers 18 or over, conduct, that money to Patreon buys server costs and slots (entry 88), and the fan-project disclaimer. Then do the code half: age out the sign-in log by time rather than by count, put an expiry on linked rows, and either move the character roster behind the ticket check or strip the locations out of it. Finally enter both URLs in the Discord developer portal so the consent screen links them.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes; locally, requests to /privacy and /terms return the rendered markdown with no sign-in; `grep -n "privacy\|terms" tools/docs-app/public/home.html` shows both in the footer. For the retention code, a stub test: write 600 sign-in rows with timestamps spread over a year, run the ageing function, and assert only rows inside the window survive.

**Traps:** This page is a promise, so it must describe what the code does today, not what it should do: if you write that chat is not recorded and entry 64 then starts recording it, the page becomes false, which is why the row says the retention decision has to be declared here. The address in the sign-in log comes from x-forwarded-for, which means Caddy is in front of it; if entry 22's work changes that, the field changes meaning. What money buys is Patrick's to state, per entry 88, and the age gate wording ties to entries 6 and 61. CLAUDE.md's wording rules apply to public pages: customers, people, testers, and no em dashes. Do not run Deploy-Docs; show the command.

**Links:** https://gdpr-info.eu/art-13-gdpr/ , https://gdpr-info.eu/art-5-gdpr/ , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/oauth2

## 84. The public report form has no rate limit, and the inbox drops the oldest, so real reports can be flushed out

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified exactly, and the shared-inbox detail makes it worse than the row says. tools\docs-app\server.js:1017-1038 is the /api/report route: it parses up to 20,000 bytes of JSON, requires only a non-empty title, and writes straight into the inbox with no authentication, no token, no honeypot and no limiter anywhere in the file. The trim is at :1034, `putJson(INBOX, list.slice(0, 2000))`, exactly as cited. The important part the row only hints at: the Discord bot's tickets go into the same INBOX through /api/ticket at :1040-1067, with its own identical `list.slice(0, 2000)` at :1063. Since the list is one global array trimmed to a fixed 2000 and new rows are unshifted at the front, 2000 anonymous posts to /api/report push every real application, report and help ticket off the end permanently. Nothing rate limits either route, and /api/ticket at least checks cfg.botKey; /api/report checks nothing at all.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\server.js:1017-1067, both routes together, because the shared cap is the whole finding. 2. tools\docs-app\server.js:455-470, send() and body(), where a limiter would sit so it covers every route rather than one. 3. tools\discord\bot.py:555-565, push_async, so you know what the bot expects from /api/ticket and do not break it while adding limits.

**How:** Four small changes in server.js. (1) Per-address limit: keep a Map of address to {count, windowStart}, refuse with 429 past, say, five reports an hour, and clear old entries on a timer. Take the address the same way noteSignIn does at :603-604, x-forwarded-for first then socket.remoteAddress. (2) Honeypot: add a hidden input to the report form in home.html and reject at :1021 when it is non-empty; that alone stops most drive-by scripts and costs nothing. (3) Stop the shared cap doing the damage: replace `list.slice(0, 2000)` at :1034 with a per-sender cap, keep the most recent few per address or per name, and at :1063 trim tickets separately from public reports, or better, keep two files. (4) Push new reports out through the bot to a staff channel using cfg.botKey, so they are read the day they arrive, which also matters because entry 77 deletes the hub where they are currently read.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` passes; against a local instance with a throwaway data folder, a loop of ten POSTs to /api/report from one address gets 429 after the fifth, and after posting 50 reports the ticket rows written earlier through /api/ticket are all still present in the inbox file. `node -e "const l=require('<tmp>/inbox.json');console.log(l.filter(r=>r.kind==='ticket').length)"` is the check.

**Traps:** Entry 77 deletes the hub, which is the only place anybody reads this inbox today, so if that lands first and no bot command or staff channel post exists, reports go into a file nobody opens. The page promises somebody will read it, so a limiter that silently drops is worse than one that answers 429 with a sentence. Entry 46 is the separate problem that there is no way to report a person at all, and entry 63 is the bot's fallback posting reports publicly, so all three touch how a report travels. The address is only as trustworthy as the proxy in front of the site, so read x-forwarded-for only when Caddy is definitely terminating; entries 21 and 22 are about that layer. Do not run Deploy-Docs.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Status/429 , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html

## 85. There is no security contact and no public place to report a problem

**Category:** GitHub & CI  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The GitHub-hygiene half is verified: `.github` holds exactly one file, `.github/workflows/build.yml`, and listing both repo roots (`gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/contents` and `.../Thornswood-Releases/contents`) shows no SECURITY.md, no CODEOWNERS, no .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE and no dependabot.yml. `gh issue list` returns nothing for either repo. The public repo's README is two lines and names no contact. The dependabot half cannot be done as written, because there is no manifest for Dependabot to read: tools/docs-app has no package.json and server.js:27-32 requires only Node built-ins (http, net, https, fs, path, crypto), and tools/discord has no requirements.txt, its dependencies being pip-installed unpinned by Install-Bot.ps1:210 (discord.py, pillow, certifi). Also note CODEOWNERS is asked for three times in this list: here, entry 114 and entry 142. Create it once, under entry 142.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "The dependabot half cannot be done as written, because there is no manifest for Dependabot to read."  ->  There is one. .github/workflows/build.yml:16 pins actions/checkout@v4 and :42 pins actions/upload-artifact@v4, which is the github-actions ecosystem and needs no new manifest: a six line dependabot.yml would work today. The pip and npm halves do need a manifest first, which the rest of the entry gets right, but "cannot be done as written" tells the implementer to skip work that is already possible.

**Read first:** 1. .github/workflows/build.yml, the only thing in .github today, so you can see what the repo's CI surface actually is. 2. tools/Install-Bot.ps1 lines 200-215, which is how the bot's dependencies really get installed and therefore what a requirements.txt has to pin. 3. CLAUDE.md, the section on how work moves between the two machines, because the repo that holds the code is private and that decides where a security contact can usefully live.

**How:** Put SECURITY.md at the root of Thornswood-Releases, the public repo, not the private one: name a quiet reporting address that is not a public Discord channel, and say supported versions are the current release only. Turn on private vulnerability reporting in that repo's Settings, Security. For Dependabot, first write tools/discord/requirements.txt pinning the three packages Install-Bot.ps1:210 installs at the versions running on the VPS, then add .github/dependabot.yml with a single pip entry whose directory is /tools/discord. Skip npm entirely, there is nothing to watch. Add .github/ISSUE_TEMPLATE/bug.yml on the public repo only.

**Done when:** `gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood-Releases/contents/SECURITY.md --jq .name` returns the file, the public repo's Security tab shows the policy, and after the dependabot.yml lands the Insights, Dependency graph, Dependabot tab lists one pip entry with a last-checked time. All read-only checks, no game or launcher involved.

**Traps:** The code repo is private, so a SECURITY.md in it is invisible to the person who would use it. tools/discord/__pycache__/*.pyc is tracked in git, so a tidy-up commit in that folder should leave them alone or remove them deliberately, not by accident. tools/discord/news-webhook.txt and dev-webhook.txt sit in that same folder, are credentials and are gitignored: never reference them in a template or example. CLAUDE.md bans em dashes in anything written for people, which includes SECURITY.md.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/getting-started/adding-a-security-policy-to-your-repository , https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/security-advisories/guidance-on-reporting-and-writing/privately-reporting-a-security-vulnerability , https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/dependabot/dependabot-version-updates/configuration-options-for-the-dependabot.yml-file

## 86. The repository's own docs still describe the old mod product

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Every content claim is verified. README.md is exactly 97 lines, titled `# the old mod` (line 1), says "Five player co-op Skyrim" (line 3), carries the Google Drive link (line 8), "Password is in Discord" (lines 60-61), "`F2` party, who's online, teleport to someone, chat" (line 63) and "Screenshot it and send it to Patrick" (line 92). SETUP.md is exactly 66 lines and describes `gh repo create old-mod --private` (line 16), a read-only PAT (19-29) and `Build-OldMod.ps1` (line 52); `ls tools/Build-OldMod.ps1` fails, the script does not exist. config.example.json still carries ServerAddress 192.135.119.166, port 25565 and UpdateRepo EvilPatrick06/old-mod. The em dash count is wrong: seven in README.md (lines 8, 16, 45, 63, 77, 80, 86) and one in SETUP.md, eight in all, not four. "Message to friends.txt" and config.example.json contain none. Overlaps to settle before starting: entry 9 owns deleting the Drive link, entry 137 owns the Patrick-to-role rename and claims README.md:92 too, and entry 154 explicitly defers its README item here. One rewrite closes all three.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "`git rm SETUP.md \"tools/Message to friends.txt\" \"tools/READ ME FIRST.txt\"`"  ->  tools/READ ME FIRST.txt is not a leftover, it is a shipped file. tools/Package-Thornswood.ps1:451-454 copies it into the customer installer alongside Setup-Thornswood.ps1, Install.bat and Setup.bat, and prints "READ ME FIRST.txt missing next to this script" when it is gone. Deleting it silently ships an installer with no read-me. Its contents are stale (it says THE OLD MOD and "Ask Patrick for the password"), so it needs rewriting, not removing, or line 451 has to change in the same commit. SETUP.md has the same problem more quietly: CLAUDE.md:97 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:319 both list it, and the howTo does not mention updating either.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md, the What this is and Words sections, which are the two paragraphs a new README has to agree with and which ban em dashes and the word player. 2. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 1-46, the pieces table, so the README can point at it instead of repeating it. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/join.md, the current and correct install story, so the README does not invent a third one.

**How:** Replace README.md with about thirty lines: what Thornswood is, that this repo is the launcher, the plugins, the site, the bot and the scripts, and links to SECOND-DEVELOPER.md and the site. Then `git rm SETUP.md "tools/Message to friends.txt" "tools/READ ME FIRST.txt"` and either delete config.example.json or rewrite it with the current address and port and an empty UpdateToken. Branch name of the shape patrick/readme-is-thornswood, one pull request.

**Done when:** `grep -ci "old mod" README.md` returns 0, `grep -c "—" README.md` returns 0, `grep -rn "drive.google.com" . | grep -v .git | grep -v _to_delete` returns nothing, and the build check on the pull request is green.

**Traps:** This is documentation, so it reaches strangers on the next push rather than with a release; it does not need a coordinated client and server release, unlike most of this list. The Drive link is a redistribution problem in its own right (entry 9) and should go first or in the same change. _to_delete/ holds stale copies of some of these files and is gitignored, do not edit those. One fix per pull request, so do not fold the licence and notices work (entry 87) into this branch.

## 87. There is no licence on this project and no third-party notices file anywhere

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 110-132 and 244, which is where three Microsoft WebView2 redistributable files are compiled into the shipped exe, one of the things a notices file has to cover. 2. tools/Patch-Client.ps1 lines 1-45, which records that the shipped client bundle is a GPLv3 SkyMP build patched here, the source offer from entry 10. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/credits.md, which is the public page that already names what the project stands on and is the natural companion to the notices file.

**How:** Checked both repo trees by name and by listing: no LICENSE, COPYING, NOTICE or THIRD-PARTY at the root of EvilPatrick06/Thornswood or EvilPatrick06/Thornswood-Releases. Leave the source unlicensed, that is normal for a product. Add THIRD-PARTY.md at the repo root listing, with its licence and a link: skymp5-client and skyrim-platform (GPLv3), skymp5-server (AGPLv3), the three Microsoft WebView2 files embedded by Build-Launcher.ps1, and the Nexus collection mods by author. Put the same list, in plainer words, on a site page beside credits.md, and put the GPL written offer from entry 10 in the same file.

**Done when:** THIRD-PARTY.md exists and every binary the installer or the launcher ships that was not written here appears in it. Cross-check mechanically: grep Build-Launcher.ps1 for every `/resource:` and every file under tools/lib, and confirm each one is either yours or listed.

**Traps:** The notices file is where the entry 10 source offer lives, so those two entries ship together or the offer has no home. The installer payload also carries Bethesda assets and about forty third-party mods (entry 8), which is a redistribution problem a notices file does not solve and must not be used to paper over. Secrets rule still applies: name locations, never values, if any config example ends up quoted.

**Links:** https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-3.0.en.html , https://www.gnu.org/licenses/gpl-faq.html , https://spdx.org/licenses/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/microsoft-edge/webview2/concepts/distribution

## 88. Paid tiers sell character slots on a stack of other people's mods, while the credits page says nothing is sold

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The page citations are right or nearly right. support.md has the tiers heading at line 9 and the table at 11-19, with the Characters row at 14 reading 3, 3, 4, and line 21 saying everybody gets two without paying. credits.md:3 is exactly "Thornswood is a free fan project. Nobody is paid and nothing is sold." In classes.md the "None of this is for sale" heading is at 123 and the "Not real money" paragraph is 127-130, not 126-130. One clause is wrong: the live feed does not carry the Patreon link. A case-insensitive grep of tools/news-public.json for patreon returns zero. The only Patreon link on the site is a hidden footer anchor, public/home.html:555 `id="footPatreon" hidden`, which Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 -Patreon fills in later; the visible nav and footer both point at /support (home.html:322 and 554). The contradiction is real but narrower than stated: credits.md says nothing is sold while support.md sells a tier that grants a fourth character slot and classes.md:127-130 says the Patreon buys nothing in the world.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "One clause is wrong: the live feed does not carry the Patreon link. A case-insensitive grep of tools/news-public.json for patreon returns zero... The only Patreon link on the site is a hidden footer anchor, public/home.html:555... the visible nav and footer both point at /support."  ->  The original row was right and this "correction" is wrong. The live feed is /api/public, not tools/news-public.json: tools/docs-app/server.js:964-975 builds it from cfg.patreon plus the news file, and a GET of https://thornswood.net/api/public returns "patreon":"https://www.patreon.com/thornswood". tools/docs-app/public/home.html:974-978 then rewrites the visible nav anchor (navSupport, line 322) to that address and unhides footPatreon, so the live front page carries a Patreon link in the nav and the footer today. The repo's own board says so: tools/docs-app/data/board.json:1016-1025, row s6r2, "done": true, "The link is live... thornswood.net now points at patreon.com/thornswood in the nav and the footer." Grepping only news-public.json proved nothing about the feed.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/site/support.md, all of it, for what is actually offered and the What it will never get you section that is already carefully worded. 2. credits.md line 3, the sentence that contradicts it. 3. classes.md lines 123-135, the third statement of the same policy and the best written of the three, which is the wording to copy.

**How:** Edit credits.md:3 so it says the world and the mods are not sold and that the Patreon pays for the machine, the domain and the time, reusing the wording already at classes.md:127-130 so all three pages say one thing. Add one line under the tiers table in support.md saying the same. Do not change the tiers themselves.

**Done when:** `grep -rn "nothing is sold" tools/docs-app/data/site/` returns nothing, and reading the three pages in order leaves no disagreement about whether money changes hands. Verify in the repo: the site only updates when a person runs Deploy-Docs.ps1.

**Traps:** Site pages are markdown under tools/docs-app/data/site and reach the public only through Deploy-Docs.ps1, which is a VPS script the person runs, never an agent. Entry 77 wants the hub deleted and entry 83 wants a terms page, both of which touch the same tree and server.js; sequence them so two people are not editing the site in the same week. The word player is banned by CLAUDE.md and slips easily into a page about tiers.

## 89. /help is a developer's command list, and the unknown-command reply advertises a command everybody is then refused

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (minor):** "the help string at 3045 correctly filters /tp with `isAdmin(profileId) ? ' /tp <place> moves you.' : ''`"  ->  That filter is tools/gamemode.js:3047. Line 3045 is `'/where prints your cell, position and facing. ' +`. The rest of the entry's citations (3042, 3048, 3065-3066, 2928-2931) are exact.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js lines 2860-3067, the whole of handle(), so you can see every command and which ones are permission-filtered. 2. tools/gamemode.js lines 2831-2833, toProfile, which POSTs to the voice service /say route: this is why chat replies do reach somebody's screen, unlike the T.note channel in entries 42 and 43. 3. tools/gamemode.js header lines 1-20, which explains hot reload and the event contract, so you know a syntax error takes the live handlers down.

**How:** Verified line by line: 3042 is `if (word === '/help' || word === '/commands') {` through the return at 3048, and the help string at 3045 correctly filters /tp with `isAdmin(profileId) ? ' /tp <place> moves you.' : ''`. The fallback at 3065-3066 is `'No command called ' + word + '. ' + 'There is /ooc, /where, /tp <place> and /help.'` with no filter, and the refusal it leads to is at 2928-2931, `if (!isAdmin(profileId)) { toProfile(profileId, 'That one is not yours to run.'); return; }`. /where at 2901-2914 prints the cell description plus rounded x, y, z and facing. To fix: in the fallback string at 3065-3066 use the same isAdmin ternary the /help branch already uses two lines above; replace the single /help line with four or five short lines naming controls, talking, rules, staff and what to do first; and in /where print the `cell` value from 2907 alone, leaving the numbers on the log line at 2912-2913.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and `grep -n "/tp <place>" tools/gamemode.js` shows the string only inside an isAdmin branch. The wording can be confirmed entirely by reading the file, because toProfile is a one-line POST; the deploy is Deploy-Gamemode.ps1, which a person runs.

**Traps:** skymp5-server hot-reloads gamemode.js when the file changes, so a syntax error takes the live handlers down: always node --check before handing the deploy command over. tools/gamemode.js is the shipping file and is edited directly; tools/thornswood-src holds an older generator (body.js, weave.py, build.py) whose output path is a sandbox directory that does not exist on this machine, so never regenerate gamemode.js from it. Entry 90 edits a string ninety lines away in the same function and entry 159 sweeps the same file: do them in one branch. Server-only change, but CLAUDE.md still wants it shipped as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 90. Somebody who types /restart is told to run a PowerShell script

**Category:** In-game  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js lines 2952-2975, which is the comment recording why /restart was removed and then the line itself. 2. tools/Request-Restart.ps1, the path the comment says is the real one, so you know what the replacement sentence should point people at. 3. tools/gamemode.js lines 2831-2833, toProfile, so you know this text lands in the person's chat window.
