## 91. Whisper and yell do nothing: the feature is dead end to end

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/voice/voice-server.js lines 474-500, the entire message dispatch, which is where you will see there are only four message types. 2. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js lines 45-55 and 984-1010, the three modes and setMode, the in-game side that already sends vmode. 3. tools/launcher/voice-host.html lines 88-116 and 288-298, the launcher's second implementation of the same protocol, which has to stay in step.

**How:** Verified: ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js:602 posts `{ t: 'vmode', mode: myMode }` on connect and setMode at 991-1006 posts it again on every change; voice-host.html:95 puts `mode` inside the join, 111-115 handles an incoming vmode and 293-297 sends one. tools/voice/voice-server.js dispatches at 474-496 and handles only join, sdp, ice and chat; grep for vmode in that file returns nothing, and onJoin at 180-189 drops msg.mode too. Fix: beside the sdp and ice branch at 480-486 add a branch for `msg.t === 'vmode'` that stores the mode on the peer record created in onJoin and sends `{ t: 'vmode', from: myId, mode }` to every other peer, and add the mode to the rows built by roster() at 169-172 so a late joiner learns it. reconcile() at 631-636 already expects `p.mode` and will apply it.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js` passes; `grep -n "vmode" tools/voice/voice-server.js` shows the new branch and the roster field. A stronger check without the game: run the service locally on a spare port and connect two Node WebSocket clients, send a vmode from one and assert the other receives it.

**Traps:** The voice service that is running is not this file. Entry 7 found /health answering with a build and routes that do not exist in tools/voice/voice-server.js, so a change here may not be what is deployed: settle entry 7 first or the fix goes nowhere. Both clients already send vmode, so the service change alone completes the feature and needs no client release, but two client implementations exist and any future message must be added to both. Install-Voice.ps1 is a VPS script a person runs. Entries 4, 55, 58, 64, 65, 71, 92, 94 and 96 all want to change this same file: batch them or you will queue nine deploys of one service.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/WebSocket , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/PannerNode

## 92. Relay mode never leaves the room, because the method it calls does not exist

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The dead method is real but it does not throw. ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js:892 reads `try { var n = net(); if (n && n.leave) n.leave(); } catch (e) { }`, guarded twice, and window.thornswoodNet in thornswood-chat.js:216-284 exposes say, state, on, post, peer, isUp and setName, with no leave. The call is silently skipped, not an exception. Two things are worse than the entry says. First, `on(fn)` at 249-252 only pushes onto a listeners array with no way to remove one, so the voice file's handler registered at 606 keeps running after enterRelay(). Second, reconcile() at 631-648 has no relay guard at all: inRelay() is checked at 228, 300, 363, 921, 976 and 995 but not there, so the next peers broadcast calls open() again for every peer and rebuilds the RTCPeerConnections the handoff just dropped. That is exactly the "the server sees two of everybody and each hears a ghost of the other" that the comment at 880-883 is trying to prevent.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "Add two methods to window.thornswoodNet in thornswood-chat.js: `leave`, which closes and reopens the socket the way setName at 274-283 already does (voice-server.js ws.onclose at 537-539 deletes the peer and announces, so the roster cleans itself)."  ->  Closing and reopening does not leave, it rejoins. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js:135-141 calls retry() on every close and 148-155 reconnects after about a second, and ws.onopen at 90-96 sends a fresh join, so tools/voice/voice-server.js onJoin (180-189) hands out a new peer id and announce() puts the person straight back in the roster. Everyone else's reconcile then opens to that peer again, and open() at 653-681 still builds a recvonly RTCPeerConnection when mic is null, so the ghost the comment at 879-883 warns about survives. Worse, that socket is chat: it is the only one, so a real leave would take in-game chat down with it, and the reopen version shows the customer "Chat lost the server. Trying again." (chat.js:136) every time relay mode starts. The reconcile guard, the other half of the same howTo, is the fix that actually works; the leave method should not be written.

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js lines 875-896, enterRelay and the comment stating what it is for. 2. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js lines 216-284, the whole socket API, so you can see what does not exist and how setName already does a clean reconnect. 3. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js lines 583-648, connect, the listener it registers and reconcile, which is what undoes the handoff.

**How:** Add two methods to window.thornswoodNet in thornswood-chat.js: `leave`, which closes and reopens the socket the way setName at 274-283 already does (voice-server.js ws.onclose at 537-539 deletes the peer and announces, so the roster cleans itself), and `off(fn)`, which splices a listener out of the array. Then add a relay guard at the top of reconcile in thornswood-voice.js: `if (inRelay()) { conns.forEach(function (_, id) { drop(id); }); return; }`.

**Done when:** `node --check` passes on both files, `grep -n "inRelay" tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js` shows a hit inside reconcile, and `grep -n "leave:" tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js` shows the new method. Without a browser: stub window and call enterRelay(), then feed a peers message and assert conns.size stays at zero.

**Traps:** These are in-game UI files loaded by the front plugin, so they ship with a client release, one complete release with whatever else changes. Do not open a second socket: the comment at 579-582 records that chat owns the one socket and voice listens on it, deliberately. The launcher's voice-host.html is a second implementation of the same protocol and any message-set change must be made in both, the same lesson as entry 91. Entry 56 proposes replacing the mesh with a relay entirely, which would rewrite this code; check whether that is being done first.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/RTCPeerConnection/close , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MediaStreamTrack/stop

## 93. Comments describe fixes the code never got, in four places, including two constants that still hold the values the comment explains were the problem

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Verified, with a refinement and a fifth site. The two constants that still hold the old value are both in ts-plugins/thornswood-voice.js: the comment at 356-370 says "three times rather than twelve" and "Twelve of them, 1200 ms apart, was fourteen and a half seconds", while line 371 still reads `if (tries >= 12)`; the comment at 434-453 says "Two clear checks, about two seconds. This was eight checks", while line 455 still reads `if (clear >= 8)`. The two in gamemode.js drift the other way: the comment at 490-501 argues for "One second" above `const CHARGEN_TICK_MS = 500;` at 502, and the comment at 1417-1419 says "on your feet for eight seconds" above a gate that tests MOVED_ENOUGH_MS, which is 500 at line 543. There is a fifth site: the comment at 535-542 says "And down again to one" above that same 500. So five places, not four, and the still-holds-the-problem-value half is true only of the two in the plugin.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** "line 371 still reads `if (tries >= 12)`... line 455 still reads `if (clear >= 8)`", with the howTo "change line 371 to `if (tries >= 3)` and line 455 to `if (clear >= 2)`".  ->  Both line numbers are wrong in the same file. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-voice.js:371 is `if (phase === 1 && now - at > 1200) {`, the timing gate; `if (tries >= 12)` is line 375. Line 455 is `micOpened = true;`; `if (clear >= 8) {` is line 454. An agent that follows the howTo literally replaces the 1200 ms phase gate with a try counter (configure() would then fire every tick with no interval) and replaces `micOpened = true;` with a conditional, leaving the block that follows unguarded. This ships to everyone's PC.

**Challenged (minor):** doneWhen: "`grep -n \"tries >= \\|clear >= \\|CHARGEN_TICK_MS = \\|MOVED_ENOUGH_MS = \" tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-voice.js tools/gamemode.js` lists five hits"  ->  It lists four: thornswood-voice.js:375, thornswood-voice.js:454, gamemode.js:502, gamemode.js:543. I ran it. The entry's own "five places" count includes the second comment block at gamemode.js:522-542, which sits above the same constant at 543 and produces no separate grep hit, so the test can never print five and an implementer checking against it will think the work is unfinished.

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-voice.js lines 350-380 then 430-460, the two where the constant is genuinely wrong. 2. tools/gamemode.js lines 484-543, the two constants and the three comments around them. 3. CLAUDE.md, the Settled design section, because comments recording an incident are treated as load-bearing here and a wrong one is not a typo.

**How:** For the plugin, decide which half is the decision. If the comment is, change line 371 to `if (tries >= 3)` and line 455 to `if (clear >= 2)`. If the code is, rewrite the two comments to say twelve and eight and why. Do not do both halves in one motion. For gamemode.js, edit the three comments at 490-501, 535-542 and 1417-1419 to name the number the constant actually holds, and put the reasoning on the line above each const so it travels with the value.

**Done when:** `grep -n "tries >= \|clear >= \|CHARGEN_TICK_MS = \|MOVED_ENOUGH_MS = " tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-voice.js tools/gamemode.js` lists five hits, and reading the comment above each one gives the same number as the code. `node --check` passes on both files.

**Traps:** The 434-453 comment records a real incident: opening a capture device took the foreground, Windows handed back the DirectInput device, SkyrimPlatform's hook never re-acquired it and the keyboard died; the wait was hiding that, and the hook re-acquires now. Changing the number without reading that paragraph is how the keyboard bug comes back. The plugin is client-side and gamemode.js is server-side, so touching both is a coordinated release. Behaviour changes here are settled-design adjacent and want a line in #dev first; prose fixes do not.

## 94. The dice everyone settles things with are rolled on the roller's own machine and sent as ordinary text

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** One citation is off. The roll is client-side as stated: rollOf() at ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js:185-214 uses Math.random() at line 204, and say() at 217-241 sends the result as an ordinary `{ t: 'chat', text }` at 232, indistinguishable from a typed line. But the comment stating the trade-off is at 174-177, not 165-173: "Rolled here and sent as an ordinary line, so everybody sees the same words and the relay needs no idea that dice exist. Among five friends that is the right trade: making it uncheatable means changing the service and redeploying it". Lines 165-173 are the syntax and limits comment. Also worth knowing: the game server has no /roll at all. gamemode.js:3044 tells people "/roll is in the chat itself", and voice-server.js:513 pushes any line starting with a slash onto the command queue rather than broadcasting it, so a server-side roll would arrive at handle() and would need a reply through toProfile or a broadcast.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo: "delete the ROLL interception at 222-231"  ->  The interception is tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js:221-230, starting at `var dice = ROLL.exec(text);` on 221. Line 231 is `if (ws && ws.readyState === 1 && joined) {`, the send that must stay. Deleting through 231 as written removes the guard on the socket send.

**Challenged (minor):** doneWhen: "require gamemode.js with a stubbed mp, using the pattern that already exists at tools/thornswood-src/..."  ->  The pattern exists (tools/thornswood-src/test.js drives the real gamemode.js against a stubbed global.mp) but the harness will not run on either machine as committed: line 2 is `const PLUGIN = '/mnt/user-data/outputs/thornswood/gamemode.js';`, a sandbox path that exists on neither Patrick's nor Djinn's PC. Whoever takes this entry has to repoint that constant first, and the doneWhen does not say so.

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-chat.js lines 157-241, the dice, the limits, the send and the comment recording that this was a decision rather than an oversight. 2. tools/voice/voice-server.js lines 495-534, where a slash line goes today and what would have to change. 3. tools/gamemode.js lines 2863-3067, handle(), where a /roll branch would live, plus toProfile at 2831 and the local range test /ooc uses at 2986-3040.

**How:** Two places. In gamemode.js handle(), add a /roll branch that parses the same spec rollOf() accepts (sides, optional NxM, the limits at chat.js:181-183), rolls server-side, and puts the result into the room through the same local range test /ooc already uses, marked as server-rolled. Then in thornswood-chat.js delete the ROLL interception at 222-231 so the line goes to the server like any other slash command, keeping the refusal text for a bad spec.

**Done when:** `node --check` passes on both files, and a harness run proves the caller cannot choose the number: require gamemode.js with a stubbed mp, using the pattern that already exists at tools/thornswood-src/test.js, feed a /roll command and assert the broadcast text was not supplied by the caller.

**Traps:** The comment at 174-177 records this as a considered trade-off for five friends; opening to strangers is the reason to revisit it, and CLAUDE.md wants that argued in #dev before code rather than settled by an agent. Client and server both change, so it is one complete release. The voice service in front of the gamemode is not the file in the repo (entry 7) and every slash line passes through it, so confirm what is deployed before relying on the queue. Entries 65 and 71 touch the same chat path.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html

## 95. The server-down watchdog in the game client can switch itself off for the rest of the session

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js lines 1620-1650, the probe constants and the HttpClient, so you know the timings. 2. lines 1669-1729, the flag, checkServer and onStrike, which is the defect itself. 3. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js line 109, guard(), because every callback in this file is wrapped by it and your new code should be too.

**How:** Verified: `probing` is declared at 1670, set true at 1680 and cleared only at the top of the probe callback at 1683 or in the catch at 1707-1708, while checkServer returns immediately at 1677 whenever it is true. One callback that never arrives disables the check for the rest of the session. PROBE_MS is 10000 (1623), PROBE_STRIKES is 12 (1639), RESTART_GRACE_MS is 600000 (1642) and probe is an sp.HttpClient to http://HOST:3000 built at 1645. Fix: add a `probeStartedAt` beside `lastProbe`, set it where probing is set true at 1680, and before the guard at 1677 add `if (probing && now - probeStartedAt > 30000) { probing = false; }`. Thirty seconds is three probe intervals.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` passes. Better: lift checkServer into a small Node harness with a stub probe.get that never calls back, advance a fake clock past the deadline and assert a second request goes out.

**Traps:** Client-side, so it goes out with a full release. Do not shorten PROBE_STRIKES: twelve strikes at ten seconds is two minutes and the comment at 1714-1720 shows the generosity is deliberate so a restart is not read as an outage. Keep new code inside guard(). The message this watchdog eventually prints goes through say(), which is log-only, so a stranger still sees nothing on screen: that is entries 42 and 44, not this one. The file carries the same 34-line whose-game gate as the other four plugins (entry 99).

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/http.md

## 96. The command channel between the plugins and the server has no acknowledgement, and drops the oldest on overflow

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/voice/voice-server.js lines 405-420, the loopback-gated GET /commands that drains the queue in one shot. 2. tools/voice/voice-server.js lines 495-534, where a slash line is queued and where the overflow rule lives. 3. tools/gamemode.js lines 3333-3359, poll(), the other end, and in particular the parse at 3343.

**How:** Verified: voice-server.js:410 is `if (req.method === 'GET' && route === '/commands')` and 412 is `const out = commands.splice(0, commands.length);`, so the queue is emptied before the gamemode has seen anything; line 520 is `while (commands.length > 200) commands.shift();`, which drops the oldest. gamemode.js poll() at 3333 makes an http.request to 127.0.0.1:VOICE_PORT/commands every 1500 ms (the interval at 3356-3359), and its parse at 3343 is `try { list = (JSON.parse(body) || {}).commands || []; } catch (e) { return; }`, so a truncated or malformed body silently discards a whole batch that has already been removed from the service. Smallest useful change: make the drain non-destructive. Return the queue with an id per item, add a loopback-only POST /commands/ack that removes acknowledged ids, and have poll() ack after handle() succeeds. Then change line 520 to refuse the newest and tell the sender.

**Done when:** `node --check` on both files, plus a loopback test with Node: POST a chat line into a locally running copy, GET /commands twice and assert the second GET still returns it until an ack arrives.

**Traps:** /commands and /say are already loopback-gated by isLoopback at 411 and 421, so this is not the identity hole; the sender's profileId comes from the join message, which is entry 15. The deployed voice service is not this file (entry 7). Entry 1 is live right now: the dev gamemode polls the live service on 7778 and drains this very queue, so testing on dev eats live commands until entry 1 is fixed. Gamemode and service both change, so one complete release.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/http.html

## 97. A bare return with thirty-one lines of dead code behind it

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js lines 678-740, the whole block, including the comment that is the reason not to delete it. 2. lines 740-760, the type 24 path, which is where lastForced is written in live code, so you can see the dead block is not the only user. 3. Entry 45 in this list, which is the same trap in the gamemode: a reviewer's fix rejected because it would reinstate a bug the file records removing.

**How:** Verified exactly: `if (type === 29) {` at 685, a comment at 686-705, a bare `return;` at 706, thirty-one unreachable lines at 707-737 and the block closing at 738. Do not delete it. Replace the bare return at 706 with a named flag declared beside the others near line 569, for example `const DOOR_RESCUE = false;` and then `if (!DOOR_RESCUE) { return; }`, leaving the comment where it is so the next reader meets the decision before the code.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` passes and `sed -n '700,740p' tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js` shows a named flag and the comment together, with no bare return.

**Traps:** The comment at 687-705 is an incident record dated 2026-09-16 and names the exact symptom it caused: the server's own activation of an inn door read as an unanswered press, the rescue fired, the person was pulled through the same load door every three seconds, "loads in, loads in again, then keeps flashing". Deleting the code deletes the shape of the bug that has to be avoided when cage gates are finally solved. Client-side, so it ships with a full release. This file is 1913 lines and several other entries are in it (95, 98, 99), so co-ordinate rather than opening four branches on it.

## 98. Maps that only ever grow, and a roster file rewritten whole on the simulation thread every four seconds

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The maps are exactly as described. gamemode.js:352 is `const lastUse = new Map();   // "actorId:refrId" -> timestamp`, written by harvest() at 1777 and by the station cooldowns, never pruned; the roster `book` is loaded at 2069-2073 and only added to; ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js:569 is `const lastForced = {}` (written at 747 in live code, and at 711 in the dead block entry 97 covers) and 588 is `const lastLogged = {}` (written at 638), both keyed by form id, neither pruned. One detail is overstated: the roster is not rewritten every four seconds. write() is at 2209-2212, the interval at 2268 is EVERY_MS 4000, but the call is `if (dirty) { dirty = false; write(); }` at 2267, so it writes at most once per four seconds and only after something changed. It is still a synchronous whole-file fs.writeFileSync at 2211 on the server's JS thread, which is the part worth fixing.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js lines 2045-2073, the roster module's own explanation of why rows are kept after somebody disconnects, which is the constraint any eviction rule has to respect. 2. lines 2200-2268, write(), dirty and the interval. 3. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js lines 562-590, the two client maps and what they are for.

**How:** For lastUse, sweep inside the tick that already runs: walk the Map and delete entries older than the longest cooldown (COOLDOWN_MS at 351, HARVEST_COOLDOWN_MS nearby) once its size passes a few thousand. For the roster, keep every profile but age out the fields that only answer "last seen", and write through a temp file plus rename instead of in place. For the two client maps, delete entries older than a few minutes in the same handlers that write them, at 638 and 747.

**Done when:** `node --check` on both files, plus a harness assertion: with tools/thornswood-src/test.js repointed at tools/gamemode.js (see entry 110) you can require the gamemode with a stubbed mp and assert lastUse.size stops growing after N simulated harvests.

**Traps:** Do not rebuild the roster from onlinePlayers: the comment at 2050-2053 records that it was built that way and came out empty exactly when the launcher reads it. characters.json is one of the files entry 2 wants moved out of the publicly served data folder, so co-ordinate before changing how it is written. The server hot-reloads gamemode.js on change, so a bad edit takes live handlers down. Client and server both change, so one complete release.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html

## 99. The same thirty-four lines are copied verbatim into five plugins

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance and the count are right, the line ranges are not. The block really is 34 lines and really is the same everywhere: hashing each copy's function body with leading whitespace stripped gives one identical hash for chargen, emotes, mark and voice, and front differs only by two spaces inside a trailing comment on one line. But the guard line `if (!thornswoodGame()) { return; }` and the function that follows sit at chargen.js 40-74, emotes.js 50-84, front.js 40-74, mark.js 32-66 and voice.js 15-49. The entry's chargen 21-66, emotes 31-76 and mark 21-66 are wrong; front's 29-74 is right only if you count from the whose-game comment at 29.

**Read first:** 1. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-chargen.js lines 18-74: first the comment explaining that plugins share one global scope and why everything is wrapped, then the gate itself. 2. CLAUDE.md, the Settled design section, because the whose-game gate and the session note are settled design and a refactor of them needs a line in #dev first. 3. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-voice.js lines 575-582, the comment recording that two files fetched in the same pass cannot rely on which runs first, which is the exact hazard a shared-object refactor has to survive.

**How:** Put the function in one plugin that also publishes it. front.js already does half of this at its line 79 with `const T = (globalThis.Thornswood = globalThis.Thornswood || {});`. The safe shape is for each of the other four to keep a three-line stub that uses `globalThis.Thornswood && globalThis.Thornswood.whoseGame` when it is there and returns true when it is not, so nothing depends on load order. Keep the hardcoded address in exactly one place while you are in there.

**Done when:** `for f in tools/ts-plugins/*.js; do node --check $f; done` passes and `grep -c "skymp5-client-settings.txt" tools/ts-plugins/*.js` shows the body in one file and absent from the other four.

**Traps:** Skyrim Platform loads every file in Data/Platform/Plugins in an order nobody controls, and a top-level function name is shared global state: that is precisely what the comment at chargen.js:20-23 is about, and a naive front-publishes-it refactor fails silently for whichever plugin loads first. The gate is settled design in CLAUDE.md (plugins idle unless the client points at our server and a fresh session note is there), so this is a #dev conversation before code. The address 192.135.112.23 is in all five copies and in forty other files, and Set-Vps.ps1 is what rewrites them (entry 104). All five plugins ship to everybody, so one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/tree/main/docs/skyrim_platform

## 100. A release is a diff against one machine's memory, not against the last release

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/Publish-Update.ps1 lines 168-203, the comment and the lines that put the baseline in %LOCALAPPDATA%, including why it was moved out of _dist. 2. lines 378-447, the scan and the diff, which is where size-and-timestamp is used instead of a hash. 3. tools/Ship.ps1 lines 355-406, the dry-run copy-aside, which exists only because the baseline is mutable local state.

**How:** Verified: line 177 is `$StateDir = Join-Path $env:LOCALAPPDATA 'Thornswood'` and 178 the published.json beside it, with the dev baseline at 183-190; the scan loop at 381-389 stores `'{0}:{1}' -f $f.Length, $f.LastWriteTimeUtc.Ticks`, that is size and modified time, while only the two profile files at 400 and the launcher source at 412 get SHA-256; the diff is at 439-442 and Save-State is at 1214, after the push. Change the value at line 388 to `(Get-FileHash $f.FullName -Algorithm SHA256).Hash`, which this script already does twice elsewhere, then write that map out as a manifest file and commit it beside version.json in the same push around 1180-1205.

**Done when:** Two `.\tools\Ship.ps1 -DryRun` runs (a person runs them) over an unchanged tree produce an identical manifest, and the releases commit contains a manifest whose entry count equals the "mod files" number the scan prints at line 390.

**Traps:** Hashing every file in the build is far slower than reading sizes and times and this runs on every Ship: measure before making it the default. The dry-run copy-aside at Ship.ps1:365-406 exists because an earlier dry run left the baseline and the feed changed and "looked exactly like a broken release"; keep it working until the manifest replaces the baseline. Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's do-not-edit-without-a-#dev-line list, and only Patrick's machine ships main. Entries 101, 102 and 103 all depend on this one.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases , https://semver.org/

## 101. The feed is an append-only chain of 192 deltas with no rollback and no known-good state

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/Publish-Update.ps1 lines 795-813, where packs are appended and never pruned. 2. tools/app/Updater.cs lines 346-354 and 440-462, Pending() and ApplyPack(), the client end that replays the chain. 3. Entry 100 in this list, because this one cannot start until the manifest exists.

**How:** Verified against the live feed, read-only: `gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood-Releases/contents/version.json` is 83,059 bytes and carries 192 pack entries, 164 of them pointing at api.github.com asset URLs under the now-private EvilPatrick06/Thornswood and 28 at the public repo, with launcherVersion 1.1.47. The copy committed in this repo is the stale 59,496-byte one with 164 packs that entry 59 measured. Once entry 100 commits a per-release manifest, change Updater.cs to fetch the manifest the feed names and sync to it instead of walking `packs`, and cut version.json down to launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, the server fields and a pointer to the current manifest.

**Done when:** version.json drops to a few hundred bytes; a fresh install with no applied.txt reaches the current build without touching a single private-repo asset URL, checkable by fetching the feed and asserting zero entries match /repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/.

**Traps:** C# 5 only in the launcher: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Package-Thornswood seeds applied.txt precisely so fresh installs never replay the 164 dead assets (entry 11); do not remove that seeding before the manifest path is live. Launchers already installed must still be able to read the feed, so the old shape cannot simply be deleted in one release. Updater.cs is a shared file needing a #dev line, and entry 26 rewrites the self-update in it at the same time.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases

## 102. The shipped exe is compiled on your desktop while CI compiles the same source and throws it away

**Category:** GitHub & CI  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. .github/workflows/build.yml, all of it, it is short. 2. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 220-260, the csc invocation, so you know what a runner build would have to reproduce, including /noconfig and the hand-listed references. 3. tools/Ship.ps1 line 253, where the shipped exe is actually built today.

**How:** Verified: build.yml is one job on windows-latest with a PowerShell parse step, a build step at 33-39 calling tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 and actions/upload-artifact@v4 at 41-46 with retention-days 7, and nothing anywhere downloads that artifact; the shipped exe comes from Ship.ps1:253 calling the same script on the shipping machine. Add a second workflow on `push: tags: ['v*']` that checks out, runs Build-Launcher.ps1 and attaches the exe plus a checksums file to the release, then make Ship.ps1 consume that asset instead of building locally.

**Done when:** A tag push produces a release whose exe has the same SHA-256 as a local build of the same commit, compared with Get-FileHash. Until the signing identity in entry 14 exists, that comparison is the only evidence the two builds agree.

**Traps:** Signing (entry 14) and this go together: the key must only ever touch the runner, which means the local build has to stop being the shipping build in the same change. The runner image and both actions are unpinned today, which entry 113 wants fixed while you are in the file. Ship.ps1 is shared and needs a #dev line. Only Patrick's machine ships main and Ship refuses a main release that is not on main or is behind origin, so a CI-built artifact still has to reach that machine or the rule has to change deliberately.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/actions/concepts/workflows-and-actions/workflow-artifacts , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/tutorials/store-and-share-data , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/releases , https://github.com/softprops/action-gh-release

## 103. If your machine dies, the source survives and the release process does not

**Category:** Release  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 47-70, the release path, naming Ship.ps1 and the baseline at %LOCALAPPDATA%\Thornswood\published.json at line 58. 2. lines 150-166, the first-publish baseline and the sentence that the #news webhook lives in tools/discord/news-webhook.txt and is handed over only to somebody who will ship main releases. 3. .gitignore, which shows which of those four things are deliberately absent from the repo.

**How:** All four named things check out as machine-local: the baseline (Publish-Update.ps1:177-178), the news webhook (present in the working tree, gitignored), the update token (config.json, DPAPI-protected, Config.cs:89 and 247, put there by Set-UpdateToken.ps1) and the shipping role (only Patrick's machine ships main). Treat this row as the acceptance test for entries 100, 101, 102 and 104 rather than a task of its own: do those first. The two pieces that belong only here are moving the news webhook and the update token somewhere two people can reach, and writing down in SECOND-DEVELOPER.md which machine may ship and what the second machine needs to take over.

**Done when:** A checkout on the second machine, plus the credentials, produces the same release artifact as the first: same exe hash for the same commit, and Ship's preflight passes there.

**Traps:** This entry is a roll-up and cannot be picked up alone; its effort tag (large) sits oddly beside entry 101's (rewrite) even though it contains it. Secrets are named by location only, never printed, pasted, committed or logged. Entry 108 asks for the same credentials-with-two-holders from the handover side, so write the plan once and cross-reference. Changing who may ship main is settled-design territory and belongs in #dev first.

## 104. Deploys are one laptop, one hardcoded address, and no versioned artifact

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The shape is right, the count is not. 192.135.112.23 appears in 45 files in the working tree, 41 outside _to_delete: 32 PowerShell scripts under tools\, all five ts-plugins, tools/app/Config.cs, version.json, tools/docs-app/data/board.json, SECOND-DEVELOPER.md and tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md. Set-Vps.ps1's own header at line 11 says "twenty three files", which is itself stale. Deploy-Server.ps1:33 is the dist default `'C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\build\dist\server'` and the address is on line 34, not 33; line 46 is the Steam Data path. One thing to know before anybody rebuilds this: Set-Vps.ps1 does not hold a fixed list. Lines 102-110 walk the whole repo, skipping .git, node_modules, bin, obj, _dist and packages, rewrite every text file carrying the old address, show a preview first and add a redirect so launchers already installed move themselves. So the twenty-two-files framing understates the count and overstates the pain.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Set-Vps.ps1 lines 1-110, what already exists for exactly this problem. 2. tools/Deploy-Server.ps1 lines 33-47, the three machine-specific defaults in one param block. 3. tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md, the written procedure a config file would be replacing.

**How:** Cheap half: give the scripts one shared defaults file, for example tools/vps.json holding the address and the ports, read through a small helper, and leave Set-Vps.ps1 as the migration path for the sources that legitimately bake the address in (the plugins, Config.cs, version.json). Expensive half is entry 102's release job publishing a versioned server payload that a deploy job installs by version.

**Done when:** `grep -rl "192\.135\.112\.23" . | grep -v .git | wc -l` falls from 45 to the handful that genuinely embed it, and `.\tools\Set-Vps.ps1 -Preview` (a person runs it) lists only those.

**Traps:** Never hand-edit the address anywhere: Set-Vps.ps1 exists because a partial edit left the site deploying to a dead box for two days. Eleven of these scripts also carry StrictHostKeyChecking=no (entry 22) and three put secrets on ssh command lines (entry 23); a shared helper is the natural home for all three, which makes one pull request too big under the one-fix-per-pull-request rule, so split it deliberately. The plugins' copy of the address lives inside the 34-line gate entry 99 wants deduplicated.

## 105. The dev server is a scratch copy on the same box, and its load order is a one-time snapshot nothing refreshes

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The citations are right: vps-dev-instance.ps1 lines 9-12 are the port table (live game 7777 with resources 3000, dev 7787 with 7788, live voice 7778, dev voice 7789) and lines 49-55 rewrite server-settings.json with the dev port, the dev name and the load order paths repointed to C:/Thornswood-Server-Dev/. Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 lines 9-20 are the comment explaining why Deploy-Server must not be pointed at the dev root, and line 82 is the single scp of gamemode.js. But "a one-time snapshot nothing refreshes" is not quite right. Re-running vps-dev-instance.ps1 does refresh the load order: the robocopy at line 30 re-copies program files and plugins, and lines 50-54 rebuild server-settings.json from the live one every time. What never refreshes is the data folder: door.json, allowed.json and manifest.json are copied only when absent, at lines 37-40. And nothing in the normal dev flow runs that script at all, because Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 sends gamemode.js alone.

**Read first:** 1. tools/vps-dev-instance.ps1 lines 1-55, what dev is made of and which parts are copied only once. 2. tools/Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 lines 1-25, why the live deploy script is refused here and what the dev path actually sends. 3. CLAUDE.md, How work moves between the two machines, for the rule that there is one dev feed, the last dev Ship wins, and you say so in #dev when you take it.

**How:** Smallest correct change: make the dev deploy refresh what the snapshot froze. Either have Deploy-DevGamemode.ps1 also run the settings and data steps of vps-dev-instance.ps1, or split those steps into a script the dev deploy calls. Then move the ports and the door URL into a per-environment file so dev is a configuration of the same artifact rather than a copy of a folder.

**Done when:** After a load order change and a dev deploy, the dev server's server-settings.json loadOrder count matches the live one. Both numbers are already printed: vps-dev-instance.ps1:55 prints the plugin count and Deploy-DevGamemode prints the listening port. These are VPS scripts, so a person runs them.

**Traps:** Entry 1 is live right now and is the sharp form of this: the dev gamemode is hardcoded to voice port 7778, the live service, and its GET /commands and GET /choices drain the live queues. Do not exercise anything on dev that touches voice until entry 1 is fixed. Deploy-Server.ps1 must never be pointed at the dev root; its own comment at 9-15 names the two ways that breaks live. The dev feed is shared between both machines, so announce in #dev before taking it.

## 106. The second developer cannot restore service alone at 2am from what is written down

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is verified; one of the three citations does not support it. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md's headings are What Thornswood is, The pieces, How a release goes out, Two machines shipping, Setting up a machine, Secrets, The three launchers problem, Separate Skyrim folders and Where things are written down. There is no recovery section, no rollback instruction, and the only mentions of restarting anything are the watchdog row at line 41 and Request-Restart at line 72. tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md is about moving to another box, not restoring service. But CLAUDE.md:84-92 does not support the claim: those nine lines are the what-a-Claude-does-and-what-the-person-does section, about tool permissions, not handover. The supporting material in CLAUDE.md is nearer the top, the pieces and the where-things-are list.

**Read first:** 1. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md once end to end, 326 lines, for what is already written down and where the gap is. 2. tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md, as the template: it is what a procedure written for this project looks like. 3. tools/Watch-Services.ps1, for the service names, the ports and the three-strikes rule, which is the raw material for a one-minute triage.

**How:** Write one page, IT-IS-DOWN.md at the repo root, with a row per piece: game server, voice, site, bot, Caddy, watchdog. For each: how to tell in under a minute (the port or URL to try), the exact restart command, where the log is, how to roll back the last deploy, and who to wake. Take the names from tools/Watch-Services.ps1 and the paths from SECOND-DEVELOPER.md's pieces table at lines 26-45.

**Done when:** Somebody who has never touched the VPS can name the command for each of the six pieces from that page alone. Mechanical check: every command in the page exists as a script in tools\ or as a scheduled task named in SECOND-DEVELOPER.md.

**Traps:** The page has to live in the repository, not on the box it describes. Every command in it is one a person runs, not an agent: CLAUDE.md reserves Deploy-*, Request-Restart, Install-Bot, Rotate-ServerPassword and Publish-* for the person. Secrets by location only. The watchdog writes its log to the same box it watches (entry 20), so the page cannot tell somebody to read that log first. Entry 108 wants an ownership page and entry 141 wants per-component READMEs: three different documents, all wanted, none of them the same page.

## 107. Deploying the gamemode or the server is impossible on any machine but yours

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 lines 35-45, the param block, and line 107, the throw. 2. tools/Deploy-Server.ps1 lines 33-47, the same pattern with a second machine-specific default, the Steam Data path. 3. Entry 109 in this list, because the dist tree these default to is the uncommitted fork build and there is nowhere else to point until that is fixed.

**How:** Verified exactly: Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:37 is `[string]$Dist = 'C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\build\dist\server'` and :107 is `if (-not (Test-Path -LiteralPath $Dist)) { throw "server dist not found: $Dist" }`. Change that throw to say plainly that a local skymp build tree is required, that -Dist overrides it, and that on a machine without one the deploy belongs to whoever has the build. Do the same for Deploy-Server.ps1. The real fix falls out of entries 104 and 109.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 -Dist C:\nope` fails at line 107, before any network call, and the message names the switch. Note the script does reach the VPS further down, so only this early failure is safe for anyone but the person who owns the box.

**Traps:** Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 talks to the VPS, so it is a person's script; only the failing-early form is safe to exercise. Pointing -Dist elsewhere has nowhere to point until the fork's build is versioned (entry 109). CLAUDE.md's Words rules apply to the new message: plain sentences, no em dashes, no filler.

## 108. Five things exist only on your laptop or in your head

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The citations check out. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:43-45 is the fork and its build tree ("Patrick's alone for now (C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp); server binaries are copied, not rebuilt"), line 58 is the baseline at %LOCALAPPDATA%\Thornswood\published.json, lines 164-166 are the #news webhook in tools/discord/news-webhook.txt, tools/COLLECTION-METHOD.md records the collection decision along with the machine-specific Vortex paths it was read off, and Backup-Server.ps1's header says it pulls everything onto "this machine". What the entry never does is name the five things, which makes it hard to pick up cold: from the citations they are the SkyMP fork and its build tree, the publish baseline, the #news webhook, the update token in config.json, and the collection method. One of the five is wrong in the same way entry 109 is: the fork is in a repository, it is the uncommitted changes in it that exist on one disk only.

SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 26-70 and 168-181, the pieces table, the release path and the secrets table, which is already half of an ownership page. 2. tools/COLLECTION-METHOD.md, a decision recorded together with the machine state it was read off, which is the pattern to follow. 3. tools/Backup-Server.ps1 header, for what is currently considered irreplaceable and what is missing from that list.

**How:** Write one table: asset, where it lives, who holds it, who the second holder is, how to reissue it. Name locations, never values.

Entry 103 wants the same credentials moved to a shared vault: write the plan once. This overlaps entries 106 and 141, which are three different documents; merging them into one long page is how none of them get read.

## 109. The SkyMP server fork exists on one machine and in no repository

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** wrong

**Correction:** The fork is in a repository. C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp is a git clone with remotes origin https://github.com/EvilPatrick06/skymp.git and upstream https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp.git, on branch main tracking origin/main, HEAD at f926944b, an upstream commit dated 2026-09-08, so the base commit is recorded and recoverable. What exists on one machine only is the change set: `git status --porcelain` shows 19 modified tracked files and nothing committed, `git diff --shortstat` is 19 files changed, 911 insertions, 73 deletions, across libespm, skymp5-client (loadGameService, remoteServer, singlePlayerService, sweetCameraEnforcementService, sync/equipment.ts, view/worldView), skymp5-server (ScampServer.cpp, ActionListener.cpp, WorldState.cpp and .h, manifestGen.ts, systems/spawn.ts) and skyrim-platform (Validators, BrowserApi, FileInfoApi, TPInputService, DInputHook, MyChromiumApp). No branch, no commit, no stash: one `git checkout .` or one dead disk loses all of it. This also explains entry 215: the equipment fix at skymp5-client/src/sync/equipment.ts is inside that uncommitted set, which is why it is absent from the shipped bundle. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:43-44 is quoted correctly and still says "Patrick's alone".

**Read first:** 1. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 43-45, what the document claims. 2. The clone itself: `git -C C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp status` and `git -C C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp diff --stat`, which is what is actually at risk. 3. Entry 215 in this list, which is the cost already paid for not committing these: a fix that exists on disk and not in the bundle everybody runs.

**How:** On that machine: `git -C C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp checkout -b thornswood/engine-changes`, then add and commit the 19 files with a message naming what each group is for, then push to origin. The push is the person's call. Afterwards record the base commit f926944b and the branch name in SECOND-DEVELOPER.md beside line 43, and make Deploy-Server's -Dist default point at a named build of that branch rather than whatever happens to be in build/dist/server.

**Done when:** `git -C C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp status --porcelain` prints nothing, and `git -C C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp log --oneline -1 origin/thornswood/engine-changes` shows the commit on the remote.

**Traps:** Committing 911 lines across 19 files in one commit is exactly the eight-things-in-one-branch CLAUDE.md warns against, but getting them off one disk beats tidiness here; split them afterwards. skymp is GPLv3 for the client and Skyrim Platform and AGPLv3 for the server, so publishing this branch is most of the entry 10 source offer, which makes the two entries connected. The server binaries in build/dist/server are copied, not rebuilt, and Deploy-Server.ps1:33 points straight at them, so a rebuild changes what the live server runs and is a full release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/docs_skyrim_platform.md

## 110. There are no tests, and three runners are already installed on both machines

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** wrong

**Correction:** There are tests, and they are better than the entry imagines. tools/thornswood-src/test.js is 349 lines and its first line says "Drive the real gamemode.js against a stubbed server API": it stubs global.mp, requires the whole gamemode and asserts against it, which is exactly the thing the entry describes as a reviewer's experiment. tools/thornswood-src/test-chargen.js is another 176 lines. The README beside them says "test.js: 82 assertions against a stubbed mp. Run it on every change." They cannot run as checked in: test.js line 2 is `const PLUGIN = '/mnt/user-data/outputs/thornswood/gamemode.js';`, a path from the sandbox they were written in, and `node test.js` fails with Cannot find module. With that one line repointed at tools/gamemode.js (verified by running a copy in a scratch directory, leaving the repo untouched) the harness loads today's 3363-line gamemode.js and runs: 31 assertions pass, 26 fail, and it then throws at line 257 on a chargen expectation. The failures look like five days of drift between a 12 September harness and a 17 September gamemode, possibly with an incomplete stub, rather than proof of regressions, but that is triage somebody has to do, not something I can assert. The runners half is right: Node 24.15.0, Python 3.9.13 and Pester 3.4.0 are present here. I did not check the CI runner's Pester version.

**Read first:** 1. tools/thornswood-src/README.md, what the pipeline is and the line telling you to run test.js on every change. 2. tools/thornswood-src/test.js lines 1-64, the mp stub and how the gamemode is loaded. 3. tools/thornswood-src/test-chargen.js, the chargen assertions, which is where the failures cluster.

**How:** Change test.js line 2 to `const PLUGIN = require('path').resolve(__dirname, '..', 'gamemode.js');` and run `node tools/thornswood-src/test.js`. Triage the 26 failures one at a time: each is either a stale expectation to update or a change in behaviour worth knowing about. Then add it as a third step in .github/workflows/build.yml, and move on to the pure logic entries 30 and 112 name.

**Done when:** `node tools/thornswood-src/test.js` exits 0 and prints its "N passed, 0 failed" summary, and the same command is green in CI. No game and no launcher involved.

**Traps:** build.py in the same folder writes to the same sandbox path, so do not treat tools/thornswood-src as a live generator: tools/gamemode.js is the shipping file, is edited directly and is five days ahead of the pipeline. Regenerating it from body.js would throw away those edits. The README's warning about editing body.js and never gamemode_body.js applies inside the pipeline, not to the shipping file. Adding a test step to build.yml makes it part of every pull request check, which is what entries 113 and 114 want, so co-ordinate. No new dependency is needed and adding one would want a #dev line.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/test.html , https://docs.python.org/3/library/unittest.html , https://pester.dev/docs/quick-start

## 111. Patch-Client's four blocks are exact text matched against a bundle nobody here builds, checked only at Ship time

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools/Patch-Client.ps1 lines 1-45, the header, which is the incident record: the bundle is another server's build, hand edits to it were silently overwritten on 16 September and shipped away, and that is why every change is a block here. 2. lines 249-280, the matcher and the write, the part that would change. 3. CLAUDE.md, Settled design, which fixes that the bundle is not built here and ships as patched.

**How:** Verified: the file is 280 lines, `$blocks` runs 96-244 with exactly four blocks named at 98 'offline login', 129 'locks, activators and furniture', 161 'locked refs are not announced' and 231 'difficulty'; the apply loop is 249-266, skipping a block whose Marker is present (250), joining the Old lines with the file's own line ending and matching with IndexOf Ordinal (253), refusing on a miss (254-259) and on a second match (260-263). There is no hash anywhere in the file. Ship.ps1 calls it at line 338 and dies on a non-zero exit. Steps: schedule a `-Check` run (the switch exists and exits before the write at 278, so it is read-only); replace each long Old array with the smallest distinctive anchor plus a regex; record the input bundle's SHA-256 beside the script so an unrecognised bundle is flagged; and after the write, re-read the file and assert every Marker is present rather than trusting the exit code.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Patch-Client.ps1 -Check` exits 0 and prints "already patched" on a patched game folder, and exits 1 naming the blocks on an unpatched copy. It reads the game folder, so it is the person's command to run, but it never starts the game.

**Traps:** The bundle legitimately changes identity whenever another launcher runs, the other server's until 16 September and Daedric Online's since, so a hard hash gate will refuse a bundle that is fine: record the hash as an expectation, not a wall, until entry 215 builds the bundle here. Entries 215 and 216 want the same hash recording for a different reason (today the bundle is identified by grepping for the word thornswood, which is only there because Patch-Client put it there), so do it once. The four blocks are the diff that entry 10's GPL source offer has to publish. A refusal here stops a release by design: do not soften the exit codes.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 112. The updater's pure logic is untested, and the reviewer reports three bugs sitting in it

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** All seven line numbers are exact: IsNewer 76, Parts 99, RepoCandidates 125, Inside 273, ClaimedElsewhere 302, Pending 346, CheckUrl 384. Two things in the description are not right. Three of the seven are not pure: Pending calls Applied(), which reads applied.txt; ClaimedElsewhere enumerates Paths.PacksDir and reads every .files list; CheckUrl returns nothing and throws. The genuinely pure four are IsNewer, Parts, RepoCandidates and Inside. And the entry gives nobody a way to act on "three bugs sitting in it" because it never names them, so a stranger cannot confirm or refute anything: treat the three as unverified until somebody writes them down. The one candidate that looked real is not: Inside does no canonicalisation, so a `..` segment would pass its fence, but every caller runs Path.GetFullPath first (see 527 and 717), which is itself the property a test should pin.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Updater.cs lines 60-135, IsNewer, Parts and RepoCandidates, each with a doc comment recording the incident that shaped it: a machine overwriting its own fresh build, and a repository rename that broke every launcher. 2. lines 265-310, Inside and ClaimedElsewhere, the two that decide what may be deleted. 3. CLAUDE.md, Settled design, for the C# 5 restrictions that constrain how any harness can be written.

**How:** Do not add a test framework. Compile a second small exe with the same csc invocation Build-Launcher.ps1 uses, with a Main that calls the four pure functions over a table of cases and returns non-zero on a mismatch, and run it from build.yml. Start with the cases the doc comments already describe: IsNewer must return false when remote is older than local; Parts must return null for anything non-numeric; RepoCandidates must produce both the old and the new repository name; Inside must reject C:\Thornswood2\x against C:\Thornswood, and the test should also assert callers canonicalise first.

**Done when:** The harness runs in CI and fails when any of those four cases regresses. Locally it compiles with the same csc line Build-Launcher.ps1 uses, so no game, no launcher and no network are needed.

**Traps:** C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Note Parts already uses `int.TryParse(bits[i], out o[i])`, which is legal because the variable exists; do not mistake it for out var. The build uses /noconfig and lists references by hand (Build-Launcher.ps1:224-232) because csc.rsp caused CS1703 on machines with the reference assemblies installed, so a harness has to be compiled the same way. Updater.cs is on the do-not-edit-without-a-#dev-line list. Entry 26 rewrites the self-update in this file and entry 101 changes what Pending does, so write assertions about behaviour, not shape.

**Links:** https://pester.dev/docs/quick-start

## 113. CI is one 36 second job that compiles and parses, and checks nothing else

**Category:** GitHub & CI  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Substance holds, two numbers off. .github/workflows/build.yml is 46 lines, not 47. It is one job (`launcher`) with three steps: parse every .ps1, run Build-Launcher.ps1, upload the exe. Runner is `windows-latest` and both actions are float-pinned at `@v4` (actions/checkout, actions/upload-artifact), so 'moving runner image with float-pinned actions' is exact. Duration: the last eight runs took 26, 27, 26, 43, 29, 26, 32 and 41 seconds, so '36 second job' is a fair average but not a constant. The one claim that does not hold up: 'one start of the launcher on the runner, which would have caught the missing Installed apps entry on day one'. Shell.cs:244 calls Register.Ensure(cfg) on every launch, so a launcher start writes that key and would have shown it PRESENT. The real gap in entry 121 is that tools/Setup-Thornswood.ps1 writes no Uninstall key at install time, which no launcher start can catch. Note this entry overlaps entry 30 heavily: both are work on the same build.yml, 30 being the incident-regression half and 113 the breadth half. They should be planned as one CI change.

**Challenged (minor):** "It is one job (`launcher`) with three steps: parse every .ps1, run Build-Launcher.ps1, upload the exe."  ->  .github/workflows/build.yml has four steps in that job: `- uses: actions/checkout@v4` at line 16, then the three named ones. The 46-line count, the single `launcher` job, windows-latest and both actions floating at @v4 all check out.

**Challenged (minor):** "the last eight runs took 26, 27, 26, 43, 29, 26, 32 and 41 seconds, so '36 second job' is a fair average".  ->  The eight durations are right (gh run list gives the same multiset), but they average 31 seconds, not 36. The next run back is 37. Calling 36 a fair average of numbers that average 31 is the sort of thing the next reader will re-measure.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\.github\workflows\build.yml, all 46 lines: it is the entire CI surface and its header comment states the contract (green means the branch would not stop Ship at the build step). 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1, especially lines 21 ($Root default is C:\Thornswood-DevBuild), 169-170 (it kills running processes) and 349-412 (it writes a Desktop shortcut and restarts explorer.exe). CI calls this script, so half of what CI runs is install work, not build work. That is entry 119 and it should land first. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Check-Controls.ps1, lines 1-27: a finished XAML-versus-C# checker sitting unused. Wiring it in is the cheapest job to add.

**How:** In .github/workflows/build.yml, split the single `launcher` job into several named jobs so a failure names itself. Add, each as its own job on `windows-latest`: (a) `analyse` running `Install-Module PSScriptAnalyzer -Force -Scope CurrentUser` then `Invoke-ScriptAnalyzer -Path tools -Recurse -Severity Error`; (b) `plugins` running `Get-ChildItem tools/ts-plugins,tools/ts-ui -Filter *.js -Recurse | ForEach-Object { node --check $_.FullName }` plus `node --check tools/gamemode.js` and `node --check tools/docs-app/server.js` and `node --check tools/voice/voice-server.js`; (c) `bot` running `python -m compileall -q tools/discord`; (d) `xaml` running `.\tools\Check-Controls.ps1`, which already compares ui.xaml against the F<T>("Name") lookups in tools/app/*.cs; (e) `tests` once entry 110 has any. Pin the runner to a dated image (`runs-on: windows-2022`) and pin each action to a commit sha instead of `@v4`. Leave the existing compile step alone. Do not add a launcher-start job until entry 119 has split Build-Launcher.ps1, and if you do add one, run the exe with a timeout and kill it, because it is a WinExe that opens a window and never exits on its own.

**Done when:** On a scratch branch, break one thing per language on purpose: a stray brace in a tools/*.ps1, a stray brace in tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js, a bad indent in tools/discord/bot.py, and rename one x:Name in tools/ui.xaml that Shell.cs looks up. Push and open a pull request. Each of the new jobs goes red and names its own file, and the existing compile job still passes for the XAML rename, which is the whole point. Then revert the branch. Locally you can prove each job's command first without GitHub: run `Invoke-ScriptAnalyzer -Path tools -Recurse -Severity Error`, `node --check tools/gamemode.js`, `python -m compileall -q tools/discord` and `.\tools\Check-Controls.ps1` from the repo root.

**Traps:** Making a job REQUIRED is a repository setting, not a file, and today branch protection on main has no required status checks at all and `enforce_admins` is false, so a new job blocks nothing until somebody changes that (entry 114). CI runs tools/Build-Launcher.ps1, which at line 169-170 kills running Thornswood and game processes and at 349-363 writes Thornswood.lnk to the Desktop: harmless on a throwaway runner, ugly if anybody copies that step to a real machine. Build-Launcher.ps1's default -Root is C:\Thornswood-DevBuild, so CI passes -Root explicitly; keep doing that. The launcher is C# 5 only, compiled by the .NET Framework 4 csc, so do not add a job that builds it with a modern SDK. CLAUDE.md requires a line in #dev before touching Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs or Publish-Update.ps1, and a CI change that forces edits to those inherits that rule.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/security-for-github-actions/security-guides/security-hardening-for-github-actions , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/utility-modules/psscriptanalyzer/overview , https://pester.dev/docs/quick-start , https://nodejs.org/api/test.html

## 114. The review routine both documents describe has never happened, and the settings exempt the only person it would bind

**Category:** GitHub & CI  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is true and the settings claims are exactly right; the counts have moved on. Read live from the API today: `enforce_admins.enabled` is false (the admin is exempt, verified), `required_status_checks` is absent from the protection object entirely (the build check is not required, verified), and `required_pull_request_reviews.required_approving_review_count` is 1 with `require_code_owner_reviews` false and `require_last_push_approval` false. Merged pull requests are now 58, not 54; the 54 figure matches the number still sitting at reviewDecision REVIEW_REQUIRED. Exactly 2 are APPROVED, verified. 56 of the 58 were authored by EvilPatrick06 and 2 by WiccanDjinn. 32 of 58 were merged within two minutes of being opened, not 'the rest ... within about a minute', so the claim is true of a bit over half. No .github/CODEOWNERS file exists, verified by name. CLAUDE.md:60-64 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:85-88 both say what the entry says they say, verified.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: make the build check required by adding "the check named 'Launcher builds' (the `name:` of the job in build.yml)", while entry 113 says the two entries "should be planned as one CI change" and its howTo splits the single `launcher` job into several named jobs.  ->  Neither write-up says these two have to land in the right order. The job name is real (build.yml line 13, `name: Launcher builds`), but a required status check is matched by that string. If 113's split renames or removes the `launcher` job after 114 has pinned 'Launcher builds' as required, every pull request sits at "Expected, waiting for status" forever and nothing can merge, including the fix. Whoever picks these up needs to be told to change the required-check list in the same change as the job names.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\CLAUDE.md lines 55-72, the section 'How work moves between the two machines': it is the rule as written, including the note that pre-17-Sep branches use a `gavin/` prefix and are the same person. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 83-105, the long form of the same rule plus the protected-files list. 3. The live settings, read with `gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/branches/main/protection`, because the documents and the settings are what disagree and only one of them is enforced.

**How:** This is a decision first and a settings change second, and it belongs in #dev before anything is typed. Whichever way it goes, make the build check required: on GitHub, Settings, Branches, the main rule, tick 'Require status checks to pass before merging' and add the check named 'Launcher builds' (the `name:` of the job in build.yml). Then pick one: (a) if reviews are real, tick 'Do not allow bypassing the above settings' so enforce_admins becomes true, and edit CLAUDE.md:60-64 to say nothing beyond what is enforced; or (b) if the owner self-merges, leave enforce_admins false and rewrite CLAUDE.md:60-64 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:85-88 to say plainly that Patrick self-merges and Djinn's branches get read. Either way add .github/CODEOWNERS naming the six shared files from CLAUDE.md, one line each, for example `tools/app/Shell.cs @EvilPatrick06 @WiccanDjinn`, covering Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs and tools/Publish-Update.ps1, then tick 'Require review from Code Owners'. This is the same file entry 142 asks for, so do them together.

**Done when:** `gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/branches/main/protection` returns a `required_status_checks` object listing the build check, and `enforce_admins.enabled` matches whichever answer was chosen. Open a throwaway pull request touching tools/app/Shell.cs: GitHub's merge box shows the build check as required and, with CODEOWNERS in place, shows a code owner review request. `gh api repos/EvilPatrick06/Thornswood/codeowners/errors` returns no errors, which catches a mistyped path or handle.

**Traps:** Settings changes on GitHub leave the machine, so under CLAUDE.md the person makes them, not a Claude. Turning enforce_admins on with a required check will block Ship.ps1's own version commit, which pushes straight to main after publishing (that is entry 29 and it must be fixed first or the next release stops dead). Ship.ps1 also refuses a main release that is not on main or is behind origin, so a protection change that blocks the push breaks shipping, not just merging. CODEOWNERS handles must be usernames or teams with push access or GitHub silently ignores the line, which is why the codeowners/errors endpoint is the real check. The repository is private and Thornswood-Releases is public; protection on one says nothing about the other.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-protected-branches/about-protected-branches , https://docs.github.com/en/rest/branches/branch-protection , https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners

## 115. Two thirds of catch blocks are silent, so a broken button looks like nothing happened

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The total is exact and the finding is real, but two details are wrong. 581 catch clauses across tools/app/*.cs, verified exactly. Empty ones: 374 by a regex matching `catch` with an optional parameter and a body containing only whitespace, not 367. The seven-block gap is almost certainly whether a comment-only body counts as empty; state your rule when you set the baseline. The 'worst in' list is off: by empty count it is Shell.cs 79, OwnGame.cs 22, Icons.cs 21, Voice.cs 20, Updater.cs 20, Checks.cs 20, Downgrade.cs 18, Uninstall.cs 16. Icons.cs is third worst and is not named; Uninstall.cs is named but sits eighth behind Updater.cs and Downgrade.cs. The important correction: 'the global handler at Shell.cs:436-444 swallows the rest' is WRONG on both counts. Shell.cs:436 is `var app = new Application();`; the handler is `app.DispatcherUnhandledException` at 445-451, and it does not swallow. It logs the exception type and message through Log() and appends the full `fault.Exception.ToString()`, stack and all, to Paths.LogFile, then sets Handled = true. The comment above it at 441-444 says exactly why. All four named non-swallow targets exist and do have silent catches: Config.Save at Config.cs:257 with `catch { }` at 274, Swap.Restore at Swap.cs:40, Hold.Release at Hold.cs:105, Keep.Unpark at Keep.cs:407.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: add tools/app/Swallow.cs with `public static void Note(string where, Exception e) { try { Shell.Log(...); } catch { } }` and replace the empty catches with it.  ->  Shell.Log is private. tools/app/Shell.cs:20 declares `static partial class Shell` and line 708 declares `static void Log(string m)` with no access modifier, so a call to `Shell.Log` from another type is CS0122 and the build stops. Nothing in tools/app/*.cs calls Shell.Log cross-class today. Worse, even after widening it: Shell.Log line 711 is `txtStatus.Text = m;`, a WPF control touch. Called from a catch on a pool thread (Updater, Voice, Downgrade, SkyMp all have them) it throws InvalidOperationException, and the howTo's own `catch { }` swallows that, so the helper writes nothing for exactly the background failures it was added to surface. It also NREs before Bind() runs. Any real version has to write to Paths.LogFile directly and never touch txtStatus.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen (repeated in 116 and 117): "Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`".  ->  That command has side effects outside $Root that the doneWhen does not mention, and entry 119 documents them two rows later. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:349-363 unconditionally writes Thornswood.lnk to `[Environment]::GetFolderPath('Desktop')` pointing at $exe, so running this repoints the customer-facing Desktop shortcut at a temp build that then gets cleaned up. Lines 396-412 also kill explorer.exe, delete IconCache.db and iconcache_*.db and restart Explorer whenever the artwork hash changed, and 353-354 delete any 'OldMod.lnk'. A doneWhen that tells someone to run this needs to say "then run the real build again to put your shortcut back".

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Shell.cs lines 430-455: the Application setup and the real global handler, so you do not re-add logging that is already there. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Config.cs lines 257-280: Save with its silent catch and a finally block, the single highest-value place to stop swallowing, because a silently failed Save is the tester-facing symptom in the finding. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\CLAUDE.md lines 40-46 for the C# 5 restriction, before you write a helper.

**How:** Add one static helper in a new file tools/app/Swallow.cs (a new file is picked up automatically, Build-Launcher.ps1 globs tools/app/*.cs). C# 5 only, so no string interpolation and no expression-bodied member: `public static void Note(string where, Exception e) { try { Shell.Log("swallowed in " + where + ": " + e.GetType().Name + ": " + e.Message); } catch { } }`. Then replace `catch { }` with `catch (Exception e) { Swallow.Note("Config.Save", e); }` file by file, smallest files first. For the four named methods go further and let the caller see the failure: in Config.Save, Swap.Restore, Hold.Release and Keep.Unpark, return false or rethrow rather than logging and carrying on, and have the button that called it say something. To stop the count growing, write tools/Check-Swallow.ps1 that counts empty catch clauses per file, compares against a checked-in tools/swallow-baseline.txt, and exits 1 when any file's count rises, then add it as a job in build.yml (entry 113).

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` and it compiles clean with the .NET Framework csc, which is the whole risk of a mechanical change like this. Then run your new tools/Check-Swallow.ps1 and confirm the number it prints matches the baseline, and that adding one deliberate `catch { }` to any file makes it exit 1. No game start is needed for any of this.

**Traps:** Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs and Hold.cs are four of the six files CLAUDE.md says need a line in #dev before editing, and they are four of the worst offenders, so this change cannot be done quietly. The launcher is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, and the Framework csc will reject all of them. Several catches are silent on purpose and say so in a comment beside them, for example Shell.cs:474 'a bad monitor layout is not worth refusing to start over'; leave those as swallows and just log. Doing this without entry 116 (no .pdb is built) means every logged exception still names no line, so the two are worth shipping together. Everything ships as one complete release under CLAUDE.md, launcher, game files and server together.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-options/errors-warnings , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/utility-modules/psscriptanalyzer/overview

## 116. No symbols are built, so the log the whole diagnostic story depends on can never name a line

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: "Compare the exe's byte length against a b[uild without it]" as the proof that adding /debug:pdbonly leaves optimisation alone.  ->  Byte length cannot prove that, and the two builds will not match anyway: /debug:pdbonly makes csc add a DEBUG directory entry to the PE and embed the absolute path of the pdb (for example C:\Thornswood-DevBuild\Launcher\Thornswood.pdb, or the runner's RUNNER_TEMP path). A length difference is expected, so the test reads as a failure when nothing is wrong. The embedded build-machine path is also worth a line in the write-up given tools/Ship-Rules.ps1 exists for what must not leave a machine. The /optimize+ claim is fine on its own: pdbonly does not disable it, and the flag goes on the right array (tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:231-236), and $exe is $Root\Launcher\Thornswood.exe (lines 157, 163) so the pdb does land where the doneWhen looks.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 225-245: the $cscArgs array (231-236) carries /noconfig, /nologo, /target:winexe, /platform:anycpu, /optimize+, /out, /win32icon and /resource, and nothing else. A grep of the whole file for 'debug' or 'pdb' returns zero hits, so there is no switch to flip and no pdb anywhere. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Shell.cs lines 441-451: the fault handler that writes Exception.ToString() to Paths.LogFile. That is the string that gains file and line numbers the moment a pdb exists, and it is the reason this is worth doing. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship-Rules.ps1, to see what is allowed to leave the machine in a release before you decide whether the pdb ships or is only kept.

**How:** In tools/Build-Launcher.ps1, add `'/debug:pdbonly'` to the $cscArgs array at line 232, beside '/optimize+'. pdbonly keeps the optimiser fully on and only emits the symbol file, which is what you want for a shipping build: csc writes Thornswood.pdb beside Thornswood.exe. Then decide where it goes. The cheap answer is to keep it: have tools/Ship.ps1 copy the pdb into a per-version folder on the shipping machine so the symbols for every released build survive. The better answer for support is to upload it as a release asset beside the exe so anyone can resolve a stack from a log. Check tools/Ship-Rules.ps1 first and add an explicit rule either way, because a file that is neither allowed nor denied is the kind of thing that ships by accident.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`, then `Get-ChildItem $env:TEMP\tsbuild\Launcher` shows Thornswood.pdb beside Thornswood.exe. Compare the exe's byte length against a build without the switch: /debug:pdbonly should leave it essentially unchanged, which proves the optimiser is still on. To prove the payoff without starting anything, write a four-line throwaway C# file that throws, compile it the same two ways, and confirm ToString() names the file and line only in the pdb build.

**Traps:** A pdb reveals your source file paths and method names to anybody who has it, so publishing it as a public release asset is a disclosure decision, not a build detail, and it belongs in #dev. /debug:full would change code generation; /debug:pdbonly does not, which is why it is the right switch here. The pdb must match the exact exe: a rebuild makes a new pdb and an old one resolves to nonsense, so it has to be versioned with the release, never left loose in a folder. CLAUDE.md requires every change to go out as one complete release; a pdb that exists on the shipping machine but is not wired into Ship.ps1 is half a change. This entry is worth pulling forward because it multiplies entry 115: logged exceptions with no line numbers are much less useful than logged exceptions with them.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-options/code-generation

## 117. No application manifest: the window is blurry on mixed DPI and the exe declares no identity

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** Marked [verified] with a howTo that sets dpiAwareness to PerMonitorV2 in a new tools/app/Thornswood.manifest. The entry's own "Where it lives" column names tools\app\Downgrade.cs:540-552 as the hand DPI work-around, and the write-up never says the manifest would invalidate it.  ->  tools/app/Downgrade.cs:532-563 ClickCommandLine computes the Steam console click position from `scale = GetDpiForWindow(steamWindow) / GetDpiForSystem()`, and the comment at 535-542 states the assumption in plain words: "Steam draws itself at its monitor's scale, and this process sees window coordinates at the system's, so the offset is scaled by the ratio of the two." That only holds while the launcher is system-DPI-aware, which is what WPF on the .NET Framework is by default. Declaring PerMonitorV2 moves GetWindowRect into unvirtualised physical coordinates, so `r.Bottom - 72 * scale` lands in the wrong place on any mixed-DPI desktop and the Steam console click silently misses. That breaks the 1.6.1170 downgrade path, which is the one thing a new tester has to get through.
