The manifest also needs the WPF per-monitor opt-in to be worth anything, and tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 writes no app.config at all (grep for supportedRuntime/configuration returns nothing), so the blur this entry set out to fix would not be fixed either.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: set "the legacy `dpiAware` to `true/PM` for ol[der Windows]", citing the Microsoft page on setting default DPI awareness.  ->  That page's own sample uses `<dpiAware>true</dpiAware>`, system awareness, as the legacy fallback beside `<dpiAwareness>PerMonitorV2</dpiAwareness>`, and its table lists `true/pm` as Per-Monitor v1, not as a system-aware fallback. Choosing true/pm makes the process per-monitor aware on Windows 8.1 and on Windows 10 before 1607 as well, where WPF on the .NET Framework does not handle WM_DPICHANGED, so the window comes out the wrong size rather than merely blurry, and Downgrade.cs's coordinate assumption breaks there too.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: run Build-Launcher.ps1, then `(Get-Item ...Thornswood.exe).VersionInfo` still reports the version resource, "proving the ...".  ->  That test cannot fail and proves nothing about the manifest. The version resource is produced from assembly attributes written into a generated source file (tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:188-216, New-Info writing AssemblyTitle/AssemblyFileVersion), not from /win32res, so /win32manifest cannot disturb it. The known csc conflict is /win32res against /win32icon and /win32manifest, and this build uses /win32icon only (line 234). A real test reads the manifest back out of the exe and checks the process DPI awareness at runtime.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 225-245: no /win32manifest anywhere, and a grep of the whole file for 'manifest' or 'dpi' returns nothing, so the exe genuinely ships with no application manifest. Lines 175 onward show it does set a version resource, so there is already a precedent for writing Windows-facing metadata here. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Downgrade.cs lines 535-556: ClickCommandLine works around the missing DPI awareness by hand, calling GetDpiForWindow and GetDpiForSystem and scaling a 72 pixel offset by the ratio, with a comment saying a Windows before 10.0.1607 gets the ratio 1. Changing the process's DPI awareness changes what those two calls return, so this code is directly coupled to the manifest. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Shell.cs lines 460-475, the window placement maths against SystemParameters.WorkArea, which is the other thing a DPI mode change moves.

**How:** Create tools/app/Thornswood.manifest as a plain XML file with an assemblyIdentity naming Thornswood, a `<trustInfo>` requestedExecutionLevel of `asInvoker` with uiAccess false, a `<compatibility>` block listing the supported Windows GUIDs including Windows 10 and 11, and an `<application><windowsSettings>` block setting `dpiAwareness` to `PerMonitorV2` and the legacy `dpiAware` to `true/PM` for older Windows. Then in tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 add one entry to the $cscArgs array at line 236: `"/win32manifest:$((Resolve-Path (Join-Path $Here 'app\Thornswood.manifest')).Path)"`. Do not also try to set awareness from code: a manifest and an API call fight each other and the manifest wins. After the manifest goes in, re-read Downgrade.cs:535-556 and confirm the scale ratio still does the right thing, because under PerMonitorV2 this process now sees per-monitor coordinates rather than system-scaled ones and the ratio may need to become 1.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`, then in PowerShell `(Get-Item $env:TEMP\tsbuild\Launcher\Thornswood.exe).VersionInfo` still reports the version resource (proving the manifest did not displace it), and `[System.Reflection.Assembly]::LoadFile()` is not needed: read the embedded manifest back with `mt.exe -inputresource:Thornswood.exe;#1 -out:check.xml` if the Windows SDK is on the machine, or simply confirm csc emitted no CS1668 or manifest warning and the file grew by roughly the manifest's size. The blurry-window half genuinely needs the launcher on screen, which is not allowed here, so leave that check to the person.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md forbids a Claude starting the launcher or the game, so the visual half of this cannot be confirmed from here; say so rather than claiming it works. The manifest changes the DPI contract for the whole process, and Downgrade.cs already hand-scales a synthesised click at a measured pixel offset into Valve's own window (entry 37); getting the manifest right and that offset wrong means the launcher types into the wrong place in somebody else's application. Entry 37 proposes replacing that code with DepotDownloader, which would remove the coupling, so check whether it has landed before you tune the ratio. csc only accepts one /win32manifest and it conflicts with /win32res, so if anybody later adds a full resource file these have to be merged. The declared identity in the manifest is also what Windows shows in the file's properties and in the SmartScreen prompt, which ties this to entry 14 (code signing); a name here that does not match the signing identity there looks worse than no name.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/sbscs/application-manifests , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/hidpi/setting-the-default-dpi-awareness-for-a-process , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winuser/nf-winuser-setprocessdpiawarenesscontext , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-options/code-generation

## 118. ui.xaml is parsed at runtime, so a broken or drifted window is found by the person, not the build

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is right, the anchors drift. Shell.cs:221-224 is exact: the ui.xaml resource stream is opened, read to a string and handed to XamlReader.Parse, and win is cast to Window. But ':471' is wrong, F<T> is a one-line helper at Shell.cs:477. '473-593 (120 lines of lookups by string)' is close but misplaced: Bind() is declared at 479 and runs to about 586 (the next doc comment starts at 588), which is roughly 108 lines, and it contains 71 F<...>("Name") lookups. 'eight dead fields' is exact and checkable: tools/control-baseline.txt lists exactly eight names, BoxAddress, BoxPort, BtnSaveServer, BtnServerStart, BtnServerStop, BtnShowServer, TxtServerState and TxtUpdateSource. Check-Controls.ps1 exists, its header (lines 1-27) describes precisely this problem and its three verdicts NEW, known and unused, and it appears nowhere in .github/workflows/build.yml, so 'not wired into CI' is verified.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "add a job to .github/workflows/build.yml that runs `.\tools\Check-Controls.ps1` and fails the build on any NEW verdict".  ->  Check-Controls.ps1 never sets an exit code. grep for `exit` in tools/Check-Controls.ps1 returns nothing; the only flow-control line is 126, `if (-not $NoPause) { [void](Read-Host 'Enter to close') }`. I ran it read-only with -NoPause: it prints the eight known names, four unused names and "Every name the C# looks up exists in ui.xaml", and $LASTEXITCODE comes back empty. A CI job that just runs it is green forever, including with a NEW verdict on screen in red. The script has to be changed to `exit 1` when $bad.Count is non-zero first, and the job has to pass -NoPause or it hits Read-Host. The howTo presents it as wiring in something that already works.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "Bind() is declared at 479 and runs to about 586 ... and it contains 71 F<...>("Name") lookups", offered as a correction of the original entry's "120 lines of lookups".  ->  71 is the number of lines, not lookups. Shell.cs:479-586 contains 127 `F<Type>("Name")` calls across 71 lines, because most lines carry two (Shell.cs:481 is `txtServer = F<TextBlock>("TxtServer"); txtPing = F<TextBlock>("TxtPing");`). The whole file has 129. The original entry's "120" was closer to the truth than this correction, and 127 is the number of typed fields the expensive half of the howTo would have to produce.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Check-Controls.ps1 lines 1-27: the header explains the failure mode better than anything else in the repo and tells you what control-baseline.txt is for. Read this before you consider compiling the XAML, because running it is most of the benefit for none of the risk. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Shell.cs lines 218-230 and 477-586: how the window is built and the 71 lookups Bind() performs. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 lines 225-245: ui.xaml goes in as an embedded resource via /resource, which is exactly what compiling the XAML would replace, and csc alone cannot do it.

**How:** Do the cheap half first and treat the rest as a separate, larger decision. Cheap half: add a job to .github/workflows/build.yml that runs `.\tools\Check-Controls.ps1` and fails the build on any NEW verdict, and keep tools/control-baseline.txt as the list of deliberately-removed controls. That turns a renamed or deleted control into a red pull request without touching a line of C#. Expensive half, only after a line in #dev: compiling the XAML means moving the launcher from a bare csc invocation to an MSBuild project with a `<Page>` item for ui.xaml, which generates a partial class with typed fields and makes every name a compile-time check. That would delete Bind() and its null guards and turn the eight names in control-baseline.txt into compile errors to remove. It also replaces Build-Launcher.ps1's whole compile step and changes what CI runs, so it is not a small change dressed as one.

**Done when:** For the cheap half: run `.\tools\Check-Controls.ps1` from the repo root and confirm it reports the eight baseline names as 'known' and nothing as NEW. Then rename one x:Name in tools/ui.xaml that Shell.cs looks up, run it again, and confirm it reports NEW and exits non-zero. Revert the rename. For the expensive half: the project builds with msbuild, produces a byte-comparable exe size, and deleting a control from ui.xaml becomes a compile error rather than a silent null. No game start is involved either way.

**Traps:** Shell.cs is on CLAUDE.md's list of six files that need a line in #dev before editing, and the expensive half rewrites hundreds of its lines. The launcher is C# 5 compiled by the .NET Framework 4 csc; an MSBuild migration is exactly where somebody quietly picks up a newer language version, so pin LangVersion to 5 if you go that way. Build-Launcher.ps1 embeds ui.xaml, the logo, the icon and three WebView2 files as resources, and Voice.cs hands the managed WebView2 assemblies back from an AssemblyResolve handler while writing the native loader out beside the exe at startup, so any build rewrite has to preserve all of that or voice breaks. control-baseline.txt is a deliberate list, not dead weight: each name is a control taken out of the UI with guarded C# left behind, so deleting the C# and deleting the baseline entry are one change, not two.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.windows.markup.xamlreader , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/xaml/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/msbuild/msbuild-reference

## 119. Build-Launcher.ps1 is an installer wearing a build script's name

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All four anchors check out, with one scoping note. Line 169-170 are `$null = Stop-Game -Root $Root` and `$null = Stop-Launcher -Root $Root`, with a comment above saying both match on full path because the multiplayer client shares the launcher's file name. Line 288 is the `# ---- setup stub` banner, so 'the seam' is exact. Within 349-413: 349-363 is the shortcut loop writing Thornswood.lnk to both the Desktop and $Root, 368-380 tells Explorer to redraw via SHChangeNotify, 381-390 runs ie4uinit, and 392-412 kills explorer.exe, deletes the icon cache databases and restarts Explorer. The scoping note: the setup stub work is at 288 to about 340, before the 349-413 range, so the entry's parenthetical groups it with lines it does not cover. The contradiction it describes is real and verified: line 21 sets `[string]$Root = 'C:\Thornswood-DevBuild'` as the default, while the shortcut loop at 349-363 unconditionally writes a Desktop shortcut named Thornswood.lnk, so every hand build points the customer-facing Desktop shortcut at the dev build.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Split at line 288 ... Everything above it (gather sources, write the version resource, build $cscArgs, run csc, build the setup stub) stays in tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 and is what CI runs. Everything below ... becomes an install step that only Ship calls."  ->  The setup stub is below the seam, not above it. Line 288 is the `# ---- setup stub` banner and the stub compile runs 289-330, so splitting at 288 moves it into the install-only half. The entry contradicts itself in one sentence, and it matters: tools/Package-Thornswood.ps1:685-689 looks for tools/setup-stub.exe and says "THIS PACKAGE HAS NO INSTALLER" if it is absent, and CI currently compiles Bootstrap.cs only as a side effect of running the whole script. The real seam for this split is line 332, the `# ---- shortcut` banner.

**Challenged (minor):** "368-380 tells Explorer to redraw via SHChangeNotify, 381-390 runs ie4uinit, and 392-412 kills explorer.exe, deletes the icon cache databases and restarts Explorer."  ->  Each range is shifted by about two lines. SHChangeNotify is 366-378 (Add-Type at 371, the call at 376, catch closing at 378); ie4uinit is 380-388; the explorer kill and cache clear is 390-413 (taskkill at 399, Start-Process explorer at 410, stamp written at 411). The 169-170 anchor, the 288 seam and the 349-363 shortcut loop are all exact, and the contradiction the entry describes is real: line 21 defaults $Root to C:\Thornswood-DevBuild while 349-363 writes the Desktop shortcut unconditionally.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 in full, 413 lines, with lines 21, 169-170, 288 and 349-412 in mind: it is the file being split and the comments record why several of these steps exist. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\.github\workflows\build.yml lines 33-39: CI calls this script with an explicit -Root, so whatever you split, CI must keep compiling. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship.ps1, the build step, because Ship is the only caller that should get the install half.

**How:** Split at line 288, the seam the entry names. Everything above it (gather sources, write the version resource, build $cscArgs, run csc, build the setup stub) stays in tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 and is what CI runs. Everything below (shortcut creation at 349-363, SHChangeNotify at 368-380, ie4uinit at 381-390, the explorer.exe kill and icon cache clear at 392-412, the stale OldMod-Launcher.ps1 cleanup at 415-416) moves into a new tools/Install-Launcher.ps1 that takes the built exe and a -Root and is called only by Ship.ps1. Move the process-killing at 169-170 with it, since a build does not need to close anything. Add `-OutDir` to Build-Launcher.ps1 so it writes to a folder and nothing else. As the interim, if the split has to wait, guard the install half with `if ($Root -eq 'C:\Thornswood') { ... }` so a hand build against the dev root stops writing a Desktop shortcut.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` on a machine with a Thornswood.lnk already on the Desktop. Afterwards: Thornswood.exe exists under $env:TEMP\tsbuild\Launcher, the Desktop shortcut's target is unchanged, explorer.exe was never restarted (its process start time is older than the build), and no Thornswood or game process was killed. Then run `.\tools\Install-Launcher.ps1` and confirm it does all of that and nothing else. Push the branch and confirm the CI job still goes green.

**Traps:** This is the file CI runs, so a mistake here turns every pull request red and, per CLAUDE.md, stops Ship at the build step. The explorer.exe kill at 392-412 is guarded by an .iconstamp file and a comment saying it is 'too rude to do on every build but exactly right on the one build where the icon changed'; that reasoning is deliberate, so move it, do not delete it. The shortcut loop also tidies away an old 'OldMod.lnk' so nobody ends up with two, which is the same rename history that makes Get-LauncherExe look for both exe names; keep that. Ship.ps1 is the only thing that should call the install half, and Ship is run by the person, never by a Claude. Everything ships as one complete release, so a split that leaves Ship calling a script that no longer exists breaks the release, not just the build.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-options/code-generation , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions

## 120. Uninstall from Windows Settings opens the whole launcher rather than an uninstaller

**Category:** Packaging  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All three anchors check out, with one line-number note. Register.cs:106-107 writes UninstallString as `"<exe>" -root "<root>" -uninstall`, and the comment explaining the choice starts at 98, not 105, so the cited 105-113 catches the tail of that comment plus the SetValue and the NoModify/NoRepair pair at 112-113. Shell.cs:198-244 is exact: askedToUninstall is declared at 203, set from the -uninstall argument at 211, and used at 239 as `ShowPage(askedToUninstall ? PageSettings : PagePlay)`, with every Build* and Refresh* call running first at 226-235. DoUninstall is at Shell.cs:3130 and the flow runs to about 3280, so 3124-3280 is right.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: on -uninstall, "build only the small window the uninstall needs, call the existing DoUninstall() path, and exit when it finishes", skipping BuildModList, RefreshDiscord and the rest.  ->  DoUninstall cannot run against a different window. tools/app/Shell.cs:3139 is `bool keep = chkKeepSaves.IsChecked == true;` with no null guard, so an uninstall window that does not carry the Settings page controls NREs on the first line after WhyNot(). Shell.cs:3269 and 3278 set `btnUninstall.IsEnabled`, also unguarded. Only chkDeleteMods is guarded (3142: `chkDeleteMods == null || ...`). The workable shape is to keep Bind() and the ui.xaml window and skip only the expensive refreshes, which is not what the howTo says.

**Challenged (minor):** "Register.cs:106-107 writes UninstallString" and (entry 121) "the reasoning is written down at Register.cs:119-123".  ->  Both are off by one. The SetValue call is Register.cs:105-106, and the EstimatedSize comment is 118-122. Everything else in these two entries is exact: the explanatory comment starts at 98, NoModify/NoRepair are at 112-113, Register.Ensure is 78-126, it is called from Shell.cs:244, askedToUninstall is declared at 203, set at 211 and used at 239, and tools/Setup-Thornswood.ps1's only registry work is the three Valve Steam keys at line 95.

**Challenged (minor):** "DoUninstall is at Shell.cs:3130 and the flow runs to about 3280, so 3124-3280 is right."  ->  DoUninstall is 3130-3286. Stopping the range at 3280 cuts off the two lines that matter most to this entry: 3285 `Application.Current.Shutdown();` and the closing brace at 3286. 3124 also sits inside DrawDevState, not DoUninstall.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Register.cs lines 78-126, especially the comment at 98-105: it argues deliberately that the Settings button should open the launcher rather than delete anything behind the customer's back, and names the two questions the uninstall asks. Do not undo that reasoning; refine it. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Shell.cs lines 203-244: everything Run() does before it reaches ShowPage, which is the cost you are trying to skip. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Uninstall.cs, for WhyNot, OurGameFiles, Measure, Scratch, Begin and Finish, the actual work an uninstall does.

**How:** In tools/app/Shell.cs Run(), parse -uninstall as it already does at 211, then branch before the expensive setup. When askedToUninstall is true, skip the update check, the presence work, BuildModList, RefreshDiscord and the two second update timer, build only the small window the uninstall needs, call the existing DoUninstall() path, and exit when it finishes. Keep Register.Ensure(cfg) out of the uninstall branch too, since re-registering on the way out is pointless. Concretely: hoist the argument loop above Paths.Init if you need to, add `if (askedToUninstall) { RunUninstallOnly(); return; }` and write RunUninstallOnly as a small method that parses ui.xaml, binds only the controls DoUninstall touches, and calls it. C# 5 only, so no expression-bodied members and no string interpolation.

**Done when:** You cannot start the launcher here, so prove it statically and hand the run to the person. Confirm `Thornswood.exe -root C:\Thornswood -uninstall` is what the registry says by reading it back: `Get-ItemProperty 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*' | Where-Object DisplayName -eq 'Thornswood' | Select-Object UninstallString`. Confirm the build compiles with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`. Then the person runs the exe with -uninstall once and confirms no update check appears in Profile\launcher.log for that run, which is a checkable line in a file rather than a claim.

**Traps:** Shell.cs needs a line in #dev before editing per CLAUDE.md. The comment at Register.cs:98-105 records a settled decision that Windows' uninstall button must not delete anything silently, because the uninstall asks whether to keep the game files and whether to keep the characters and puts the Windows Skyrim folders back first; a 'one small window' must still ask both. Register.cs also sets NoModify and NoRepair deliberately, with the reason written down, so do not add Modify or Repair buttons. The launcher replaces itself in place on update rather than being reinstalled, so an install made before the old mod rename still carries the old exe name and the uninstall path has to tolerate both. Everything ships as one complete release.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/uninstall-registry-key

## 121. Installed apps lists Thornswood only after the first launch, and without a size

**Category:** Packaging  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both halves check out exactly. Register.Ensure is declared at Register.cs:78 and its try/catch closes at 126, so 78-126 is precise; it is called from Shell.cs:244 with the comment 'Done here rather than in the installer so that every install that already exists gets one on its next launch', which is the reason the entry describes. tools/Setup-Thornswood.ps1 writes no Uninstall key: its only registry work is reading three Valve Steam keys at line 95, verified by grepping the file for HKCU, HKLM and 'registry'. One thing the entry does not say and whoever picks this up must know: the missing size is deliberate and the reasoning is written down at Register.cs:119-123, which says EstimatedSize is in KB, Windows shows it in the Size column, and working it out means walking thirty thousand files on every launch, so 'an empty column is honest; a number invented once and left to rot is not'.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Register.cs lines 78-126, with the EstimatedSize comment at 119-123 read carefully: it is a settled argument against exactly the fix the entry proposes, unless the number comes from the installer rather than from a walk. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1, the section that moves the payload into place, because that is the one moment a true size is already known for free. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs lines 716-812 and 979-1026, the stub that downloads and unpacks the payload, which is the other candidate for writing the key.

**How:** Have the installer write the same key the launcher writes, under the same hashed subkey name so the launcher updates that row rather than adding a second. Read Register.cs's KeyName() and Root to get the exact subkey, then in tools/Setup-Thornswood.ps1 (or Bootstrap.cs, whichever finishes the install) write DisplayName, DisplayVersion, Publisher, InstallLocation, DisplayIcon, UninstallString, NoModify and NoRepair with the same values, plus EstimatedSize as a DWord in KB taken from the size of the payload it has just moved, which it already knows and does not have to walk for. Leave Register.Ensure alone except for one change: have it write EstimatedSize only when the value is absent, so the launcher never overwrites the installer's honest number with nothing. In PowerShell the key is under HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\<hashed name>.

**Done when:** Without installing anything, prove the subkey name matches: print Register.cs's KeyName() logic and the name your installer code builds, side by side, and confirm they are identical for the same root. Then, on a throwaway machine or a copy, run the installer and check `Get-ItemProperty 'HKCU:\Software\Microsoft\Windows\CurrentVersion\Uninstall\*' | Where-Object DisplayName -eq 'Thornswood'` returns exactly one row with a non-zero EstimatedSize and a working UninstallString, and that launching the launcher afterwards still leaves exactly one row.

**Traps:** The 'same hashed name' requirement is the whole risk: get it wrong and Windows shows Thornswood twice, one row from the installer and one from the launcher, which is worse than the current behaviour. Register.cs:119-123 is a written decision against inventing a size; the only defensible number is one the installer measured, so do not add a file walk to the launcher's startup path. Register.cs is not on CLAUDE.md's protected list but Shell.cs, which calls it at 244, is. The launcher is C# 5. This has to ship as one complete release: an installer that writes a key the launcher's KeyName() does not agree with is a half change, and CLAUDE.md forbids shipping a half. Entry 120 touches the same UninstallString value, so do the two together or the second one will rewrite the first.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/msi/uninstall-registry-key

## 122. The public site, the installer host and the game's identity door are one process, with one instance, and it is production

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both numbers check out. tools/docs-app/server.js is exactly 1,510 lines. Route count: 25 dispatch conditionals inside the single http.createServer handler at line 740, two of which match two paths each ('/download' or '/install' at 958, '/api/allowed' or '/dev/api/allowed' at 1308-1309), giving 27 routes. It is a hand-written node http server, not express, so the routes are a flat run of `if (p === ...)` and `if (route === ...)` checks. tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1 has one $Vps parameter with a single hardcoded default and no staging path anywhere in the file, verified by grepping for 'staging'. Note the overlap with entry 135: 122 splits the site by blast radius, 135 splits the box by tenant, and the payload-to-object-storage step appears in both. They should be sequenced, not done twice.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\server.js, at least lines 740-760 (the handler entry and the try-less dispatch, which is entry 13) and the route list, so you can see which of the 27 routes are public pages, which serve the payload, and which are the identity door (/api/play/join at 1243, /api/allowed at 1309, /api/play/owns at 1356, /api/play/who at 1403). Those four are the ones that must never be down. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Deploy-Docs.ps1 lines 1-60 and 130-185: the header records that documents are deliberately not overwritten by a redeploy and that -SeedDocs has thrown away an evening of edits before. Any split has to preserve that. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md lines 96-138, the only restore instruction, which names the site's data folder as the thing that matters most.

**How:** Three pieces, in increasing order of risk. (a) Static pages: everything under tools/docs-app/public and the markdown under data/site becomes files served by Caddy's file_server or a CDN, which gives Range, ETag and conditional requests for free. Caddy is already the TLS terminator on the box (tools/Setup-Domain.ps1), so this is a Caddyfile block, not a new dependency. (b) Payload: move the installer download off the node process entirely, which is entry 54 and shares this work. (c) Identity API: lift the four /api/play/* and /api/allowed routes into their own small node service on its own port behind Caddy, with a /health route, so the thing the game depends on is not the thing serving half-gigabyte downloads. Before any of it, add a documented one-line local run to tools/docs-app (for example `node server.js` with a sample config) and a README, so a change can be seen working before it reaches production; that is entry 141's work and it makes this entry safe.

**Done when:** Locally, with the site running from tools/docs-app on a spare port, `curl -i http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/play/who` still answers after you have deliberately made a public page route throw, which proves the blast radii are separated. On the box, after the split, `curl -I https://thornswood.net/` is served by Caddy with a Cache-Control and an ETag, while the identity service answers its own /health on loopback. Both checks are read-only HTTP and need no game.

**Traps:** Everything that touches the VPS is run by the person, never by a Claude: Deploy-Docs.ps1, Publish-*, Request-Restart and the rest are all on CLAUDE.md's list. Deploy-Docs.ps1 -SeedDocs flattens every document with the repo copy and has destroyed a day's edits before; its own header says so, and the site's data folder holds the linked Discord accounts and every document anyone has typed. Any restructuring that moves data/ has to preserve that folder byte for byte, and there is no restore script to fall back on (entry 134). The site is also the game's login: taking it down to restructure it logs everybody out, so this is a maintenance-window change and, under CLAUDE.md, part of one complete release with whatever client change goes with it. Caddy is currently an unwatched scheduled task with a three-day execution time limit (entry 21), so moving more onto Caddy without fixing that moves the risk rather than removing it.

**Links:** https://nodejs.org/api/http.html , https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/file_server , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Headers/Cache-Control

## 123. Content pages are rendered in the browser from another company's CDN, with no integrity check

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is right, two of the three citations are off. home.html:21-24 is exact: preconnect to fonts.googleapis.com at 21 and fonts.gstatic.com at 22, the marked 12.0.2 script from cdnjs.cloudflare.com at 23, the Google Fonts stylesheet at 24. index.html is 10-13, not 11-13: preconnect at 10 and 11, the Google Fonts stylesheet at 12, the cdnjs marked script at 13. '613-621 (the page fetches and renders)' is wrong; 613-625 is the og:description BLURB table and a small meta rewrite. The actual fetch-and-render is home.html:654-657, `fetch('/api/page/' + here.slice(1))` then `show(marked.parse(d.md))`, with further marked.parse calls at 669, and index.html renders the same way at 554-555, 670, 910, 1182 with marked.setOptions at 1580. 'no integrity check' is verified: grepping all of tools/docs-app/public for `integrity=` returns nothing, so neither third-party asset carries a Subresource Integrity hash.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\public\home.html lines 21-24 and 640-700: the two third-party loads and the client-side render loop they exist for. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\server.js around 1128 and the PAGES table: the server already rewrites a template per route, which is the hook a server-side render plugs into, so this is less work than it looks. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\data\site\ (classes.md, credits.md, join.md, rules.md, support.md, world.md): the six documents that would be rendered, and the ones customers are pointed at.

**How:** Two steps, and the first one is minutes. (a) Self-host: download marked.min.js 12.0.2 once, put it at tools/docs-app/public/marked-12.0.2.min.js, and change home.html:23 and index.html:13 to `<script src="/marked-12.0.2.min.js"></script>`. Do the same for the two font faces (Cinzel and Spectral) by saving the woff2 files under public/ and replacing the Google Fonts stylesheet at home.html:24 and index.html:12 with a local @font-face block. Deploy-Docs.ps1 already sends everything in public/ by listing the folder rather than a named list (see its comment at lines 137-139), so new files go up with no script change. (b) Render on the server: in server.js, where the PAGES route already rewrites the template near 1128, read the markdown, run it through marked on the server, sanitise the result, and substitute it into the template so the page arrives as HTML. Keep the client-side path as a fallback only if you must. Sanitise with a maintained sanitiser rather than a regex, because the markdown is editable from the site.

**Done when:** Locally: run the site, then `curl -s http://127.0.0.1:<port>/rules | Select-String '<h2'` returns real headings, proving the page is HTML on arrival rather than a shell. Block cdnjs.cloudflare.com and fonts.googleapis.com in your hosts file, reload the page, and the rules text and the fonts are still right. `Select-String -Path tools/docs-app/public/*.html -Pattern 'cdnjs|fonts.googleapis'` returns nothing. None of this needs the game.

**Traps:** The public markdown under data/site is editable from the site itself, so server-side rendering turns 'somebody typed into a document' into 'somebody wrote HTML on the public front page' unless you sanitise; that is the one genuinely new risk this change introduces. The site's own hub is admin-gated and entry 77 deletes it, so do not spend effort making index.html pretty. Every response currently says Cache-Control: no-store (server.js:464-471), which is entry 82; self-hosting assets is only worth it alongside real cache headers or you have swapped one round trip for another. Deploying the site is a person's job under CLAUDE.md, not a Claude's. The site is also the game's identity door, so a server.js change here ships with everything else as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/markedjs/marked , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/Security/Subresource_Integrity , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Headers/Cache-Control

## 124. Two session shapes are off inside an otherwise sound auth layer

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** One of the three claims is anchored wrongly and one needs narrowing. The bot key in a query string is verified and is the part that survives entry 77: server.js:1311 reads `url.searchParams.get('key')` and compares it against cfg.botKey, and the comment at 1297 says the game server's door asks for this every five minutes with the bot key, so the secret lands in any access log or proxy log in front of it. '449-455 (no expiry inside the signed cookie)' is wrong: 449-455 is the tail of who()'s return object and the two-line `allowed` helper. The finding itself is true but lives elsewhere: the Discord cookie is minted at server.js:884 as `me.id + '|' + name + '|' + sign(me.id + '|' + name)`, with no timestamp and nothing else in the signed payload; it is verified at 414-421 with no expiry check; and it is set with Max-Age 30 days at 898, which is a browser hint only, so a copied cookie value is good forever. '819-829 (no per-attempt sign-in state)' is roughly right: /auth/discord is at 817 and the OAuth state is built at 831-832 as base64url(back + NUL + port + NUL + nonce) plus a signature. It is signed, and the launcher path does supply a per-attempt nonce, so the gap is narrower than 'no state': for a plain browser sign-in, back is '/', port is empty and nonce is empty, so the state is a constant value that never changes and cannot distinguish one attempt from another.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\server.js lines 385-421 (sign, cookies, the member token and the Discord cookie reader) and 817-912 (the whole OAuth round trip, including the nonce comment at 826-830 explaining why the launcher's nonce exists). Read these before changing anything, because the launcher half of the flow depends on the exact shape. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\docs-app\server.js lines 1290-1330, the /api/allowed door, for how the key travels today. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\gamemode.js around 2306-2308 and 2406, the side that sends the key, since both ends change together.

**How:** Take the key out of the query string first, because it is the only part that survives entry 77 deleting the hub. In server.js at 1311, accept the key from a request header instead, for example `req.headers['x-thornswood-key']`, keeping the searchParams read as a fallback for exactly one release so an old game server keeps working, then delete the fallback. On the other side, change tools/gamemode.js where it builds the /api/allowed request to send the header rather than append ?key=. Do this in the same change as entry 2, which rotates the key and moves door.json anyway. For the cookie, add an issued-at stamp inside the signed payload at 884 (`me.id + '|' + name + '|' + issuedAt`, signed over all three) and reject anything older than the window at 414-421. For the sign-in state, generate a random value per attempt at 831, store it briefly server side or in a short-lived HttpOnly cookie, and require it to match on the callback at 845.

**Done when:** Locally, with the site on a spare port: `curl -i 'http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/allowed?key=<the key>'` returns 401 and `curl -i -H 'X-Thornswood-Key: <the key>' http://127.0.0.1:<port>/api/allowed` returns 200, proving the move. For the cookie, hand-build a cookie value with an issued-at older than the window and confirm the site treats you as signed out. For the state, replay a previously used state value on /auth/discord/callback and confirm it is refused. All three are curl checks with no game.

**Traps:** Never print, paste or log the key while testing; it lives in the site's config.json on the VPS and in door.json on both game servers, and it is entry 2's secret to rotate. Changing the key's transport touches both the site and tools/gamemode.js, which means the site deploy and the gamemode deploy have to go out together, and CLAUDE.md requires one complete release, launcher, game files and server, never a half. Entry 77 deletes the hub, which removes most of the cookie and sign-in-state surface, so check whether it has landed before spending effort there; this entry says as much itself. Entry 62 is about the same standing() lookup failing and must not be undone by a change here. The launcher catches the OAuth ticket on a loopback port and the signed nonce is what proves the ticket belongs to the button its owner pressed (the comment at 826-830 says so), so do not simplify the state shape without reading tools/app/Discord.cs on the other side.

**Links:** https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cross-Site_Request_Forgery_Prevention_Cheat_Sheet.html , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html

## 125. The bot runs as SYSTEM under a hand-written loop, stopped by matching a command line, with a log that never rotates

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All three anchors are exact. Install-Bot.ps1:166-171 stops the bot by listing Win32_Process and matching `$_.Name -like 'python*' -and $_.CommandLine -like '*Thornswood-Bot*'`, with a comment saying it is done by command line 'rather than by task, because a bot started by hand once is still a bot holding the token and answering tickets'. Lines 360-367 are the wrapper: `while ($true) { & cmd /c "python bot.py >> bot.log 2>&1"; Start-Sleep -Seconds 15 }`, with no failure count and no backoff. Line 388 is `Register-ScheduledTask ... -User 'SYSTEM' -RunLevel Highest`. The log never rotates because of the `>>` at 366. tools/Show-BotLog.ps1 exists. Overlap worth flagging: entry 69 targets the same loop at Install-Bot.ps1:360-388 and asks for a failure count and a webhook, and entry 131 asks for rotation on the same bot.log. Replacing the loop with a service does 69's half and 131's bot half at once, so these three should be one piece of work or two of them will be written twice.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Install-Bot.ps1 lines 160-175 (how it stops), 300-320 (how it writes the site's config.json) and 350-392 (the wrapper and the task registration): the whole install is in this one file and its comments record why the pipeline was replaced by cmd redirection on 2026-09-16. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\discord\bot.py lines 100-140, so you know what the process does on startup and what the log lines look like. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 168-177, the secrets table, because moving the bot to a low-privilege account means moving the ACL on bot.env.json with it.

**How:** Replace the run.ps1 loop and the scheduled task with a real service. With WinSW: drop WinSW.exe and a ThornswoodBot.xml beside C:\Thornswood-Bot, with `<executable>python</executable>`, `<arguments>bot.py</arguments>`, `<workingdirectory>` set, `<log mode="roll-by-size">` with a sizeThreshold and keepFiles, `<onfailure action="restart" delay="10 sec"/>` repeated with growing delays, and `<serviceaccount>` naming a dedicated low-privilege local account. With NSSM the same shape comes from `nssm install`, `nssm set ThornswoodBot AppStdout`, `AppStderr`, `AppRotateFiles 1`, `AppRotateBytes`, `AppThrottle` and `ObjectName`. Then in tools/Install-Bot.ps1: delete the run.ps1 heredoc and the Register-ScheduledTask block at 380-388, replace the command-line-matching stop at 166-171 with `Stop-Service ThornswoodBot`, and change the ACL block (which today grants SYSTEM and Administrators) to grant the new service account read on bot.env.json instead of SYSTEM. Create the account with a strong password stored where the other VPS credentials are, named by location only.

**Done when:** On the VPS, run by the person: `Get-Service ThornswoodBot` reports Running; `Get-Process python | Select-Object Id, UserName` shows the bot running as the new account and not as SYSTEM; killing the python process and waiting shows the service restarting it and the restart appearing in the Windows event log rather than nowhere; `Get-ChildItem C:\Thornswood-Bot\*.log*` shows a rolled file appearing once the threshold is passed; and `Get-Acl C:\Thornswood-Bot\bot.env.json` lists the service account and no longer needs SYSTEM. In the repo, before any of that, `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile()` on the edited Install-Bot.ps1 reports no errors, which is exactly what CI checks.

**Traps:** Install-Bot.ps1 talks to the VPS, so the person runs it, never a Claude. The ACL comment at the end of the config block warns that taking SYSTEM off bot.env.json is how the bot once ended up starting with no token and exiting immediately, so change the ACL and the service account in the same step or you will reproduce that. The cmd redirection at 366 is itself a fix: piping through PowerShell and Out-File re-encoded the text and left bot.log unreadable past a point, and a slow pipeline could hold the bot's own print() calls on its event loop; whatever replaces it must not reintroduce a pipeline. Killing by command line exists because a bot started by hand still holds the token; a Stop-Service alone will not catch a hand-started copy, so keep a one-off sweep. The bot token and the site's bot key are both written by this script (entry 126), so a rewrite here is the natural place to make rotation one command, but never print either value. Entries 69 and 131 overlap this one; agree in #dev which one owns the loop before anybody starts.

**Links:** https://nssm.cc/usage , https://github.com/winsw/winsw , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/register-scheduledtask , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/process-security-and-access-rights , https://12factor.net/logs

## 126. One bot token lives in two files and a backup zip, and rotating it is two scripts nothing ties together

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every anchor checks out and so does the premise of the proposed fix. Install-Bot.ps1 reads the token as a SecureString at 221-226, packs it with a freshly generated 32 character key into a base64 payload at 241-243, and writes bot.env.json on the VPS with an ACL protected to SYSTEM and Administrators; the cited 219-303 covers that span. Set-DiscordLogin.ps1:244 is `if ($botToken) { $d.botToken = $botToken }` and 270-273 merges the patch into the site's config.json, so the same token is written to a second file by a second script. Backup-Server.ps1:33 says in the header 'The zip has the bot token, the Discord client secret and the server password in it, because a backup without them is not a backup', and 246-248 repeats it on screen, so the third copy is real and deliberate. The fix's premise is verified: Install-Bot.ps1:311-317 already reaches into $DocsRoot\config.json and sets `$cfg['botKey']`, so the script that writes the bot key into the site's config could write the token there too.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Install-Bot.ps1 lines 215-320: the token prompt, the base64 payload, the ACL on bot.env.json, and the block at 305-320 that already writes into the site's config.json. This is where a single rotation command would live. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 lines 240-280: the second writer, and note it also base64s the payload so neither secret is ever a word on a command line, which is the pattern to keep. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 168-177, the secrets table, which is the document that has to end up telling the truth (see also entry 138).

**How:** Write one tools/Rotate-BotToken.ps1 that does the whole job: prompt for the new token as a SecureString (never a parameter, never a command-line word), base64 the payload as both existing scripts already do, write bot.env.json on the VPS, write discord.botToken into C:\Thornswood-Docs\config.json using the read-set-write merge that Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 already does at 265-276, then restart the bot and the site and report which consumers were updated. Have Install-Bot.ps1 and Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 call into a shared helper for the write rather than each keeping a copy, which is the same shared-helper shape entries 22 and 23 want for ssh. Separately, move tools/discord/news-webhook.txt and dev-webhook.txt and the backup zips out of the repository folder to a path named in the secrets table, so a zip-it-and-send-it can never carry them. Name every secret by location only, in the script's output as well as in the documents.

**Done when:** Run the new script against the dev instance first. Afterwards, on the VPS: the bot's log shows it reconnecting as the same application, `Get-Content C:\Thornswood-Docs\config.json` parses and has a discord.botToken present (check presence, never print the value), and the site's Discord-dependent routes answer. Prove the no-secrets-on-the-wire property statically: `Select-String -Path tools/Rotate-BotToken.ps1 -Pattern '\$token' ` shows it only ever inside the base64 payload and never in an ssh argument. In the repo, the script parses under the same PowerShell parser CI uses, and `git ls-files tools/discord` no longer lists any webhook file.

**Traps:** Rotating a token is a live operation against Discord and the VPS, so the person runs it, not a Claude, and CLAUDE.md is explicit that secrets are named by location only and never printed, pasted, committed or logged. A rotated token that reaches bot.env.json but not the site's config.json leaves the site's Discord lookups failing, which is exactly the shape of entry 62 (every launcher being told they are not in the Discord); rotate both or neither. Install-Bot.ps1 restarts the bot as part of its run, so a rotation during an evening throws whoever is mid-ticket. The backup zip deliberately contains the secrets, so after a rotation every older zip is a copy of a dead token and a live client secret; decide in #dev how long zips are kept. Entry 22 notes that these scripts use StrictHostKeyChecking=no, so on a hostile network a machine answering as the VPS receives whatever this script sends; fixing the ssh helper first makes this safe.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/procthread/process-security-and-access-rights

## 127. on\_ready re-registers views, re-syncs commands and re-edits the panel on every reconnect

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The cited span is right to within a line or two. `@client.event` is at bot.py:779 and `async def on_ready():` at 780. `client.add_view(TicketPanel())` and `client.add_view(TicketTools())` are at 781-782, with comments saying they exist so the panel keeps working after a restart and so Close works on every old ticket. The guild command copy and sync loop is at 796-801 (`tree.copy_global_to(guild=g)` then `await tree.sync(guild=g)`), and the global clear at 805-807. `for g in client.guilds: await ensure_panel(g)` is at 811-812. ensure_panel itself is at 717 and its unconditional edit is at 770, `await m.edit(content=PANEL, view=TicketPanel())`. So all three behaviours the entry names happen on every on_ready.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\discord\bot.py lines 717-777, ensure_panel, especially the comment at 764-769: it records that leaving the old panel message alone is exactly why a ticket kind added later never appeared for anybody, because a dropdown's options live in the posted message and not in the View this process builds. That comment is the reason the edit is unconditional and it must survive the fix. 2. bot.py lines 779-815, on_ready, including the comment at 785-794 explaining why commands are copied to the guild and the global list cleared afterwards, and why clearing globals is done last. 3. https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html on setup_hook versus on_ready, so you know which hook runs once.

**How:** Three separate moves in tools/discord/bot.py. (a) Views: move the two `client.add_view` calls out of on_ready and into `async def setup_hook(self)` on the client, which discord.py runs once before the first connect; persistent views only need registering once per process. (b) Commands: guard the copy_global_to and sync block behind a flag, for example an environment variable THORNSWOOD_SYNC_COMMANDS or a file stamp holding a hash of the command names, so a reconnect does not re-sync. Keep the ordering comment's rule: clear globals last. (c) Panel: in ensure_panel, compare before editing. The message you find already has `m.content`, so `if m.content == PANEL and <the view's option labels match>: log('panel already current'); return` before the `await m.edit(...)`. Compare the dropdown option labels too, or you reintroduce the exact bug the comment at 764-769 records. Wrap each sync in the existing try/except so a rate-limited sync still logs rather than killing startup.

**Done when:** Run the bot against the dev Discord, not the live one. Start it, stop it, start it again, and read bot.log: the second start prints 'panel already current' rather than 'ticket panel updated', prints no '/close is registered' line unless the flag is set, and Discord's audit log shows no message edit for the second start. Then add a ticket kind to the panel's options and restart: the panel is edited exactly once and the new kind appears in the dropdown, which proves the guard did not reintroduce the old bug. In the repo, `python -m compileall -q tools/discord` passes.

**Traps:** The unconditional edit is deliberate and the reason is written in the file; a naive `if m.content == PANEL: return` compares only the text and misses a changed dropdown, which is precisely the failure the comment describes. on_ready fires on every full reconnect, not on every RESUME, so the symptom is bursty rather than constant and a short test may not show it. Commands synced to a guild are immediate but a global sync can take an hour and reach people unevenly, which the comment says looks exactly like one person's Discord being broken, so do not 'simplify' by going global. The bot is restarted by a wrapper loop every fifteen seconds on failure (entry 125), so a crash in setup_hook means a tight restart loop hammering Discord's identify limit; test on dev. Install-Bot.ps1 deploys bot.py to the VPS and is run by the person, never a Claude.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html#discord.Client.setup_hook , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html , https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits

## 128. Publish-Discord has no memory of what it announced, so a re-run posts a release twice

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every anchor is right. Publish-Discord.ps1's own header at 15-18 says it 'always posts whatever is newest in the feed and has no idea it has posted it before', and tells anybody trying the script out to use -DryRun, so the finding is the script's own documented behaviour. Lines 89-104 resolve the webhook: $hookName picks dev-webhook.txt or news-webhook.txt, the file is read from tools/discord, and a missing file returns quietly. Lines 293-299 are the POST and its catch, which writes 'could not post to Discord' in DarkGray and swallows the failure. Ship.ps1 calls it at 574-588 inside `if (-not $DryRun)`, branching on -Dev, and catches the failure with a DarkGray line of its own, so a failed announcement is twice invisible.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Publish-Discord.ps1 lines 1-30 and 280-300: the header states the defect in plain words and the tail is where the state file has to be written. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship.ps1 lines 565-590: the caller, and the -Dev branch, since dev and main announce through different webhooks and need separate state. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship-Rules.ps1: the state file must not ship, so check the rules before choosing where it lives.

**How:** Add a small state file, for example tools/discord/announced.json, holding `{ "main": "1.1.47", "dev": "1.1.47.3" }`. In Publish-Discord.ps1, after resolving the version it is about to announce and before the POST at 294, read that file and return early with a plain line ('1.1.47 was already announced to #news, use -Force to repeat') when the version matches the channel's last announced value. Write the file only after Invoke-RestMethod returns without throwing, which is the same rule entry 28 asks of Publish-Update. Add a `[switch]$Force` to the param block to override. In Ship.ps1, change the catch at 583 from a DarkGray line to a Yellow one and add the failure to whatever end-of-run summary already collects yellow lines, so a release that shipped but was not announced says so. Make sure announced.json is covered by tools/Ship-Rules.ps1 or .gitignore, since it is machine state, not source.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Publish-Discord.ps1 -DryRun` twice against a test webhook: the first run says it would post, the second says the version was already announced, and `-Force -DryRun` says it would post again. Delete tools/discord/announced.json and confirm the first run treats it as absent rather than throwing. Confirm the file does not appear in `git status` and is not picked up by Ship-Rules. The script parses under the same parser CI uses. No game or VPS is involved.

**Traps:** Publish-Discord.ps1 is named in CLAUDE.md's list of scripts the person runs, because it posts to a live Discord; a Claude shows the command. The webhook itself lives in tools/discord/news-webhook.txt on a shipping machine and is git-ignored, and CLAUDE.md forbids printing or pasting it, so never echo the resolved $Webhook while debugging. Dev and main announce to different channels through different webhook files and the dev feed is shared between both machines, with the last dev Ship winning; keep the two versions in separate keys or a dev run will suppress a main announcement. The embed and the ordering are already right and the entry says so, so do not rewrite them while you are in there, and CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule applies. Ship.ps1 commits and pushes source on a main release, so a state file left tracked would be committed into the release commit, which is exactly the accident entry 29 describes.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/topics/rate-limits

## 129. The stranger waits on the site's timeout after pressing Submit

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both spans are right. bot.py:564-565 is `await push_async(self.kind, '%s: %s' % (...), detail, str(who), who.id)` and it sits immediately before the three `interaction.followup.send(..., ephemeral=True)` branches at 567-580, so the acknowledgement genuinely waits on the site call. push() is defined at 112 and its network work is at 132-133, `with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=12) as r`, so the worst case wait is the full twelve seconds plus DNS and connect. push_async at 138-140 hands push to a thread pool executor, so the event loop is not blocked, but the interaction handler still awaits it, which is what the person feels. The 'two blocks swapped' fix is accurate.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\discord\bot.py lines 500-582: the whole ticket submission path, including the thread creation, the fall-back to the channel at 540-551, and the three different acknowledgement texts. Read all of it, because entry 63 also rewrites this exact region and the two changes will collide. 2. bot.py lines 108-141: push, push_async, and the comment at 113-115 saying a failure here must never lose the ticket because the Discord thread is the record and the site push is the convenience. That comment is the licence to move the call after the acknowledgement. 3. https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/interactions/api.html on followup and the three second interaction deadline.

**How:** In tools/discord/bot.py, move the `await push_async(...)` call at 564-565 to after the if/elif/else block that ends at 580. Better still, do not await it at all: replace it with `asyncio.create_task(push_async(...))` and keep a reference so the task is not garbage collected, since push_async already swallows its own errors and logs them. The acknowledgement then goes out as soon as the thread or the channel message exists, which is what the person is waiting to hear. Leave the ordering of posted, fell_back and the DM fallback exactly as it is. Nothing else in the function needs to change, and the site copy still lands because push runs in the executor either way.

**Done when:** Against a dev Discord: submit a ticket with the site deliberately unreachable (point SITE at a port nothing listens on). The ephemeral 'Opened ...' reply appears immediately rather than after twelve seconds, and bot.log afterwards contains a 'site push failed' line, proving the push still ran and still reported. Time it with a stopwatch if you want a number. In the repo, `python -m compileall -q tools/discord` passes and `git diff` shows only the moved block.

**Traps:** Entry 63 rewrites the same fall-back region (bot.py:531-551 and 571-575) to fail closed into the staff-only log channel instead of the public tickets channel, and that is a safety fix with higher priority than this one. Agree in #dev which lands first, or one will silently revert part of the other, and CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule means they should be two branches that know about each other. Discord gives three seconds to acknowledge an interaction before it errors, so if the handler is not already deferred, moving work around can expose that; check for the defer call above line 500 before assuming there is slack. The comment at bot.py:113-115 is the design statement that the Discord thread, not the site, is the record; do not invert that while reordering. bot.py is deployed by tools/Install-Bot.ps1, which the person runs.

**Links:** https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/interactions/api.html#discord.Interaction.followup , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/api.html

## 130. gamemode.js is six programs concatenated into one 3,363 line file, with no version, no lint and no tests

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Almost all of it is exact and one detail is not. tools/gamemode.js is 3,363 lines, verified exactly, and 218,536 bytes. The two inlined JSON lines are line 23 at 73,325 characters and line 616 at 6,760 characters, together 80,085 bytes, which is 36.6 percent of the file, so '37 percent of the bytes are two lines of inlined JSON' is verified. The main body does run to 1863 and there are exactly six appended sections, each wrapped in its own IIFE and fenced by begin and end banners: thornswood voice feed 1865-2031, thornswood character roster 2032-2272, thornswood door 2274-2487, thornswood server manifest 2489-2643, thornswood gear check 2645-2770, thornswood chat commands 2773-3363. The wrong detail: only ONE banner names the script that added it, `// ==== thornswood voice feed, added by Add-VoiceFeed.ps1 ====` at 1865. The other five name only the section, so a stranger cannot tell from the file which script wrote them. 'no version, no lint and no tests' is verified: no version or build constant anywhere in the file, no tools/package.json, no eslint config, and no test files in the repository.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\gamemode.js lines 1-100: the header and the log() function, including the logBroken flag at 81 and 87 that disables logging permanently after one failed write (that is entry 131). Then skim the six banner pairs listed above so the shape is clear before you touch anything. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Add-VoiceFeed.ps1 and the other Deploy-Gamemode/Publish-Server scripts, because the appended sections exist because scripts append them, and a bundler has to replace that mechanism, not sit beside it. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1, which is what puts this file on the VPS and which already computes the load order header logic entry 39 wants to reuse.

**How:** Move the source into tools/gamemode/ as modules, one per existing section plus the main body, and add one bundler command that emits the single tools/gamemode.js the SkyMP loader needs. esbuild is the smallest option: `npx esbuild tools/gamemode/index.js --bundle --platform=node --format=cjs --outfile=tools/gamemode.js`. Pull the two giant JSON lines out into tools/gamemode/data/*.json and import them, which alone removes 37 percent of the file from view. Turn each IIFE into a named exported function called from index.js. Delete Add-VoiceFeed.ps1's append behaviour once the section it appends is a module. Add a version: stamp the release version and the git short sha into a constant at bundle time and log it on load, which is also half of entry 34. Add an eslint config and a `node --check` job in CI (entry 113). Do not start refactoring behaviour while you are moving files; move first, then change.

**Done when:** The bundler's output is byte-comparable in behaviour to the current file: load both into a Node vm with a stubbed `mp` object and confirm the same set of properties and handlers is registered, which the audit says a reviewer already did successfully with all 3,363 lines and no refactor. Concretely, `node -e "const vm=require('vm'); ..."` loading each file with the same stub and printing a sorted list of registered hook names gives identical output. Then `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and the emitted file still contains the six sections' behaviour. No game start is needed and no deploy.

**Traps:** This file is the server, so a mistake is a live outage for everybody in the world, and only the person deploys it (Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 and Publish-Server.ps1 are on CLAUDE.md's list). The gamemode carries no build or protocol number today (entry 34), so there is nothing to refuse a mismatched client with, which means a gamemode change and the launcher it pairs with must go out as one complete release, never a half. Every identity, door and chargen fix still ahead lands in this file, so a move that conflicts with entries 5, 15, 43, 45 and 71 will cost more to merge than it saves; sequence it in #dev. The dev gamemode currently hardcodes the live voice port (entry 1), so any refactor that touches the port constants must fix that at the same time rather than copying it into six modules. SkyMP's loader wants a single file, which is why the bundler is required rather than optional.

**Links:** https://esbuild.github.io/api/ , https://rollupjs.org/introduction/ , https://nodejs.org/api/test.html

## 131. Nothing rotates, so last night's crash is at the end of a file that grows forever

**Category:** Operations  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Four of the five citations support the claim and the fifth contradicts it. Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1:152 builds `cmd /c "node dist_back/skymp5-server.js >> server.log 2>&1"`, Install-Voice.ps1:167 builds the same shape for voice.log, and Install-Bot.ps1:366 the same for bot.log, all appending forever with nothing trimming them, so those three are verified. gamemode.js: the claim is right but the cite points at the write. The append is at line 85; the permanent give-up is the `if (logBroken) { return; }` at 81 and the `logBroken = true` at 87, whose comment says 'Once. A log that throws every tick is worse than no log.' The fifth is wrong: Watch-Services.ps1:146-154 is the one place that DOES bound its files. It reads $log and $hist and, when either passes 2000 lines, rewrites it with the last 1000. Listing it under 'nothing rotates' is misleading. It discards rather than rotates, which is a smaller and different problem. Note the overlap: entry 25 already asks for exactly this rotation on voice.log and the site log ('voice.log becomes voice.log.1, keep five'), and entry 125 asks for it on bot.log as part of moving the bot to a service. Between 25, 125 and 131 the same rotation is specified three times.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Install-ThornswoodServer.ps1 lines 145-160, including the comment at 148-150 explaining that redirecting is the whole reason cmd is in the chain: whatever replaces `>>` must keep stdout and stderr captured or the server's startup failures become invisible again. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 144-155, the trim that already exists, as the pattern to either copy or replace. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\gamemode.js lines 75-92, the log function and the logBroken flag, which is the one part of this entry that is a code change rather than a hosting change.

**How:** Pick one mechanism and apply it to all four logs rather than four different ones. The cheapest that changes nothing about how the processes are started: a single tools/Roll-Logs.ps1 run by the existing five-minute watchdog task, which for each of server.log, voice.log, bot.log and the site's log checks the size and, past a threshold, renames x.log to x.log.1, x.log.1 to x.log.2 and so on, keeping five and deleting the rest. Renaming a file that a cmd redirection holds open fails on Windows, so the roll has to copy-then-truncate (`Copy-Item` then `Set-Content -Value ''`) unless the process is stopped. The cleaner answer is the one entry 125 already proposes: run each service under WinSW or NSSM and let the service manager do roll-by-size, at which point the `>>` redirections disappear. Separately, in tools/gamemode.js, change the logBroken path at 87 so it raises rather than only printing to the console: keep the flag so it does not throw every tick, but have it set a state the health or manifest route reports, so a server logging nothing is visible from outside. Fix the Watch-Services trim at 146-154 to roll rather than discard while you are there, or leave it and say plainly in the runbook that it keeps only the last 1000 lines.

**Done when:** On the VPS, run by the person: create a large dummy voice.log, run the roll script, and confirm voice.log.1 appears, voice.log is empty, and the voice service is still writing to voice.log afterwards (that last part is what catches the file-handle trap). `Get-ChildItem C:\Thornswood-Voice\voice.log*` shows at most five files. For the gamemode change, in the repo: point logPath() at an unwritable path in a Node vm harness, call log() twice, and confirm it prints the carrying-on message once and sets whatever state you added, rather than going silent with no trace. No game start.

**Traps:** All four of these scripts write to the VPS, so the person runs them; a Claude reads and shows the command. The cmd redirection is deliberate and its comment says so: a previous version piped through PowerShell and Out-File, which re-encoded the text and left the file unreadable past a point, and could hold the bot's own print() calls on its event loop. Do not put a pipeline back. On Windows you cannot rename a file another process holds open for append, which is why naive rotation silently does nothing; test the roll against a running service, not a cold one. Entry 25 and entry 125 both claim part of this work, so agree in #dev who owns it before three people write three rotators. Deleting a log to make room is how the evidence for the last crash disappeared in the first place, which is entry 25's point; roll, never delete.

**Links:** https://12factor.net/logs , https://nssm.cc/usage , https://github.com/winsw/winsw , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/register-scheduledtask

## 132. Nothing watches the disk, and the disk takes everything down at once

**Category:** Operations  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The code half is exact. tools/Watch-Services.ps1:109-114 is the $checks array and it holds four entries and nothing else: website on port 3001, game on 3000, voice on 7778, and bot with Port 0 which routes to BotAlive. There is no disk check anywhere in the file, and Caddy is not in the list either (that is entry 21). The capacity figures need a caveat for a stranger: '79.7 GB with 64.6 GB free' is not written anywhere in the repository. What the repository says is tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:13, '80 GB disk, 81 percent free', read off the box on 15 September 2026. 64.6 of 79.7 is 81.05 percent, so the two are consistent and the audit's numbers evidently came from the box rather than a file, but do not go looking for them in git.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 105-155: the $checks array, the three-strikes rule at 121-126 (which is good and should stay), the restart and re-check at 128-142, and the trim at 146-154. The whole watchdog is this one heredoc, deployed as watch.ps1 on the box. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md lines 5-36: what the box actually is, one 80 GB volume shared by all four services. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Check-Site.ps1, a read-only check you may run, as the existing example of how a check is written here.

**How:** In tools/Watch-Services.ps1, inside the heredoc that becomes watch.ps1, add a disk check after the service loop and before the trim at 146. Something like: `$d = Get-PSDrive C; $freePct = [math]::Round(100 * $d.Free / ($d.Free + $d.Used), 1); if ($freePct -lt 10) { Note ('DISK CRITICAL: ' + $freePct + ' percent free on C:') } elseif ($freePct -lt 20) { Note ('DISK LOW: ' + $freePct + ' percent free on C:') }`. Note() is the function the file already uses. Add a state file beside $hist so the warning is raised once per crossing rather than every five minutes. Then wire Note's alerting path to a webhook into a private alerts channel, which is entry 20's work and is what turns this from a line in a file on the box into something a person sees. Do the same for Caddy while you are in the array (entry 21), since both are one-line additions to the same structure.

**Done when:** Run the generated watch.ps1 by hand on the box: it prints the free percentage. Then force the branch without filling the disk, by temporarily changing the thresholds to just above the current free percentage, and confirm the warning line appears in the watchdog's log and, once entry 20 lands, in the alerts channel. Revert the thresholds. `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile()` on the edited Watch-Services.ps1 reports no errors, which is what CI checks. No game start.

**Traps:** Watch-Services.ps1 deploys to the VPS, so the person runs it; a Claude edits and shows the command. The three-strikes rule at 121-126 is deliberate and good, and entry 20 says explicitly it should stay; do not replace it with a disk-triggered restart, because restarting a service does not free a disk. The sentence the watchdog writes when it gives up currently goes to a log on the same box that may itself be the thing that is gone, which is entry 20's point and the reason a disk alert that only writes to that log is worth very little on its own. Everything on this box shares one volume (MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:21-22), so a disk alert is a whole-box alert, not a per-service one. Watch-Services.ps1 is also one of the eleven scripts using StrictHostKeyChecking=no (entry 22).

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/get-psdrive , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-query

## 133. Two of the six scheduled tasks are missing from the backup, including the web server and the watchdog

**Category:** Operations  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every number and every anchor is exact. tools/Backup-Server.ps1:171 is the hand-written list `foreach ($t in @('Thornswood Docs','Thornswood Bot','Thornswood Voice','Thornswood Server'))`, four names, each exported with `schtasks /query /tn $t /xml` into a tasks folder in the zip. The two that are missing: 'Thornswood Web', registered at tools/Setup-Domain.ps1:148 by `Register-ScheduledTask -TaskName 'Thornswood Web'` (that is Caddy, the TLS terminator), and 'Thornswood Watch', created at tools/Watch-Services.ps1:187 by `schtasks /create /tn '$Task' /sc minute /mo 5` where $Task defaults to 'Thornswood Watch' at line 35 (that is the watchdog). Six tasks, four backed up, and the two missing ones are exactly the web server and the thing that restarts everything else, as the entry says.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Backup-Server.ps1 lines 165-180: the whole task export, fifteen lines, with the comment at 169 saying importing these beats rebuilding them by hand. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md lines 96-138, step 9 of which says 'Import the scheduled tasks from the tasks folder in the backup, so the three services come back after a reboot'. Note it says three, the script exports four, and the box has six; the runbook and the script and the machine all disagree, which is entry 134's problem showing up here. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Setup-Domain.ps1 lines 140-155 and tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 183-190, the two registrations that are missed.

**How:** In tools/Backup-Server.ps1, replace the hand-written array at 171 with an enumeration filtered on the prefix, so a task added later is picked up without anybody remembering. Inside the remote heredoc: `foreach ($t in (Get-ScheduledTask | Where-Object { $_.TaskName -like 'Thornswood*' } | Select-Object -ExpandProperty TaskName)) { ... }` and keep the existing `schtasks /query /tn $t /xml` export body unchanged. Print the count and the names so the run says what it took, and print a warning when the count is below six so a task that failed to register is noticed at backup time rather than at restore time. While you are there, fix MOVING-THE-SERVER.md step 9 to say 'every task in the tasks folder' rather than 'the three services'. Prefix filtering is the right shape because every one of the six is already named Thornswood-something.

**Done when:** On the box, run by the person: `Get-ScheduledTask | Where-Object TaskName -like 'Thornswood*' | Select-Object TaskName` lists six. Run the backup and confirm the tasks folder inside the zip holds six XML files including Thornswood-Web.xml and Thornswood-Watch.xml. Before that, in the repo, the edited script parses under the same PowerShell parser CI uses. To prove the restore half genuinely works, import one of the exported XML files onto a throwaway machine with `Register-ScheduledTask -Xml (Get-Content Thornswood-Web.xml -Raw) -TaskName 'Thornswood Web Test'` and confirm it registers.

**Traps:** Backup-Server.ps1 reaches the VPS, so the person runs it; a Claude edits and shows the command. The script's own header and its closing lines say the zip deliberately contains the bot token, the Discord client secret and the server password, so the zip is a secret and CLAUDE.md's rule applies: name it by location, never open it into a chat, never commit it. A restore from today's backup would come up with no web server and no watchdog, which means no TLS and nothing restarting anything, so this is a small change guarding a large failure. The exported XML carries the task's configured user account; Thornswood Web and Thornswood Watch both run as SYSTEM today, and entries 22 and 125 want that changed, so a backup taken before that change restores the old accounts. Entry 12 is the backup content problem (the world folder is not in the zip at all) and entry 134 is the restore half; this row only fixes the task list, so do not mark the backup sound when it lands.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-query , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/register-scheduledtask

## 134. The new-box runbook does not survive being followed, and there is no restore script at all

**Category:** Operations  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong
