**Correction:** Two of the three claims hold and the third does not. tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:96-138 is verified as the only restore instruction in the repository: the heading 'If it is a new machine, new address' is at 96 and the fourteen numbered steps end at 138 with Set-Server.ps1. There is no counterpart to Backup-Server.ps1: the only Restore-* script in the repo is tools/Restore-KzlContent.ps1, which is about the other server content and has nothing to do with the server. But 'its capacity numbers contradict what the box reports' is not supported by anything I can find. MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:13 says '80 GB disk, 81 percent free', which is consistent with the 79.7 GB and 64.6 GB figures entry 132 quotes. The contradiction that IS in the document is a different one, at line 98: 'The installer on the site is about 18 GB today', against the 527 MB payload entries 8, 32 and 54 all measure. That line predates the Vortex collection switch and is now badly wrong, and it is load-bearing because step 1 of the runbook tells you to do the Vortex switch first on account of it.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md in full, 140-odd lines, with lines 96-138 read as the thing being replaced and line 98 read as a known-stale sentence. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Backup-Server.ps1, all of it, because a restore runbook is written against exactly what the zip contains, and today that is four config files and four task XMLs and no world folder (entry 12). 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 168-177, the secrets table, since a restore has to put every one of those back and the table is currently wrong in at least one row (entry 138).

**How:** Write tools/RESTORING-THE-SERVER.md beside the backup script, and then actually execute it once. Structure: a stated recovery time objective ('back up within N hours'), a prerequisites list (a machine, the backup zip, the ssh key, the DNS access, the Discord developer portal), then numbered steps that each name the script or command and what proves it worked. Reuse steps 1 to 14 at MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:96-138 as the skeleton, but fix what is wrong there: step 9 says three services and there are six tasks (entry 133), and the world folder is not in the zip at all so the restore has to say where characters come from (entry 12, which must land first or the runbook documents a restore that loses everybody's character). Add a tools/Restore-Server.ps1 that does the mechanical parts: unpack the zip, put the four config files back, import every task XML in the tasks folder, and report what it restored and what was missing. Then book an afternoon, order a throwaway box, follow the document without improvising, and write every gap you hit back into it. Also correct MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:98 to the real payload size.

**Done when:** The document has been followed end to end, by a person, against a throwaway box, and the result answers: the site returns 200 over HTTPS on its own hostname, the game server's port answers, the voice service's health route answers, the bot appears online in a test Discord, and the linked accounts and documents from the site's data folder are present. Record the elapsed time in the document. Anything you had to work out on the day goes into the text before the box is destroyed. Until that run has happened the document is a plan, not a runbook, and it should say so at the top.

**Traps:** Every step of this leaves the machine, so the person runs all of it; a Claude writes the document and shows the commands. The backup zip deliberately holds the bot token, the Discord client secret and the server password, so the rehearsal box is handling live secrets and must be destroyed afterwards, and CLAUDE.md forbids printing or pasting any of them. Entry 12 is the hard prerequisite: the 15 September zip was opened during the audit and contains 47 entries, 195 KB, and nothing under the world folder, so a restore rehearsed today proves you can bring back a server with no characters in it. MOVING-THE-SERVER.md also records a decision at lines 38-40 to buy the 16GB plan, which may or may not have happened; check the box before writing capacity numbers into the new document. Deploy-Docs.ps1 -SeedDocs during a restore would flatten every document with the repo copy, which its own header warns has thrown away an evening of edits, and MOVING-THE-SERVER.md step 7 already says not to use it; keep that warning.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows-server/administration/windows-commands/schtasks-query , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/scheduledtasks/register-scheduledtask

## 135. One five-core box runs all four services, and nothing measures any of them

**Category:** Scale  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The citation is exact. tools/MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:7-8 names BisectHosting, KVM, VirtFusion, the address and the 8GB plan at $32 a month; 10-13 are the figures read off the box on 15 September 2026, 'Windows Server 2022 Standard, AMD EPYC-Milan, 5 cores, 8 GB RAM, 2.6 GB in use, 80 GB disk, 81 percent free'; 15-19 warn the advertised CPU is not what this box got; 21-22 say in plain words that the website, the game server, the Discord bot and the voice server all run on this one machine; 26-36 are the tier table. 'nothing measures any of them' is verified against tools/Watch-Services.ps1, whose $checks array at 109-114 only tests whether a TCP port answers, with no CPU, memory, disk or bandwidth measurement anywhere. One thing a stranger must know that the entry does not say: this same document records a decision at lines 38-40 to buy the 16GB plan, so there is already an agreed direction that is 'bigger box', not 'split the tenants', and the two need reconciling in #dev rather than in a pull request.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md lines 1-60: the box, the tier table, and the existing decision at 38-40, which is the thing this entry implicitly argues against. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 105-155, which is the entire measurement story today. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Check-Load.ps1, a read-only script the runbook already tells you to use to confirm the numbers on a resized box, and the natural place to grow real measurement.

**How:** Measure before moving anything, because nothing today would tell you which tenant is the problem. Extend tools/Check-Load.ps1, or add a small collector run by the existing five-minute watchdog task, that records per-process CPU and working set for the four services by name (node for the game server, node for voice, node for the site, python for the bot), plus disk free and the NIC's bytes in and out, appending a line to a CSV on the box. Let it run for a week that includes a busy evening. Then split by what the numbers say, in this order of cheapness: (1) static downloads leave the origin entirely, which is entries 54 and 32 and removes the largest single load; (2) the public pages go to Caddy or a CDN, which is entry 122; (3) voice moves to its own box if the bandwidth numbers say so, because entry 55 measures the unculled position broadcast at 24.5 MB/s at two hundred people against an 8 TB monthly cap; (4) the game server keeps the box, since a simulation wants clock speed and this one has an EPYC Milan. Write the result back into MOVING-THE-SERVER.md so the recorded decision matches what was done.

**Done when:** A week of collected lines exists and can be read: a CSV or log on the box with per-service CPU, memory, disk free and network throughput at five minute intervals, covering at least one busy evening, and a short note saying which service dominates each resource. That is checkable without starting a game. The splitting itself is done when each moved tenant answers its own health check from outside the box and the origin's traffic graph drops by the amount you predicted.

**Traps:** Everything here is VPS work the person runs, never a Claude. MOVING-THE-SERVER.md:38-40 already records a decision to buy the 16GB plan; an entry that says 'no amount of money fixes this without splitting them up' contradicts a decision Patrick and Djinn made on 15 September, and CLAUDE.md says a change to settled design is a few lines in #dev first, agreed between them, not settled by a Claude. Moving the game server changes the address, which cascades: MOVING-THE-SERVER.md step 12 says Set-Vps.ps1 repoints twenty three files in the repo and adds a line so installed launchers move themselves, and step 14 Set-Server.ps1 is the one that moves everybody. That makes any split a coordinated client and server release, which CLAUDE.md requires to ship as one complete release. Entry 122 overlaps this one directly; agree which owns the site and payload move. The box has no monitoring and no alerting at all (entry 20), so a split multiplies the number of unwatched machines unless that lands first.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/get-psdrive , https://caddyserver.com/docs/caddyfile/directives/file_server

## 136. There is no launcher architecture document, because the one that is pointed at is a finished to-do note

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** verified

**How:** Write tools/app/ARCHITECTURE.md and retire HOOKUP.md. Four sections. (1) Entry point: Shell.Run(string[] args) at Shell.cs:205, what it reads (-root, -uninstall), and the startup order listed above with the file and line for each step. (2) The subsystems, one paragraph each, naming the file and what state it owns: Paths, Config, Keep (parks other servers' plugins and puts ours back), Hold (keeps files open read only while the game runs), Swap, Verify (load order), Updater (the feed, packs, self update), Door (who may connect), Voice, Creation, OwnGame, Uninstall, Register. (3) The Play sequence written out in order, every step with the file and method and what it leaves behind on disk, since Play is the flow every person hits and it currently cannot be followed from the written record at all. (4) A short table of what must not change without a line in #dev, matching CLAUDE.md's six protected files. Then add a class-level `/// <summary>` to Shell.cs saying what the class is and pointing at the new page, and change CLAUDE.md:97 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:320 to point at ARCHITECTURE.md. Delete HOOKUP.md in the same commit so there is one answer, not two.

**Done when:** A competent stranger can follow the Play sequence in the document from the button to the game starting, opening each named file at the named line, without asking a question. Test it literally: hand the document to someone who has not seen the repo and ask them to name every file that is written or locked between pressing Play and the game window appearing. Mechanically, `Select-String -Path CLAUDE.md,SECOND-DEVELOPER.md -Pattern 'HOOKUP'` returns nothing, and `git log --oneline -1 -- tools/app/HOOKUP.md` shows the deletion.

**Traps:** Shell.cs is on CLAUDE.md's protected list, so even adding a class comment to it wants a line in #dev first. The Play sequence is not stable ground right now: entries 5, 15, 40, 41 and 62 all change what happens between the button and the world, so a document written today needs a rule for who updates it, which is entry 139's durable-versus-dated distinction. CLAUDE.md line 97 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md line 320 both point at HOOKUP.md, and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:31 and 132-138 carry other launcher claims (entry 141), so a rename with a stale pointer left behind makes the problem worse. The `djinn/own-game-folder` branch changes where the game runs from and therefore what Keep, Hold and OwnGame do; CLAUDE.md says the two shapes must not be mixed in one release, so an architecture page has to say which shape it describes, or describe both and mark which one ships.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/xaml/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.windows.markup.xamlreader

## 138. The secrets table says the game server has a password; the script that manages it says it does not

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both sides check out, with a one line offset on the second. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:172 is the table row 'Server password (sPassword, sAdminPassword) | VPS server-settings.json; the launcher gets it from the site at check in | Nobody types it', inside the section headed 'Secrets, by name, none of them in the repo' at 168. Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 says the opposite at 35-36, not 36-44: 'It no longer touches the game server or restarts it, because there was nothing to write there and the restart threw everybody out for nothing.' The cited 36-44 runs from the second half of that sentence through the CmdletBinding and into the param block. The substance is exact: the table lists a credential protecting the game server, and the script that manages it records that there was nothing to write on the game server at all. Entry 3 is the same confusion in code and it says the published four digit value is byte-identical to the secret in use today, so a stranger fixing this row must read entry 3 first or they will write a truthful-looking table entry for a value that is about to be replaced.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 168-177, the whole secrets table, five rows: the server password, the site's botKey and Discord secrets, the bot token and THORNSWOOD_KEY, the door key, and the #news webhook. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 lines 25-50, the header and the parameters, which describe what the script actually does now (writes the password into the site's config so the launcher can be handed it) and what it stopped doing. 3. Entry 3 of this same list, plus tools/app/Config.cs:72-76 and tools/docs-app/server.js:1292, because the same value appears as a dead ServerPassword field in the launcher and in the join reply, and the table should describe the end state rather than today's muddle.

**How:** Rewrite the table so each row answers four questions and admits when the answer is nothing: name, where it lives (by location only, never the value), what it actually protects, and who can reissue it. For the first row, replace 'Server password' with the truth: the game server does not enforce a password, offlineMode is set by Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:185, and the field survives in the launcher and the join reply as dead weight (entry 3 deletes it). Add a column or a plain phrase 'not enforced' and use it. Add the rows the table is missing while you are there: the voice service's secret.txt, the dev webhook, and the ssh key. Then add a one-line rule under the table saying a control that does not exist is listed as not enforced rather than omitted, so the next person does not restore the fiction. Do this after entry 3 has replaced the four digit value, not before, so you write the table once.

**Done when:** Every row in the table can be checked by a person against the machine in under a minute: the file exists at the named path, the thing it claims to protect refuses without it, and the named script reissues it. Walk the five existing rows that way and record the result. For the row this entry is about, the check is that either the game server now refuses a connection without the credential (in which case the row is true) or the row says 'not enforced' (in which case it is honest). Mechanically, `Select-String -Path SECOND-DEVELOPER.md -Pattern 'Server password'` shows the corrected wording, and nothing in the table contains an actual secret value.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md is explicit: secrets are named by location only and are never printed, pasted, committed or logged. That applies to the fix as well as the file, so while verifying a row, confirm presence, never print the value. Entry 3 is a hard prerequisite: the value in that first row is a four digit string that is simultaneously a dead join password, the live character register secret, and in public git history on Thornswood-Releases between two named commits, so touching the documentation before the rotation means documenting a compromised value as though it were fine. Rotate-ServerPassword.ps1 reaches the VPS and is run by the person. The header at 38-40 also notes that a launcher older than 1.0.197 reads the password out of version.json rather than the site, so old installs keep whatever they last read, which is another 'not enforced' fact worth putting in the table. Entry 126 wants one rotation command for the bot token; the table is the natural place to say which script reissues each secret, so the two entries should agree on the wording.

## 139. HANDOFF.md is a dated snapshot being used as the standing operations document

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Three of the four claims are exact and the fourth is false. HANDOFF.md:1 is '# Handoff, 17 September 2026', a dated title, verified. Lines 9-10 are 'main is at 9b0c294, which is 1.1.45, shipped by Patrick from his machine on 17 Sep. Live launcher: 1.1.45', verified, and already stale: main today is ddfd2da at 1.1.47, two releases past what the standing operations document asserts, which is the entry's point proving itself. Line 361 is the last line of the file, verified (361 lines total), and it is an open question about whether a dev build is installed on a named person's machine, which is exactly the kind of fact that rots. But 'and it is untracked' is FALSE. `git ls-files HANDOFF.md` returns the file, `git status --porcelain HANDOFF.md` returns nothing (tracked and clean), and `git check-ignore -v HANDOFF.md` matches no rule. It is tracked and committed, which if anything strengthens the entry: a dated snapshot is being versioned with the code as though it were durable reference.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\HANDOFF.md lines 1-20 and the last twenty lines: the dated title, the stale version claims, and the trailing open questions. Read enough to sort its contents into the two kinds before splitting. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\CLAUDE.md lines 94-99, which points at HANDOFF.md as 'the open work list and what is live, as of 17 Sep', the pointer that has to change. 3. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\SECOND-DEVELOPER.md, the existing durable reference, which is where the facts worth keeping should end up.

**How:** Split the file by lifetime, not by topic. Go through HANDOFF.md line by line and sort each item into one of two piles. Durable facts, the ones that stay true until a code change invalidates them (for example that the resource server refuses real plugin names, why the door enforce flag is where it is, how the dev channel behaves), move into SECOND-DEVELOPER.md under the section they belong to, and the rule is that the change which invalidates one updates it in the same pull request. Dated status, the ones that go stale on purpose (what main is at, what is live right now, what is half-done, open questions about somebody's machine), stay in a file that says so at the top and is meant to be thrown away, for example STATUS.md with a line 'this file is a snapshot and is stale the moment something ships'. Then update CLAUDE.md:96 to point at the right one for the right purpose. Optionally stop tracking the status file, but only after the durable half has been moved, or you delete institutional memory.

**Done when:** HANDOFF.md no longer contains any sentence that a release would falsify, which you can test mechanically: `Select-String -Path HANDOFF.md -Pattern '1\.1\.\d+|[0-9a-f]{7}'` returns nothing, because version numbers and commit shas are the signature of dated content. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md contains the durable facts that were moved, and CLAUDE.md's pointer names the right file for each purpose. Confirm nothing was lost with `git diff --stat` across the split commit: the lines removed from one file appear in the other.

**Traps:** Do not delete HANDOFF.md before the durable facts are moved; it is the only written record of several things, and the entry's own example, the resource server refusing real plugin names, is the kind of fact nobody rediscovers cheaply. The file is tracked, so removing it from git is a real deletion with a real history, not a tidy-up. CLAUDE.md:96 points at it by name, and so does HANDOFF.md's own opening, which tells a reader to read CLAUDE.md first then this then AUDIT-2026-09-17.md; leaving a dangling pointer is how the HOOKUP.md problem in entry 136 happened. HANDOFF.md also carries ten occurrences of the old owner name (entry 137), so the two sweeps touch the same lines and should be one branch or two that know about each other. CLAUDE.md's wording rules apply to whatever you write: customers, people, testers, never 'player', and no em dashes.

## 140. Ninety-nine scripts, no discoverable help, no index, and Ship's own header lists five of its eleven options

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every number is exact. There are 99 .ps1 files under tools\, all at the top level of that folder, and exactly zero of them contain a .SYNOPSIS block, so PowerShell's own `Get-Help` discovery returns nothing for any of them. Ship.ps1's header at 9-14 lists five options: -NoBump, -Version, -ModsOnly, -DryRun, -Package. The param block at 29-51 declares twelve parameters: $Notes (mandatory, positional), $Version, $NoBump, $ModsOnly, $DryRun, $Package, $Root, $MoveServer, $NoPause, $AnnounceMinutes, $NoAnnounce and $Channel. So eleven options besides the mandatory notes string, five of them documented. And the one the entry says the whole dev workflow depends on is -Channel, declared at line 51 with a ValidateSet of main and dev, documented only in a comment inside the param block at 46-50 and absent from the header a reader would actually look at. CLAUDE.md itself documents `.\tools\Ship.ps1 -Channel dev` as the dev workflow, so the header is contradicted by the project's own instructions.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\Ship.ps1 lines 1-52: the header block and the param block, side by side, which is the whole finding in one screen. 2. C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\CLAUDE.md lines 66-70, which tells both developers to use `-Channel dev` and warns there is one dev feed and the last dev Ship wins, so the undocumented option is also the dangerous one. 3. Any two other scripts for contrast, for example tools/Check-Controls.ps1 and tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1: both open with a good hand-written `<# ... #>` block, so the repo's habit is strong, it is just not the form PowerShell can read.

**How:** Two changes, and the second one is what keeps it true. (a) Fix Ship.ps1 first, because it is the highest-traffic script: rewrite the header block at 1-26 into comment-based help, .SYNOPSIS, .DESCRIPTION, one .PARAMETER per declared parameter, and .EXAMPLE lines for the main and dev cases. Keep the prose about the installer package, it is genuinely useful; put it under .NOTES. Once the block is comment-based help, `Get-Help .\tools\Ship.ps1 -Full` lists every parameter from the param block itself, so the list cannot fall behind again. (b) Generate an index: write tools/New-ScriptIndex.ps1 that walks tools/*.ps1, reads each one's .SYNOPSIS (falling back to the first non-empty comment line), and writes tools/README.md as a table of script name and one-line purpose. Run it in CI as a job that fails when the committed tools/README.md differs from what it would generate, which is what stops it drifting. Work through the 99 headers over time; a script with no .SYNOPSIS shows up as a blank row in the index, which is its own to-do list.

**Done when:** `Get-Help .\tools\Ship.ps1 -Full` prints a synopsis, a description and all twelve parameters including -Channel with its ValidateSet, without you having opened the file. `Get-Help .\tools\Ship.ps1 -Parameter Channel` prints the dev-feed warning. tools/README.md exists and lists 99 rows. Deliberately add a thirteenth parameter to Ship.ps1 without touching the help, run the index generator, and confirm CI would go red on the diff, then revert. All of it runs locally with no VPS, no game and no release.

**Traps:** Ship.ps1 is the release script and the person runs it, never a Claude; editing its header is safe, running it is not. Do not renumber, rename or reorder its parameters while documenting them: $Notes is Mandatory and Position 0, so anything that disturbs positional binding breaks every recorded command including the ones in CLAUDE.md. Comment-based help must sit immediately before the param block or PowerShell will not associate it, and a stray blank line pattern can silently detach it, which is why the Get-Help check above is the real test rather than reading the file. Every .ps1 is parsed by CI (build.yml lines 18-31), so a malformed help block that breaks parsing turns every pull request red. The dev channel is shared between both machines and the last dev Ship wins, so documenting -Channel properly is a safety improvement as much as a documentation one; say that in the .PARAMETER text. CLAUDE.md's wording rules apply to everything you write into these headers.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/scripting/developer/help/writing-comment-based-help-topics , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/workflow-syntax-for-github-actions

## 141. Four deployable components have no README, and the dev release path has never been proven end to end

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Six of the cited directories have no README, not four. tools\docs-app, tools\voice, tools\ts-plugins, tools\ts-ui, tools\setup and tools\launcher all contain no .md file at all; the only two under tools\ are tools\app\HOOKUP.md and tools\discord\README.md. The entry says "Four deployable components" but lists six paths and never names which four, so a stranger cannot tell what is in scope. The citations are right: SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:31 is the Releases repo row naming the dev worktree Thornswood-Releases-dev, SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:132-138 is the clone block (it clones Thornswood and Thornswood-Releases only), and Ship.ps1:132-136 is the -Dev guard. The second half is overstated: Ship.ps1:108-128 (Ensure-DevReleases) creates the dev worktree itself from the main releases clone, so that prerequisite is not missing. What the setup block genuinely omits is the dev build at <drive>:\Thornswood-DevBuild, which Ship.ps1:132-134 dies on and which only the main launcher's Settings page installs.

**Challenged (minor):** "tools\docs-app, tools\voice, tools\ts-plugins, tools\ts-ui, tools\setup and tools\launcher all contain no .md file at all; the only two under tools\ are tools\app\HOOKUP.md and tools\discord\README.md."  ->  tools\docs-app holds 13 .md files: data\active.md, archive.md, help.md, known.md, suggestions.md, tests.md, todo.md and data\site\{classes,credits,join,rules,support,world}.md, which are the site's own pages and are named in CLAUDE.md's "Where things are". Other .md under tools\ beyond the two named: COLLECTION-METHOD.md, GOURMET-FISHING-PATCH.md, JKS-SKYRIM.md, MOVING-THE-SERVER.md, art\ART-PROMPT.md and thornswood-src\README.md. The narrower conclusion (none of the six has a README) does hold; the stated evidence does not.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Three paragraphs each, lifted and expanded from the matching row of SECOND-DEVELOPER.md's The pieces table" for all six directories, and the entry calls all six "deployable components".  ->  Two of the six have no matching row and are not deployable components. The pieces table is SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:27-38 and its rows are Source repo, Releases repo, Launcher, Dev build, Game, Plugins, Game server, Dev game server, Voice, Site and Bot. tools\launcher holds one file, voice-host.html, the WebView2 page the launcher itself hosts (referenced at Build-Launcher.ps1:253). tools\setup holds one file, Bootstrap.cs, the installer stub source. tools\ts-ui has no row of its own either; it appears only inside the Plugins row ("UI in `tools\ts-ui`").

**Read first:** 1. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 26-45, The pieces table: it already states, per component, what it is and where it runs in production, which is two of the three paragraphs each README needs. 2. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 120-150, the machine setup block: this is where the missing dev-build prerequisite has to be added, in the same block as git, gh, node and Python. 3. tools\Ship.ps1 lines 105-140: Ensure-DevReleases plus the if ($Dev) guard, the only place in the repo that spells out what the dev channel actually requires.

**How:** Create six files: tools\docs-app\README.md, tools\voice\README.md, tools\ts-plugins\README.md, tools\ts-ui\README.md, tools\setup\README.md, tools\launcher\README.md. Three paragraphs each, lifted and expanded from the matching row of SECOND-DEVELOPER.md's The pieces table: what it is, how to run it locally, what it needs, where it runs in production. For docs-app say `node server.js` and the 127.0.0.1:3001 behind Caddy; for voice say `node voice-server.js` and ports 7778 (main) / 7789 (dev); for ts-plugins and ts-ui say they are Skyrim Platform JavaScript copied into Data\Platform by the launcher and are never built; for setup and launcher say they are compiled by tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 with the .NET Framework 4 csc. Then add one line to the setup block in SECOND-DEVELOPER.md after the gh auth status step: that the dev channel additionally needs the dev build installed from the main launcher's Settings page (The dev build), which puts it at <drive>:\Thornswood-DevBuild, and that Ship.ps1 -Channel dev refuses without it.

**Done when:** `Get-ChildItem tools\docs-app,tools\voice,tools\ts-plugins,tools\ts-ui,tools\setup,tools\launcher -Filter README.md` returns six rows, and `Select-String -Path SECOND-DEVELOPER.md -Pattern 'Thornswood-DevBuild'` returns a hit inside the setup block (lines 120-160). Both are read-only checks.

**Traps:** Do not try to prove the dev path by running Ship.ps1 -Channel dev yourself: that is a person-runs-it command under CLAUDE.md, and there is one dev feed, so the last dev Ship wins and it has to be announced in #dev first. The README text is people-facing, so CLAUDE.md's Words section applies: no em dashes, never the word "player", say customers or testers. Do not name Patrick or Djinn in the READMEs; entry 137 is removing names from operational documents. tools\launcher is not the launcher source (that is tools\app); it holds voice-host.html and the runtime page files, so do not describe it as the C# project.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree , https://www.makeareadme.com/

## 142. Protected files is prose in two documents with no mechanism behind it

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The CLAUDE.md line reference is wrong. CLAUDE.md:96-98 is the "Where things are" list (HANDOFF.md, SECOND-DEVELOPER.md, README.md, SETUP.md, tools\app\HOOKUP.md...). The protected-files rule is CLAUDE.md:79-80: "Do not edit Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs or Publish-Update.ps1 without a line in #dev first; both machines touch those." CLAUDE.md is 100 lines total. Everything else verified: SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:103-105 carries the same rule in prose, .github holds exactly one file (.github/workflows/build.yml), and there is no CODEOWNERS file anywhere in the working tree.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: create CODEOWNERS "with one line per protected file, each assigning the other developer's GitHub account as owner, for example `/tools/app/Shell.cs @EvilPatrick06`".  ->  @EvilPatrick06 is not the other developer, it is the repo owner and the git user in this working tree (CLAUDE.md: "Owner: Patrick (EvilPatrick06). Second developer: Djinn"). GitHub never requests a code-owner review from a pull request's own author, so for every pull request Patrick opens, this file does nothing at all, which is most of them since only his machine ships main. The second developer's GitHub account is never named anywhere in the write-up, and the entry does not say that auto-requesting a reviewer is advisory unless branch protection also requires code-owner review.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md lines 74-81, the end of the "How work moves between the two machines" section: the rule itself, and the pull-request workflow the enforcement has to hook into. 2. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md lines 96-108: the same rule written as prose beside the settled-design list, so both copies get updated together. 3. .github/workflows/build.yml: the only thing in .github today, so you can see the directory convention the new file sits beside.

**How:** Create .github/CODEOWNERS with one line per protected file, each assigning the other developer's GitHub account as owner, for example `/tools/app/Shell.cs @EvilPatrick06` and the same for Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs and /tools/Publish-Update.ps1. Paths in CODEOWNERS are gitignore-style and rooted at the repo, so use a leading slash for exact files. Then add a two-line header comment at the top of each of the six files saying it is a shared file and what breaks if it is changed without a #dev note (Shell.cs: the Play sequence for everybody; Keep.cs and Hold.cs: the shared Data\Platform; Updater.cs: how every install picks up releases; Publish-Update.ps1: what goes into a pack). Fix the stale citation in whatever document points at CLAUDE.md:96-98 to point at CLAUDE.md:79-80 instead. CODEOWNERS only produces a review request; to make it a gate, branch protection on main with "Require review from Code Owners" has to be switched on in the repo settings, which is a person's action in the GitHub UI, not a file change.

**Done when:** `Test-Path .github\CODEOWNERS` is True and `Get-Content .github\CODEOWNERS` lists all six paths. GitHub's own check: open any pull request that touches tools/app/Shell.cs and confirm the owner is auto-requested as reviewer. The syntax can be checked without pushing by reading the file against the CODEOWNERS doc; GitHub also surfaces syntax errors on the file's page once it is on the default branch.

**Traps:** The two prose copies must stay in step: CLAUDE.md:79-80 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:103-105 say the same thing, and entry 114 wants the same CODEOWNERS file, so this is one change, not two. Adding the C# header comments touches Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs and Updater.cs, which is exactly what the rule says needs a #dev line first, so post the note before the branch. The C# comments must not use string interpolation or any C# 6+ syntax anywhere near them, and the launcher must still compile under the .NET Framework 4 csc. CODEOWNERS cannot enforce anything on its own without branch protection, so do not write in the docs that it is enforced until that switch is on.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/managing-your-repositorys-settings-and-features/customizing-your-repository/about-code-owners , https://docs.github.com/en/repositories/configuring-branches-and-merges-in-your-repository/managing-protected-branches/about-protected-branches

## 143. There is no way to say anything to everybody when an outside thing breaks

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "add a Border with a TextBlock at the top of the main grid in tools\ui.xaml, name it BarNotice, find it in the wiring method the way the other named controls are found".  ->  The outer grid has three fixed rows, tools\ui.xaml:361-365 (46 / * / 28), with TitleBar at Grid.Row="0" (368), content at Grid.Row="1" (392), status bar at Grid.Row="2" (1038) and the Overlay spanning all of them with Grid.RowSpan="3" (1054). Adding a row at the top means renumbering every Grid.Row and bumping the RowSpan, none of which the write-up says; as written the new Border lands on top of the 46px title bar. The repo already has exactly the pattern being asked for and the howTo never mentions it: UpdateBar (ui.xaml:598), CharBar (610) and DowngradeBar (620) are named notice Borders wired the same way.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** The howTos for 143, 145, 146 and 147 hand out edits to Shell.cs, Checks.cs and Updater.cs with no mention of the gate on those files.  ->  CLAUDE.md:79-80: "Do not edit Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs, Updater.cs or Publish-Update.ps1 without a line in #dev first; both machines touch those." Entry 143 edits Shell.cs and Updater.cs, 145 edits Shell.cs and Updater.cs, 146 edits Checks.cs, 147 edits Shell.cs and Updater.cs. None of them says a #dev line comes first, which is the whole subject of entry 142 sitting two rows above.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Shell.cs lines 4020-4075: the update-check callback that reads the feed document. Line 4062 is exactly `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(v.message)) Log(v.message);`, and the lines just above it show the pattern the other feed fields already use (discordAppId, siteUrl, presenceImage written into cfg and saved). 2. version.json in the repo root: the feed shape, which today carries launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, serverPort, an empty "message" and the packs array. 3. tools\app\Updater.cs: where the feed document class is defined and deserialised, which is where any new field has to be declared before Shell.cs can read it.

**How:** Three parts, smallest first. (a) Replace the Log call at Shell.cs:4062 with a persistent bar: add a Border with a TextBlock at the top of the main grid in tools\ui.xaml, name it BarNotice, find it in the wiring method the way the other named controls are found, and set its Text and Visibility from v.message instead of logging it. (b) Add two fields to the feed class in Updater.cs: a minimum supported launcher version string and a maintenance boolean. Compare the minimum against the running version with a plain string or numeric comparison, not string interpolation. (c) When maintenance is set, or the running version is below the minimum, set the Play button's IsEnabled to false and put the reason in the bar. Publishing the new fields into version.json is a person's job (Ship.ps1 or Publish-Server.ps1), never yours.

**Done when:** Build the launcher with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` and confirm Thornswood.exe is produced; that is the compile gate CLAUDE.md explicitly allows. Then check the wiring without running the app: `Select-String -Path tools\ui.xaml -Pattern 'BarNotice'` and `Select-String -Path tools\app\Shell.cs -Pattern 'BarNotice'` both hit, and `Select-String -Path tools\app\Shell.cs -Pattern 'Log\(v.message\)'` no longer hits. The GitHub build check (.github/workflows/build.yml) runs the same compile on the pull request.

**Traps:** Shell.cs is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79): a few lines in #dev before the branch. Updater.cs is on the same list. C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, so write `"Maintenance: " + v.message`, not an interpolated string, and `if (v != null && v.message != null)`, not `v?.message`. A field added to the feed class must tolerate an absent value, because every launcher already in the world reads the same version.json and an older exe must not break on a document it does not understand. Disabling Play from the feed is a kill switch: the field has to be safe when the feed is unreachable (default to enabled), or a site outage locks everybody out. The repo-root version.json still reads launcherVersion 1.0.196 and is not the live feed; the live one is version.json in the Thornswood-Releases clone, per Set-Server.ps1:39-42.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/framework/wpf/controls/textblock , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.runtime.serialization.datamemberattribute

## 144. Nothing outside the project is watched at all

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: "push the branch and use the Actions tab's workflow_dispatch button to run it once".  ->  GitHub only shows the Run workflow button for a workflow file that is present on the repository's default branch, so a watch-outside.yml that exists only on a feature branch cannot be dispatched. Combined with this repo's rule (CLAUDE.md: merged into main from GitHub, never pushed over main), the proposed proof cannot be run until after the pull request is merged, which is the opposite of what the doneWhen promises. The honest test before merge is a local run of the same fetch-and-HEAD steps in pwsh.

**Read first:** 1. .github/workflows/build.yml, all of it: the only workflow in the repo, triggered by `on: pull_request` and `on: push: branches: [main]` with no `schedule:` key, so nothing runs unprompted. It is also the template for the new job. 2. tools\Watch-Services.ps1 lines 109-114: the $checks array, which is four local port/task pairs (website 3001, game 3000, voice 7778, bot via BotAlive) and nothing that leaves the box. 3. tools\app\Downgrade.cs lines 38-47 and tools\app\Config.cs lines 36-37: the external things worth watching, the three Steam depot manifests and the Nexus collection URL.

**How:** Add a second workflow file, .github/workflows/watch-outside.yml, with `on: schedule: - cron: '0 6 * * *'` plus `workflow_dispatch:` so it can be run by hand. Give it one job on windows-latest with steps that: fetch the live feed (version.json from the Thornswood-Releases raw URL) and fail if the HTTP status is not 200; for every entry in packs[] and for launcherUrl, issue a HEAD request and fail on anything that is not 200; resolve the collection URL from tools\app\Config.cs:37 and fail on a non-200; and check the three depot manifest ids from tools\app\Downgrade.cs:42-44 against the current Steam build. The depot check needs credentials, so either leave it as a documented manual step or drive steamcmd with an anonymous session where that works. Write the whole thing in pwsh steps so it matches the existing workflow's style, and let a non-zero exit fail the job so GitHub sends the notification.

**Done when:** `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` over the new file is not applicable to YAML, so instead: push the branch and use the Actions tab's `workflow_dispatch` button to run it once, and confirm it goes green. Locally, you can prove the check logic without GitHub by running the same pwsh block in a terminal and confirming it exits 0 today and exits 1 when you point it at a deliberately wrong URL. No game or launcher needed.

**Traps:** A scheduled workflow only starts running once the file is on the default branch, so it will not fire from a branch or a pull request; the workflow_dispatch trigger is what lets you test it before merge. GitHub disables scheduled workflows on repositories with no activity for 60 days. Do not put any token, key or webhook in the YAML: the feed and the collection are public URLs, and anything that needs a credential goes in repository secrets, named by location only. Nexus rate-limits and may answer a bare HEAD with a challenge page, so a 200 from the collection URL is weak evidence; say so in the job rather than claiming it proves the collection installs. This workflow runs on GitHub, not the VPS, so it is not a replacement for Watch-Services.ps1 and must not be wired to restart anything.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/events-that-trigger-workflows , https://docs.github.com/en/actions/writing-workflows/choosing-when-your-workflow-runs/events-that-trigger-workflows#schedule , https://developer.valvesoftware.com/wiki/SteamCMD

## 145. The Skyrim build number and the depot ids are compiled into the exe rather than carried in the feed

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Line-accurate with one nit: Downgrade.cs:41-45 is the Depots array (three depot/manifest pairs, 489831/8442952117333549665, 489832/8042843504692938467, 489833/1914580699073641964) and the newer build's size, `public const long NewerExeBytes = 37910440;`, is at line 47, one line past the stated range. Config.cs:77 is `[DataMember] public long ExpectedExeBytes = 37157144;` and Config.cs:206 is `if (ExpectedExeBytes <= 0) ExpectedExeBytes = 37157144;`, both exact. Confirmed that the feed does not carry any of this: version.json's fields are launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, serverPort, message and packs, and Shell.cs's feed handler only ever adopts discordAppId, siteUrl, presenceImage, serverAddress, serverPort and message.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "Confirmed that the feed does not carry any of this: version.json's fields are launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, serverPort, message and packs, and Shell.cs's feed handler only ever adopts discordAppId, siteUrl, presenceImage, serverAddress, serverPort and message." Marked [verified].  ->  Both enumerations are wrong. The feed class VersionDoc at C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Repo\tools\app\Config.cs:379-422 declares launcherVersion, launcherUrl, launcherSha256, serverAddress, collectionUrl, serverPort, maxOnline, serverPassword, updateRepo, voicePort, discordAppId, siteUrl, presenceImage, message, packs, optional, mainVersion and news. The published feed in the scratchpad (live-version.json) carries 13 top-level keys, including news, updateRepo, voicePort, collectionUrl, maxOnline and mainVersion. OnVersionDoc at Shell.cs:4021-4062 adopts serverAddress (4027), collectionUrl (4028), serverPort (4029), maxOnline (4030), voicePort (4041), updateRepo (4045), discordAppId (4052), siteUrl (4053), presenceImage (4054) and message (4062). This matters beyond the nit: entry 147's whole premise is "the collection URL carries no revision", and collectionUrl is already a feed field the launcher adopts into cfg.CollectionUrl.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Add fields to the feed document class in tools\app\Updater.cs". Entries 143 ("Add two fields to the feed class in Updater.cs") and 147 ("add a collectionRevision field to the feed class in tools\app\Updater.cs") repeat it.  ->  There is no feed class in Updater.cs. `grep -n "DataMember\|DataContract" tools/app/Updater.cs` returns nothing; the only classes there are `Updater` (line 11) and `Quick : WebClient` (line 20). The feed document is `[DataContract] public class VersionDoc` at tools\app\Config.cs:378-379. Three separate howTos send the reader to the wrong file.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Change Downgrade.cs to read the depot list from cfg rather than from the static readonly Depots" with no mention of the other copy.  ->  The same three depot/manifest pairs are also hardcoded at tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1:47-52 ($Depots ordered hashtable, 489831/8442952117333549665 and the other two), and Downgrade.cs:39-40's own comment points straight at it: "Same three as Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and Build-Thornswood.ps1." Doing only the launcher half leaves a fresh install pinned to script constants while the launcher follows the feed, so the two silently disagree the first time the pin is moved, which is the exact split the entry exists to remove. (Build-Thornswood.ps1 no longer carries them at all, so that half of the comment is already stale.)

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Downgrade.cs lines 36-47: AppId 489830, the three depot/manifest pairs and NewerExeBytes, the constants that pin the build. The comment at 39-40 says the same three ids are duplicated in Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and Build-Thornswood.ps1. 2. tools\app\Config.cs lines 70-80 and 195-215: the ExpectedExeBytes field and the migration block, which is the model for how a feed-supplied value is defaulted and then overwritten. 3. tools\app\Shell.cs lines 4040-4065: the feed handler, the exact place a new feed field gets adopted into cfg, and the pattern to copy.

**How:** Add fields to the feed document class in tools\app\Updater.cs: expectedExeBytes (long), newerExeBytes (long) and a depots array of {id, manifest} pairs. In Shell.cs's feed handler, beside the existing `if (!string.IsNullOrEmpty(v.siteUrl))` lines, adopt them into cfg when present and non-zero, and save. Change Downgrade.cs to read the depot list from cfg rather than from the static readonly Depots array, keeping the current values as the fallback when the feed has not supplied any (exactly the shape Config.cs:206 already uses for ExpectedExeBytes). Leave the compiled defaults in place so a launcher that has never reached the site still works, which is the same reasoning Config.cs:32-35 gives for the collection URL. The matching ids in tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1 should then read from the same feed, or Build-Thornswood.ps1 should be deleted outright (entry 166).

**Done when:** Compile: `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe. Then prove the plumbing without the game: `Select-String -Path tools\app\Downgrade.cs -Pattern '8442952117333549665'` should now only hit inside a clearly marked fallback block, and `Select-String -Path tools\app\Shell.cs -Pattern 'expectedExeBytes'` should hit in the feed handler. Add a sample feed document with the new fields to a scratch file and deserialise it in a small csc test if you want proof the DataContract round-trips.

**Traps:** Shell.cs and Updater.cs are both on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79), so a #dev line comes first. C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Every launcher already installed reads the same version.json, so the new fields must be optional and an older exe must ignore them without failing to deserialise. Changing the pin is a coordinated act: the exe size, the depot manifests and the collection all describe one build of Skyrim, so moving one without the others hands people a game the checks call wrong. CLAUDE.md's "every change goes out as one complete release" applies. Do not run Downgrade or anything that talks to Steam from here.

**Links:** https://developer.valvesoftware.com/wiki/SteamCMD , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.runtime.serialization.datacontractattribute , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.runtime.serialization.json.datacontractjsonserializer

## 146. The launcher never checks the Address Library, so a whole class of failure reads as green

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (minor):** howTo gives the sample line `l.Add(new Check("Address library", File.Exists(...), "versionlib 1.6.1170"));` and in the same sentence says to use "a failure detail that names the fix ("Address Library is missing, install it in Vortex"), matching the Auto load row's wording".  ->  The sample contradicts the instruction. The Auto load row it says to match, tools\app\Checks.cs:456-460, picks its detail conditionally: `StartOnSaveInstalled() ? "walks past the main menu" : "Start On Save is missing, install it in Vortex"`. The sample passes one constant string, so a failing row would read "versionlib 1.6.1170" and name no fix. The rest of the entry checks out: Setup-Thornswood.ps1:575 does test versionlib-1-6-1170-0.bin, Checks.All() runs exactly nine rows (416, 417, 418, 423, 424, 431, 437, 456, 462) and none of them is the Address Library, and the file is present in the live build.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Checks.cs lines 409-463, the All(Config cfg) method: it is exactly nine l.Add(new Check(...)) calls, Game build, Script Extender, Multiplayer, Client, Mods, Mod list, Other servers, Auto load, Steam, and none of them touches the Address Library. The Auto load block at 447-462 is the model to copy: it explains why the row exists and what the person should do. 2. tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1 lines 570-582: the $checks hashtable, where line 575 is `'Address library' = (Test-Path (Join-Path $GameDir 'Data\SKSE\Plugins\versionlib-1-6-1170-0.bin'))`, and the comment below it explains that the script extender and the address library both come out of the collection. 3. tools\app\Shell.cs lines 3512-3525: the only mention of the Address Library in the launcher, a comment explaining that the working directory must be the game root or SKSE reports "couldn't load the appropriate address library".

**How:** In tools\app\Checks.cs, add one row to All() beside the Auto load row: `l.Add(new Check("Address library", File.Exists(Path.Combine(Paths.Game, "Data\\SKSE\\Plugins\\versionlib-1-6-1170-0.bin")), "versionlib 1.6.1170"));` with a failure detail that names the fix ("Address Library is missing, install it in Vortex"), matching the Auto load row's wording. Give it a small helper next to StartOnSaveInstalled() so the test is in one place. For the second half of the recommendation, add a method that reads the script extender log at %USERPROFILE%\Documents\My Games\Skyrim Special Edition\SKSE\skse64.log after a run and reports any line naming a plugin that failed to load; surface it as a log line first, not a blocking check.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe, and `Select-String -Path tools\app\Checks.cs -Pattern 'versionlib'` hits inside All(). Count the rows with `(Select-String -Path tools\app\Checks.cs -Pattern 'l.Add\(new Check' | Measure-Object).Count` and confirm it has gone from 9 to 10. tools\Check-Build.ps1 and the other Check-*.ps1 scripts are read-only if you want a second opinion on the game folder without launching anything.

**Traps:** Checks.cs is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79): a few lines in #dev before the branch. C# 5 only, and Checks.cs uses double-backslash escapes in path literals throughout, so copy that style rather than introducing verbatim strings inconsistently. The exact filename matters: it is versionlib-1-6-1170-0.bin for the 1.6.1170 pin, and it changes if entry 145 ever moves the pin, so tie the two together rather than hardcoding a second copy of the build number. A new blocking check gates Play for everybody on the next release; if the collection does not reliably install the Address Library, a hard block turns a warning into a lockout, so ship it as a visible row before making it blocking. Adding a row changes what people see on the Play page, so it goes out as one complete release with the matching game files.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition/mods/32444 , https://skse.silverlock.org/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.file.exists

## 147. Your collection is out of date is decided by one hardcoded sentinel file, and the collection URL carries no revision

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Shell.cs lines 4243-4273: CollectionProblem(), whose body is exactly 4256-4268. It returns "none" when Checks.EnabledPlugins() <= 40 and "stale" when Checks.StartOnSaveInstalled() is false, and that single file test is the entire out-of-date signal. 2. tools\app\Checks.cs lines 15-31: StartOnSaveInstalled(), which is nothing but File.Exists on Data\SKSE\Plugins\po3_StartOnSave.dll. 3. tools\app\Config.cs lines 30-37: CollectionUrl and the compiled-in Collection constant, a bare Nexus collection URL with no revision in it.

**How:** Two halves. (a) Record the revision: add a collectionRevision field to the feed class in tools\app\Updater.cs and to cfg in Config.cs (defaulted to 0 so an old config still loads), adopt it in Shell.cs's feed handler beside siteUrl, and have Ship.ps1 write the revision the build was made against into version.json. (b) Compare it: Vortex stores what it installed under %APPDATA%\Vortex; read the installed collection revision from there and, in CollectionProblem(), return "stale" when the installed revision is below cfg.CollectionRevision, keeping the StartOnSaveInstalled() test as a fallback for the case where Vortex's data cannot be read. Do not delete the plugin-count test at 4260; it is what catches "never installed". Pair it with the scheduled job from entry 144 so a clean collection install is exercised on a timer.

**Done when:** Compile with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`. Then, read-only: `Select-String -Path tools\app\Shell.cs -Pattern 'collectionRevision'` hits inside CollectionProblem, and `Get-ChildItem $env:APPDATA\Vortex -Recurse -Filter *.json | Select-String 'ombo2a'` shows you which file on this machine actually holds the installed revision, which is the thing to read. If the Vortex file cannot be found on either machine, say so and keep the sentinel fallback rather than shipping a check that always reports stale.

**Traps:** Shell.cs, Checks.cs and Updater.cs are all on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79). C# 5 only. CLAUDE.md is explicit that nothing touches Vortex's own files: read the profile data, never write it. The sentinel currently used, po3_StartOnSave.dll, is also the subject of its own check row (Checks.cs Auto load, lines 447-462), so the same missing file already produces two different messages; decide which one speaks and do not leave both. Entry 38 is the load-order half of the same problem and should land in the same thinking. The threshold `EnabledPlugins() <= 40` at Shell.cs:4260 and the `plugins > 40` test in Checks.cs All() are two copies of the same magic number; the live reference list has 96 starred plugins, so either both move together or the number goes into one place.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition/mods/32444 , https://help.nexusmods.com/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.file.exists

## 148. Caddy and Node are fetched latest, with no version and no checksum, and the bot's libraries are unpinned

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The Caddy half is exact: tools\Setup-Domain.ps1:86-92 downloads https://caddyserver.com/api/download?os=windows&arch=amd64, whatever the vendor currently serves, guarded only by `if (-not (Test-Path (Join-Path $dir 'caddy.exe')))`, with no version and no checksum. The bot half is exact: tools\Install-Bot.ps1:210 is `& python -m pip install --quiet --disable-pip-version-check discord.py pillow certifi` with no versions, and there is no requirements.txt anywhere in tools\discord. The Node claim is misattributed: Setup-Domain.ps1 never downloads Node, it only resolves an already-installed one at line 119 with `(Get-Command node).Source`. Node is installed by tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 lines 75-85, and it is not "latest": it pins the major with $NodeMajor = 'v24' (line 32), reads https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json and takes the newest v24.x. So Node is partially pinned, to a major, with no exact version and no checksum.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** doneWhen: "Read-only proof, no VPS needed: [System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile over each edited script reports no errors (this is exactly what .github/workflows/build.yml does, so the pul[l request]...)".  ->  That test cannot see the edited lines. Both cited lines are inside here-strings that are sent to the VPS for remote execution, which is why the variables are backtick-escaped: Setup-Domain.ps1:86-92 reads `if (-not (Test-Path (Join-Path `$dir 'caddy.exe')))` and Install-Bot.ps1:210-211 reads `if (`$LASTEXITCODE -eq 0)`. ParseFile parses the outer script and treats the whole remote block as one string literal, so a syntax error in the new Caddy checksum code or the new pip/venv code parses green, and the build.yml check (which runs the same ParseFile over tools\*.ps1) stays green too.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Setup-Domain.ps1 lines 80-95: the Caddy block, inside a here-string that is executed on the VPS, so backticks are escapes and $ signs are escaped too. Read the quoting before editing anything in it. 2. tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 lines 28-35 and 50-90: the $NodeMajor parameter, the Install-Msi helper and the Node block, which is where an exact version and a hash check belong. Lines 18-19 explain why the major is pinned at all (scam_native.node is a Node-API addon). 3. tools\Install-Bot.ps1 lines 200-215: the pip block and the certifi comment explaining why three packages are installed rather than two.

**How:** Caddy: replace the api/download URL at Setup-Domain.ps1:89-91 with a pinned GitHub release asset URL (caddy_<version>_windows_amd64.zip from github.com/caddyserver/caddy/releases), add a $CaddyVersion and $CaddySha256 parameter at the top of the script, and after the download compare `(Get-FileHash $file -Algorithm SHA256).Hash` against the expected value, throwing on a mismatch. Caddy publishes checksums with each release. Node: add a $NodeVersion parameter beside $NodeMajor in Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 (for example 'v24.5.0'), select that exact entry from index.json rather than the first match at line 83, and verify the msi against the SHASUMS256.txt that nodejs.org publishes for each release directory. Bot: create tools\discord\requirements.txt with `discord.py==<version>`, `pillow==<version>`, `certifi==<version>` pinned to what is on the VPS today, create a virtual environment in the install script, and change Install-Bot.ps1:210 to `python -m pip install --quiet --disable-pip-version-check -r requirements.txt`.

**Done when:** Read-only proof, no VPS needed: `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` over each edited script reports no errors (this is exactly what .github/workflows/build.yml does, so the pull request check covers it). Then `Select-String -Path tools\Setup-Domain.ps1 -Pattern 'api/download'` returns nothing, `Select-String -Path tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 -Pattern 'Select-Object -First 1'` no longer sits in the Node block, and `Test-Path tools\discord\requirements.txt` is True with every line carrying `==`. To get the current versions to pin, read them off the VPS with the existing read-only tooling rather than guessing.

**Traps:** Setup-Domain.ps1 and Install-Bot.ps1 both build here-strings that are shipped to the VPS and run there, so a stray backtick or an unescaped $ silently changes what executes on the server. Test the parse, and read the surrounding escaping style before adding a line. These are all scripts that talk to the VPS: CLAUDE.md says the person runs Deploy-*, Install-* and anything that reaches the server, so write the change and show the command, do not run it. Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 also carries the broken guard from entry 162, so anyone editing this file should fix that at the same time or at least not make it harder. Pinning to an exact Node version has a hard constraint recorded at Bootstrap-VPS.ps1:18-19: the voice server's native addon is Node-API, so the major must stay compatible; do not pin to a version older than what the build machine runs. Never print the VPS credentials that Setup-Domain.ps1 and Install-Bot.ps1 handle.

**Links:** https://caddyserver.com/docs/install , https://github.com/caddyserver/caddy/releases , https://nodejs.org/dist/index.json , https://pip.pypa.io/en/stable/reference/requirements-file-format/ , https://docs.python.org/3/library/venv.html , https://packaging.python.org/en/latest/guides/installing-using-pip-and-virtual-environments/

## 149. A Steam library on a second drive silently disqualifies the own game folder

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact. tools\app\OwnGame.cs:147-149 is `if (!SameVolume(src, Paths.Root)) return "Skyrim is on " + Volume(src) + " and Thornswood is on " + Volume(Paths.Root) + ". Links only work inside one drive, so Thornswood has to be installed on the same drive as Skyrim for this.";` and line 150 is the `return null;` that means everything is fine. It is a flat refusal with no alternative offered.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs is where the install root is chosen, so the volume check belongs there as a preference, not as a post-hoc refusal."  ->  Bootstrap.cs is the installer for the shape that ships today, and its own on-screen text at Bootstrap.cs:411 says the install "puts Thornswood into the Skyrim you already have on Steam... It does not make a second copy of the game." The volume requirement exists only for the own game folder, which CLAUDE.md lists under settled design: it lives on djinn/own-game-folder (still unmerged, visible in `git branch -a` as remotes/origin/djinn/own-game-folder), "Until that branch is on main, what ships is the Steam copy with Vortex, and the two shapes must not be mixed in one release." Making the installer steer the install drive for the own-game-folder feature is that mix, and the write-up never says so or says it needs a line in #dev.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\OwnGame.cs lines 120-152: the whole precondition method, which checks Vortex deployment first (counting missing plugins) and then the volume. Read how it returns a human sentence rather than a code, because whatever you add has to fit that shape. 2. CLAUDE.md lines 30-40, the settled-design section: Thornswood's own game folder is built from Vortex's deployment with the big files as hard links precisely so the collection is not stored twice, which is the reason the same-volume rule exists at all. 3. tools\New-GameFolder.ps1: the script that does the same job for the other server and Daedric, so you can see whether it has the same restriction and whether a fix has to be mirrored.

**How:** Change the refusal into a choice, in tools\app\OwnGame.cs and the settings page in Shell.cs that calls it. Best answer: ask for the install location during setup, before anything is written, defaulting to the volume Skyrim is on. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs is where the install root is chosen, so the volume check belongs there as a preference, not as a post-hoc refusal. Failing that, keep the check but return a structured result rather than a sentence: same-volume ok, or offer to move the Thornswood folder to Skyrim's volume, or fall back to real copies with the disk cost stated in GB (compute it from the deployed Data folder's size with DirectoryInfo, and show the number before the person agrees). Copies are correct but expensive, which is exactly why the hard-link design exists, so the number has to be on screen.

**Done when:** Compile with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`. Read-only behaviour proof without starting anything: write a tiny csc console harness that calls the same SameVolume/Volume helpers with a C:\ and a D:\ path and prints the branch taken, or simply confirm `Select-String -Path tools\app\OwnGame.cs -Pattern 'has to be installed on the same drive'` no longer returns a dead end and that the new copy-fallback path exists. On a machine with two drives you can also check the size figure the new code would show with `(Get-ChildItem '<Steam>\Data' -Recurse -File | Measure-Object Length -Sum).Sum / 1GB`.

**Traps:** This is settled design under CLAUDE.md: the own-game-folder shape was decided on 17 Sep by Patrick and Djinn, and the branch djinn/own-game-folder is not on main yet, so until it merges the shipping shape is the Steam copy with Vortex and "the two shapes must not be mixed in one release". Changing how the own folder is built is a #dev conversation first, not a quiet rewrite. Hard links are a Windows filesystem limit, not a bug: they genuinely cannot cross volumes, so do not try to make links work across drives. Falling back to copies doubles the collection on disk, which is the exact cost the design was built to avoid, so it must be a stated choice and never a silent default. OwnGame.cs mirrors Ship-Rules.ps1's foreign-file rules (Ship-Rules.ps1:116-117 says to change the two together), so check whether your edit touches that pairing. C# 5 only.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/fileio/hard-links-and-junctions , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.driveinfo , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-createhardlinkw

## 150. Which browser the in-game interface uses is inferred from another server's file, and recorded nowhere

**Category:** Compatibility  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Substantively exact, with a slightly loose range. tools\app\Chars.cs: PlatformIni is at 717-720, the doc comment explaining the two backends runs 722-763, and WriteBrowserBackend() is 764-828, so the method ends at 828 rather than 830. The decision itself is at 780-781: `bool nirnlab = File.Exists(Path.Combine(Paths.Game, "Data\\NirnLabUIPlatform\\NirnLabUIPlatform.dll"));`, called from line 588. Nothing records the answer anywhere a person can see it. The reasoning at tools\app\Checks.cs:56-90 is also real: lines 61-70 are the note saying NirnLabUIPlugin.dll was on the foreign list, must never go back, and that Data\NirnLabUIPlatform is this build's own browser.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "have WriteBrowserBackend() return the chosen name (the local variable `want`) instead of void, and at its call site (line 588) write that value both to the launcher log ... and into a small field on cfg".  ->  The method has two early returns that would defeat this: tools\app\Chars.cs:768 `if (!File.Exists(PlatformIni)) return;` and, more importantly, line 820 `if (next == text) return;`. Line 820 is the steady state, the ini already says the right thing, which is every normal run after the first. A `return want;` added at the end of the method therefore yields nothing exactly when the new Settings line is meant to show a backend, and the field would read blank on a healthy install. The returns at 768 and 820 have to return the value too, and the `catch { }` at 827 needs an answer as well.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Chars.cs lines 722-828: the full doc comment plus WriteBrowserBackend(). The comment is the hard-won reasoning the entry says must stay, including why both backends draw out of process and why NirnLab wins when present (it puts its own directory on the process PATH). 2. tools\app\Checks.cs lines 56-90: the ForeignNames block and the warning that NirnLabUIPlugin.dll must never be listed as foreign, with the consequence spelled out (every press of Fix deleted the plugin that draws the whole interface). 3. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 lines 135-142: Test-ShipOtherServer, which does treat Data\NirnLabUIPlatform as another server's. Read this third, because it contradicts the other two and you need to know that before you touch anything.

**How:** Do not change the inference. Add a record of it. In tools\app\Chars.cs, have WriteBrowserBackend() return the chosen name (the local variable `want`) instead of void, and at its call site (line 588) write that value both to the launcher log via the existing Log path and into a small field on cfg so it survives a restart. Then surface it: add a read-only line on the Settings page in tools\ui.xaml plus its wiring in Shell.cs, worded as something a tester can quote back, for example "In-game browser: nirnlab" or "In-game browser: tilted". Make sure the value also lands in Profile\launcher.log so a bug report that includes the log answers the question without asking.

**Done when:** Compile with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause`. Then, without starting anything, confirm the two states agree: `Test-Path 'C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\NirnLabUIPlatform\NirnLabUIPlatform.dll'` tells you which backend the code would pick, and `Select-String -Path 'C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\SkyrimPlatform.ini' -Pattern 'BackendName'` tells you what was last written. If those two disagree on this machine, that is itself the finding. After the change, `Select-String -Path 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\launcher.log' -Pattern 'browser'` should show the recorded choice on the next run (which a person does, not you).

**Traps:** Chars.cs is not on CLAUDE.md's protected list but Shell.cs and Checks.cs are (line 79), and touching the Settings page means touching Shell.cs, so post the #dev line. C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. The repo currently contradicts itself about NirnLabUIPlatform: Checks.cs:61-70 says it is ours and must never be treated as foreign, Chars.cs:735-737 says it is not ours, and Ship-Rules.ps1:141-142 excludes it from every pack and installer. Do not "fix" that contradiction as a side effect of this entry; note it and raise it. The file being written, SkyrimPlatform.ini, is not the launcher's own file: the comment at Chars.cs:822-824 records that it arrives with a byte order mark and the encoding must be preserved, so keep `new UTF8Encoding(true)`. Do not add NirnLabUIPlugin.dll to any foreign list; the comment says a previous attempt deleted the plugin that draws the whole interface.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/api/winbase/nf-winbase-getprivateprofilestringw , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.text.utf8encoding

## 151. The gamemode registers four event handlers, so nothing server-side watches for griefing

**Category:** Moderation  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact and complete. `grep '^mp\.on' tools/gamemode.js` returns precisely four lines: mp.onDeath at 1581, mp.onRespawn at 1618, mp.onReadBook at 1627 and mp.onActivate at 1789. All four are wrapped in the same `guard(...)` helper. There is no handler for item creation, inventory change, chat or any administrative action, so nothing server-side sees griefing.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "There is no handler for item creation, inventory change, chat or any administrative action, so nothing server-side sees griefing." Marked [verified].  ->  The chat and administrative half is false. tools\gamemode.js:2773-3363 is a server-side chat command handler (/ooc, /where, /tp, /goto, /help, /restart). /tp and /goto are gated by an allow list: `if (!isAdmin(profileId)) { toProfile(profileId, 'That one is not yours to run.'); return; }` at 2928-2931, with isAdmin defined at 3106. /restart is deliberately refused at 2969-2975, and the comment at 2956-2968 spells out why (the profile id is a claim the page makes, not something the server checked). So administrative actions are handled, allow-listed and reasoned about server-side today. The four `mp.on*` count (1581, 1618, 1627, 1789) is right; the conclusion drawn from it is not.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "register the remaining mp.on* events SkyMP exposes, and in each, resolve the acting profile, check it against an allow list, and return false unless..." offered as the way to stop item creation griefing.  ->  Item creation does not arrive as an mp.on* event, so this fix cannot reach it. tools\gamemode.js:2777-2784 records the mechanism: "SkyMP's client forwards exactly five console commands to the server, in consoleCommandsService.ts: additem, equipitem, placeatme, disable and mp... There is no setting for this and no way to add a sixth command without recompiling the client." The shipped bundle confirms it: additem, equipitem and placeatme all appear in C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js. Anyone following this howTo would add mp.on* handlers and still leave additem and placeatme untouched, which is the one thing the entry names.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js lines 1780-1815: mp.onActivate, the one handler that already makes an allow/deny decision. The comment at 1787-1788 records the rule that matters, that an explicit false counts as a block and falling off the end of the function is the safe answer, which is the shape any new gate must follow. 2. tools\gamemode.js lines 100-160: the log() and notify() helpers, because every allowed action has to be logged through the existing one rather than a new mechanism. 3. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md: what the world's consent rules actually say, since the point of the work is to put something behind them.

**How:** Work inside tools\gamemode.js. First write down, as a comment block near the handlers, the list of things a client is allowed to ask the server to do. Then add handlers for the actions that are currently unwatched and make each one default to deny: register the remaining mp.on* events SkyMP exposes, and in each, resolve the acting profile, check it against an allow list, and return false unless the action is permitted. Item creation in particular should be admin-only and logged by name and quantity through the existing log() helper. Keep every new handler wrapped in the same `guard(name, fn)` used at 1581, 1618, 1627 and 1789 so a thrown error cannot take the server down. Log what was allowed, not only what was blocked, because the audit trail is the point.

**Done when:** Without starting a server: `node --check tools\gamemode.js` parses cleanly, and `Select-String -Path tools\gamemode.js -Pattern '^mp\.on'` now lists more than four handlers, each one visibly wrapped in guard(). Then confirm the deny-by-default shape by reading each new handler for an explicit `return false` on the unhandled path. A real behavioural test needs the dev server, which is a person's action via Ship.ps1 -Channel dev after a note in #dev.

**Traps:** This is a server change with real blast radius: a handler that returns false too eagerly blocks ordinary doors, chests and items for everybody at once, which is exactly what the comment at 1787-1788 warns about. gamemode.js is 3,363 lines of six concatenated programs with no version, no lint and no tests (entry 130), so anything you add sits in a file nobody can bisect; add it near the other handlers, not at the end under a new banner. Entry 178 records that most script events are blocked server-side already, so check which events actually fire before writing a handler for one that never will. Deploying the gamemode is Deploy-Gamemode.ps1, which is a VPS script and therefore a person's command. CLAUDE.md's one-complete-release rule applies if any of this needs a matching client plugin change. Say customers, people or testers in any in-game message text, never "player".

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html

## 152. The two exclusion lists have drifted again, and packs ship ninety files the installer deliberately leaves out

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The line citations are exact and the drift is real, but the mechanism described is not quite what is in the repo, and the count is unverifiable. Exact: Publish-Update.ps1:331-335 are $vanillaDataFiles, $vanillaDataPatterns, $vanillaDataDirs, $vanillaRootFiles, $vanillaRootDirs; Package-Thornswood.ps1:133-186 is the same family plus extras; Ship-Rules.ps1:1-20 is the header recording the auth-data-no-load.js night. Not as stated: both scripts already dot-source Ship-Rules.ps1 (Publish-Update.ps1:239, Package-Thornswood.ps1:215) and both use Test-ShipRuntime / Test-ShipMachine / Test-ShipOtherServer from it, so the shared list does exist. What has drifted is the vanilla-and-foreign lists that are still inline in each script: Package-Thornswood has $foreignDataDirs (NirnLabUIPlatform, Nemesis_Engine, FNIS Behavior SE 7.6, CalienteTools, tools, Source, ModderResource, FOMod, Docs, Docs and Screenshots, readmes, ShaderCache), eight extra root files (skse64_loader.exe, skse64_1_6_1170.dll, skse64_readme.txt, skse64_whatsnew.txt, VERSION, steam_appid.txt, imgui.ini, d3dx9_42.log) and two extra root dirs (BStarData, DaedricData); Publish-Update has none of them.
