The "ninety files" figure cannot be checked from the source: it depends on which files changed since the last baseline. For scale, the live game folder holds 11,116 files under the dirs Package-Thornswood excludes and Publish-Update does not, plus six root files and a six-file BStarData.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1, the whole header (lines 1-24) and then the three Test-Ship* functions: the header states the rule this entry is about, that two lists which must agree will not, and names the night it cost. 2. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 lines 133-215: the full inline list plus the dot-source of Ship-Rules, so you can see exactly which parts are shared and which are not. 3. tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 236-360: the matching region, the dot-source at 239 and the thin wrappers at 354-356, to see what is missing.

**How:** Move the inline lists into tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 as new exported tests, following the naming already there: add Test-ShipVanilla($rel) covering $vanillaDataFiles, $vanillaDataPatterns, $vanillaDataDirs, $vanillaRootFiles, $vanillaRootDirs, and Test-ShipForeignFramework($rel) covering $foreignDataDirs. Delete the inline copies from both tools\Publish-Update.ps1 (331-335 and its Test-Vanilla) and tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 (133-186 and its Test-Vanilla), and replace each with a one-line wrapper exactly like Publish-Update.ps1:354-356 already does. Take the union of the two current lists, not the intersection, and add a comment for each entry saying why it is there, because several of them (skse64_*, BStarData, DaedricData) have reasons already written in Package-Thornswood's comments that would otherwise be lost.

**Done when:** Parse both scripts with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` (the same check .github/workflows/build.yml runs) and confirm no errors. Then prove the lists are gone: `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Update.ps1,tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 -Pattern 'vanillaDataFiles|foreignDataDirs'` should hit only the wrapper lines, and `Select-String -Path tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 -Pattern 'vanillaDataFiles|foreignDataDirs'` should hit the definitions. Then dot-source Ship-Rules.ps1 in a scratch session and assert the functions agree on a sample of real relative paths, for example `Test-ShipVanilla 'Data\Skyrim.esm'`, `Test-ShipForeignFramework 'Data\ShaderCache\x.bin'`.

**Traps:** Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79): a #dev line before the branch. Ship-Rules.ps1's own header is the standing instruction that this must be one list, so it is the file the fix belongs in, not a third one. There is a live contradiction to walk into carefully: Ship-Rules.ps1:141-142 already excludes Data\NirnLabUIPlatform as another server's, Package-Thornswood.ps1:146-159 calls it another roleplay server's UI framework, and tools\app\Checks.cs:61-70 says the opposite, that it is this build's own browser and must never be treated as foreign. Merging the lists will make that contradiction load-bearing, so raise it in #dev rather than quietly picking a side. Verifying the ninety-file claim means inspecting a built pack, which is a person's action; do not run Publish-Update.ps1 or Package-Thornswood.ps1. Entry 8 (redistribution) depends on this mechanism, so they are one piece of work.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_scripts#script-scope-and-dot-sourcing , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/get-filehash

## 153. What the eighteen-plus gate actually achieves, and what adult content obliges

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All four citations are exact. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:471-475 is the ChkAge CheckBox with the text "I am 18 or over. Thornswood is an adult server."; 524-529 is the code that finds it and gates btnGo.IsEnabled on it, with the comment at 520-523 saying a box somebody can ignore is decoration so it gates the button. tools\discord\bot.py:320-352 is AGE_Q, AGE_KINDS, the _NO/_YES tuples and said_yes(), whose docstring already says it never rejects anything by itself and the worst it does is put a line at the top of the thread. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md carries the eighteen-and-over section starting at the heading on line 10.

**Read first:** 1. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md lines 10-26: the statement as people actually see it, including the sentence that applying, being in the Discord or connecting is a statement of age. This is the text everything else has to match. 2. tools\discord\bot.py lines 314-355: AGE_Q and said_yes(), whose comment at 316-319 already states the honest position, that it is a record rather than a gate because Discord modals have no tickbox. 3. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs lines 465-535: the installer checkbox and the button gate, and the else branch at 531-535 that enables Install anyway if the control is missing.

**How:** Three small changes, no new machinery. (a) Terms page: add a plain paragraph to the site's terms or rules page saying what the server is and that the checkbox and the Discord answer are kept as a record of a statement the person made, not as verification. tools\docs-app\data\site\rules.md already has the right words at lines 12-13; extend rather than duplicate. (b) Put the statement on the door: make sure the adult-server line appears before somebody downloads or applies, not only inside the installer, which means the site's home and the Discord join card, not just Bootstrap.cs:475. (c) Use the platform's own controls: mark the Discord channels that carry adult content as age-restricted in Discord's own settings, which is a server-settings action a person does, and note it in the bot's README so it is not lost. Leave said_yes() generous as it is; its docstring already justifies that.

**Done when:** Read-only: `Select-String -Path tools\docs-app\data\site\*.md -Pattern 'eighteen|18 or over'` shows the statement on the terms page as well as rules.md, and `Select-String -Path tools\docs-app\public\home.html -Pattern '18|eighteen'` shows it before the download. `python -m py_compile tools\discord\bot.py` still passes if you touched the bot. `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` still produces an exe if you touched Bootstrap.cs. No game, no bot restart needed.

**Traps:** Bootstrap.cs is C# 5 under the .NET Framework 4 csc like the rest of the launcher: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. The XAML at 465-475 lives inside a C# verbatim string with doubled quotes, so every `"` in markup is `""`; get that wrong and the build fails. The else branch at Bootstrap.cs:531-535 deliberately enables Install when the checkbox control cannot be found, so do not tighten that into a hard failure without thinking about the installer nobody can start. Site page text is people-facing: CLAUDE.md's Words section forbids em dashes and the word "player". Entry 6 is the one automated check here and it is recorded as going quiet in the case it exists for, so do not assume it covers you. Do not write anything that claims the server verifies age; it does not, and saying so would be worse than saying nothing.

**Links:** https://discord.com/developers/docs/resources/channel , https://discordpy.readthedocs.io/en/stable/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/controls/checkbox-overview

## 154. Trademark and naming: the disclaimer is right, the README still says the old mod, and Thornswood is unregistered

**Category:** Legal  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The verdict is right and the README half is exact, but two of the three citations do not support the claim. Verified: README.md line 1 is `# the old mod`. Verified: the Bethesda disclaimer exists and is correct, at tools\docs-app\data\site\credits.md lines 12-14, "Thornswood is not affiliated with or endorsed by Bethesda Softworks. The Elder Scrolls and Skyrim are their trademarks, and you need your own copy to play." Wrong: tools\docs-app\public\home.html contains no occurrence of "The old mod" at all. Line 7 is a meta description ("A Skyrim roleplay server set fifty years after the civil war...") and line 106 is a CSS comment about the hero artwork. The landing page does carry its own Bethesda disclaimer, at home.html:568. So there is nothing to do on home.html, and the only action really is README.md, which the entry already points at entry 86.

**Read first:** 1. README.md lines 1-10: the title and opening, the single thing that needs changing. 2. tools\docs-app\data\site\credits.md lines 8-16: the disclaimer as written, which is the wording to keep and reuse if it is ever needed elsewhere. 3. tools\docs-app\public\home.html around line 568: the landing page's own copy of the disclaimer, so you can confirm for yourself that the public door is already covered.

**How:** Change README.md line 1 from `# the old mod` to `# Thornswood`, and read the first paragraph underneath for any other stale naming (it currently opens "Five player co-op Skyrim", which also breaks the Words rule by using "player"). That is the whole action. Do not touch credits.md or home.html; both disclaimers are already correct and in the right place. Leave the unregistered-name question alone: a fan project name with no registration is normal and is not worth money at this stage, which is what the entry itself concludes.

**Done when:** `Get-Content README.md -TotalCount 1` returns `# Thornswood`, and `Select-String -Path README.md -Pattern 'the old mod'` returns nothing. Confirm the disclaimers are untouched with `Select-String -Path tools\docs-app\data\site\credits.md,tools\docs-app\public\home.html -Pattern 'Bethesda'`, which should still return two hits.

**Traps:** README.md is people-facing, so CLAUDE.md's Words section applies to the whole file, not just line 1: no em dashes (there are seven in it today, see entry 156) and never "player". This overlaps entry 86 and entry 156, so do all three in one pass rather than three pull requests that each touch README.md, which would otherwise collide. Do not remove or reword the Bethesda disclaimer while you are in there; it is correct as written and it is the one legally useful sentence on the site. Do not add a trademark symbol or a registration claim to the Thornswood name: it is unregistered and saying otherwise would be a false statement.

**Links:** https://www.uspto.gov/trademarks/basics , https://help.bethesda.net/

## 155. A secret scan over the working tree, the built pack and the built installer, as a gate

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** All three anchors confirmed, with one detail worth knowing. tools\discord holds exactly two webhook files in clear on disk, news-webhook.txt and dev-webhook.txt, plus dev-channel.txt; all three are in .gitignore (lines naming news-webhook.txt and dev-webhook.txt explicitly, with the reason written out), so they are not committed. The repo root holds server-secret.txt (also gitignored) and config.example.json (committed, and an example by design). tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 lines 10-14 record the night: the installer carried Data\Platform\PluginsNoLoad\auth-data-no-load.js, the SkyMP client's saved sign-in token from the machine it was built on. Additional finding the entry does not mention: two compiled Python files are genuinely tracked in git, tools/discord/__pycache__/bot.cpython-39.pyc and content.cpython-39.pyc, which is entry 168's other half.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 lines 1-24: the header is the incident report and the standing rule, and it names the exact file that shipped. 2. .gitignore, all of it: it already covers config.json, server-secret.txt, the two webhook files, *.exe, *.7z, *.zip and logs, so you can see what the tree-level scan would still be adding on top. 3. .github/workflows/build.yml: the only CI that exists, and the file the scan step gets added to.

**How:** Two halves. Free half first: switch on the repository's own secret scanning and push protection in GitHub settings for both Thornswood and Thornswood-Releases. That is a person's action in the web UI, so show them where it is rather than doing it. Second half: add a job to .github/workflows/build.yml (or a second workflow file) that runs a secret scanner over the working tree on every pull request and fails the build on a hit, with a config file in the repo listing the known-safe example values so config.example.json does not fail it forever. Then extend it to the artefacts: after the launcher build step, unpack the built exe or pack and run the same scan over the extracted contents, because the auth-data-no-load.js incident was in a built artefact, not in the source. Scanning the artefact is the part that is missing everywhere today.

**Done when:** Push a branch and confirm the new job goes green on a clean tree and red on a deliberately planted dummy value in a scratch file (use an obviously fake string, never a real credential). Locally, before pushing: `git ls-files | Select-String 'webhook|secret|\.env|config\.json'` should return nothing but tools/app/Secrets.cs, and `git log --all --name-only | Select-String 'webhook'` should show the webhook files were never committed historically either.

**Traps:** Never print, paste, echo or log any of these values, including in CI output: a scanner that prints the match it found has just published it in the build log. Configure the scanner to report file and line only. The two webhook files and server-secret.txt exist on disk on the shipping machine and are correctly gitignored; do not delete them, they are what Publish-Discord reads. Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79), so if the artefact scan needs a hook there, post the #dev line. A scan over the built pack means building a pack, which is Publish-Update.ps1 and Package-Thornswood.ps1, both person-runs-it; design the CI step so it scans what the workflow itself built rather than asking anybody to run a publish. Entries 2 and 3 are what the absence of this looks like in practice, and the tracked .pyc files are a live example of something that got in despite .gitignore.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/secret-scanning/introduction/about-secret-scanning , https://docs.github.com/en/code-security/secret-scanning/introduction/about-push-protection , https://github.com/gitleaks/gitleaks , https://trufflesecurity.com/trufflehog

## 156. The repo's own writing rules are written down and nothing enforces them

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is right, the count is wrong, and the other files named are clean today. README.md contains seven em dashes, not four. tools\app\Help.cs, tools\ui.xaml, tools\discord\content.py and every tools\docs-app\data\site\*.md page contain zero em dashes. On the banned-word half: "player" appears once in README.md and once in tools\app\Help.cs, and not at all in ui.xaml or content.py. So the lint would have exactly nine things to say today, eight of them in README.md, which makes it a cheap change and an easy first green build.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md lines 20-26, the Words section: the actual rule (customers, clients, people, testers, never "player", no em dashes, no filler, no placeholders, secrets named by location only), which is what the lint encodes. 2. README.md, all of it: every violation in the repo bar one is in this file, so it doubles as the test corpus. 3. .github/workflows/build.yml: the existing pwsh-step style the lint job should match, including the `::error file=,line=::` annotation format already used by the script-parse step.

**How:** Write tools\Check-Words.ps1, read-only, about twenty lines. Take a list of paths that hold text people read: README.md, SETUP.md, tools\app\Help.cs, tools\ui.xaml, tools\discord\content.py, tools\docs-app\data\site\*.md, tools\docs-app\public\home.html. For each, Select-String for the em dash character, for `\bplayers?\b` case-insensitively, and for the old owner name (entry 137). Emit each hit as `::error file={path},line={line}::{message}` so GitHub annotates it, and exit 1 if any were found. Add it as a step in .github/workflows/build.yml beside the existing parse step. Then fix the nine current hits: replace the seven em dashes in README.md with a comma, a colon or a full stop as the sentence wants, and reword the two uses of "player" (README's "Five player co-op" becomes "five person co-op" or "co-op for five").

**Done when:** `.\tools\Check-Words.ps1` exits 0 on a clean tree and lists exactly the nine current hits before you fix them. Reproduce the current count yourself with `(Select-String -Path README.md -Pattern ([char]0x2014) -AllMatches | ForEach-Object { $_.Matches } | Measure-Object).Count`, which should read 7 before and 0 after. The GitHub build check running the new step green on the pull request is the real proof. Entirely read-only, no game.

**Traps:** Match the em dash by code point ([char]0x2014), not by pasting the character into a PowerShell script, because the file encoding of the script itself will otherwise decide whether the check works. En dashes and hyphens are not banned, so do not widen the pattern. The word check needs word boundaries or it will fire on "multiplayer", which is a correct word used throughout Checks.cs and the UI. Keep the script read-only: CLAUDE.md lets a Claude run Check-*.ps1 precisely because they change nothing, so do not add an auto-fix mode that rewrites files. Do not scan lore\ with the same rules; in-world writing is not operational text and the rules section is about what people read as documentation and interface. The name check overlaps entry 137, and the README fixes overlap entries 86 and 154, so coordinate rather than opening three pull requests that all edit README.md.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/actions/using-workflows/workflow-commands-for-github-actions#setting-an-error-message , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/select-string , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/base-types/regular-expression-language-quick-reference

## 157. Nobody has ever installed this on a clean machine and written down what happened

**Category:** Testing  |  **Fact check:** unverifiable

**Correction:** The size claim is exact: tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 is 864 lines and tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs is 1,045, so 1,909 lines of installer. Whether anybody has ever run it on a clean machine is a claim about history that cannot be settled from the repository. What can be said: there is no record of such a run anywhere in the tree. A search for "clean machine", "clean install", "fresh vm" or "virtual machine" across every .md file in the repo returns nothing, so if the run happened, it was not written down, which for the purpose of this entry amounts to the same thing.

**Read first:** 1. tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs, the Start() path and everything it calls: this is the sequence a stranger actually walks, and it is where the stopwatch stops and starts. 2. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 lines 110-230: what goes into the installer and what is deliberately left out, so you know what the clean machine is supposed to receive. 3. SETUP.md and tools\docs-app\data\site pages: the written instructions a first-time person is given, which are the script the walkthrough is testing.

**How:** This is a human exercise, not a code change, and it is the person's to run. Prepare it so it can be executed in one sitting: write tools\CLEAN-INSTALL-RUN.md as a numbered checklist, one line per step, each with a place to record the wall-clock time and the exact text of any prompt or error seen. Steps in order: fresh Windows VM, fresh Steam account, buy or license Skyrim Special Edition, fresh Nexus account, install Vortex, install the collection, download the Thornswood installer, run it, and stop at the point where the launcher's Play page is drawn (do not start the game). Add a column for "what a stranger would reasonably do instead", because the prompts that are wrong are the ones where the honest answer is not obvious. Where a step can be automated (fetching the installer, checking hashes, verifying the nine Checks.cs rows), script it in a read-only Check-*.ps1 so the human checklist shrinks each time it is run.

**Done when:** The file tools\CLEAN-INSTALL-RUN.md exists, and after the first real run every row has a time and an observation in it. The checklist itself is done when a competent stranger can follow it with no questions; the cheapest proof of that is handing it to the second developer and seeing whether they ask anything. The automated parts are done when `.\tools\Check-Manifest.ps1` and `.\tools\Check-Build.ps1` (both read-only) pass against the artefact the run produced.

**Traps:** You cannot do the run yourself: CLAUDE.md says the person runs anything that leaves the machine and that a Claude never starts the launcher or the game. Write the checklist, show the commands, hand it over. The installer path being tested must be the one that ships: today that is the Steam copy with Vortex, because the own-game-folder branch (djinn/own-game-folder) is not on main, and CLAUDE.md says the two shapes must not be mixed in one release. Testing the wrong shape produces a runbook for something nobody has. A clean VM will not have the Address Library or Start On Save unless the collection delivers them, which is exactly what entries 146 and 147 are about, so expect those to fail and record them rather than fixing them mid-run. Keep every credential out of the written record: name the account, never the password. Entry 134 is the restore runbook with the same "until it has been run it is a plan" logic, and the two should be written to the same standard.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/dev-environment/ , https://developer.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/downloads/virtual-machines/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/virtualization/hyper-v-on-windows/quick-start/enable-hyper-v

## 158. The overlay rescans the whole document on every change and writes files on every key press

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Every line citation is exact, but one of the two headline claims is overstated. Exact: tools\ts-ui\thornswood-bridge.js:458-474 is trim() plus `new MutationObserver(trim).observe(document.documentElement, { childList: true, subtree: true })`, which really is the whole document; 479-491 is draw(), which pushes a fresh array and then calls hideNonRp(), trim() and blurInput() in a setTimeout; 277-283 is a `setInterval(..., 2000)` typing-idle poll. Exact: tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js:119-135 are readChannel/writeChannel, both synchronous fs.readFileSync/writeFileSync; 509-514 is writePtt; 527-530 is the `sp.on('update', ...)` per-frame handler. Overstated: it does not write on every key press. writePtt dedupes at line 511 with `if (line === lastPttWritten) { return; }`, so it writes only when the push-to-talk state or mode actually changes. The real per-frame cost is at 527-530, a synchronous file read inside the render-thread update handler, throttled to once every 400 ms, plus the document-wide observer.

**Read first:** 1. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-bridge.js lines 440-495: the comment at 453-457 admits the current design, that removing nodes does not depend on anything because React puts them back and this takes them out again, a few nodes a second while chat is redrawn. That comment is the argument you are arguing against. 2. tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js lines 110-140 and 500-545: CHANNEL_DIRS, the sync read/write helpers, and the sp.on('update') handler that calls them. 3. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js: the other consumer of the same page, so a scoped observer does not break it.

**How:** Four changes, each independently shippable. (a) Scope the observer: in bridge.js, wait for the chat container element to exist and call observe() on that node instead of document.documentElement, keeping `{ childList: true, subtree: true }` but on a far smaller tree. (b) Stop removing and reinserting: instead of el.parentNode.removeChild(el) in trim(), inject a stylesheet once that sets `display: none` on the same selectors in HIDE, so React's re-render is a no-op rather than a fight. (c) Budget the per-frame work: wrap trim() in a requestAnimationFrame-coalesced callback so several mutations in one frame produce one pass. (d) Get the disk off the render thread: in voice.js, replace the readFileSync inside the sp.on('update') handler with a value refreshed by a timer using the async fs.readFile, and have the update handler read the cached value only. Leave writePtt's dedupe at line 511 exactly as it is; it is already correct.

**Done when:** `node --check tools\ts-ui\thornswood-bridge.js` and `node --check tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js` both pass. Then read-only greps prove the shape changed: `Select-String -Path tools\ts-ui\thornswood-bridge.js -Pattern 'document.documentElement'` returns nothing, `Select-String -Path tools\ts-ui\thornswood-bridge.js -Pattern 'removeChild'` returns nothing inside trim(), and `Select-String -Path tools\ts-plugins\thornswood-voice.js -Pattern 'readFileSync'` no longer appears inside the sp.on('update') callback. Real frame-time proof needs the game, which is not allowed here, so hand the before-and-after to the person to try on the dev channel.

**Traps:** These files ship to every machine as loose files in Data\Platform, and CLAUDE.md requires every change to go out as one complete release, launcher, game files and server together. The plugins idle unless the client points at our server and a fresh session note is present, so do not add work that runs before that gate. The comment at bridge.js:453-457 records the reasoning behind the current approach; if you change it, update the comment rather than leaving a stale justification. The in-game browser may be either tilted CEF or NirnLab depending on what is on disk (Chars.cs:764-828), so anything you rely on has to work on both, and there is currently nothing recording which one ran (entry 150), which makes a performance report hard to attribute. CHANNEL_DIRS in voice.js writes to two directories on purpose (Data/Platform and ../Profile), per the comment above line 117; do not collapse it to one. Do not start the game to measure.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MutationObserver , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/MutationObserver/observe , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/requestAnimationFrame , https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html#fsreadfilesyncpath-options , https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html#fsreadfilepath-options-callback , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/visibility

## 159. Ten small things worth sweeping while you are already in these files

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Every location cited is real code and each one has something worth cleaning, but the entry never names the ten things, and it cites eight sites, so a stranger cannot act on it without re-deriving the list. What is actually at each: gamemode.js:122-129, notify() swallows the Papyrus call failure with a comment saying it is expected; gamemode.js:1806-1807, onActivate sends the same text twice, once via notify() and once via say(); gamemode.js:3257, `var REQUEST = 'data/restart.json'`, a path relative to the process working directory; thornswood-chat.js:117-119, `if (msg.t !== 'chat') { fanout(msg); return; }` immediately followed by `if (msg.t === 'chat') {`, a condition that is always true; voice-host.html:98-102, ws.onclose reconnects on a fixed 2000 ms timer with no backoff; voice-server.js:159, `if (op !== 0x1) continue;` so WebSocket close frames (0x8) are ignored; voice-server.js:242-251, readBody calls req.destroy() when the body exceeds the limit and never sends a response; voice-server.js:444-450, the /health route reports peers, tracked, waitingChoices and queuedCommands but no version and no uptime.

**Read first:** 1. tools\gamemode.js lines 100-160: the log/notify/say helpers, because three of the cleanups are about which of them gets called and what happens when one fails. 2. tools\voice\voice-server.js lines 140-260: the hand-rolled WebSocket frame loop and readBody, the two places where the protocol is being implemented by hand and small omissions matter. 3. tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js lines 100-135: the message dispatch, the clearest of the dead branches and the easiest first commit.

**How:** Before anything else, rewrite the entry itself into a numbered list of the eight or ten concrete items, one line each, with file and line, so the work is reviewable. Then, in one branch per file, not one per item: in thornswood-chat.js delete the redundant `if (msg.t === 'chat')` wrapper at line 119 and de-indent its body; in voice-server.js:242-251 respond with 413 before destroying the request, and at line 159 handle opcode 0x8 by replying with a close frame and tearing the socket down; add version and uptime to the /health payload at 444-450 so the watchdog can tell a restart from a hang. In voice-host.html:98-102 replace the flat 2000 ms with an exponential backoff capped at about 30 seconds. In gamemode.js resolve REQUEST at 3257 against a path derived from the script rather than the working directory, and pick one of notify() or say() at 1806-1807 rather than sending both.

**Done when:** `node --check` passes on tools\gamemode.js, tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js and tools\voice\voice-server.js. Per item: `Select-String -Path tools\ts-ui\thornswood-chat.js -Pattern "msg.t === 'chat'"` returns nothing; `Select-String -Path tools\voice\voice-server.js -Pattern '413'` returns a hit inside readBody; `Select-String -Path tools\voice\voice-server.js -Pattern 'uptime'` returns a hit inside the /health route. The health change can be proved end to end without the game by starting the voice server locally on a spare port and curling /health, which touches no VPS and no game.

**Traps:** gamemode.js is 3,363 lines of six concatenated programs with no version, no lint and no tests (entry 130), so a change here has no safety net; keep each edit surgical and near the code it touches rather than appending a new section. Deploying the gamemode or the voice server is a VPS action and belongs to the person. The client-side files ship to every machine, so CLAUDE.md's one-complete-release rule applies: a chat.js change goes out with the launcher and the server, not on its own. voice-server.js implements WebSocket framing by hand, so changing opcode handling can break every connected client at once if you get the response frame wrong; read RFC 6455 section 5.5.1 before touching line 159. Do not fold this entry into a larger refactor; its whole value is that it is an afternoon's sweep while somebody is already in these files for a bigger entry.

**Links:** https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6455#section-5.5.1 , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/WebSocket/close_event , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Status/413 , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html#event-clienterror , https://nodejs.org/api/process.html#processcwd

## 160. Twelve files carry old-executable fallbacks with no removal date, and the updater never renames an old install

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The three C# citations are exact and the finding is real, but the count is thirteen, not twelve. Exact: tools\app\Icons.cs:60 is `foreach (string n in new[] { "Thornswood.exe", "OldMod.exe" })`; tools\app\Uninstall.cs:365 is `foreach (string name in new[] { "Thornswood.lnk", "OldMod.lnk" })`; tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs:891 is `foreach (string n in new string[] { "SkyrimSE", "skse64_loader", "OldMod" })`. The full set of files carrying an old-executable, old-shortcut or old-process-name fallback is thirteen: tools\app\Icons.cs, tools\app\Links.cs, tools\app\Uninstall.cs, tools\setup\Bootstrap.cs, tools\Build-Launcher.ps1, tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1, tools\Close-Launcher.ps1, tools\Fix-Icons.ps1, tools\Move-Install.ps1, tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1, tools\Publish-Update.ps1, tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and tools\Ship.ps1. One of those, Build-Thornswood.ps1, is slated for deletion anyway by entry 166, which would bring it to twelve.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Updater.cs: the update flow that has to learn to rename, and the file that has to change first, before any fallback can be deleted. It is on CLAUDE.md's protected list. 2. tools\app\Icons.cs lines 50-68: the smallest and clearest example of the pattern, an ordered probe that prefers Thornswood.exe and falls back to OldMod.exe. 3. tools\app\Uninstall.cs lines 350-372: the shortcut cleanup, which is the other half, because renaming the exe without repointing the shortcut leaves a dead Start Menu entry.

**How:** Ship the migration first, then the deletions, in that order and as two releases. Migration: in tools\app\Updater.cs, when the running process path ends in OldMod.exe, write the downloaded build to Thornswood.exe in the same folder, repoint the Start Menu and desktop shortcuts to it (the same spots Uninstall.cs:350-372 already enumerates), and schedule the old file for deletion on the next start since a running exe cannot delete itself. Once that build has been out long enough that nobody is still on an older one, delete the fallbacks: the second array element at Icons.cs:60, the "OldMod.lnk" element at Uninstall.cs:365, the "OldMod" process name at Bootstrap.cs:891, and the corresponding lines in the ten scripts. Put a removal date in a comment on the migration code itself so the second half does not get forgotten.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces an exe after each step. After the deletions, `(Select-String -Path tools\app\*.cs,tools\setup\*.cs,tools\*.ps1 -Pattern 'OldMod\.exe|"OldMod"|OldMod\.lnk' | Measure-Object).Count` is zero. Reproduce the current count first so you know what you started from: the same command returns thirteen distinct files today. The rename behaviour itself needs a real install to prove, which is the person's to run, not yours.

**Traps:** Updater.cs is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79): a line in #dev before the branch, and the entry itself says so. Never delete the fallbacks in the same release as the migration: a person who has not updated since 12 September runs the old exe, and if the fallback is gone before their launcher has learned to rename itself they are stranded with no route to a fix. Two releases, with a gap. Uninstall.cs and Icons.cs both probe for the old name for different reasons (self-location versus shortcut cleanup) and deleting one without the other leaves a half-migrated install. tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1 is in the list but entry 166 says to delete the whole file, so do not spend time editing it. Every change here goes out as one complete release under CLAUDE.md, and the shortcut repoint touches files outside the install folder, so be conservative about what gets deleted.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.file.move , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/shell/links , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.diagnostics.process.getprocessesbyname

## 161. Ship-Rules still lists two old runtime folders as ours, and the rest of that family has no rule at all

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact on every point. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1:39-40 are `'Data\OldModReborn\cache\',` and `'Data\OldModReborn\logs\',`, both inside $ShipRuntimeDirs, which the header at lines 26-29 describes as things written at runtime by the game or a tool, treated as Thornswood's own output. Line 116-117 is the comment the entry refers to: "OwnGame.Foreign in the launcher leaves the same files out of Thornswood's own game folder; change the two together." And the rest of the OldModReborn family has no rule at all: Test-ShipOtherServer covers the other server and Daedric by name and prefix, but nothing matches Data\OldModReborn generally.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 lines 26-45: the $ShipRuntimeDirs list and the comment above it explaining the consequence, that leaving a folder out here makes the next release list its files as removed and the launcher take them off every install. That consequence is why moving an entry between lists is not cosmetic. 2. tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 lines 108-145: Test-ShipOtherServer, the list this belongs in, and the comment at 116-117 telling you to mirror it. 3. tools\app\OwnGame.cs, the Foreign list: the launcher-side mirror that the comment says must change at the same time.

**How:** Delete lines 39 and 40 from $ShipRuntimeDirs in tools\Ship-Rules.ps1. Add one prefix rule to Test-ShipOtherServer instead, alongside the existing otherserver-backup pattern, matching the whole family: `if ($rel -match '^Data\\OldModReborn($|\\)') { return $true }`. Add a comment saying what it is (the old mod Reborn's own runtime folder, another multiplayer client's, never ours) and why it is in the other-server list rather than the runtime list (other-server files are never shipped and never removed, runtime files are listed as removed and swept off installs). Then mirror the same prefix into the Foreign list in tools\app\OwnGame.cs, because the comment at Ship-Rules.ps1:116-117 says to change the two together.

**Done when:** Parse the script: `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile` over tools\Ship-Rules.ps1 reports no errors, which is the same check the GitHub build runs. Then dot-source it in a scratch session and assert the behaviour directly: `. .\tools\Ship-Rules.ps1; Test-ShipRuntime 'Data\OldModReborn\cache\x.dat'` returns False and `Test-ShipOtherServer 'Data\OldModReborn\cache\x.dat'` returns True, and the same pair for a path under Data\OldModReborn\logs and for any other subfolder of it. Dot-sourcing Ship-Rules.ps1 runs no publish and touches nothing.

**Traps:** Moving an entry between the two lists changes behaviour in a way the file itself documents: a runtime-list folder gets its files listed as removed in the next release and swept off every install, while an other-server folder is never shipped and never removed. That difference is the entire point, so do not move it back if a pack suddenly stops listing removals. Publish-Update.ps1 dot-sources this file and is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79), so post the #dev line even though you are editing Ship-Rules.ps1 rather than Publish-Update.ps1 itself. The mirror in OwnGame.cs is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Use an anchored prefix match, not a bare substring: Ship-Rules.ps1:118 already warns against the bare word daedric for exactly this reason, and a loose pattern here would catch ordinary files. Both machines ship, so the rule has to be in the shared file or a pack from the other machine can still carry it.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_comparison_operators#-match , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/base-types/regular-expression-language-quick-reference

## 162. The guard that stops the VPS provisioning script running on a workstation tests a folder that no longer exists

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact. tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1:41-43 is `if (Test-Path 'C:\OldMod\Game\SkyrimSE.exe') { throw "SkyrimSE.exe is present, so this looks like the gaming PC, not the VPS. Nothing was changed." }`, under the comment "# --- refuse to run on the gaming machine". Confirmed on this machine that C:\OldMod does not exist; the install is at C:\Thornswood (Game, Launcher, Profile). So the guard cannot fire, and the script would go on to the Node block at lines 75-85 and install Node and Python as though it were the VPS.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 lines 28-50: the parameter block and the guard, so you see what the script is about to do immediately after the check it is failing to make. 2. tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 lines 50-130: Install-Msi, the Node block and the pwsh/node/git verification at 129, the actual damage a mis-fire would do on a workstation. 3. tools\MOVING-THE-SERVER.md lines 1-40: what the VPS is and how it differs from a workstation, which is where a positive identifying property comes from.

**How:** Replace the negative test at tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1:41-43 with a positive one. Query the operating system product type: `$pt = (Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).ProductType` returns 1 for a workstation, 2 for a domain controller and 3 for a server, so `if ($pt -eq 1) { throw "This is a Windows workstation, not a server. Bootstrap-VPS only runs on the VPS. Nothing was changed." }`. Keep a belt-and-braces negative check too, but point it at a path that exists today: `if (Test-Path 'C:\Thornswood\Game\SkyrimSE.exe') { throw ... }`. Add a comment saying why a positive test is used, so the next move of the install folder does not silently disarm it again.

**Done when:** Run the check in isolation on this workstation without running the script: `(Get-CimInstance Win32_OperatingSystem).ProductType` should return 1 here, which proves the new guard would fire. Parse the edited script with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`, the same check the GitHub build runs. Then `Select-String -Path tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 -Pattern 'C:\\OldMod'` returns nothing and `Select-String -Path tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 -Pattern 'ProductType'` returns a hit above the Node block.

**Traps:** Do not run tools\Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 to test the guard. Even with a correct guard, running a provisioning script on the wrong box is exactly the outcome this entry exists to prevent, and CLAUDE.md puts every VPS script in the person's hands. Test the expression, not the script. The guard must sit above everything, including the $tmp directory creation at line 49 and Install-Msi, so it fires before anything is written. ProductType is 3 on Windows Server and 1 on Windows 11 Pro, which is what both development machines run, so this is a clean discriminator today; do not use the computer name, which changes. Other scripts in tools\ reference C:\OldMod deliberately as a leftover-cleanup target (Audit-Leftovers.ps1:41, Clean-Leftovers.ps1:42); those are correct and must not be swept up in a find-and-replace. Bootstrap-VPS.ps1 also carries the unpinned Node download from entry 148, so decide whether the two land together.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/get-ciminstance , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/cimwin32prov/win32-operatingsystem , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.management/get-computerinfo

## 163. Deploy-Gamemode ignores the master flag in the plugin header, which the game honours before the list order

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The defect is real and exactly located, but the reason given for it being latent is wrong. Verified: tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1's Read-PluginHeader at 113-136 reads the TES4 header flags at offset 8 and tests only `-band 0x200` (the light/ESL bit) at line 122; it never tests 0x1, the master bit. The same omission is in tools\Align-LoadOrder.ps1's Test-Light at 34-44, which also tests only 0x200. tools\app\Verify.cs uses Checks.MustBeLight, a hardcoded name list at Checks.cs:149, and reads no header flags at all. The order at Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:174 preserves $wanted order with no stable sort of masters to the front. Wrong: "no plugin in the list carries that flag". Two do. Water for ENB.esm and Embers XD.esm are starred at lines 4 and 5 of C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, and reading their TES4 headers directly gives flags 0x00000001 on both, so the master bit is set. It is latent only because those two happen to sit at the top of plugins.txt already, so the naive order coincides with what the game computes. The day a master-flagged plugin appears further down the list, the server's form indices diverge from every client's.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 lines 113-180: Read-PluginHeader, the $wanted construction (which prepends the five implicit Bethesda masters), the light-plugin drop with its master-cascade loop, and the $keep line that fixes the final order. 2. tools\Align-LoadOrder.ps1 lines 1-60: the header comment explains the whole problem this family of scripts exists to solve, that the server counts form ids as fileIdx * 0x01000000 + shortFormId and nothing translates. 3. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt: the reference list, 98 lines, 96 starred, with the two .esm mods at lines 4 and 5.

**How:** In tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1's Read-PluginHeader (113-136), add a Master property beside Light: after reading the flags dword at offset 8, set `$master = (($flags -band 0x1) -ne 0)` and also treat a .esm extension as master, mirroring how $light already treats .esl. Return both on the pscustomobject at line 136. Then, where $keep is built at line 174, stable-sort: take the entries whose Master is true in their existing relative order, then the rest in their existing relative order, and concatenate. PowerShell's Sort-Object is stable, so `$keep | Sort-Object -Property @{Expression={-not $_.Master}}` works if you carry the objects rather than the names. Finally, print anything in $wanted that landed in $missing, which is collected at 152-156 and currently goes nowhere visible. Mirror the same master read into tools\Align-LoadOrder.ps1's Test-Light (rename it to a Get-PluginFlags that returns both bits) and into the launcher's Verify.cs check.

**Done when:** Parse both scripts with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`. Then prove the header read directly, without deploying anything: in a scratch session, open each .esm and .esp named in plugins.txt, read four bytes at offset 8, and print the name with the 0x1 and 0x200 bits. Today that must report Water for ENB.esm and Embers XD.esm as masters and the three .esl entries as light. Reproduce the raw evidence with `Format-Hex -Path 'C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Water for ENB.esm' -Offset 8 -Count 4`, which shows 01 00 00 00. Then run Deploy-Gamemode.ps1's list-building logic in isolation (copy the function into a scratch script) and confirm the output order puts every master-flagged entry first. Do not run Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 itself.

**Traps:** Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 writes to the VPS, so it is the person's to run; work on the logic in a scratch copy. Changing the server's plugin order changes every form id the server computes, which desynchronises everybody at once, so this is a coordinated client and server release under CLAUDE.md's one-complete-release rule, even though the change looks like a sort. tools\app\Verify.cs feeds the Mod list row in Checks.cs All(), which blocks Play, so a change there can gate everybody. The $wanted list already prepends the five implicit Bethesda masters at Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:148 because plugins.txt omits them; a naive sort that re-orders those would be worse than the bug. Sorting must be stable, because LOOT's ordering within the master block is meaningful. The light-plugin drop and its master-cascade loop at 160-171 exist for a stated reason (the server will not load a plugin whose master is absent, per Align-LoadOrder.ps1:15-17); do not disturb it while adding the master sort.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format , https://loot.github.io/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.bitconverter.touint32

## 164. A leftover instruction still tells the shipper to upload a 102 line load order file to the server

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact, including the count. tools\Publish-Update.ps1:1150-1168 is the block: the comment at 1150-1154 says ModCheck compares against the server's own Data/loadorder.txt, and 1155-1168 writes $DistDir\server\loadorder.txt from Profile\Default\loadorder.txt and prints three yellow lines telling the shipper to upload it to the server's Data folder and restart. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\loadorder.txt is 102 lines, matching the entry exactly. And it does include the light plugins: loadorder.txt carries every entry, while tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:160-171 deliberately drops light plugins from the server list and cascades that drop to anything mastering them. So the two files disagree by construction, and the instruction sends the shipper to overwrite the real one with the wrong one.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Publish-Update.ps1 lines 1145-1172: the whole block, including the reasoning comment, so you can see that the mechanism it describes (ModCheck) is asserted rather than demonstrated anywhere else in the repo. 2. tools\Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 lines 139-180: how the server's plugin list is actually built today, from plugins.txt, with light plugins dropped and paths rewritten into server-settings.json. This is the real mechanism. 3. Entry 39 in this same list: the check that is supposed to replace the instruction.

**How:** Delete the block at tools\Publish-Update.ps1:1150-1168 and everything it writes into $DistDir\server. Replace it with the real check from entry 39: after a release, compare the plugin list the release was built from against what the server is actually configured with (the loadPlugins array in the server's server-settings.json, which Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 writes at lines 176 onward), and print a clear failure when they differ. If you cannot reach the server from the shipping machine, at minimum change the three Say lines at 1165-1167 to name Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 as the thing to run, and stop writing the 102-line file at all, because its only current effect is to tempt somebody into uploading it.

**Done when:** Parse the script with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`, the same check .github/workflows/build.yml runs. Then `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Update.ps1 -Pattern 'ModCheck'` returns nothing and `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Update.ps1 -Pattern "server's Data folder"` returns nothing. Confirm the two lists really do differ before and the discrepancy is understood: `(Get-Content 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\loadorder.txt').Count` is 102 and `(Get-Content 'C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt' | Where-Object { $_ -match '^\*' }).Count` is 96, with three .esl entries among them that the server list drops. All read-only.

**Traps:** Publish-Update.ps1 is on CLAUDE.md's protected list (line 79): a line in #dev first, and both machines touch this file. Do not run Publish-Update.ps1; it publishes. The block only fires when $profileChanged is non-empty, so it is easy to miss in testing and easy to believe is harmless; it is not, because the one person who follows the printed instruction carefully does the wrong thing carefully. Anything that changes what the server loads changes every form id, so if the entry-39 replacement check ever auto-corrects rather than reports, it becomes a coordinated client and server release. Entry 163 is the other half of the same load-order family and touches Deploy-Gamemode.ps1; decide whether the two ship together. Do not simply delete the yellow warnings and leave the file being written: an orphaned loadorder.txt in the distribution folder is the same trap with no sign on it.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_comment_based_help

## 165. Publish-Server writes its news to a file nobody reads and pushes whatever branch is checked out

**Category:** Server  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact on both. tools\Publish-Server.ps1:194-195 is `if (-not $RepoDir) { $RepoDir = Split-Path -Parent $Here }` followed by `$VersionFile = Join-Path $RepoDir 'version.json'`, so the news entry is written into the source repo's version.json rather than the releases clone's. tools\Publish-Server.ps1:295-305 is the push block: Push-Location $RepoDir, then `git add version.json`, `git commit -m "Server: $($entry.title)"` and `git push`, from whatever branch happens to be checked out. The correct pattern is right there in the repo for comparison: tools\Set-Server.ps1:39-42 says "version.json is published from the public releases clone, not the source one" and resolves $ReleaseDir to the sibling Thornswood-Releases clone, falling back to the source repo only if that clone is not there.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Set-Server.ps1 lines 8-45 and 85-115: the correct version of this logic, including the comment stating the rule and the ReleaseDir resolution with its fallback. Copy this. 2. tools\Publish-Server.ps1 lines 185-215 and 285-315: the two blocks to change, and the comment above 193 explaining why a news entry is written for a server-only change at all. 3. SECOND-DEVELOPER.md line 31: the Releases repo row, which states where version.json per branch actually lives.

**How:** Two edits in tools\Publish-Server.ps1. First, replace the $RepoDir resolution at 194-195 with the Set-Server.ps1 pattern: add a `[string]$ReleaseDir` parameter, and when it is not supplied, guess the sibling clone with `$guess = Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent $RepoDir) 'Thornswood-Releases'` and use it if it has a .git directory, otherwise fail loudly rather than silently falling back to the source repo. Point $VersionFile at $ReleaseDir. Second, delete the push block at 295-305 entirely and let Set-Server.ps1 or the news publisher own the commit and push, which is what Set-Server.ps1:112 already does with `git -C $ReleaseDir add version.json`. If a push must stay, use `git -C $ReleaseDir` so it can never act on whatever branch the source repo has checked out.

**Done when:** Parse the script with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`. Then read-only: `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Server.ps1 -Pattern 'Join-Path \$RepoDir ..version.json'` returns nothing, `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Server.ps1 -Pattern 'Push-Location \$RepoDir'` returns nothing, and `Select-String -Path tools\Publish-Server.ps1 -Pattern 'Thornswood-Releases'` returns a hit. Side by side, `Select-String -Path tools\Set-Server.ps1,tools\Publish-Server.ps1 -Pattern 'ReleaseDir'` should now show the same shape in both files. To confirm the target actually exists on this machine before changing anything: `Test-Path (Join-Path (Split-Path -Parent (Get-Location)) 'Thornswood-Releases\.git')`.

**Traps:** Publish-Server.ps1 talks to the VPS and pushes to GitHub, so it is entirely the person's to run: write the change, show the command, never execute it. The `git push` at line 304 is the dangerous part, because CLAUDE.md says main is what ships and is never pushed over, and a push from an arbitrary checked-out branch in the source repo is exactly how that rule gets broken by accident. Removing it is the safest change in this entry, so do that even if you do the rest later. The repo-root version.json is not dead: it is the shipped default and is read as a template, so do not delete it, just stop writing news into it. Publishing news for a server change is the whole point of the script, per the comment at 188-192; do not remove the news step while fixing the path. The dev channel has its own releases worktree (Thornswood-Releases-dev, per SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:31), so the ReleaseDir resolution has to account for which channel is being published or it will write main's feed during a dev publish.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-push , https://git-scm.com/docs/git-worktree , https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rev-parse

## 166. The old build script is still in the repository, and the current one tells people to run it

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every line is exact. tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1:61 is `Head 'the old mod - build'`; line 87 is `foreach ($n in 'SkyrimSE', 'skse64_loader', 'OldMod', 'Vortex') {`; line 102 is the out-of-space Die naming `-Root "D:\OldMod"`; line 141 is `$stray = Join-Path $GameDir 'Data\OldModReborn\Data'`; and 186-192 is the config write, which produces `ServerAddress = '192.135.119.166'` and `ServerPort = '25565'`. Those two values are precisely the dead pair that tools\app\Config.cs:203-204 migrates away from on load (`if (ServerPort == "25565") ServerPort = "7777";` and `if (ServerAddress == "192.135.119.166") ServerAddress = "192.135.112.23";`), so the script writes a config the launcher has to repair. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:119 is the Die that names it: "No build at $GameDir, and no Steam copy of Skyrim either. Run Build-Thornswood.ps1, or install the collection in Vortex."

**Read first:** 1. tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1 lines 180-195: the config write, the clearest proof the script cannot produce a working build, since the address and port it writes are the ones Config.cs exists to undo. 2. tools\app\Config.cs lines 195-215: the migration block, which is the evidence. 3. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 lines 110-125: the Die that points people at the dead script, and the only line that has to change when the script goes.

**How:** Delete tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1 with `git rm tools/Build-Thornswood.ps1`. Then change the message at tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1:119 so it names the current path instead: "No build at $GameDir, and no Steam copy of Skyrim either. Install the collection in Vortex, or build Thornswood's own game folder from the launcher's Settings page." Grep the rest of the tree for any other reference and fix each one the same way. Check tools\Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and SECOND-DEVELOPER.md in particular, since Downgrade.cs:39-40 records that the three depot manifest ids are duplicated in both Setup-Thornswood.ps1 and Build-Thornswood.ps1, so deleting one copy is also a small win for entry 145.

**Done when:** `Test-Path tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1` is False, and `Select-String -Path tools\*.ps1,tools\app\*.cs,tools\setup\*.cs,*.md -Pattern 'Build-Thornswood'` returns nothing. The GitHub build check still passes, since its PowerShell parse step enumerates tools\*.ps1 recursively and one fewer file cannot break it. Reproduce the evidence first if you want it in the pull request: `Select-String -Path tools\Build-Thornswood.ps1 -Pattern '192.135.119.166'` and `Select-String -Path tools\app\Config.cs -Pattern '192.135.119.166'` side by side show the script writing what the launcher undoes.

**Traps:** Read the whole script before deleting it, not just the cited lines, in case something in it is still the only copy of a piece of knowledge; the depot manifest ids and the stray-address-library cleanup at 139-142 are the two candidates, and if either is genuinely unique, move it before deleting. Do not run Build-Thornswood.ps1 to confirm it is broken; the config it writes would point a launcher at a server that no longer exists, and CLAUDE.md forbids starting the launcher or the game anyway. tools\Package-Thornswood.ps1 is not on the protected list but it is the installer, and it dot-sources Ship-Rules.ps1, so keep the edit to the one message string. Git history keeps the file, so deletion is recoverable; say that in the pull request so nobody argues to keep it "just in case". One fix per pull request under CLAUDE.md, so this is its own branch even though it also touches entries 145 and 160.

**Links:** https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rm , https://git-scm.com/docs/git-log

## 167. Dead key bindings and mod links are still compiled in, including a party menu row on the Controls page

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Exact on every count. tools\app\Keys.cs:40 is TweaksIni pointing at Data\SKSE\Plugins\OldModTweaks.ini and line 42 is TradeIni pointing at Data\SKSE\Plugins\OldTradeMod.ini; neither file exists in C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins. The eight bindings are exactly right: two on TweaksIni at 332-338 ("The party menu" and "The connection readout") and six on TradeIni at 357-374 (trade, tradeadd, traderem, goldadd, goldrem, tradecancel). The seven link entries are exactly right: tools\app\Links.cs lines 155-161 are the multiplayer block, Map["OldMod"], OldModTweaks, OldMorphMod, OldTradeMod, OldTradeModMCM, OldMessagingPluginAPI and OldMessagingPluginBridge. The F2 collision is real and already documented in the file itself: the party menu at line 334 is bound to virtual key 0x71, which is VK_F2, and the comment at Keys.cs:344-346 warns that the SkyMP client holds F2 and that pressing it hides the overlay, taking the chat and the wheel with it.

**Read first:** 1. tools\app\Keys.cs lines 35-50 and 328-375: the ini path properties and the All() list. The comment at 340-349 is the important part, because it records the F1 incident (F1 was moved because the client binds it to name toggling) and then names F2 and F6 as also held by the client, which is the argument for deleting the party-menu row rather than rebinding it. 2. tools\app\Links.cs lines 150-175: the multiplayer block and the patch-plugin block below it, so you can see which OldTradeMod and OldMessagingPlugin entries are in scope and which are P() patch entries. 3. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt: confirmation that none of these mods are in the shipping list.

**How:** In tools\app\Keys.cs, delete the TweaksIni property at line 40 and the TradeIni property at line 42, then delete the eight l.Add calls that reference them: the two Vk(...) calls at 332-338 and the six Dx(...) calls at 357-374. Leave MenuIni, MapIni, TdmIni and SkyUiIni alone; those mods are real. In tools\app\Links.cs, delete lines 155-161, the whole multiplayer block, and also review the P("OldTradeMod", 188710) and P("OldMessagingPlugin", 188907) patch entries below it, which have nothing left to attach to. Where deleting leaves an empty group heading on the Controls page, remove that too so the page does not show an empty section.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root $env:TEMP\tsbuild -NoPause` produces Thornswood.exe. Read-only proof: `Select-String -Path tools\app\Keys.cs -Pattern 'OldModTweaks|OldTradeMod'` returns nothing, `Select-String -Path tools\app\Links.cs -Pattern 'OldMod|OldTradeMod|MorphSync|OldMessagingPlugin'` returns nothing, and `(Select-String -Path tools\app\Keys.cs -Pattern 'l\.Add\(' | Measure-Object).Count` has dropped by eight. tools\Check-Controls.ps1 is read-only and exists for exactly this page, so run it too.

**Traps:** The bindings are invisible today only because Keys.cs drops any binding whose settings file is absent. That is the whole hazard: a single stray OldModTweaks.ini left behind by an old old-mod install puts a party-menu row back on the Controls page, telling somebody that F2 opens a party menu. It does not; per the comment at Keys.cs:344-346 the SkyMP client holds F2 and pressing it hides the overlay, so the person loses their chat and their emote wheel and has no idea why. Deleting the rows is the fix; rebinding them is not, because the mods are not installed. Keys.cs and Links.cs are not on CLAUDE.md's protected list, but the change alters what people see on the Controls page, so it goes out as one complete release. C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Do not touch the MenuIni block at 350-355; the F4 default there is deliberate and the comment above it records why it is not F1.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/inputdev/virtual-key-codes , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/directinput/dik-constants

## 168. Eleven dev scripts still default to the old install folder, and a Python bytecode file is committed

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The count is right by files but wrong by description, and two of the eleven should not be touched. Eleven files reference C:\OldMod, and the folder does not exist on this machine (the install is C:\Thornswood). But only nine of them default a parameter to it: tools\Add-Accuracy.ps1:25, Add-Bodies.ps1:25, Add-CartTravel.ps1:23, Add-JContainers.ps1:26, Add-McmShortcut.ps1:28, Add-NoticeBoards.ps1:22, Add-SkyrimUnbound.ps1:22 and Add-TempleRespawn.ps1:23 all carry `[string]$Root = 'C:\OldMod'`, and tools\Move-Install.ps1:26 carries `[string]$OldRoot = 'C:\OldMod'`. The other two are correct as written and must be left alone: tools\Audit-Leftovers.ps1:41 and tools\Clean-Leftovers.ps1:42 name that path deliberately, as a leftover from the old old-mod build to find and clean up. So it is nine bad defaults, not eleven, and "the Add-* scripts and three more" over-counts: there are eight Add-* scripts with the default, not all of them (Add-Characters.ps1, Add-VoiceFeed.ps1 and Add-VpsKey.ps1 do not have it). The bytecode half is exactly right: git tracks tools/discord/__pycache__/bot.cpython-39.pyc and tools/discord/__pycache__/content.cpython-39.pyc, two files, and .gitignore has no __pycache__ rule.

**Read first:** 1. tools\Add-Accuracy.ps1 lines 1-30: a representative Add-* script, its comment block and its parameter block, so you can see both the wrong default at line 25 and the fact that these are one-shot mod installers whose mods are now in the collection. 2. tools\Move-Install.ps1 lines 20-35: the odd one out, where the old path is the whole point of the script and $OldRoot may legitimately keep a historical default. 3. .gitignore: it already ignores config.json, server-secret.txt, the webhook files and logs, and is where the __pycache__ rule belongs.

**How:** Two separate jobs. Bytecode: `git rm -r --cached tools/discord/__pycache__` then add `__pycache__/` and `*.pyc` to .gitignore and commit. That removes them from tracking without deleting the local files. Paths: for the nine bad defaults, decide per script whether it is still called. Several Add-* scripts installed mods that are now in the Nexus collection, in which case delete the script rather than fix its default. For whichever ones the runner still calls, replace the literal with the same discovery the rest of the tools use: the scripts already find the install on whichever fixed drive holds it, per SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:32, so lift that helper rather than hardcoding C:\Thornswood, which would just be the same bug in a newer place. Leave Audit-Leftovers.ps1:41 and Clean-Leftovers.ps1:42 exactly as they are.

**Done when:** `git ls-files | Select-String '__pycache__'` returns nothing, and `git check-ignore -v tools/discord/__pycache__/bot.cpython-39.pyc` reports the .gitignore rule that now covers it. For the paths: `Select-String -Path tools\*.ps1 -Pattern "C:.OldMod"` returns only Audit-Leftovers.ps1 and Clean-Leftovers.ps1, down from eleven files today. Parse every edited script with `[System.Management.Automation.Language.Parser]::ParseFile`, which is the same gate the GitHub build applies. All read-only checks, no game.

**Traps:** Do not do a blind find-and-replace of C:\OldMod: two of the eleven hits are correct and removing them would break the leftover cleanup that finds old installs on testers' machines. Do not replace the default with a hardcoded C:\Thornswood either; the install root is not C: on every machine, which is why SECOND-DEVELOPER.md:32 says the scripts find it on whichever fixed drive holds it and that -Root overrides. Hardcoding a second wrong answer is how this happened the first time. `git rm --cached` without `-r` fails on a directory, and using plain `git rm` would delete the local .pyc files, which is harmless but unnecessary. Deleting an Add-* script is only safe if nothing calls it: grep tools\Go.ps1 and tools\Run Everything.cmd before removing anything. Djinn is the person this entry is written for, so the fix is judged by whether a second developer can trust the defaults, not by whether the greps are clean.

**Links:** https://docs.python.org/3/tutorial/modules.html#compiled-python-files , https://github.com/github/gitignore/blob/main/Python.gitignore , https://git-scm.com/docs/git-rm , https://git-scm.com/docs/gitignore , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.core/about/about_functions_advanced_parameters

## 169. The build is the compiler that ships inside Windows, with no project file, and that is the root of most of the rest

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Line citations are exact. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:5-7 is the honest reason ("Uses csc.exe, which is part of the .NET Framework that ships inside Windows. Nothing to install."), 46-50 is the csc.exe probe, 53-108 is the reference resolution (56 lines, not 55: 53 opens $refDirs, 108 is the Die on missing assemblies). "No project file" is correct: a repo-wide search for *.csproj and *.sln returns nothing. Wrong figure: "two hundred and fifty lines of reference hunting" matches nothing. The whole script is 421 lines; reference hunting is 56 of them; Ship.ps1 already calls it in ONE line (Ship.ps1:253). The "one package" is WebView2, vendored as three DLLs in tools/lib/webview2 and both referenced and embedded (Build-Launcher.ps1:110-134). XAML is not compiled today: it is passed as /resource:ui.xaml (line 235) and parsed at runtime by XamlReader.Parse (Shell.cs:221-224), so "XAML compiled" is a real change of behaviour, not a build detail.

**Challenged (minor):** doneWhen: "Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root C:\Thornswood-dev -NoPause` for the old one".  ->  C:\Thornswood-dev is not a root this repo uses. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:21 defaults to `C:\Thornswood-DevBuild`, and the header at lines 9-16 explains that default exists because a hand-run build once overwrote C:\Thornswood\Launcher. The script creates whatever root it is given (line 157-158), so this silently produces a third stray build folder instead of comparing against the dev build everyone else means.

**Challenged (minor):** "53-108 is the reference resolution (56 lines, not 55: 53 opens $refDirs, 108 is the Die on missing assemblies)".  ->  Line 53 is the `# ---- references` banner comment; `$refDirs = @(` opens at line 54. Counting the code, 54-108 is 55 lines, which is the figure the original entry already gave. This correction replaces a right number with a wrong one. The rest of the entry's citations are exact: 5-7 is the honest reason, 46-50 is the csc probe, 108 is the Die, the WebView2 block runs 110-134, /resource:ui.xaml is line 235, XamlReader.Parse is Shell.cs:221-224, Ship.ps1:253 is the single call, the script is 421 lines, and no .csproj or .sln exists in the repo.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1 in full (421 lines) because every paragraph of the header comment is a constraint the replacement has to keep: the dev-install default root, the generated AssemblyInfo, the embedded logo, the Stop-Game/Stop-Launcher calls. 2. tools/Ship.ps1 around line 253 to see the single call site and what -Root and -NoPause mean to it. 3. .github/workflows/build.yml because that runner also compiles with csc and is what turns a pull request red.

**How:** Add tools/app/Thornswood.csproj as an old-style (non-SDK) WPF project or an SDK-style project with <TargetFramework>net48</TargetFramework> and <UseWPF>true</UseWPF>. Move ui.xaml in as <Page> (or keep it as <EmbeddedResource> with LogicalName ui.xaml if you want to leave Shell.cs:221-224 alone, which is the smaller change). Add app.ico as ApplicationIcon, logo.png and logo-192.png as EmbeddedResource with the same logical names Art.cs expects, and WebView2 as a PackageReference to Microsoft.Web.WebView2 instead of the three loose DLLs. Keep the generated AssemblyInfo step: either keep New-Info writing the file and include it, or move the version into the csproj and have the script patch it. Then reduce Build-Launcher.ps1 to argument handling plus one `dotnet build` or `msbuild` line, keeping the -Root dev-install default and the Stop-Game/Stop-Launcher calls, because those are safety, not build.

**Done when:** Build the new project and the old script into two different roots and diff the outputs. Run `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root C:\Thornswood-dev -NoPause` for the old one, build the csproj for the new, then compare: the exe runs, `(Get-Item Thornswood.exe).VersionInfo` shows the same AssemblyTitle, AssemblyVersion and FileVersion, and the file size is within a few percent. Then confirm the GitHub build check is green on the branch, since that runner is the second machine that has to compile it.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md lists Build-Launcher.ps1's C# 5 rule as settled design and says a change to settled design is a few lines in #dev first, agreed between Patrick and Djinn, before code. This entry does not itself change the language version but it removes the only stated reason for the rule, so it is entry 170's precondition and belongs in the same #dev conversation. The price named in the entry is real: the SDK or build tools have to exist on Patrick's machine, Djinn's machine and the GitHub runner, and today none of the three needs anything installed. WebView2 must stay embedded in the single exe: Build-Launcher.ps1:119-123 explains that the updater publishes exactly one asset and SelfUpdate replaces exactly one file, so sidecar DLLs would break every launcher mid-upgrade. A PackageReference that emits loose DLLs is a regression, not a cleanup. Ship must still build into the release root, not the dev one.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/compiler-options/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/visualstudio/msbuild/msbuild , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/overview/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/standard/frameworks

## 170. The rule banning modern C# is a rule about the compiler, not the framework, and it costs real code

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** Entry 170 is marked [verified], and its howTo repeats the entry's premise that raising the C# language version is what unlocks "async and await in Play, the update check, the uninstall and the character sync".  ->  async and await ARE C# 5. The in-box compiler the repo uses prints its own identity as "Microsoft (R) Visual C# Compiler version 4.8.9221.0 / for C# 5" (C:\Windows\Microsoft.NET\Framework64\v4.0.30319\csc.exe, probed at tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:46-50). Nothing has to be raised to write async in Play. The launcher already links the Task types (tools/app/Voice.cs) and simply has zero async/await today (grep over tools/app/*.cs returns none). The C# 5 rule in CLAUDE.md bans string interpolation, ?., nameof, expression-bodied members and out var; async is not on that list. Marking this "verified" blesses a false premise for the single largest benefit the entry claims.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** howTo: "the compiler is the constraint, not the framework, so raising the language version needs a build that can pass /langversion or a csproj with <LangVersion>."  ->  That csc cannot be raised by a flag. Running it with /langversion:6 in the scratchpad returns "This compiler is provided as part of the Microsoft (R) .NET Framework, but only supports language versions up to C# 5" and fails. A csproj with <LangVersion> does not help by itself either; it only helps because MSBuild brings a Roslyn compiler with it. Anyone who tries the "/langversion" half first loses the time and concludes the entry is wrong. The honest statement is: a different compiler (the .NET SDK, or the Microsoft.Net.Compilers package) is required, which is the real cost this entry and 169 share.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: `grep -rn "DispatcherFrame\|PushFrame" tools/app/*.cs` "should return none when this is finished", while the howTo says to do "the hot paths named in the entry and nothing else".  ->  Those two cannot both hold. The frame lives inside the shared helper tools/app/Waiting.cs:36-59, which has 11 call sites, all in Shell.cs. Converting only Play (3380), CheckUpdatesBackground (3989) and CheckUpdatesInPlay (4163) leaves the other callers synchronous, so Waiting.cs keeps its DispatcherFrame and the grep keeps returning it. Creation.cs:405 and 447 are a second, independent pump. Zero matches is a whole-sweep finish line attached to a deliberately scoped piece of work, so this test can never pass for the work the howTo describes.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md, the "Settled design, not up for a quiet rewrite" section, for the exact rule: "Launcher code is C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression bodied members, no out var." 2. tools/Build-Launcher.ps1:5-7, which is the only reason given for it anywhere. 3. tools/app/Stall.cs (94 lines) and tools/app/Waiting.cs, because those two files are what the rule costs: Stall.cs exists purely to write a line to the log when the window thread stops answering, and Waiting.cs is the nested pump.

**How:** This is a #dev conversation before it is a branch. If it is agreed: the compiler is the constraint, not the framework, so raising the language version needs a build that can pass /langversion or a csproj with <LangVersion>. Do entry 169 first. Then pick the hot paths named in the entry and nothing else: Shell.Play (Shell.cs:3380), Shell.CheckUpdatesBackground (3989) and CheckUpdatesInPlay (4163), Uninstall.cs, and CharSync.cs. Replace the DispatcherFrame plus Dispatcher.PushFrame pattern in Waiting.cs:36-59 with await, and the same pattern in Creation.cs:405-447. Once no code pushes a nested frame, Stall.cs can go with it.

**Done when:** Before anything else: `grep -rn "DispatcherFrame\|PushFrame" tools/app/*.cs` returns exactly two files today (Waiting.cs and Creation.cs). It should return none when this is finished. Compile clean with the new language version, then confirm the launcher no longer needs the stall watch by checking that Profile\launcher.log carries no stall line across a session. Do not measure this by starting the game, which is not allowed here; measure it by the grep and by the compile.

**Traps:** This is settled design in CLAUDE.md and CLAUDE.md is explicit that a Claude does not settle it. Get the #dev line first: the problem, the approach, what it touches. Shell.cs, Checks.cs, Keep.cs, Hold.cs and Updater.cs also each need a #dev line of their own before editing, because both machines touch them, and a language sweep touches all of them. The entry is right that the syntax is the small part: converting ?. and string interpolation across 48 files is a large diff with no benefit and would break the repo's one-fix-per-pull-request rule. Keep it to the four hot paths. Note that the launcher and the game files and the server go out as one complete release, so a launcher that will not compile on the runner stops the whole release at the build step.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/configure-language-version , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/whats-new/csharp-version-history , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/asynchronous-programming/async-scenarios , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.windows.threading.dispatcherframe

## 171. Shell.cs is a god object by any measure

**Category:** Launcher code  |  **Fact check:** verified
