**Correction:** Counts confirmed with one refinement: tools/app/Shell.cs is exactly 4,417 lines; Wire() runs from line 750 to the next method at 1308, which is 558 lines exactly; static method declarations count 79 by grep (the entry says seventy-seven, close enough that it is not a defect); static fields are 132 named fields, not "about a hundred", because several declarations carry many names on one line (lines 102-108 alone declare 33 Buttons). Also worth knowing and not in the entry: Shell is already `static partial class`, and four other files extend it (Pages.cs, ShellOwnGame.cs, Stall.cs, Waiting.cs), so the splitting mechanism is already in the repo and proven.

**Read first:** 1. launcher/app/ARCHITECTURE.md, which is the launcher class map and the document that says what each file is for. 2. tools/app/Shell.cs lines 720-750, the page constants, PageTitles and ShowPage, because that is the cheapest seam and the one entry 187 also wants. 3. tools/app/Pages.cs, an existing `partial class Shell` file, as the worked example of how a slice looks when it is done.

**How:** Do not rewrite. Add a new file under tools/app/ declaring `static partial class Shell`, move one group of methods into it with their fields, and change nothing else. The groups the entry names are real and findable: Play (3380) with EnsureServer (3343), AskOnline (3591), PresenceLog (3586) and the unwind; the update flow is CheckUpdatesBackground (3989), OnVersionDoc (4021), CheckUpdatesInPlay (4163), Unblock (4012) and RunUpdate (4329); the character cards are NameOf (3626) through BuildChars (3895); the news list is BuildNews (2516); the pack rows are BuildPackRows (1852), PackRow (1913) and PackAction (2044). Build-Launcher.ps1:140 globs tools/app/*.cs so a new file is picked up with no build change.

**Done when:** `wc -l tools/app/Shell.cs` is smaller by the size of the slice, the new file compiles, and `git diff --stat` on the branch shows only moves: the same method bodies, no changed lines inside them. Build with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root C:\Thornswood-dev -NoPause` (that root is the dev install, not the one anybody plays on) and confirm it exits 0.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md: do not edit Shell.cs without a line in #dev first, because both machines touch it. One move per pull request, which is the repo's own rule and also what keeps the diff readable as a move. Launcher code is C# 5 only, so any refactor that reaches for expression-bodied members, nameof or out var will not compile with csc. The fields are the hard part, not the methods: several static fields on lines 76-108 are shared across groups that look separate, so moving a method without its field leaves a compile error that points at the wrong file. Moving fields between partials of the same class is free, so prefer that over making anything internal or public.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/keywords/partial-type , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/fundamentals/object-oriented/objects

## 172. Two mods you believe are already removed are still shipping

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Checked on the machine today. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt line 48 is `*Respawn - Death Overhaul.esp` and line 86 is `*TavernGames.esp`, exactly as claimed. C:\Thornswood\Profile\OwnGame\plugins.txt carries the same two at lines 47 and 85. Both .esp files are present in C:\Thornswood\Game\Data, along with TavernGames.bsa, TavernGames - Textures.bsa. Note the extra tail nobody has listed: Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp (plugins.txt line 78) is a patch, and TavernGames carries two BSAs that have to come out with the esp.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** doneWhen: grep TavernGames/Respawn out of Profile\Default\plugins.txt, Profile\OwnGame\plugins.txt and Game\Data, and the removal is proved.  ->  That test passes while the server is still desynced. The file Publish-Update.ps1 actually reads is loadorder.txt, not plugins.txt: tools/Publish-Update.ps1:1158 is `$lo = Join-Path $ProfileDir 'Default\loadorder.txt'`, and the block above it (1151-1153) says ModCheck compares against the server's own Data/loadorder.txt and "Change the plugin list here and forget that file, and the server refuses everyone". Both mods are still in C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\loadorder.txt at lines 53 and 91, and in Profile\OwnGame\loadorder.txt at the same two lines. The doneWhen never names loadorder.txt, so a person can tick this entry off and ship a server list that still carries both, which is exactly the load-order desync this audit is meant to prevent.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo lists only the collection, Vortex, the repackage and the server plugin list as what changes.  ->  Four other places in the repo name these two mods and none is mentioned: tools/app/Links.cs:77 `A("TavernGames", 1668);` is a Nexus link the launcher shows, which goes dead on removal; tools/Add-TempleRespawn.ps1 is a whole one-shot installer for Respawn - Death Overhaul (see its header at lines 2-12 and `$respawnEsp = 'Respawn - Death Overhaul.esp'` at line 104) and is one of the eleven stale scripts entry 168 covers; tools/docs-app/data/known.md:34 and tools/docs-app/data/board.json:394 both still list Respawn - Death Overhaul as a mod that does not work as installed; tools/mod-audit.json:126-148 tracks TavernGames.bsa and TavernGames - Textures.bsa. A removal that leaves these behind leaves the launcher pointing at a mod that is no longer shipped.

**Challenged (minor):** "Note the extra tail nobody has listed: Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp (plugins.txt line 78) is a patch".  ->  The line number is right, but the patch belongs to SunHelm, not to either mod entry 172 is about. Entry 172 is TavernGames and Respawn - Death Overhaul; the SunHelm removal is entries 52 and 179. Filed here it reads as though this patch has to come out in the same change, which would remove an Embers XD patch for a mod that is still shipping.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, the reference list the server is derived from, to see the exact positions. 2. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 30-36, where both mods are already documented as "in the collection and do nothing useful" with the reason for each. 3. CLAUDE.md, the settled-design section, for why a plugin removal is a whole release rather than a file edit.

**How:** The removal happens in the Nexus collection first, because that is where the mod list lives now, then Vortex redeploys, then the build is repackaged. In the repo the only thing that changes automatically is the server plugin list: tools/Publish-Update.ps1 around line 1159 reads the load order file and writes the server's copy, one name per line, and reports the count. So: pull both mods from the collection, let Vortex deploy, rebuild, confirm plugins.txt has dropped to 94 entries and that TavernGames.esp, TavernGames.bsa, TavernGames - Textures.bsa, Respawn - Death Overhaul.esp are all gone from Game\Data, then let Ship regenerate the server list and deploy the server and the client in the same release. Batch this with entry 190 and with entry 52's two mods so the order moves once.

**Done when:** `grep -n "TavernGames\|Respawn" C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt` returns nothing, the same for Profile\OwnGame\plugins.txt, and `ls C:\Thornswood\Game\Data | grep -i "TavernGames\|Respawn"` returns nothing. Then check the server list the publisher wrote has the same count as the client list. All of that is checkable without starting the game.

**Traps:** Taking a plugin out moves the load order for every machine at once. CLAUDE.md is explicit that every change goes out as one complete release, launcher, game files and server together, never a half, and known.md:54 spells out the consequence: the server and every machine must carry the same plugins in the same order with the same bytes, and one machine out of step gets LOAD ORDER ERROR and cannot connect. So this cannot be shipped to the server without shipping the client in the same release. Do not hand-edit plugins.txt: it is derived from what Vortex deployed, and an edit there will be overwritten on the next build while the actual .esp stays on disk. Nothing touches Vortex's own files. The two BSAs are loaded by TavernGames.esp and must come out with it or the game logs a missing-master style complaint.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format

## 173. The tester checklist tells people the launcher should reach 1.1.34, and everything it describes lives in a hub that is being deleted

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The substance is right and worse than stated; the number is wrong. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md line 7 reads "Build 1.0.193 and up. The version is at the bottom of the launcher." It does not say 1.1.34 anywhere; the only 1.1.34 in the repo is a comment in tools/app/Keep.cs:33 and 139. Main is on 1.1.47 (tools/app/Updater.cs:13). So the checklist is behind by far more than thirteen releases. Two more things the entry does not say. First, tests.md is not served from the hub: tools/docs-app/server.js:211 routes it at /testthese, but server.js:154-158 puts `tests: 'admin'` in LEVEL, and the comment at 149-153 says the tester tier was deliberately removed and "a playtester never needs this page at all". So today no tester can open the tester checklist. Second, tests.md line 141 still says "Ask Patrick to send a test restart", which is the old name.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Either add `tests` to PUBLIC_DOCS and '/tes[tthese]' to PUBLIC_PAGES" to make the checklist reachable.  ->  That puts an internal admin page on the open internet, which is wider than the tier server.js records as deliberately removed. The comment the entry itself quotes, tools/docs-app/server.js:149-153, removed the tester tier so the gate could be "one role check rather than two tiers", and the page it would expose carries staff instructions (tests.md:131 "Ask [the owner] to send a test restart"), the log paths and the whole internal checklist. PUBLIC_DOCS today holds only known and help, and the comment at server.js:167-172 explains why those two specifically had to come out from behind the sign-in. This option should be named as a policy change for #dev, not offered level with "a pinned Discord post".

**Challenged (minor):** "tests.md line 141 still says 'Ask Patrick to send a test restart'" and howTo: "change 'Ask Patrick' on line 141 to 'Ask Patrick'".  ->  It is line 131, not 141. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:131 is "1. Ask Patrick to send a test restart while you are playing." Line 141 is "2. Press Apply without changing it. Start the game. Nothing should look different.", part of the field-of-view test. Everything else the entry says about tests.md checks out exactly (line 7 is the 1.0.193 line, 1.1.34 appears only at Keep.cs:33 and 139, main is 1.1.47 at Updater.cs:13).

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md, the whole file, 17 tests and a "Do not report these" list at the end. 2. tools/docs-app/server.js lines 127-219, which is DOCS, BOARDS, LEVEL, PUBLIC_PAGES and PAGES in one block and answers who can actually reach this page. 3. tools/app/Updater.cs:13 for the real current version.

**How:** Delete the version line. Edit tools/docs-app/data/tests.md and remove line 7 entirely, because the checklist is about behaviour and the version only ever goes stale. While in there, change "Ask Patrick" on line 141 to "Ask Patrick". Then decide where the page lives: it is currently admin-only at /testthese, which means it reaches nobody it was written for. Either add `tests` to PUBLIC_DOCS and '/testthese' to PUBLIC_PAGES in server.js (lines 164-174), or drop the markdown and put the checklist in a pinned Discord post or a bot command beside roadmap and help. If the version line is kept instead of deleted, generate it from the feed: the site already has the number, since version.json in the releases clone carries launcherVersion.

**Done when:** `grep -n "1\\.0\\.19\\|Patrick" tools/docs-app/data/tests.md` returns nothing. If the page is being made reachable, `grep -n "testthese" tools/docs-app/server.js` shows it in PUBLIC_PAGES as well as PAGES, and starting the site locally and opening /testthese without signing in serves the page. No game needed for any of it.

**Traps:** Entry 77 is deleting the hub, so do not spend effort making the hub copy nicer; decide the destination first. The site data files are markdown on disk deliberately (server.js:175-176) so the wording can be fixed from the site rather than by a deploy, which means an edit here may need a site deploy to reach the VPS and the person runs that, not a Claude. Do not confuse the two todo sources: there is a markdown tools/docs-app/data/todo.md and a JSON board in tools/docs-app/data/board.json under the `todo` key, and they hold different content. CLAUDE.md says the word is testers or customers, never "player", and server.js:152 still uses "playtester" in a comment.

## 174. The known issues page calls things permanent that are on the roadmap as work

**Category:** RP fit  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and there is a fourth place, not three. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 11-14 head the table "Limits of a shared Skyrim ... These follow from the server owning the world instead of your own machine. They are not going to change", and line 18 puts "NPCs stand still when nobody is nearby" inside it. The roadmap in tools/docs-app/data/board.json, section s10rm "Next", carries rNPC1 "Thinning the crowd" and rNPC2 "NPCs with their own work", both done=false. The tester note repeats it: tests.md:162 "Do not report these: ... NPCs stand still when nobody is near them." And tools/docs-app/server.js:192 sets the link preview for /known to "Permanent limits of how a shared Skyrim works", so the text a Discord unfurl shows also says permanent. known.md lines 7-9 already hedge ("Not all of it is permanent"), which makes the contradiction internal to the same file.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "Leave the genuinely permanent rows (no co-save, blocked Papyrus events, blanked animation events) where they are."  ->  Two of those three are live work items on the same list the entry is part of. known.md:20 ("Mod settings menus reset every session ... there is no co-save") is entry 180, graded "change it, medium", proposing per-character settings stored server side. known.md:22 ("Killmoves and stagger do not show on other people | Those animation events are blanked") is entry 181, also "change it, medium", proposing the event be replicated rather than blanked. Telling the reader to leave those two under the heading "They are not going to change" recreates in known.md the exact contradiction entry 174 exists to remove.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md, whole file, 58 lines, because the fix is to move two rows between two headings in it. 2. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, the `roadmap` key, section s10rm, for the exact wording of rNPC1 and rNPC2. 3. tools/docs-app/server.js:184-199, the BLURB map, because the /known preview text has to change with the page.

**How:** Pick one and say it once. Move "NPCs stand still when nobody is nearby" and "NPC behaviour differs between people near the same enemy" out of the "Limits of a shared Skyrim" table in known.md and into a new heading, something like "Being worked on", with a line saying it is on the roadmap. Leave the genuinely permanent rows (no co-save, blocked Papyrus events, blanked animation events) where they are, or move those too if entries 180 and 181 land. Then change server.js:192 so /known no longer unfurls as "Permanent limits". Then take the NPC line out of the "Do not report these" list at tests.md:162, or reword it to say what is worth reporting and what is not.

**Done when:** Read known.md top to bottom and confirm no row appears under a "not going to change" heading that also appears on the roadmap. Mechanically: for each row title under the Limits table, grep board.json's roadmap section for the same subject; there should be no hits. Confirm `grep -n "Permanent" tools/docs-app/server.js` shows the /known blurb has been reworded.

**Traps:** This is three files that have to agree, and one of them is a JSON board edited through the site rather than by hand, so an edit to board.json in the repo can be overwritten by somebody saving that board in the hub (server.js:297-298 says two people on the same board overwrite each other, last save wins). Make the known.md change and the board wording in one pass. The site data files are served from the VPS; a Claude edits them in the repo, the person runs the deploy. Entry 177 is the same subject from the other end: 177 is the work, 174 is what the page says while the work does not exist. Do not close both with one edit and call the work done.

G." then "WheelKey = 34", and DirectInput scan code 34 is G. The roadmap in tools/docs-app/data/board.json, section s19rm "Later", row r21rm, says "Emote wheel | Wanted since the start, never started", done=false. One extra contradiction worth catching in the same pass: tools/docs-app/data/todo.md lines 83-85 already record the emote wheel as done but say "B opens it", while the ini says G. Tab and N are right (PageKey = 15 is Tab, StopKey = 49 is N).

The 24 is copied from tools/docs-app/data/todo.md:85, which is the same stale note whose "B opens it" the entry itself catches as wrong. All ten rows already in the Done section (s28rm) of tools/docs-app/data/board.json carry "done": false, as does every row in every roadmap section. r21rm would become the only true in the file and may render unlike its neighbours. The section membership is what marks a roadmap row as shipped here; the `done` flag is the todo board's convention, which the same agent describes correctly under entry 176.

While there, fix tools/docs-app/data/todo.md:84 from "B opens it" to "G opens it".

Make the edit once and deploy, do not leave it half in the repo and half in the hub. The roadmap is a PUBLIC page (server.js:164-165 lists '/roadmap' in PUBLIC_PAGES), so this is copy customers read, not internal notes. Do not go further and start changing the emote wheel itself: the plugin has a hard-won comment at lines 381-386 saying that calling Debug.sendAnimationEvent straight from the browserMessage handler throws every time, which means a naive refactor breaks the feature and the log line is the only warning.

## 176. Your own board's Broken right now section lists three things that are fixed

**Category:** Handover  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

Broken right now", with exactly three rows all done=false: s1r0 "The installer will not build", s1r1 "The news feed publishes nothing", s1r2 "The bot is not running". The 1.0.196 quote is in s1r1's second cell. Two of the three claims of fixedness check out on this machine: C:\Thornswood\_dist\Thornswood-Setup.zip is 527,227,436 bytes dated 16 September 22:07, and C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Releases\version.json carries launcherVersion 1.1.47 with 40 news entries. The bot could not be verified, because checking it means reaching the VPS and this audit is read-only. Note also that todo.md still opens with the heading "# Patrick" while server.js:143 has already renamed the board to "Patrick".

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, section s1, which is the actual thing to clear. 2. tools/docs-app/data/todo.md, the separate markdown file with a different and more current list, so you do not clear the wrong one. 3. tools/docs-app/server.js:135-158, which shows that `todo` is a board (titled "Patrick") behind an admin gate at /patrick.

**How:** Delete section s1 from the `todo` board in board.json, or set all three rows done=true if the board's convention is to keep ticked rows (its own intro says "Anything ticked is done and kept so you can see it"). The installer row and the news row are demonstrably done. For the bot row, do not guess: ask Patrick to confirm, or have him run `.\tools\Install-Bot.ps1` output or look for the panel in #tickets, because anything that talks to the VPS is his to run. Then renumber the remaining sections, since they are named "1." through "11." by hand and deleting section one leaves the numbering wrong. While in the file, change todo.md's "# Patrick" heading to "# Patrick" to match server.js:143.

**Done when:** `python -c "import json,io; d=json.load(io.open('tools/docs-app/data/board.json',encoding='utf-8')); print([s['name'] for s in d['todo']['sections']])"` no longer lists "1. Broken right now", and the remaining names are numbered 1 upward with no gap. `grep -rn "1\\.0\\.196" tools/docs-app/` returns nothing. `grep -n "^# Patrick" tools/docs-app/data/todo.md` returns nothing.

**Traps:** Two todo sources with the same name is the trap that caught this entry itself: data/todo.md and board.json's `todo` key are different lists and both are live. Boards are edited from the hub as well as the repo and last save wins (server.js:297-298), so an edit made here and not deployed can be silently lost. The section numbers are part of the cell text, not generated, so renumbering is a manual edit in every affected section name. Do not run Install-Bot.ps1, Deploy-Docs.ps1 or anything else on the VPS: CLAUDE.md puts every VPS script on the person's side of the line. Entries 183 to 192 are the rest of this board, so clearing s1 should be one pull request and the rest should not ride along.

## 177. NPCs stand still when nobody is nearby, and behave differently for two people stood beside the same enemy

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The behaviour claim is verified against the project's own documentation: tools/docs-app/data/known.md line 18 says "NPCs stand still when nobody is nearby | SkyMP has no server side AI. An NPC is simulated by a nearby machine or not at all" and line 19 says "NPC behaviour differs between people near the same enemy | Hosting changes every 2 seconds, so whoever is simulating decides the outcome". The two-second figure is documented, not something I could confirm from code in this repo, because the hosting handoff lives in SkyMP itself and not in tools/gamemode.js. Also true and not said in the entry: tools/gamemode.js contains no NPC AI of any kind (no spawn control, no routines), and the collection actively adds NPCs rather than thinning them, since Populated Cities Towns Villages Legendary.esp is line 34 of Profile\Default\plugins.txt.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 11-22, the exact claim as customers read it. 2. tools/gamemode.js, at least the structure: 3,363 lines, mp.onDeath at 1581, mp.onRespawn at 1618, mp.onActivate at 1789, the guard() wrapper at 95. That is the entire server-side behaviour surface, and it is where routines would have to go. 3. The SkyMP docs linked below for what the server side can actually reach.

**How:** This is a rewrite, not a change, and it is the largest item in this slice. Do not start it as one piece of work. The tractable order is: first cut the crowd down (entry 189 for guards, the wider cut for the rest), because fewer NPCs is what makes server-driven routines affordable at all; then pick one hold and one shape of routine (a shopkeeper who is in the shop by day and the inn by night) and drive it entirely from gamemode.js by moving and animating a small set of actors on a server timer, since OnUpdate is the one event still alive; then widen. The roadmap already splits this correctly into rNPC1 "Thinning the crowd" and rNPC2 "NPCs with their own work" in board.json section s10rm. Until any of it exists, do entry 174: stop calling it permanent.

**Done when:** There is no honest test that does not involve the game, and starting the game is not allowed here. The checkable proxy for the first slice is the server log: gamemode.js already logs through a guard() wrapper, so a routine that moves N actors should write N moves per cycle to the server log and that can be read from the VPS without anybody playing. Before any of that, the doc fix in entry 174 is testable with grep.

**Traps:** SkyMP has no server-side AI, so this is not a setting to turn on, it is a system to write. Most Papyrus events are blocked (entry 178), so anything that would normally be a quest script or a package is unavailable and the work has to be done from gamemode.js on the timer. gamemode.js is server code, so a change means a server deploy, and CLAUDE.md says the person runs every script that talks to the VPS. Every change goes out as one complete release. The entry overlaps entry 174 (the doc), 189 (guards out) and 196 (law run by people); doing 177 alone without 189 leaves the world just as crowded, and doing 189 alone without 196 leaves nobody able to act. The roadmap already treats 177 as two rows, so a single pull request called "NPCs" is the wrong shape.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/events.md , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/native.md

## 178. Most scripts never fire, mods and vanilla alike, so anything scripted is decoration until the server implements it

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and the mechanism is findable. tools/docs-app/data/known.md line 21 states it: "Most mod scripts never fire | All Papyrus events except OnUpdate are blocked. Records still work, scripted behaviour does not". The code that does it is in the SkyMP client bundle, not in this repo: C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\plugins\skymp5-client.js contains BlockPapyrusEventsService, whose onceTick calls sp.blockPapyrusEvents(true) and whose onceUpdate calls sp.TESModPlatform.blockPapyrusEvents(true). The "except OnUpdate" half is TESModPlatform's documented behaviour and known.md's claim; I could not confirm it from the bundle, which contains no OnUpdate string at all. So: the blocking is confirmed in code, the one surviving event is confirmed only in the project's own documentation.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 20-36, which lists both the rule and the four mods it breaks. 2. tools/Patch-Client.ps1, all 241 lines but especially the header, because it is the only sanctioned way to change the client bundle and it explains in detail what happened on 17 September when somebody edited the bundle by hand instead. 3. The SkyMP events doc below, for what a hook can and cannot do.

**How:** Do not unblock wholesale. The practical version the entry describes is a per-event decision, and the place to make it is a new block in tools/Patch-Client.ps1 following the shape of the four that exist (lines 97, 128, 159, 230): each is a hashtable with a Name, the exact text it expects, the text it writes, and a marker so it is idempotent. A block that narrowed BlockPapyrusEventsService instead of disabling it would sit there naturally. Start by picking one event with no server-owned side effect and prove the round trip. Meanwhile the cheap half of this entry is the collection: stop shipping mods that only work through blocked events, which is entries 52, 172 and 179.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Patch-Client.ps1 -Check` reports the new block as present or absent without changing anything; that is the read-only test and it is exactly what Ship runs before every publish. A block whose expected text is not found exactly once exits 1, so a wrong patch fails loudly at build time rather than in the game.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md, settled design: the SkyMP client bundle is not built here, the copy in the game folder is patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is. Patch-Client.ps1's header records what happens when that is ignored: on 14 September fixes were made by hand in the bundle, on 16 September at 14:58 another launcher wrote a fresh build over it, Keep took the new file as ours, the 15:23 pack carried it to everyone, and doors were unlocked again on the 17th with nobody noticing. Never hand-edit skymp5-client.js. Unblocking an event is a change to how every machine behaves, so it ships as one complete release with the server. Several events the server already owns (position, health, inventory, equipment, death, door state, harvest state, actor values, per known.md:42) will simply be overwritten if unblocked, so unblocking them makes mods fight the server rather than work.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/events.md , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Category:Papyrus

## 179. SunHelm Survival and SunHelm Diseases fight the server on every timer tick, and Immersive Armors ships the scripted version

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, with the file names a stranger will need. SunHelmSurvival.esp is line 51 of Profile\Default\plugins.txt and SunHelmDiseases.esp is line 64; both .esp files plus SunHelmSurvival.bsa are in C:\Thornswood\Game\Data, along with Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp at line 78. known.md lines 32-33 give the reason: "Polls on a timer, the one script type still running. Writes health, stamina, magicka and carry weight against a server that crops and corrects them." Immersive Armors is in the build but not under that name: it ships as Hothtrooper44_ArmorCompilation.esp (line 27), with Hothtrooper44_Armor_Ecksstra.esp (line 73) and IA CBBE Patch.esp (line 59). known.md:36 records "Immersive Armors, standard version | Distributes through a script with settings that do not persist. A script free version exists." Nothing named "Immersive Armors" exists in Game\Data, which is exactly the trap that would send a stranger looking in the wrong place.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 24-42, both the broken-mods table and the "What the server owns" paragraph, because that paragraph is the list of values a survival system must own rather than write. 2. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, to see all four affected plugin names and their positions. 3. tools/gamemode.js, where a server-side hunger and cold model would live, next to the existing per-character state.

**How:** Two separate jobs, one batch. Immersive Armors is a straight swap in the Nexus collection: replace the standard build with the script-free build, let Vortex deploy, rebuild, and the plugin names may change, which moves the load order. SunHelm is the interesting one: rather than removing it, write hunger, cold and disease into gamemode.js against the same values the server already owns (health, stamina, magicka, carry weight are all on known.md's owned list), then take SunHelmSurvival.esp, SunHelmDiseases.esp and Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp out. That way the mechanic survives and stops fighting. Do the swap and the removals in the same release as entries 172 and 190 so the order moves once.

**Done when:** After the swap: `grep -n "Hothtrooper44\|IA CBBE" C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt` shows the new names, and the generated server list has the same count as the client list. After SunHelm comes out: `grep -in "sunhelm" C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt` returns nothing and `ls C:\Thornswood\Game\Data | grep -i sunhelm` returns nothing. For the server model, the honest check without the game is the server log showing the tick writing values per character.

**Traps:** Do not go looking for a file called Immersive Armors; the plugin is Hothtrooper44_ArmorCompilation.esp and there are three related files, not one. The Embers XD SunHelm patch must come out with SunHelm or it is a plugin with a missing master. Every plugin change moves the load order for everybody at once and ships as one complete release with the server (CLAUDE.md, and known.md:58 says this is why mods are added in batches rather than one at a time). SunHelm survives today only because OnUpdate is the one Papyrus event still alive (entry 178), so "fix SunHelm by unblocking its events" is not available. Nothing touches Vortex's own files: the collection is where a mod is swapped.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition/mods/19281 , https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition

## 180. Mod settings menus reset every session, because a character lives on the server and there is no save for a mod to write into

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. tools/docs-app/data/known.md line 20: "Mod settings menus reset every session | Your character lives on the server rather than in a save file, so there is no co-save for a mod to write its settings into." tools/docs-app/data/tests.md line 162 lists it under "Do not report these". MCMHelper.esp is in the load order (Profile\Default\plugins.txt line 65) and MCMHelper.dll is in Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins, along with McmRecorder.esp at line 82, so the machinery a fix would hook into is present. I could not confirm from code that no co-save exists under SkyMP; that is known.md's claim and it follows from there being no save at all.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md lines 16-22 for the claim and its neighbours. 2. tools/gamemode.js, because it already keeps per-character state and that is the store a settings feature would extend; look at how chargen state is held and written. 3. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js, the front plugin the entry names as the thing that would read settings back at login.

**How:** Scope it small and deliberately: the handful of settings people actually touch, not all of them. Add a per-character key and value store to tools/gamemode.js beside the state it already holds. Have the front plugin (tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js) ask for the map at login and apply it, and send a write when a value changes. McmRecorder is already in the list and records MCM menu walks, so it is worth reading first to see whether replaying a recorded set is cheaper than intercepting individual settings.

**Done when:** Testable without the game by round-tripping the store on the server: write a value for a character id through the gamemode path, restart the server, read it back, and confirm it survived. That is a server-side check and it proves the half that is actually missing. The in-game half cannot be tested here.

**Traps:** Every Papyrus event except OnUpdate is blocked (entry 178), so a mod cannot be relied on to tell you its setting changed through a script event; the change has to come through the interface layer or a poll. This is a server change plus a client plugin change, so it ships as one complete release. gamemode.js runs on the VPS and the person runs the deploy. known.md currently files this under "not going to change", so if this gets built, entry 174's fix has to move the row out of that table in the same release or the page will be telling people not to report a thing that now works.

**Links:** https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition/mods/21858 , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/events.md

## 181. Killmoves and stagger do not show on other people, so a fight looks different to each person in it

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, and I can give the exact location the entry leaves vague. In C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\plugins\skymp5-client.js, class DeathService registers three hooks in its constructor: hookDisableKillMoves(), hookDisableStagger() and hookDisableBlockedAnims(). Each adds an sp.hooks.sendAnimationEvent hook whose enter callback sets ctx.animEventName = "", filtered by the patterns "KillMove*" and "staggerStart". known.md line 22 records the effect. The decisive fact for whoever picks this up: that file is the SkyMP client bundle, which CLAUDE.md says is not built in this repo.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Patch-Client.ps1, all of it, because it is the only sanctioned way to change that bundle and its header explains exactly why hand edits do not survive. 2. The SkyMP hooks documentation below, for what sendAnimationEvent hooks can do and the one-wildcard limit on filters. 3. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-emotes.js lines 1-20 and 366-450, which is a working example in this repo of sending an animation event to the local character and the pitfalls around it.

**How:** Add a fifth block to tools/Patch-Client.ps1 in the same shape as the four at lines 97, 128, 159 and 230: a Name, the exact expected text from DeathService, the replacement, and a marker so it is idempotent. Instead of blanking the name, forward it: leave ctx.animEventName alone locally and send the event name to the server, then have the server replicate it to nearby machines, which apply it with Debug.sendAnimationEvent on the remote actor. That is the same shape as the position feed that already works. The emote plugin at thornswood-emotes.js:436-450 is the template for applying a received animation event, including the try/catch that exists because sendAnimationEvent throws in the wrong context.

**Done when:** `.\tools\Patch-Client.ps1 -Check` reports the new block and exits 0, and exits 1 if the expected text is not found exactly once. That is the read-only test, it is what Ship runs before every publish, and it catches the most likely failure, which is upstream changing DeathService under you. The visible result needs the game and cannot be tested here.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md, settled design: skymp5-client.js is not built here, it is patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is. Patch-Client.ps1's header records the cost of forgetting that: hand edits on 14 September were wiped on 16 September at 14:58 when another launcher wrote a fresh bundle, Keep adopted it as ours, the 15:23 pack shipped it to everybody, and locks were broken again by the 17th. The bundle in the game folder is another server's build (the other server's until 16 September, Daedric Online's since), so the expected text can change without notice, which is why each block fails the release rather than patching loosely. sendAnimationEvent hooks accept at most one wildcard in a filter. This is a client and server change together, so one complete release. known.md:22 currently calls this permanent, so entry 174's table move has to ride with it.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/events.md , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/native.md

## 182. Start On Save is in the mod list and never loads a save, and pointing it at a character that does not exist crashed a fresh install

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The facts are right, the causation is backwards. Start On Save is in the build as an SKSE DLL, not a plugin: C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\po3_StartOnSave.dll and po3_StartOnSave.ini. It is true the launcher points it at a character that does not exist, but that is the deliberate fix, not the fault. tools/app/Chars.cs:687-712 (WriteStartIni) writes sCharacterName = Thornswood-Nobody on purpose, and the comment at 668-686 explains why: bStartNewGame false is not enough, because with sSaveFile blank the mod loads the newest save in the folder whenever there is one, and sCharacterName is the only filter applied first. The crashes came from the opposite thing: bStartNewGame true made it press New Game, Skyrim Unbound's start began, and SkyMP's own load landed on top, listed as crash-2026-09-13-01-20-50 at 33s and crash-2026-09-13-01-14-07 at 30s. The sentinel claim is verified: Shell.cs:4264, inside CollectionProblem(), reads `if (!Checks.StartOnSaveInstalled()) return "stale";`, and Checks.cs:23-27 tests for the DLL. One live discrepancy: the ini on disk at C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\SKSE\Plugins\po3_StartOnSave.ini has sCharacterName blank, not Thornswood-Nobody, so that file was written by something other than the current launcher build.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Chars.cs lines 636-715, the whole comment block and WriteStartIni, which is the only honest account of this in the project and names the crash logs. 2. tools/app/Checks.cs lines 20-30 and 450-460, for StartOnSaveInstalled and how it is used. 3. tools/app/Shell.cs lines 4255-4282, CollectionProblem and ShowCollectionBar, which is what shows the collection bar to a customer.

**How:** If it comes out: remove po3_StartOnSave.dll and .ini from the collection, then fix the three places that name it. Replace the sentinel first, because removing the DLL without it makes every install report the collection as stale: change Shell.cs:4264 to test a file that is genuinely part of the current build and unlikely to be replaced. Then delete Chars.WriteStartIni and its call, delete Checks.StartOnSaveInstalled and its two uses at Checks.cs:457-458, and drop the Wants entry in tools/Run-Everything.ps1:43 and the special case in tools/New-GameFolder.ps1:201. tools/Add-Characters.ps1 is the one-shot installer for it and would go too, which overlaps entry 168.

**Done when:** `grep -rn "StartOnSave" tools/` returns nothing outside comments, the launcher compiles with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root C:\Thornswood-dev -NoPause`, and on a build with the DLL absent the collection bar does not appear, which can be checked by reading CollectionProblem's inputs rather than by launching. All of it is checkable with the game closed.

**Traps:** Do not "fix" the ini by clearing sCharacterName: the blank name is what caused the 17 September fresh install to load somebody's level 21 single-player save at the main menu while SkyMP was loading its own, which is the 30-second crash class in the comment. The name is the filter. Shell.cs and Checks.cs both need a line in #dev before editing, per CLAUDE.md, because both machines touch them. This is the collection sentinel, so removing the mod and leaving the check is a release that tells every customer their collection is out of date. Removing an SKSE DLL does not move the plugin load order, so it is less dangerous than entry 172, but it still ships as one complete release. The launcher is C# 5 only.

**Links:** https://github.com/powerof3/Start-On-Save , https://github.com/powerof3/Start-On-Save/blob/master/src/main.cpp

## 183. Nothing in the creation hall is a choice, and it is where your face is made but not anything else

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified against both the documentation and the code. tools/docs-app/data/known.md line 48: "Nothing in the creation hall is a choice | Your profession, half, class and town are chosen in the launcher before Play, and the hall holds nothing to pick up or read", and line 50: "Your face is made in the hall, not in the launcher | The race menu opens once the game has loaded." The launcher side is real: tools/app/Creation.cs holds the full picker, seven professions with their halves (Creation.cs:74-112) and five classes (133-139), and gamemode.js:616 carries the generated CHARGEN table with nine towns and their arrival cells and coordinates, so profession, half, class and town are all settled before the game starts.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Creation.cs lines 58-139, the comment block plus Professions and Classes, which is the clearest statement in the repo of what is chosen where and why. 2. tools/gamemode.js around line 616 (the CHARGEN constant) and 700-780, where a character is placed into an arrival cell, because that is the code that would change if the hall gained a purpose. 3. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-chargen.js, the client side of creation.

**How:** Two honest options and the entry is right that the status quo is not one of them. Option A, give the hall something to do: a name rule, a rules acceptance and a first look at the profession all sit naturally in a room, and they are things a customer should read once before entering the world. That is Creation Kit work in Thornswood-Content.esp plus a handler in gamemode.js. Option B, move the face into the launcher and skip the room: RaceMenu is already in the load order (RaceMenu.esp line 54, RaceMenuPlugin.esp 55, RaceMenuMorphsCBBE.esp 56, RaceMenuMorphsHIMBO.esp 13), and Chars.cs already comments about rebuilding facegen from the TESNPC record, so appearance is already something the server holds. Decide in #dev which, then do one.

**Done when:** If A, the hall's new content exists as records in Thornswood-Content.esp and gamemode.js has a handler for the activation, which can be confirmed by grep without the game. If B, the launcher's creation page carries the face step and compiles, and gamemode.js no longer routes new characters through the hall. Either way the checkable end state is that known.md line 48 is no longer true and has been rewritten.

**Traps:** Adding records to Thornswood-Content.esp changes that plugin's size and checksum, and the load order check compares every plugin by name, size and crc32 (known.md:54), so it is a full release across server and every machine, not a content drop. This entry is the same subject as entries 42 and 43, so do not solve it three times. The launcher is C# 5 only, which matters for option B. Creation.cs carries a real warning at lines 114-121: an earlier rename of the Trades array broke the build in two places that turn a saved trade key back into a label, so the old name was kept; do not tidy that name away. Chars.cs and Creation.cs are where the 17 September fresh-install crash lived, so changes to creation deserve the fresh-install test that todo.md's Now section already asks for.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 184. An optional pack can be installed and never removed, because nothing records what it wrote

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** wrong

**Correction:** This has already shipped. tools/app/Updater.cs writes a per-pack file list at install time: Install() builds a `wrote` list, tags each line N| for new or R| for replaced (line 543), and writes it to PackListFile(p.id) under Paths.PacksDir as <id>.files (lines 282-287, 555-556). applied.txt is no longer append-only either: Unapply(id) at lines 239-255 reads it and rewrites it with File.WriteAllLines, and its comment says in so many words that the file was append only and that an optional pack can come off again. RemovePack exists at line 661 and is a complete implementation: it reads the .files list, deletes only the N| lines, skips anything another pack or Vortex has claimed since (ClaimedElsewhere and DeployedNow, lines 680-703), prunes empty folders inside a fence, and tells the customer to press Scan then Fix for the R| files whose originals were not kept. Shell.cs wires it: PackAction at 2044 calls Updater.RemovePack when installed and ApplyPack when not. The entry appears to have been copied from board.json's todo section s3r2, which is stale.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Updater.cs lines 208-320 (Applied, Skip, MarkApplied, Unapply, IsInstalled, PackListFile) and 430-570 (ApplyPack and Install) and 650-760 (RemovePack), which together are the whole feature. 2. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, section s3, so you can see which card is out of date and mark it. 3. tools/app/Shell.cs lines 1852-1912 and 2044-2079, the rows and the button handler.

**How:** Close the entry and tick board.json todo section s3, rows s3r0 to s3r5, or delete the section. The only thing genuinely unfinished in this area is that nothing has been published: C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Releases\version.json has `optional: 0`, and tools/optional-packs.txt lists no packs yet, only its header. So the work is to choose what comes out of the installer, add lines to tools/optional-packs.txt in the documented `id | name shown in the launcher | path under Game` format, and have Patrick run `.\tools\Publish-Pack.ps1 -DryRun` and then for real.

**Done when:** Read it back from the artefacts, no game needed: `grep -n "RemovePack\|PackListFile" tools/app/Updater.cs` shows both, and after a pack is published, `python -c "import json,io; d=json.load(io.open(r'C:\\Users\\evilp\\Downloads\\Thornswood-Releases\\version.json',encoding='utf-8')); print(len(d.get('optional',[])))"` prints more than 0.

**Traps:** The biggest trap here is the one this entry fell into: board.json's todo board is a snapshot that has drifted from the code, so read Updater.cs before believing any card in section s3. Updater.cs needs a line in #dev before editing (CLAUDE.md names it explicitly). ApplyPack's comment at lines 452-456 records a subtle bug that was already fixed: a retry that reruns the list would write a second applied.txt line and a fresh file list in which every path reads as one this pack overwrote, putting the pack's own files out of reach of its own Remove. Do not remove the IsInstalled guard at line 457. Install() deliberately does not keep copies of files it overwrites, which is why Remove tells people to press Scan and Fix; that is a design choice, not an oversight. Publish-Pack.ps1 writes to the public releases repo, so the person runs it, not a Claude.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.compression.zipfile

## 185. A pack has no name and no size, so a page listing packs has nothing to show

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The C# half is already done; the publisher half is done too. tools/app/Config.cs:284-308 shows Pack carrying id, url, sha256, notes AND name and bytes, with a comment at 291-294 saying only the optional packs use the last two, plus a Title property that falls back to id and a Size property that formats GB, MB or KB. Shell.cs:1879-1893 already uses p.bytes to total "more available" and p.Title to filter and label. And tools/Publish-Pack.ps1:204-206 writes id, name, url, sha256 and bytes into version.json's `optional` array. The one true residue: tools/Publish-Update.ps1:804-809 still writes only id, url, sha256 and notes, but that is the required update pack path, where by design there is nothing to show and no size worth warning about. So nothing here needs doing.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Config.cs lines 284-310, the Pack class in full. 2. tools/Publish-Pack.ps1 lines 195-215, where the optional array is written. 3. tools/app/Shell.cs lines 1874-1894, which consumes both fields.

**How:** Close the entry and tick board.json todo row s3r3, which is the stale card this came from. If anybody wants to be thorough, confirm the two paths stay distinct: Publish-Update.ps1 writes `packs` (required, applied without asking) and Publish-Pack.ps1 writes `optional` (offered with a button). Do not add name and bytes to the required path; the comment at Config.cs:291-294 explains why they would be noise there.

**Done when:** `grep -n "public string name\|public long bytes\|string Title\|string Size" tools/app/Config.cs` returns all four. `grep -n "bytes" tools/Publish-Pack.ps1` shows the field being written. Both with the game closed.

**Traps:** Config.cs is read and written through DataContractJsonSerializer, which is why Build-Launcher.ps1:72-77 has to reference System.Xml explicitly under /noconfig; adding a field means adding [DataMember] or it silently will not round-trip. The launcher is C# 5, so the Size property is written as a property with a getter rather than an expression-bodied member on purpose. Do not trust board.json section s3 as a description of the code.

## 186. There is no way to publish an optional pack, and nothing stops one containing a plugin

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** wrong

**Correction:** tools/Publish-Pack.ps1 exists and is 241 lines. Its header states the rule the entry says is missing: "THE ONE RULE, and it is checked rather than trusted: a pack may not contain a plugin ... A pack with a plugin in it is refused and nothing is uploaded." The check is real at line 109: any file whose extension is .esp, .esm, .esl or .bsa is collected into a $plugins list and the run stops. It reads tools/optional-packs.txt (the same file Package-Thornswood.ps1 reads, so the installer and the packs cannot drift), zips, uploads as a release asset, and merges into version.json's `optional` array with id, name, url, sha256 and bytes (lines 197-213), replacing any row with the same id so two rows never carry the same name and different checksums. It takes -Only, -DryRun and -NoPause.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Publish-Pack.ps1, all 241 lines, especially the header (1-23) and the refusal at 99-138. 2. tools/optional-packs.txt, which is currently header only and documents the `id | name | path under Game` format. 3. tools/Package-Thornswood.ps1, because it reads the same list and leaves those paths out of the installer, so a line added in one place changes both.

**How:** Close the entry as built. The work that remains is to use it: pick what should come out of the installer (the 527 MB installer is mostly textures and meshes, so a high-resolution texture pack is the obvious first one), add lines to tools/optional-packs.txt, then have Patrick run `.\tools\Publish-Pack.ps1 -DryRun` to build and check with no upload, and `.\tools\Publish-Pack.ps1` when it reads right. -Only <id> does one pack.

**Done when:** `python -c "import json,io; d=json.load(io.open(r'C:\\Users\\evilp\\Downloads\\Thornswood-Releases\\version.json',encoding='utf-8')); print(len(d.get('optional',[])), [p.get('id') for p in d.get('optional',[])])"` prints a non-zero count today it prints 0. Before that, a -DryRun that exits 0 proves the pack builds and contains no plugin.

**Traps:** Publish-Pack.ps1 uploads to the public Thornswood-Releases repo and rewrites version.json, so the person runs it, never a Claude; CLAUDE.md puts every Publish-* script on his side. It defaults -ReleaseDir to C:\Users\evilp\Downloads\Thornswood-Releases and -Repo to EvilPatrick06/Thornswood-Releases, which are Patrick's paths, so Djinn's machine needs both passed. The refusal also rejects .bsa, which is stricter than "no plugin" and is right, because a BSA is loaded by a plugin, and the script says so at line 130: taking one optional shows as purple textures. The two scripts share one list on purpose so an installer can never ship missing something nobody can download; do not give Publish-Pack its own list.

**Links:** https://docs.github.com/en/rest/releases/assets , https://cli.github.com/manual/gh_release

## 187. The Add-ons page does not exist, and adding a twelfth page means appending to four parallel arrays

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The second half is exactly right and the first half is wrong. The four parallel things are real: tools/app/Shell.cs:720-722 declares eleven page constants PagePlay through PageSettings (0 to 10), 724-726 is PageTitles with eleven strings, 544 assigns navButtons and 549 assigns pages, and line 543 carries the comment "Same order as the PageXxx constants above, and as PageTitles." ShowPage(i) at 728 indexes all of them by number and then dispatches by if-else on the constant. But the Add-ons surface does exist: it is a tab on the Mods page, not a page. Shell.cs:64 declares `modTab` with the comment "0 server plugins, 1 script extender, 2 your own, 3 add-ons", there is a tabAddons button and a btnRefreshAddons, and BuildPackRows at 1852 draws the list from pendingDoc.optional with a count, a size total and a search filter, PackRow at 1913 draws each row, and PackAction at 2044 installs or removes on a background thread with a guard against the game being open. So the feature is built and the page-array problem is untouched.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Shell.cs lines 720-748, the constants, PageTitles and ShowPage, which is the whole indexing scheme in thirty lines. 2. Shell.cs lines 540-560, where navButtons and pages are filled in order. 3. Shell.cs lines 1852-1970 and 2044-2079, the add-ons rows that already exist, so you do not rebuild them.

**How:** Correct the entry to be only about the arrays, then fix that. Replace the four parallel structures with one array of a small Page type holding the title, the nav Button, the UIElement and an Action to rebuild it. Declare it once in the order the constants use now, have ShowPage iterate it and invoke the rebuild action instead of the if-else chain at 740-747, and delete PageTitles and the constants. That is a contained slice and it is a good first move for entry 171 because it touches one region of the file. Keep the C# 5 limits in mind: a small nested class with a constructor, not an expression-bodied member, and no out var.

**Done when:** `grep -n "PageTitles\|PagePlay" tools/app/Shell.cs` returns only the new declaration, ShowPage is under fifteen lines, and the launcher compiles with `.\tools\Build-Launcher.ps1 -Root C:\Thornswood-dev -NoPause` exiting 0. Adding a twelfth page should then be one entry in one array, which is the thing to demonstrate in the pull request description.

**Traps:** Do not build an Add-ons page from scratch: BuildPackRows, PackRow, PackAction and the add-ons tab already exist and work, and duplicating them would give customers two places to install the same thing. Shell.cs needs a line in #dev before editing because both machines touch it. Launcher code is C# 5 only. The page order is not arbitrary: it is the order of the nav buttons the customer sees, and the constants are referenced from other files, so grep the whole tools/app folder for PagePlay and its siblings before deleting them. Keep it to one pull request, per the repo's one-fix-per-request rule.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/desktop/wpf/controls/ , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/language-reference/builtin-types/enum

## 188. The Tailor has no starter kit, and a Platewright and an Edgewright start with the same materials

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in code, with one refinement and one correction to the framing. tools/app/Creation.cs:108-111 defines the Tailor with its noKit flag set true and the card text already apologising: "Cloth rather than steel. No starter kit built yet, so you begin with only what everybody gets." The comment at 65-72 gives the reason: "The key sent to the server is still the PROFESSION's, because the starter kits are form ids in Thornswood-Content.esp keyed by that and nothing else." The halves genuinely share one kit: the generated CHARGEN table at gamemode.js:616 keys gear by profession form id only and carries nothing per half. The refinement: that same comment says "Tailor and Gatherer have no kit yet", but Gatherer is a class (Creation.cs:138), and Creation.cs:127-132 says a class carries no starter kit and is not meant to, so the comment reads as if two professions were missing kits when only one is. There are seven professions (warrior, mage, alchemist, cook, smith, enchanter, tailor) and seventeen halves in total, not the fourteen the board card claims.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Creation.cs lines 40-140, the Trade and Option types, the comment explaining profession versus half versus class, and both arrays. 2. tools/gamemode.js line 616, the generated CHARGEN constant, to see the exact shape a kit takes: a form id keyed to a `key` with a `gear` array of [formid, count] pairs. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md, the public page, which already promises per-half distinction to customers.

**How:** Creation Kit work in Thornswood-Content.esp. Add a kit record keyed `tailor` in the same block as the existing sixteen, following the pattern of the neighbouring records (the gear array is form id plus count, mostly Skyrim.esm ids). Then add a small per-half list for each half so a Platewright and an Edgewright differ: the board's own suggestion of one small list per half rather than a whole second kit is the cheaper shape. Then regenerate the CHARGEN table that is pasted into gamemode.js at line 616, and clear the noKit flag on the Tailor at Creation.cs:109 and rewrite its card text so it no longer apologises. Fix the misleading "Tailor and Gatherer" comment at Creation.cs:67 while you are in the file.

**Done when:** `grep -o '"key":"tailor"' tools/gamemode.js` returns a hit, `grep -n 'true,' tools/app/Creation.cs | grep -n tailor` no longer shows the noKit flag set, and the launcher compiles. All checkable with the game closed.

**Traps:** Editing Thornswood-Content.esp changes its size and checksum, and the load order check compares every plugin by name, size and crc32 (known.md:54), so this is a full release across the server and every machine, not a content change. The CHARGEN table in gamemode.js is generated and pasted, so hand-editing it there and not regenerating means the next regeneration silently drops the work. Creation.cs carries a warning at 114-121 that renaming the Trades array broke the build in two places that turn a saved key into a label, so leave that name alone. Classes get no kit and are not meant to (Creation.cs:127-132): do not helpfully add one. Launcher code is C# 5. The profession split is what the public classes.md page sells, so a card that apologises for itself is customer-facing copy, not an internal note.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://www.creationkit.com/index.php?title=Main_Page , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/LeveledItem

## 189. Bethesda's guards are still standing in every hold, so nobody has a reason to deal with a person

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified but trivially so, and the entry understates the shape of the problem. Vanilla guard records live in Skyrim.esm, which is necessarily in the load order, so "still in the load order" is true by construction and is not something a plugin removal fixes. The collection also carries Guard Dialogue Overhaul.esp (Profile\Default\plugins.txt line 28) and a Skyrim Unbound GDO patch (line 94), which means guard behaviour is actively modded rather than merely inherited. And it pushes the other way too: Populated Cities Towns Villages Legendary.esp (line 34) and Immersive Patrols II.esp (line 45) add NPCs. tools/gamemode.js contains no NPC spawn control at all, so there is nothing server-side today that could suppress a guard. The board card this comes from is todo section s7r0, done=false, which says "Law is supposed to be people. While Bethesda's guards are standing there nobody has a reason to deal with a person about a bounty."

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, all 96 entries, to see what adds NPCs and what changes guards. 2. tools/gamemode.js, the DATA constant near line 23, which already holds a holds-to-crimeFaction map for all nine holds plus Solstheim; that map is the only crime-adjacent thing the server knows and it is what a replacement would build on. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/rules.md, because the rules page is built on the premise that law is people, so this is a promise already made.

**How:** Guards cannot be removed by dropping a plugin, so pick a mechanism and agree it in #dev. The two realistic ones: a patch plugin in Thornswood-Content.esp that disables or empties the guard leveled lists and guard NPC placements per hold, or server-side suppression in gamemode.js on the same timer used for everything else. The patch plugin is more reliable because it needs no per-frame work; the server approach avoids moving the load order. Either way, do it hold by hold and start with one so the effect can be judged. Use the crimeFaction map already in gamemode.js's DATA as the key: it maps WhiterunHoldLocation to 0x0267EA and so on for all nine holds.

**Done when:** If it is a plugin change, the check is that the patch exists and that the client and server plugin lists match after the release, which is a grep and a line count. If it is server-side, the check is the server log showing N guards suppressed per hold per cycle, readable from the VPS with nobody playing.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md is explicit that a change to settled design or to how a release ships is a #dev conversation first, and this is a world-shaping decision, not a bug fix. Doing this alone is worse than not doing it: the entry says so and entry 196 confirms it, because taking law out of the world without staff having in-world powers (entry 16) leaves nobody able to act at all. Three rows land together or none do. Any plugin change moves the load order and is one complete release across server and every machine. Note the collection currently contains two mods whose whole job is to put more people in the world (Populated Cities and Immersive Patrols II), so this work and the collection are pulling in opposite directions and that needs settling before code.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Faction

## 190. Strip the load order to Special Edition only, so Anniversary content is never required

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The count is defensible and the last sentence is already done. C:\Thornswood\Game\Data holds exactly 102 .esm, .esp and .esl files, which is where the 102 comes from; Profile\Default\plugins.txt lists 96 starred entries, and the difference is Skyrim.esm, Update.esm, Dawnguard.esm, HearthFires.esm, Dragonborn.esm and _ResourcePack.esl. The board card s7r1 this derives from says "the current 123 plugins", which is stale, so the entry's 102 is the better number. I scanned the file header of every .esp in Game\Data for master declarations and found no plugin declaring any cc*.esm, cc*.esl or cc*.esp master, which supports the board's own conclusion that this is a check rather than a cut. Wrong: "say on the download page which edition is needed" is already said. tools/docs-app/data/site/join.md:5 says "Skyrim Special Edition on Steam, on build 1.6.1170", line 10 says "Anniversary Edition is not required and never will be", and home.html:787 repeats the build number.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, the 96 entries in load order. 2. tools/Publish-Update.ps1 lines 325-340, where $vanillaDataFiles and $vanillaDataPatterns already enumerate the base game files and cc* patterns, which is the list any audit should reuse rather than reinvent. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/join.md lines 1-20, to confirm what customers are already told.

**How:** A master scan is not a full audit. Do it in three passes. First, masters: read the TES4 header of each plugin and list every MAST subrecord, then flag any cc*.es? or Anniversary-only master. That pass is clean today. Second, records: a plugin can reference a Creation Club form id without declaring the master if it was built carelessly, so run an xEdit error check over the load order and look for unresolved references. Third, assets: check for meshes or textures that only ship with the Anniversary upgrade. tools/Audit-Mods.ps1 already exists in the repo and is the natural place to put pass one. Then leave join.md alone, because it already says the right thing, and consider saying it on /downloads too since that is the page the Download button points at.

**Done when:** A script in tools/ that prints, for each of the 96 plugins, its declared masters, and exits non-zero if any master matches cc* or is not in the base game set. Run it and it exits 0 today. That is a genuine regression test that can be added to the build check and run with the game closed.

**Traps:** Do not treat a clean master scan as proof: it only catches declared dependencies. _ResourcePack.esl is present in Game\Data and is part of the free post-1.6.1130 update rather than the paid Anniversary upgrade, so do not flag it as a cut candidate without checking. Any drop or replacement moves the load order for everybody and is one complete release with the server, so batch it with entries 52, 172 and 179 as the entry says. plugins.txt is derived from what Vortex deployed, so a change is made in the Nexus collection, not in the file. Do not re-add the edition note to join.md; it is there twice already and a third statement that drifts is worse than one that does not.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Anniversary_Edition , https://www.nexusmods.com/skyrimspecialedition

## 191. Discord onboarding is written out field by field and has never been switched on

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Right about where it is not: it is in board.json, not todo.md. The section is tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, section id s24n, named "8. Discord onboarding, field by field", nine rows r25n through r34n, all done=false. The copy is indeed fully written. Two corrections to the detail. First, it is ten options, not ten citizen roles: r27n says "Every one grants Member plus its own Citizen role. The tenth grants Member only", and the tenth is "Not decided yet", so nine Citizen roles. Second, r25n contradicts itself: its middle cell says "These are nine, six writable" while its own list says "That is nine total and five writable. The first four are read-only." Nine channels with four read-only is five writable, so "six" is wrong, and Discord's minimum is exactly five writable, which means this plan has zero margin. That is why r29n already warns "If it refuses, it is the channel count."

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, section s24n, all nine rows, which is the whole script to follow. 2. Discord's Community Onboarding FAQ, linked below, for the current minimums (at least 7 default channels, at least 5 of which let @everyone view and send). 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json section s5 row s5r0, the shorter "Onboarding" card, so you do not do the work twice from two cards.

**How:** Nothing to run and no code. In Discord: Server Settings, Onboarding. Add the nine default channels from r25n (#welcome, #rules, #start-here, #news read-only; #general, #media, #character-introductions, #tales, #rp-appreciation writable). Add question one from r26n as pick-one with members-must-answer, wording "Which hold does your character call home?", and give it the ten options from r27n, each granting Member plus its Citizen role except "Not decided yet" which grants Member only. Add question two from r28n as pick-several and optional, no roles, channels only. Then the Server Guide rows r31n to r33n, which carry the exact text to paste. Finish with r34n: Server Settings, Onboarding, Preview at the top right, and walk it once. Fix the "six writable" typo in r25n while you are in board.json.

**Done when:** The Preview at Server Settings, Onboarding walks end to end and each option shows the role you meant. Before that, the checkable repo-side test is that r25n no longer contradicts itself: `python -c "import json,io; d=json.load(io.open('tools/docs-app/data/board.json',encoding='utf-8')); print([r['cells'] for s in d['todo']['sections'] if s['id']=='s24n' for r in s['rows'] if r['id']=='r25n'])"` should show five writable in both cells.

**Traps:** Five writable channels is the exact minimum, not a comfortable margin, and read-only channels do not count toward it, which r29n already anticipates. The Citizen role names have to be created before the question is built or the options cannot grant them, and the same naming discipline matters for entry 192, where Patreon matches tier names to Discord roles exactly. This is server settings, so a Claude cannot do it and there is nothing to ship; do not open a branch for it. board.json is edited from the hub as well as the repo and last save wins, so make the typo fix once. The entry is right that this is the natural home for the rules acceptance and the age question from entries 61 and 153, so agree those before switching onboarding on rather than switching it on twice.

**Links:** https://support.discord.com/hc/en-us/articles/11074987197975-Community-Onboarding-FAQ , https://support.discord.com/hc/en-us/articles/10394859532823-Community-Onboarding-Examples , https://support.discord.com/hc/en-us/articles/13497665141655-Server-Guide-FAQ , https://support.discord.com/hc/en-us/articles/360047132851-Enabling-Your-Community-Server

## 192. Patreon is not connected to Discord, so tiers hand out nothing

**Category:** Discord  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified, with one thing the entry assumes that is not true yet. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, section s6 "6. Patreon": s6r2 "The link is live" is done=true ("thornswood.net now points at patreon.com/thornswood in the nav and the footer"), and s6r1 "Connect Discord to Patreon" is done=false with the note "Tier names must match the Discord roles exactly." Section s4pt "10. Patreon, every word to paste" has r9pt "Connect Discord to Patreon" and r10pt "Point each tier at its role", both done=false. But r5pt "The page blurb" and the three tier rows r6pt, r7pt and r8pt are also done=false, so the Patreon page itself may not be published. If it is not, nobody can pay yet, which softens the entry's "somebody who pays gets a charge and no slots" into a risk rather than a live fault. Tier names are Milk Drinker ($3), Talos Worshiper ($7) and Thalmor Boot-Licker ($15).

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, `todo` key, sections s6 and s4pt, which together hold every word to paste and the exact order of operations. 2. Patreon's own "Setting up Discord for your members", linked below, because the bot's role position in the Discord hierarchy is the thing that silently breaks this. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/support.md, the public page customers read before they pay.

**How:** Order matters. First create the three Discord roles named exactly Milk Drinker, Talos Worshiper and Thalmor Boot-Licker. Then in Patreon: Settings, Apps, connect Discord (r9pt), pick the Thornswood server, and set each tier's role individually (r10pt says per tier, not once for all three). Then move the Patreon bot's own role above the three tier roles in the Discord role list, because a bot cannot assign a role that sits above it; that is what the "Discord isn't working for my members" article is about and it is not in the board card. Then r12pt: subscribe to the bottom tier yourself for a month, watch the role land, cancel. If the site link needs repointing, r11pt gives the command, `.\tools\Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 -Patreon <url> -NoSecrets`, and Patrick runs it.

**Done when:** Subscribe to Milk Drinker on your own account, wait for the sync, and confirm the Milk Drinker role appears on you in Discord, then cancel. That is r12pt and it is the only test that proves the whole chain. Repo-side, `grep -rn "patreon" tools/docs-app/data/site/support.md tools/docs-app/public/home.html` confirms the link is where the board says it is.

**Traps:** The names are the integration. Patreon matches on the tier name, so "Talos Worshipper" with two Ps, or a renamed role, breaks it silently and nobody finds out until a customer says they paid and got nothing. The Patreon bot's role has to sit above the tier roles or assignment fails with no error worth reading. Set-DiscordLogin.ps1 touches site configuration and secrets, so Patrick runs it; the -NoSecrets switch exists for exactly that reason and the actual values live in the site config on the VPS and are never printed. The tier copy in r6pt to r8pt promises extra character slots, which is a launcher and server behaviour nobody has built, so connecting Patreon before those slots exist creates the same problem in a different place. This overlaps entry 191 on role naming: do both in one sitting.

**Links:** https://support.patreon.com/hc/en-us/articles/213552323-Setting-up-Discord-for-your-members , https://support.patreon.com/hc/en-us/articles/360037465012-Discord-isn-t-working-for-my-members , https://support.patreon.com/hc/en-us/articles/212052266-Getting-Discord-access , https://support.patreon.com/hc/en-us/articles/4408372541581-Troubleshooting-Discord-issues

## 193. Property you actually own

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. The roadmap in tools/docs-app/data/board.json, section s3rm "Being built now", row r4rm reads "Property you actually own | Buy it, furnish it, lock it, defend it. Land inside a hold means a steward you speak with, not a menu you buy from", done=false. tools/gamemode.js (3,363 lines) contains nothing for plots or a steward: no such handler, and the only ownership-adjacent thing in it is the crimeFaction map per hold in the DATA constant. Note that the internal board splits this differently: todo section s7r3 is a single row "Property, with real keys" covering both this and entry 194, while the public roadmap splits them into r4rm and r5rm.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js, the structure: DATA near line 23, mp.onActivate at 1789, the guard() wrapper at 95, and how per-character state is held, because a plot is per-character state attached to a world reference. 2. tools/docs-app/data/board.json roadmap section s3rm, for the promise as written. 3. tools/docs-app/public/home.html, the landing page, because entry 76 is about the same copy and this feature is what that copy sells.

**How:** This sits on top of entry 194 and should not start before it. The shape: a plot is a world reference plus an owner character id plus a lock, held server-side in gamemode.js, and a steward is an NPC whose activation opens a server-driven interface rather than a vanilla menu. gamemode.js already owns door open state and container contents and lock state (known.md:42), and the lockpicking work shipped on 17 September gives a working lock, so the server half has the primitives. The in-world half is Creation Kit work in Thornswood-Content.esp for the plots and the stewards. Either build it or take the landing page copy down, which is entry 76.

**Done when:** Server-side and checkable with nobody playing: set an owner on a plot reference through the gamemode path, restart the server, read it back, and confirm the ownership and the lock state survived. That proves the persistence half, which is the half that is actually hard.

**Traps:** Needs entry 194 underneath it to be worth anything, which the entry says and the internal board agrees with by keeping them as one row. Most Papyrus events are blocked (entry 178), so a steward cannot be a quest script; it has to be server-side on activation. Adding plot and steward records to Thornswood-Content.esp moves that plugin's checksum and is one complete release across server and every machine. gamemode.js runs on the VPS so the person deploys it. The landing page already advertises this: either it ships or that copy comes down, and shipping half of it is the worst option because customers will look for it.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp/blob/main/docs/skyrim_platform/events.md

## 194. Real locks and real keys

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. Roadmap section s3rm row r5rm: "Real locks and real keys | The key is a thing you carry. Hand it over, have a smith cut a copy, or lose it to a pickpocket. Anybody can try the lock", done=false. The internal board card todo s7r3 adds the implementation note the entry repeats: "Needs a pool of key records in Thornswood-Content.esp."
