The floor the entry mentions is real: tools/Patch-Client.ps1 block 2 (line 128, "locks, activators and furniture") is the 17 September lockpicking fix, and its header explains that upstream's dealWithRef unlocked every reference the server tracks, so every lock in the world was simply gone until that patch, and now a locked reference keeps Skyrim's own handling until it is open.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Patch-Client.ps1, the header and block 2 at line 128, because that is the lock behaviour this stands on and the comment explains exactly what upstream does and why it had to be changed. 2. tools/gamemode.js, which already owns door open state, container contents and lock state per known.md:42. 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, todo section s7r3, for the one-line implementation note.

**How:** Creation Kit first: add a pool of KEYM (key) records to Thornswood-Content.esp, enough for every lock the design will ever need, in a contiguous form id block so the server can allocate from it. Then server-side in gamemode.js: map lock to key, hold which character carries which key, and handle the open attempt, since the server already owns door open state and inventory contents. Handing over, cutting a copy at a smith and pickpocketing all fall out of the key being a real inventory item once the server owns the mapping. Do this before entry 193 and before entry 195's "guards can tear them down", both of which assume locks and keys work.

**Done when:** Server-side round trip with nobody playing: assign a key to a character, restart the server, confirm the key and the lock mapping survived, and confirm an open attempt from a character without the key is refused in the server log. All readable from the VPS.

**Traps:** The lock behaviour lives in the SkyMP client bundle, which CLAUDE.md says is not built here and is patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is. Patch-Client.ps1's header records what happened when somebody edited the bundle by hand on 14 September: another launcher wrote a fresh build on 16 September at 14:58, Keep took it as ours, the 15:23 pack shipped it to everybody, and doors were unlocked again by the 17th. Any further lock change goes in a new Patch-Client block with expected text and a marker, never by hand. Adding key records changes Thornswood-Content.esp's checksum, so one complete release across server and every machine. Allocate the key pool generously in one go, because adding more later is another checksum change and another full release.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Key , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 195. Notice boards in every hold

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** Verified except the removal date, which I cannot confirm. Roadmap section s3rm row r6rm: "Notice boards in every hold | Pay coin to pin up work, warnings, rumours or a reward for a name. Guards can tear them down, and will", done=false. Internal board todo s7r2 adds "Replaces the notice board mod already in the list." Missives is indeed out of the build: Missives.esp is not in Profile\Default\plugins.txt and not in C:\Thornswood\Game\Data. But the repo has no commit recording the removal, because the mod list lives in the Nexus collection rather than in git, so "removed on 15 September" is unverifiable from here. What is still in the repo is tools/Add-NoticeBoards.ps1, the one-shot installer that puts Missives in, complete with the release note "The notice boards work now. Missives puts real contracts on the boards" at line 24; that is dead and belongs with entry 168's Add-* scripts.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/board.json roadmap section s3rm row r6rm and todo section s7r2, the two statements of the feature. 2. tools/Add-NoticeBoards.ps1, so you know what Missives did and why it was not enough, and so you delete it rather than resurrect it. 3. tools/gamemode.js, mp.onActivate at line 1789 and the comment at 1644 which already treats a notice board as one of the activator kinds the server handles, so the activation path exists.

**How:** The activation half already exists: gamemode.js:1644 groups a notice board with shrines, levers, chains and nirnroots as things Skyrim runs and the server answers on the message the client sends either way, and Patch-Client.ps1 block 2 deliberately stopped blocking activators and furniture for that reason. So build the board as an activator: on activation, the server serves the board's contents and an interface to pin something up, priced in coin, held server-side per board. Then guards tearing them down is a staff or guard action against the same store, which lines up with entries 189 and 196. Delete tools/Add-NoticeBoards.ps1 in the same or an adjacent pull request.

**Done when:** Server-side and checkable with nobody playing: pin a notice to a board reference through the gamemode path, restart the server, read it back, and confirm the coin was taken and the notice survived. `ls tools/Add-NoticeBoards.ps1` should fail once the dead script is gone.

**Traps:** Do not solve this by putting a notice board mod back in the collection; that is what Missives was and it was removed, and any mod that does this through scripts will not work anyway because every Papyrus event except OnUpdate is blocked (entry 178). The front page currently advertises this, so it is a promise to customers, not an idea. gamemode.js is server code and the person runs the deploy. If the board needs new world records, that is a Thornswood-Content.esp change and therefore one complete release across server and every machine. "Guards can tear them down" depends on entries 189 and 196 existing, so write the store so a takedown is possible without depending on guards yet.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 196. Guards, bounties and cells run by people

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified. Roadmap section s10rm "Next", row r15rm: "Guards, bounties and cells run by people | Get caught and somebody has to decide what to do with you, rather than a script deciding it for them", done=false. Nothing exists: tools/gamemode.js has no bounty, arrest, jail or cell handling anywhere. What it does have is the raw material, a crimeFaction id for all nine holds in the DATA constant at line 23 (WhiterunHoldLocation 0x0267EA, HaafingarHoldLocation 0x029DB0 and so on), and a minimal staff facility, isAdmin() at line 3106 with a /tp command exposed only to admins at line 3047. So staff can move somebody and nothing else, which is why the entry says this needs entry 16.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js: the DATA constant near line 23 for the crimeFaction map, isAdmin at 3106 and the command handling around 2928-3047 for what staff can do today. 2. tools/docs-app/data/site/rules.md, the public rules page, because "staff decide" is a promise already made to customers there. 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json roadmap section s10rm and todo section s7r0, the two related cards.

**How:** Three rows have to land together and the entry is right that any one alone makes the world worse: entry 189 takes the vanilla guards out, entry 16 gives staff in-world powers, and this one makes law a thing people do. Sequence it as one coordinated piece: extend the existing admin command surface in gamemode.js (isAdmin at 3106 is the gate that already exists) with the actions law needs, hold a bounty per character per crime faction using the map already in DATA, and make a cell a place the server can move somebody to and hold them in, which /tp already proves is possible. Then, and only then, remove the vanilla guards.

**Done when:** Server-side and checkable with nobody playing: set a bounty on a character in one hold, restart the server, read it back, confirm it did not leak into another hold's faction, and confirm a staff action against it is recorded in the server log. That proves persistence and scoping, which is the part that cannot be judged by looking at it.

**Traps:** Doing any one of the three rows alone makes the world worse than it is now: that is the entry's own warning and it is the most important thing on this row. This is server code on the VPS, so the person runs every deploy. Most Papyrus events are blocked (entry 178), so vanilla crime handling cannot be leaned on; the server has to own it. isAdmin is currently keyed off a profile id and the comment at 2961 warns that the earlier version keyed it off something anybody who opened a socket could claim, so read that comment before extending the admin surface. One fix per pull request is the repo's rule, but this is three rows that must ship in one release, so plan the branches and the release separately: several small pull requests, one coordinated release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Faction , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page

## 197. The climb through the ranks

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The rank machinery is confirmed exactly as described: MANUALS at tools/gamemode.js:25 holds sixteen BOOK descriptors across four tracks (mining, smithing, woodcutter, alchemy) at tiers one to four; tierOf() is at :342-345, setSkill() at :334-340, and reading a manual is the only writer (:1630-1634). mineSalt refuses at :404 on mining tier below one and burnCharcoal at :424 on woodcutter tier below one. The roadmap row 'The climb through the ranks' is in the Next column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json and its own copy says 'vanilla levelling is off'. WRONG: 'the server already zeroes skill experience' is not supported anywhere I could find. Searching SkillExp, skillExp, AdvanceSkill, sendSkillExperience, fSkillUse and LevelUp returns zero hits in tools/gamemode.js, every file in tools/ts-plugins/, the shipped C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js, and all three ini files under C:\Thornswood\Profile\MyGames. What is actually true is weaker and second hand: classes.md:3 asserts levelling is off as published copy, and known.md:42 lists 'Actor values' among the things the server owns and overwrites. Nothing explicitly zeroes experience. STRENGTHENED: the 'inherited from the other server' claim is now proven rather than suspected. The crafting recipes in Thornswood-Content.esp carry editor IDs KzlRecipeCharcoalTier1 through Tier4 and KzlRecipeWeaponPickaxe/Axe, with the Kzl prefix intact. Verified too: four tracks against seven launcher professions and sixteen server trades, so most professions have no track, and nothing anywhere ties a track to a chosen profession or half.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** howTo: "in tools/gamemode.js, add a TRACK_OF_TRADE map beside MANUALS". #204 likewise: "Write mineOre... in tools/gamemode.js" and "add the vein bases to the table". Neither mentions that these are generated lines.  ->  tools/gamemode.js:23-26 (DATA, STATIONS, MANUALS, ITEMS) are one-line JSON blobs produced by tools/thornswood-src/build.py, which substitutes /*__DATA__*/, /*__STATIONS__*/, /*__MANUALS__*/, /*__ITEMS__*/ in tools/thornswood-src/gamemode_body.js:23-26 from stations.json, manuals.json, items.json and data.json. tools/thornswood-src/README.md:29-31 says "edit body.js or chargen_body.js, never gamemode_body.js". The pipeline is stale (one commit, 0571ad9; gamemode_body.js is 804 lines against gamemode.js's 3363), so hand-editing gamemode.js is what actually ships, but a stranger who finds that README will edit the wrong file, and a vein added only to gamemode.js:24 will silently disagree with tools/thornswood-src/stations.json forever. Neither write-up names tools/thornswood-src.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: "run `node -e \"require('./tools/gamemode.js')\"` is not viable because the file needs the mp host, so instead check the load banner logic by eye". #204's doneWhen is likewise only `node --check tools/gamemode.js` plus reading the banner.  ->  The repo ships a stubbed-mp harness: tools/thornswood-src/test.js, 349 lines, whose first line is "Drive the real gamemode.js against a stubbed server API", and tools/thornswood-src/README.md:24 calls it "82 assertions against a stubbed mp. Run it on every change." `node --check` proves only that the file parses. Two caveats a write-up should carry rather than omit: test.js:2 hardcodes PLUGIN = '/mnt/user-data/outputs/thornswood/gamemode.js', a Linux path that must be repointed, and it has not been touched since the single thornswood-src commit, so it may need work before it passes.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js lines 296 to 440, the whole skill-tracks section, because MANUALS, setSkill, tierOf, mineSalt and burnCharcoal are the entire existing system and they fit on two screens. 2. tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md, the published promise of what professions, halves and classes are meant to do, which is what the build has to match. 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, the Next column, for the roadmap wording that says the climb is unbuilt when in fact a partial one exists.

**How:** Do not extend the Kzl tracks. Re-key them. Step one, in tools/gamemode.js, add a TRACK_OF_TRADE map beside MANUALS that names a track per trade key from CHARGEN.classes (warrior, hunter, mage, smith, miner, woodworker, alchemist, bard, cook, enchanter, farmer, fisher, merchant, priest, scholar, thief), so a track is a property of who you chose to be rather than of a verb. Step two, change tierOf(actorId, track) callers to resolve the track from the person's stored trade rather than a literal string, and keep the literal form for the two existing stations so nothing breaks in the same commit. Step three, decide the source of tier: either keep manuals and make them obtainable (entry 218), or replace the manual signal with something the server can award, which is a single new function beside setSkill. Step four, write down which it is in classes.md so the published page and the code stop disagreeing. Before any of this, settle the design in #dev, because this is a roadmap feature and CLAUDE.md requires the problem, the approach and what it touches to be agreed between Patrick and Djinn first.

**Done when:** Without starting the game: run `node -e "require('./tools/gamemode.js')"` is not viable because the file needs the mp host, so instead check the load banner logic by eye at tools/gamemode.js:1858-1863, which already prints the manual and station counts. Add the track count to that banner and confirm `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. Then grep that every literal track string ('mining', 'smithing', 'woodcutter', 'alchemy') appears only in the new mapping and nowhere else: `grep -n "'mining'\|'smithing'\|'woodcutter'\|'alchemy'" tools/gamemode.js` should return only the MANUALS line and the new map. Finally, confirm classes.md and the board.json Next row describe the same system.

**Traps:** Entry 218 is a hard prerequisite: every tier gate currently refuses everybody because no manual can be obtained, so a track re-key on its own changes nothing visible. The editor IDs prove this code came from the other server, so anything you find that looks deliberate may simply be another server's decision that nobody here ever made. gamemode.js is one file shipped to the VPS, and CLAUDE.md requires launcher, game files and server to go out as one complete release, so a change here that the launcher must also know about cannot ship half. Do not touch tools/app/Creation.cs in the same branch without a line in #dev, and note CLAUDE.md's one-fix-per-pull-request rule: re-keying tracks, making manuals obtainable and adding professions are three reviews, not one.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Map , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 198. Dungeons worth going into

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The roadmap row exists where the entry says. tools/docs-app/data/board.json carries 'Dungeons worth going into' with the description 'Places built to be cleared by a group, with something at the end that was worth the walk' in the column named 'Next' (the Next column begins at board.json:1636). The entry cites no code, and there is none to check: nothing in tools/gamemode.js touches dungeons, encounters or group content. The dependency it names, entry 177 (NPCs that stand still when nobody is nearby), is corroborated by tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:162, which lists 'NPCs stand still when nobody is near them' under 'Do not report these'.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, the Next column, for the exact promise the public roadmap makes. 2. tools/docs-app/data/known.md 'What the server owns' at :38-42, because the server owning position, inventory, container contents and door state decides what a dungeon can even be here. 3. tools/gamemode.js:1789-1810, mp.onActivate, which is the only place the server currently answers a button press in the world, and therefore the shape any dungeon interaction has to take.

**How:** This is a design item with no code yet, so the first concrete step is scoping rather than building. Write the one-dungeon vertical slice: one cell, a fixed set of hostiles the server spawns and owns, one container at the end whose contents the server hands out, and a reset rule. Everything about it has to go through the two routes that already work: mp.onActivate in tools/gamemode.js for anything somebody presses E on, and the addItem/takeItem pair at :369-393 for anything handed over. Do not plan on vanilla dungeon scripts, levelled lists or quest stages, because known.md:32 records that SunHelm is the only mod whose scripts still run. Put the scope in #dev before a branch exists.

**Done when:** Nothing here is testable without the game until code exists. The checkable milestone is the design: a written slice in #dev naming the cell, the spawn source, the reward source and the reset rule, with each one pointing at an existing mechanism in tools/gamemode.js by line number. When code starts, `node --check tools/gamemode.js` plus a new count in the load banner at :1858-1863 is the first thing that proves the data loaded.

**Traps:** Entry 177 is a real blocker and the entry is right to say so: the tester notes already tell people not to report standing NPCs, so a dungeon built now would be a corridor of statues and testers have been told that is expected. Vanilla quest and dungeon scripts do not run, so anything built on them is dead on arrival. Adding a new plugin for dungeon cells moves the load order, which per known.md:54-58 makes every machine and the server mismatch at once, so it must ship as one complete release and batched with any other load order change (entries 52, 172, 190 are the other movers).

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page

## 199. Parties: travel as a group in game rather than a Discord call and a guess about who is where

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both halves check out. The roadmap row 'Parties' with the copy 'Travel as a group in game, not a Discord call and a guess about who is where' is in the Next column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json. The launcher Help page text the entry points at is tools/app/Help.cs:129-130: a HelpItem titled 'Playing together' / 'There is no party system' whose body reads 'the old mod had parties, a leader, and teleporting to each other. SkyMP does not, and this build's gamemode adds none of it.' That is the comparison to the old project entry 73 is about. tools/app/Keys.cs:332 also still lists a 'The party menu' key under Multiplayer, and tools/app/Shell.cs:2436 carries a comment about having moved off the old mod while still offering an F2 party menu, so there are two more stale party references in the launcher than the entry mentions.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "tools/app/Shell.cs:2436 carries a comment about having moved off the old mod while still offering an F2 party menu, so there are two more stale party references in the launcher than the entry mentions", and the howTo says to "check tools/app/Keys.cs:332 and the comment at tools/app/Shell.cs:2436 in the same" pass.  ->  tools/app/Shell.cs:2434-2438 is not a stale reference, it is the comment documenting the fix: "Read out of the real files rather than typed here. The old list was five hardcoded lines and it went stale the moment the build moved off the old mod: it was still offering an F2 party menu that no longer exists and a trade key belonging to a mod that had been removed. A row now appears only if its binding is really there." It explains why the quick-keys list at Shell.cs:2443-2447 is generated. Editing or deleting it removes the record of a fixed bug. Separately, CLAUDE.md forbids touching Shell.cs without a line in #dev first, and the write-up does not say so.

**Challenged (minor):** "a HelpItem titled 'Playing together' / 'There is no party system' whose body reads 'the old mod had parties, a leader, and teleporting to each other. SkyMP does not, and this build's gamemode adds none of it.'" The howTo then asks to "change the body to state plainly that everyone connected is in the same world and there is no invite or leader".  ->  That is half the body. tools/app/Help.cs:130 continues: "There is nobody to invite and no leader to hand over: everyone connected is simply in the same world. Meet up by walking, or by starting in the same hold." The howTo therefore prescribes writing text that is already written. The only real change on offer is deleting the first sentence.

**Challenged (minor):** doneWhen: "confirm `grep -rn \"OldMod\\|party menu\" tools/app/` returns only what you in[tended]".  ->  That grep returns 16 hits across 11 files: Account.cs:72, AutoJoin.cs:11, Checks.cs:537, Config.cs:14, :72, :197, Help.cs:27, Help.cs:130, Icons.cs:31, Icons.cs:205, Keys.cs:18, Keys.cs:332, Online.cs:11, Shell.cs:2436, Shell.cs:3524, Uninstall.cs:365. Almost all are load-bearing history comments that must not change. "Only what you intended" over a 16-hit result is not a test.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Help.cs:128-130, the 'Playing together' item, because it is the copy that has to change or come down whichever way this goes. 2. tools/app/Keys.cs:332, the second stale party reference, so a fix does not leave half the claim behind. 3. tools/gamemode.js:1960-1990, the online roster writer, because the entry's claim that the data already exists rests on it.

**How:** Two separate jobs, and only the small one is worth doing now. The small one: edit the three stale strings so they say what is true. In tools/app/Help.cs:129-130 change the body to state plainly that everyone connected is in the same world and there is no invite or leader, without the old mod comparison, and check tools/app/Keys.cs:332 and the comment at tools/app/Shell.cs:2436 in the same pass. The large one, an actual party system, is a build and should wait behind entry 71 (local chat and /me), which the entry correctly says does more for group play. If you do build it, the roster written by gamemode.js already knows who is online and where, so the server side is a grouping property plus a filtered version of the notify() route at gamemode.js:122.

**Done when:** For the copy fix, compile the launcher with the .NET Framework csc exactly as .github/workflows/build.yml does, and confirm `grep -rn "OldMod\|party menu" tools/app/` returns only what you intended to leave. The build check on the pull request is the real gate; red there means Ship would stop at the build step.

**Traps:** Launcher code is C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Help.cs is not on CLAUDE.md's do-not-touch-without-#dev list, but Shell.cs is, so if you tidy the comment at Shell.cs:2436 you need a line in #dev first because both machines touch that file. Keep it to one fix per pull request: the copy correction and a party system are two branches.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/whats-new/csharp-version-history

Skyrim has a coastline and nothing on it' in the Next column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json. The 'Skyrim has a coastline and nothing on it' phrase in the entry's 'Where it lives' column is quoted from that roadmap copy, not an independent observation. ## 201. Switch between servers from the launcher

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every piece checks out. The roadmap row 'Switch between servers from the launcher' with the copy 'One button for Thornswood, another RP server, or your own Skyrim, with nothing overwriting anything' is in the Later column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json. The machinery the entry says is mostly already there does exist: tools/New-GameFolder.ps1 (401 lines), tools/app/Keep.cs, tools/app/Hold.cs and tools/app/OwnGame.cs are all present, and the branch origin/djinn/own-game-folder exists on the remote. The parking of other servers' plugins is real and visible at tools/Ship-Rules.ps1:99-152, which classifies anything in Data\Platform\Plugins that is not thornswood-*.js or skymp5-client.js as another server's. The entry's claim that the settled 17 September design is the hard half is fair.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** doneWhen: "run the read-only checks `.\tools\Check-Manifest.ps1` and any Check-*.ps1 that apply".  ->  Entry 213 of this same list says Check-Manifest.ps1 currently reports a mismatch that is not there and ends by advising a redeploy of a server that is already correct, because it compares the server's filtered 60 full plugins against the client's unfiltered 101. Naming it as the verification step without naming entry 213 hands a stranger a check that is known to fail falsely, which is how somebody ends up redeploying a correct server.

**Read first:** 1. CLAUDE.md, the 'Settled design, not up for a quiet rewrite' section, because the own-game-folder decision, what is hard linked, what is copied and the rule that the two shapes must not be mixed in one release are all settled there and a stranger will otherwise reinvent them. 2. tools/New-GameFolder.ps1, the whole script, which is the built folder machinery this feature would be a second caller of. 3. tools/app/Keep.cs and tools/app/Hold.cs, for how file sharing between launchers is actually done today.

**How:** Read the open branch before writing anything: `git fetch && git log origin/djinn/own-game-folder --oneline` and `git diff main...origin/djinn/own-game-folder --stat`. The feature is a UI over what that branch builds, so it cannot start until that branch is merged. When it is, the shape is a per-server folder produced by New-GameFolder.ps1, a selector in tools/ui.xaml, and Keep/Hold pointed at whichever folder is selected. Do not write a wrapper script around the launcher and do not move anything by hand in Data\Platform: CLAUDE.md forbids both by name.

**Done when:** Before the branch lands, the checkable step is reading it. After it lands: run the read-only checks `.\tools\Check-Manifest.ps1` and any Check-*.ps1 that apply, and confirm the launcher compiles under the .NET Framework csc the way .github/workflows/build.yml does. A green build check on the pull request is the gate.

**Traps:** This is the one entry in this slice that is blocked on a branch rather than on code. CLAUDE.md is explicit that until djinn/own-game-folder is on main, what ships is the Steam copy with Vortex, and the two shapes must not be mixed in one release, so a half-merged state is worse than either. Keep.cs and Hold.cs are both on the do-not-edit-without-a-line-in-#dev list. Launcher code is C# 5 only. Anything that changes the settled design needs the problem, approach and blast radius posted in #dev and agreed between Patrick and Djinn first; a Claude does not settle it.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/ , https://cli.github.com/manual/

## 202. Bard instruments render badly and nobody has looked at why

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Both citations are exact. The roadmap row is in the Later column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json: title 'Bard instruments', copy 'They render badly. Nobody has looked at why'. The tester note is tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:162, under the heading 'Do not report these' at :160, and the sentence there is 'Bard instruments look wrong.' One thing the entry does not mention that makes it more urgent: instruments are already handed out at character creation. tools/gamemode.js:616, the CHARGEN.classes table, gives the bard trade three PlayFluteLuteDrum.esp items (1d9b, 287f, d62) plus 200 gold, the largest starting purse of any trade. So people are being given the broken objects on day one.

**Challenged (would-mislead):** "instruments are already handed out at character creation... So people are being given the broken objects on day one." This is the entry's whole argument for urgency and it is marked [verified].  ->  Nobody can pick bard. tools/app/Creation.cs:74-111 is the launcher's Professions array and it holds exactly seven trades: warrior, mage, alchemist, cook, smith, enchanter, tailor. The strings "bard" and "Bard" do not appear in tools/app/Creation.cs or tools/app/Help.cs at all. The bard kit at tools/gamemode.js:616 is real but unreachable, which is exactly what entry 222 of this same list says about nine trades. So no customer has ever been handed an instrument, and #202 contradicts #222.

**Challenged (minor):** "plus 200 gold, the largest starting purse of any trade".  ->  Merchant starts with 400 gold and smith with 100. In CHARGEN.classes at tools/gamemode.js:616 the merchant gear list ends ["f:Skyrim.esm",400]; bard's 200 is second. Thirteen of the sixteen trades get 75.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:155-162, the 'Do not report these' list, because whatever you find has to come off that list or stay on it deliberately. 2. tools/gamemode.js:616, the bard entry inside CHARGEN.classes, for the three PlayFluteLuteDrum.esp form ids that are actually handed out. 3. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt, to find which enabled mod owns the meshes, and tools/docs-app/data/known.md:26-36 for the list of mods that are known to do nothing useful here.

**How:** Half an hour of read-only work, no code. First, find the owner: `grep -in "flute\|lute\|drum\|instrument" C:/Thornswood/Profile/Default/plugins.txt` and then list what is actually on disk under C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\meshes for instrument nifs, checking whether the file is a hard link from Vortex's staging or a loose copy. Second, decide which of two causes it is: a missing or wrong-path mesh or texture, which is a file problem and fixable in a normal release, or a scripted instrument, which puts it in entry 178's bucket because scripts do not run here. The three form ids at gamemode.js:616 are the exact objects to look up. Write the answer into tests.md either way.

**Done when:** You can finish this without starting the game. Done when you can name the owning plugin and say which of the two causes it is, and tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:162 has been edited either to drop the instrument sentence (if it is now fixed) or to say what is actually wrong. If it turns out to be a texture or mesh path, the check is that the referenced path exists under C:\Thornswood\Game\Data.

**Traps:** Do not disable the owning plugin to test, and do not disable PlayFluteLuteDrum.esp: that moves the load order and produces exactly the mismatch described in entry 40, and per known.md:54 one machine out of step cannot connect at all. If the answer is 'a script owns it', the fix is not a fix, it is entry 178, because known.md:32 records SunHelm as the one mod whose scripts still run. Any mesh or texture change that comes from rebuilding an archive must go out as one complete release.

**Links:** https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page , https://github.com/ousnius/BodySlide-and-Outfit-Studio

## 203. Site themes: colours, fonts and weight a person sets once and the site remembers

**Category:** Website  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The row is in the Later column, which is right, but the title is not 'Site themes'. The Later column of tools/docs-app/data/board.json carries 'Make the site yours' with the copy 'Colours, fonts and weight you set once and the site remembers'. There is a separate string at board.json:1131 reading '**Site themes and a screenshot gallery**', which is a different item in a different part of that file and also bundles a screenshot gallery the entry does not mention. So there are arguably two rows here, not one, and whoever picks this up should decide which they are closing. Everything else in the entry stands: the site is tools/docs-app (node), served by tools/docs-app/server.js with the pages under tools/docs-app/public and tools/docs-app/data/site.

**Challenged (minor):** doneWhen: "start the site with `node tools/docs-app/server.js` on your own machine".  ->  tools/docs-app/server.js:38-39 binds 0.0.0.0 unless THORNSWOOD_DOCS_LOCAL=1, and the comment immediately above it at :35-37 says why that matters: "Otherwise port 3001 stays open as a plain HTTP way past the certificate, which makes the certificate decorative. Set THORNSWOOD_DOCS_LOCAL=1 then." The doneWhen omits the variable the file itself asks for. ROOT is __dirname (:40), so running it from the repo root does otherwise work.

**Challenged (minor):** howTo: "define the palette as CSS custom properties on :root in home.html... That is the whole feature and it is perhaps eighty lines."  ->  home.html is not the only page with its own root block. tools/docs-app/public/index.html:17 has a second `:root{` of its own, alongside tools/docs-app/public/home.html:33. A theme defined only in home.html leaves the other page unthemed, so the eighty-line estimate is for half the surface.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/public/home.html, which is the single page that holds the layout, the roadmap renderer at :668-694 and the navigation, so it is where a theme system would actually live. 2. tools/docs-app/server.js, for how pages and data are served, because entries 122 and 123 change exactly that. 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, to settle whether this closes one row or two.

**How:** Do not build it yet, and the entry is right about why. If and when it is built: define the palette as CSS custom properties on :root in home.html, give each theme a small block that redefines those properties, and remember the choice in localStorage wrapped in try/catch so a blocked-storage browser still renders. That is the whole feature and it is perhaps eighty lines. Build it after entries 122 and 123 have changed how the site is served, not before, or the selector and the serving layer get written twice.

**Done when:** Testable entirely locally with no game and no VPS: start the site with `node tools/docs-app/server.js` on your own machine, open it, switch theme, reload, and confirm the choice survives. Also confirm nothing was deployed: this is a read-only local run, and the actual publish is tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1, which the person runs, not a Claude.

**Traps:** Deploy-Docs.ps1 talks to the VPS, so a Claude never runs it; show the command and let Patrick run it. Entries 122 and 123 change the serving path, so ordering matters and doing this first means doing it twice, which is the entry's own argument. The site's design is already one of the strongest things the project has, so a theme system that lets someone pick a worse version of it is a real cost, not just a build cost. Nothing here moves the load order, so this is one of the few rows that does not need a coordinated client and server release.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/CSS/Using_CSS_custom_properties , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/API/Window/localStorage , https://nodejs.org/api/http.html

## 204. Mining an ore vein plays the animation and gives nothing, because ore veins were never wired up

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** One citation is wrong and it is the kind that wastes a stranger's afternoon. STATIONS is declared at tools/gamemode.js:24, not at around :360. Line 24 is a single generated line: const STATIONS = {"saltQuarry":"dde4ef:Thornswood-Content.esp","charcoalKiln":"1547e:Thornswood-Content.esp","beehive":"dbf8b3:Thornswood-Content.esp"}. The '~360' is probably the gathering-stations section header at :346-360, which is where the cooldown helpers live. Everything else is verified exactly: mp.onActivate at :1789-1810 dispatches saltQuarry, charcoalKiln, SHRINES and HARVESTS and then plainly returns, so a vanilla ore vein matches nothing and the handler returns before it builds a message. tierOf (:342), cooldownLeft (:356), stampCooldown (:363), addItem (:369) and the four mining manuals at tiers one to four all exist as claimed. EXTRA FINDING the entry misses: the beehive is in STATIONS but has no branch in mp.onActivate and is not in the published base list at :1837-1840, so it is dead in exactly the same way an ore vein is. Whoever writes mineOre should fix both in one pass.

**Challenged (would-waste-a-day):** The howTo for writing mineOre: "Write mineOre(actorId, refrId) directly beneath mineSalt... read tierOf, refuse below tier one, call cooldownLeft, roll a yield with randInt, hand it over with addItem, then stampCooldown." It never says to add the ore vein bases to the published base list.  ->  tools/gamemode.js:1826-1840 is publishServerBases, which writes server-bases.json from saltQuarry, charcoalKiln, SHRINES and HARVESTS only. The comment above it at :1812-1824 records exactly why it exists: thornswood-front.js re-activates every ACTI it sees, so before that list existed "onActivate below ran twice for every press on anything the server already handles: a quarry strike and then 'the seam needs 20 more seconds'". The skip is at tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-front.js:742-744. Adding an ore vein branch without adding its base to publishServerBases re-introduces that already-fixed double-fire, and the symptom is the same false cooldown message. Ironically #204 spotted the identical hole for the beehive and then left it out of its own instructions.

**Challenged (minor):** "tierOf (:342), cooldownLeft (:356), stampCooldown (:363), addItem (:369)" and "stampCooldown only after the yield landed (the comment at :351-353 says why the order matt[ers])".  ->  addItem is at tools/gamemode.js:367; :369 is `if (!baseId) return false;`. And :351-353 is `const COOLDOWN_MS = 20000;`, `const lastUse = new Map();` and a blank line. The comment about order is at :354-355: "Read only: a refused action must not start a cooldown, or walking up to a kiln without firewood locks you out." This is the same class of citation slip the entry opened by correcting in the STATIONS line.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:396-440, mineSalt and burnCharcoal, because mineOre is a copy of mineSalt with a different item and track and reading them first is faster than designing it. 2. tools/gamemode.js:1780-1855, mp.onActivate and the server-bases.json publisher immediately below it, because adding a base to the dispatch without adding it to the published list causes double activation, which the comment at :1811-1818 says happened before. 3. tools/docs-app/data/known.md:26-36, so you understand why the vanilla ore script cannot be relied on to hand anything over.

**How:** Write mineOre(actorId, refrId) directly beneath mineSalt in tools/gamemode.js, modelled on it line for line: read tierOf(actorId, 'mining'), refuse below tier one with a sentence naming the manual, call cooldownLeft and refuse while it is above zero, roll a yield with randInt, hand it over with addItem, then stampCooldown only after the yield landed (the comment at :351-353 says why the order matters). Add the ore item descriptors to the ITEMS object at :26 in the same shape as the existing entries, for example iron ore is 5ace4:Skyrim.esm. Add the vanilla vein base descriptors to STATIONS at :24, or better a separate ORE_VEINS map keyed by base descriptor so one branch covers every vein type. Add one else-if branch in mp.onActivate beside the saltQuarry line. Finally add every new base to the add() calls at :1837-1840 so server-bases.json lists them, and add the beehive there too. Run `node --check tools/gamemode.js`.

**Done when:** Without starting the game: `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes; the load banner at :1858-1863 is extended to print the ore vein count and you can read the expected number off the STATIONS or ORE_VEINS literal; and `grep -n "add(" tools/gamemode.js` shows every base that mp.onActivate answers for also appearing in the publisher block. A stronger check: confirm every descriptor you added resolves, because idOf returns 0 when the owning plugin is not loaded and the publisher at :1830-1834 deliberately drops those, so a typo shows up as a missing id rather than an error.

**Traps:** The entry's own instruction 'do not block the mines' is the important one: returning false from mp.onActivate blocks the default activation, which is correct for a station whose yield the server hands out, but wrong if you want the vanilla animation and handling to continue. Read the doc comment at :1780-1788 carefully, because only an explicit false counts as a block and falling off the end is the safe answer. Adding a base to the dispatch without adding it to server-bases.json reproduces the double-activation bug described at :1811-1818: a strike then 'the seam needs 20 more seconds' on a single press. This change alone will still refuse everybody until entry 218 is fixed, because mineOre gates on the mining manual and nobody can obtain one. gamemode.js ships to the VPS and per CLAUDE.md goes out with the launcher and game files as one complete release.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Mining , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format

## 205. Mining as a designed system: tiered seams, a wait period, and a smith's quest that pays for ore

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** unverifiable

**Correction:** The design decision itself is a Discord agreement from 17 September and cannot be checked from this repo, which is fine and the entry says so. What I can confirm is that every mechanism it says it builds on is genuinely present: cooldownLeft at tools/gamemode.js:356-361 and stampCooldown at :363-365 keep a per-person, per-reference cooldown in the lastUse Map keyed 'actorId:refrId', which is exactly the per-person-per-seam wait the entry describes; COOLDOWN_MS at :350 is 20000, so the current wait is twenty seconds, not a long one; and the four mining manuals at tiers one to four exist in MANUALS at :25. What does NOT exist is any quest machinery at all: 'quest' appears five times in tools/gamemode.js (:465, :469, :1739, :1740, :1742) and every one is a comment explaining why vanilla quests must not be relied on. So the smith's quest in this entry is not a small addition on top of something, it is the first quest the server would own, which is entry 206's largest-single-build warning arriving early.

**Challenged (minor):** "cooldownLeft at tools/gamemode.js:356-361 and stampCooldown at :363-365... COOLDOWN_MS at :350 is 20000".  ->  COOLDOWN_MS is at :351, not :350 (:350 is blank). cooldownLeft runs :356-360, closing brace at :360, not :361. stampCooldown at :363-365 is right, as is the value 20000 and the lastUse key 'actorId:refrId'.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:346-440, the cooldown helpers and the two working stations, which is the whole existing model this design extends. 2. tools/gamemode.js:460-475 and :1735-1745, the comments about why vanilla quests are not depended on, because they set the boundary the smith's quest has to live inside. 3. Entry 204's write-up and the mineOre work, since this design is meaningless until an ore vein yields anything.

**How:** Split it into the two halves the entry already implies and do them in order. Half one is entry 204's mineOre plus tiered seams, which needs no quest: give higher-tier vein bases their own entries and gate them on tierOf(actorId, 'mining'), reusing the exact pattern in mineSalt at :401-419. Half two, the smith's quest, is the thing to design carefully and is the same build as entry 206's 'one quest that works end to end'. Record the no-ownership decision where it will be found again: tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md or a new page under tools/docs-app/data/site, not only in Discord, which is what the entry asks for and is the one part that can be done today.

**Done when:** Half one: `node --check tools/gamemode.js` plus the load banner at :1858-1863 printing the seam count. The no-ownership decision is done when it is written in a file under tools/docs-app/data/site and that file is in the repo, which you can verify with `git diff --stat`. The quest half has no offline test until a quest system exists.

**Traps:** COOLDOWN_MS is a single global at :350 shared by every station, so making mining a wait rather than a grind by raising it also slows the salt quarry and the charcoal kiln. If you want a different wait per station type, that is a change to cooldownLeft's signature and both existing callers. The cooldown Map is in memory only, so it resets on every server restart and does not survive a deploy: a design that depends on a long wait needs persistence that does not exist yet. The smith's quest cannot use vanilla quest stages, dialogue or aliases, because those scripts do not run. This needs a #dev line before code, as a design item touching settled ground.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Mining , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Map

## 206. Custom NPCs and custom quests, and cutting the vanilla ones back rather than out

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The central factual claim is exactly right. 'quest' appears five times in tools/gamemode.js and not one of them is a quest system: :465 notes that Unbound's start quest goes with the synthetic save, :469 says nothing below depends on a quest surviving anything, and :1739-1742 explain that an ability handed out by a start-game-enabled quest is exactly the kind of thing not to rely on because the server cannot know whether that quest ever ran for someone. So there is no quest machinery, and the comments show that was a deliberate position rather than an oversight. The claim that this reshapes entry 177 is fair: tools/docs-app/data/tests.md:162 already tells testers not to report standing NPCs, and the roadmap Next column carries both 'Thinning the crowd' and 'NPCs with their own work' as separate rows, so the pure-cut framing is already the published one and this entry contradicts it on purpose. The entry's advice to start with entry 205's smith is the single most useful sentence in it.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:460-475, the comment block explaining why nothing depends on a vanilla quest, which tells a stranger the constraint before they design against it. 2. tools/gamemode.js:1780-1810, mp.onActivate, because it is the only server-owned interaction primitive that exists and every quest step has to be expressible through it or through chat. 3. tools/docs-app/data/board.json, the Next column rows 'Thinning the crowd' and 'NPCs with their own work', which are the public promises this entry changes.

**How:** Build one quest end to end and nothing else, as the entry says. The minimum server-owned quest is: a state per person stored the way chargen state already is (see CHARGEN_PROPERTY and chargenOf at tools/gamemode.js:842-855, and setChargen beside it), one activation that advances it through mp.onActivate, one inventory check through the existing inventory property reads at :803 and :860, and one reward through addItem at :369. Reuse notify() at :122 and say() at :140 to tell the person what happened, because those are the two routes already in production. Pick the smith from entry 205. Do not add a QUST record to Thornswood-Content.esp: vanilla quest scripts do not run, so the record would do nothing and the plugin change would move nothing useful.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and the new quest state property appears in the ensureProperties block at :304-318 alongside SKILL_PROPERTY and CHARGEN_PROPERTY. Add the quest count to the load banner at :1858-1863 so a deploy log line proves the data loaded without anybody entering the world.

**Traps:** The chargen property is created with isVisibleByOwner:false at gamemode.js:313, which is entry 223's finding: if you copy that block for quest state the client will not be able to see it either, and you will spend a day wondering why. mp.makeProperty is wrapped in try/catch at :304-318 and swallows a second call silently, so a property created with the wrong visibility on one load stays wrong until the server restarts. This is the largest build on the list, so the one-fix-per-pull-request rule in CLAUDE.md matters more here than anywhere: the quest primitive, the smith, and any NPC changes are separate branches. Anything that adds records to Thornswood-Content.esp moves the load order and ships as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page

## 207. An economy with a bottom: NPCs start with gold and only have what people have spent

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The premise is correct and I checked it rather than taking it on trust. The server does own inventory: tools/gamemode.js reads and writes the 'inventory' property directly at :370, :377, :384, :391, :803, :860, :880 and :1105, and known.md:38-42 lists 'Inventory contents' and 'Container contents and open state' among the things the server owns and that any mod change on a person's own machine is overwritten or ignored for. So a server-owned purse is genuinely possible with the primitives that exist. The dependency on entry 151 is well founded and worth stating precisely: addItem at :369-380 and takeItem at :382-394 create and destroy items with no ledger and no record of origin, so today a purse would be a number that anything could top up. The link to entry 200 is also right, since trade ships are downstream of this.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:369-394, addItem and takeItem, the two functions every coin and every good would have to pass through. 2. tools/docs-app/data/known.md:38-42, 'What the server owns', which is the authority on what a server-side economy can actually control. 3. tools/gamemode.js:616, the CHARGEN.classes table, because every trade already hands out a starting purse (75 gold for most, 100 for the smith, 200 for the bard, 400 for the merchant) and that is the money supply's current only source.

**How:** Order matters and the entry has it right. First close entry 151, because an economy with a floor is only as good as the refusal to let anybody create items. Then give each trader a purse: a per-reference number stored the way the skill property is stored (SKILL_PROPERTY at :302, ensureProperties at :304-318, getSkills at :320-333), and route every buy and sell through a single function that moves coin out of one side and into the other, never creating it. Specific goods per trader rather than a universal shop, as the entry says, which in practice is a table keyed by trader base descriptor in the same generated-constant style as STATIONS at :24 and ITEMS at :26. Note the starting purses at :616 are the only mint in the world today, so decide deliberately whether they stay.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes, the purse property appears in ensureProperties at :304-318, and the load banner at :1858-1863 prints the trader count so a deploy log proves the table loaded. A stronger offline check: grep that no code path outside the mint function calls addItem with gold (f:Skyrim.esm), which is the whole invariant in one line.

**Traps:** The lastUse cooldown Map at :351 shows the pattern to avoid: in-memory state that does not survive a restart or a deploy. A purse held the same way would reset to full on every server update, which is a money printer on a schedule. Store it on the actor property, not in a Map. The server owning inventory means a mod that hands out gold on a person's own machine simply loses the gold, which is already documented for Tavern Games at known.md:35, so expect confused reports rather than exploits. This is a large build touching the file that ships to the VPS, so it goes out as one complete release with launcher and game files, and it needs a #dev line first.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/Map

## 208. Carriages and ferries are paid for, and the fare goes to the Hold's court

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** unverifiable

**Correction:** This is a Discord design from 17 September with no code to check, and the entry does not claim otherwise. What I can say from the repo: there is nothing about carriages, ferries, fares or a treasury anywhere in tools/gamemode.js, so this is a build from zero. The stated dependency on entry 207's purse is real, and there is a second dependency the entry states but is worth making concrete: a per-Hold treasury needs somewhere to live, and the hold mapping already exists and is solid. tools/gamemode.js carries DATA.cellToHold, holdOfCell, nearestCapital, capitalPos and DATA.holdToDestination (used through :255-290 in the death and respawn routing), and the load banner at :1859 prints the mapped cell count. So 'which hold does this carriage belong to' is already answerable, which is more than half the plumbing.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:255-292, the hold resolution used for respawn, because holdOfCell and nearestCapital are exactly how you decide whose court a fare belongs to. 2. tools/gamemode.js:369-394, addItem and takeItem, since a fare is a coin movement and has to go through the same route as everything else. 3. lore/accord.md and tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md, because 'the Hold's court' is a lore object before it is a data structure and the two should agree on the day it ships.

**How:** Wait for entry 207. When the purse exists, this is small: one function that takes a fare with takeItem, adds it to a per-hold treasury number resolved with the existing holdOfCell or nearestCapital, and moves the person. The move itself can reuse the arrival machinery the chargen flow already has, where CHARGEN.towns at :616 carries a cell, a descriptor, a position and a rotation for all nine towns, so a carriage destination is the same shape of record. Store the treasury as a server-side value, not in a Map, for the same restart reason as entry 207.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and the load banner at :1858-1863 prints the carriage route count and the hold count, so a deploy log line shows the table loaded. The fare arithmetic can be unit-checked offline by extracting the fare function into something callable, since it is pure coin movement.

**Traps:** Nine towns exist in CHARGEN.towns, not ten, despite the comment at :625 saying ten, so do not size a carriage network off that comment. The hold mapping treats an interior cell's coordinates as local and deliberately measures from the hold capital instead (see the comment at :279-283); if you copy the routing code without that, distances for interior stops will be measured against the origin and pick whatever sits nearest zero. This depends on entry 207, which depends on entry 151, so it is third in a chain of three. It needs a #dev line before code.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Carriage

## 209. The Free Skyrim Trading Company replaces the East Empire Company

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The lore side is confirmed: lore/accord.md and lore/history.md exist, and tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md is the site's world page and also mentions the Accord, so the setting does place the Empire as gone. But the clash the entry describes is with vanilla Skyrim content, not with anything written in this repo: 'East Empire' appears nowhere in lore/ and nowhere under tools/docs-app/data/site/. So a stranger should not go looking for an East Empire Company reference to change here, because there is not one. The work is entirely additive: new lore in lore/, a paragraph in world.md, and eventually records and traders in game. The entry's own instruction that it hangs off entry 207 for anything it trades is correct, since there is no trading machinery at all today.

**Read first:** 1. lore/accord.md, the document that establishes what the Empire leaving actually means in this setting, because a replacement company has to be consistent with it. 2. tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md, the public page that people read before they join, which is the second of the three places the entry wants kept in step. 3. lore/history.md, for the timeline the fifty years sits on.

**How:** Do the writing first and the records later, because the writing is the deliverable and the records depend on entry 207. Add the Free Skyrim Trading Company to lore/ as its own file or a section of an existing one, say what it took over and from whom, and add the matching paragraph to tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md in the same commit so the two cannot drift. Only once traders have purses does anything need to exist in game, and at that point it is trader records with the company's name rather than a new system. The entry's rule that all three go in on the same day is the right one and the only thing that keeps this from becoming another Woodworker-style drift like entry 222.

**Done when:** Fully checkable offline: `grep -rin "Free Skyrim Trading" lore/ tools/docs-app/data/site/` returns hits in both places, and `git diff --stat` shows both files changed in the same commit. The site can be rendered locally with `node tools/docs-app/server.js` to confirm the world page reads correctly, with no deploy.

**Traps:** The deploy of the site is tools/Deploy-Docs.ps1, which talks to the VPS, so a Claude shows the command and Patrick runs it. Lore is the part of this project that is already good, so the bar for adding to it is the existing writing, not a plausible paragraph. Nothing here moves the load order until in-game records appear, which is the one reason this can ship ahead of the systems it names. Keep it to one pull request: the lore and the world page together are one fix, the in-game records are another.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:East_Empire_Company

## 210. The Thalmor never signed the Accord, so there is a war to have

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** unverifiable

**Correction:** A lore and design direction from Discord on 17 September, with nothing in code to check, and the entry presents it that way. The dependencies it names are real and checkable: entry 16 (staff able to act in the world) and entry 143 (somewhere to announce it) are both outside my slice but both are genuine prerequisites in the sense that nothing in tools/gamemode.js today gives any account in-world powers or broadcasts an event. The lore ground it stands on exists: lore/accord.md and lore/history.md are both present and the Accord is referenced on the site's world page. The entry's instruction to write the lore before the systems is the strongest thing in it and is the only part actionable today.

**Read first:** 1. lore/accord.md, because the whole premise is that the Thalmor never signed it, so the text of the Accord decides what that means. 2. lore/history.md, for the fifty-year timeline the war has to fit into. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md, the public version of the setting, which is what people read before they decide whether to join.

**How:** Write it, do not build it. Add the Thalmor position to lore/, as a document that states plainly what they did not sign and what that leaves open, and update tools/docs-app/data/site/world.md so the public setting matches on the same day. Staged war events are a systems build that needs entry 16 first, because an event with no staff able to act in the world is an announcement rather than an event. When that day comes, the announcement route already exists in shape: notify() at tools/gamemode.js:122 and say() at :140 are the two ways the server speaks to people, and say() posts over loopback to the chat socket at :148.

**Done when:** Checkable offline: the Thalmor document exists under lore/, the world page paragraph exists, and `git diff --stat` shows both in the same commit. Render the site locally with `node tools/docs-app/server.js` to read the result. No deploy from a Claude.

**Traps:** Do not build events before entry 16, because a staged event that nobody can run turns into a promise the server cannot keep, which is worse than no event. Deploy-Docs.ps1 is a VPS script and is the person's to run. If any of this eventually lands as in-game records in Thornswood-Content.esp, that moves the load order and has to be batched with the other movers and shipped as one complete release. Lore and systems are separate pull requests.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Lore:Thalmor , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:White-Gold_Concordat

## 211. Dull tinted hair, the colour of a bad dye job made with flowers

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** The premise holds and the instinct that this may already be possible is well founded. RaceMenu.esp and RaceMenuPlugin.esp are both enabled in C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt at lines 54 and 55, and RaceMenuMorphsCBBE.esp and RaceMenuMorphsHIMBO.esp are enabled at 56 and 13, so the tint sliders ship. One concrete thing the entry asks for and that I could check without the game: the server does store appearance, and Thornswood's own change detector is faceMark at tools/gamemode.js:830-840, which builds its signature from raceId, isFemale, weight, hairColor, skinColor, headTextureSetId, name, the joined headpartIds and, crucially, only (a.tints || []).length. It records how many tints there are, never their values. faceMark is only used to decide whether a face changed during character creation, so this is not itself the persistence bug, but it means Thornswood's own code is blind to a tint value change and anyone debugging 'my hair colour reverted' will chase it to the wrong place if they do not know that.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:820-841, the faceMark doc comment and function, because it explains what the server considers a changed face and shows tints are counted rather than compared. 2. tools/docs-app/data/known.md:38-42, 'What the server owns', to confirm appearance is server owned and therefore a tint has to survive a round trip through the server, not just through the game. 3. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt lines 13 and 54-56, to see exactly which RaceMenu components ship.

**How:** Two possible outcomes and the work differs completely, so find out which first. The cheap path: if the tint slider is reachable in the race menu and the result survives a login, this is documentation. Add a line to tools/docs-app/data/help.md or the launcher's Help page (tools/app/Help.cs, which already carries per-trade and playing-together items) saying where the hair tint slider is. The real path: if the tint is lost on login, that is an appearance persistence bug and is worth far more than the feature, exactly as the entry says. Start it at faceMark:830-840 and at whatever writes the appearance property back, and check whether tints are being round-tripped at all or only counted. A Claude cannot run the login test; Patrick or a tester has to.

**Done when:** The checkable half without the game: confirm the RaceMenu plugins are enabled (`grep -n RaceMenu C:/Thornswood/Profile/Default/plugins.txt`), and confirm whether tints are preserved by reading what is stored, not by playing. If the outcome is documentation, done when the help text exists and the launcher compiles under the .NET Framework csc. If the outcome is a bug, done when faceMark compares tint values rather than counting them and `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes.

**Traps:** Launcher help text is C# 5: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var. Do not change faceMark casually: its doc comment at :820-829 records a real bug it was written to fix, where a client uploaded the previous character's face on connect and the server skipped creation entirely, so making it more sensitive could reintroduce a different version of that. Do not disable or reorder any RaceMenu plugin to test, because that moves the load order and produces the mismatch in entry 40. A Claude never starts the launcher or the game, so the login half of this test belongs to a person.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Character_Creation

## 212. You cannot sprint and you cannot jump, while walking and running are fine

**Category:** Known issue  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every citation checks out exactly. known.md:42 is the line 'Position and cell. Health, magicka and stamina. Death and respawn, including where. Inventory contents. Equipment. Container contents and open state. Door open state. Harvested state of plants. Actor values.' known.md:32 is the SunHelm Survival row, whose text is 'Polls on a timer, the one script type still running. Writes health, stamina, magicka and carry weight against a server that crops and corrects them.' The three plugins are all enabled in C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt: SunHelmSurvival.esp at line 51, SunHelmDiseases.esp at line 64, and Embers XD - Patch - SunHelm Survival.esp at line 78, each with the leading asterisk that means enabled. The 'checked' claim holds too: searching stamina, carryweight, regen, sprint and jump across tools/gamemode.js returns one hit and it is a log-noise comment at :42, and across every file in tools/ts-plugins/ it returns nothing at all. The only other hit anywhere is a comment in tools/ts-ui/thornswood-bridge.js:410 about clearing a UI display. So Thornswood's own code genuinely never writes stamina.

**Read first:** 1. tools/docs-app/data/known.md:26-42, the whole 'mods that do nothing useful' table plus 'What the server owns', because those two sections together are the entire diagnosis. 2. C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt lines 51, 64 and 78, to see that SunHelm ships in three pieces and all three would have to go. 3. Entry 179's row, because if the diagnosis is right this and two other reports close together.

**How:** Run the entry's own test first, because it costs nothing and it decides everything: have a tester watch the stamina bar while trying to sprint. If the bar sits at zero or snaps back after a moment, the fight is between SunHelm's timer and the server correcting actor values, and the fix is entry 179 (replace SunHelm) rather than anything in this repo. A Claude cannot run that test and must not start the game; show it to Patrick. If the bar is full and sprint still does nothing, the diagnosis is wrong and the next place to look is the shipped skymp5-client.js, since that bundle is another server's build (entry 215) and could be suppressing movement input. Either way do not write a stamina handler in tools/gamemode.js, because adding a third writer to a value two things already fight over makes it worse.

**Done when:** The offline half is already done and is recorded above: no Thornswood code writes stamina. The deciding test is the stamina-bar observation, which a person runs. The repo-side done-when is that tools/docs-app/data/known.md says which of the two causes it is, and if it is SunHelm, that this entry, entry 179 and the other two reports are marked as one fix rather than three.

**Traps:** The entry's warning is the important one and it is correct: do not test by disabling SunHelmSurvival.esp on one machine. That moves the load order, and per known.md:54 one machine out of step gets LOAD ORDER ERROR and cannot connect, which is entry 40. SunHelm ships as three enabled plugins (51, 64, 78) and SunHelmDiseases depends on SunHelm, so removing it is a three-plugin load order change that must be batched with the other movers (entries 52, 172, 190) and shipped as one complete release across launcher, game files and server. Sprint and jump being the only two stamina-costing movement actions is what makes the diagnosis fit, so if a tester reports that walking or running is also affected, the diagnosis is wrong.

**Links:** https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim:Stamina , https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp

## 213. Check-Manifest reports a mismatch that is not there, and tells you to redeploy a server that is correct

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in full, including the arithmetic, which I measured rather than took on trust. tools/Check-Manifest.ps1:118-126 reads the client list from Profile\LocalAppData\plugins.txt, keeps every line beginning with an asterisk, and prepends the five Bethesda masters, with no light-plugin filtering anywhere in the file. The live list has 96 enabled plugins, so $mineFull is 101. I ran the same TES4 header test Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 uses (extension .esl, or header flag 0x200 at offset 8) across all 96 against C:\Thornswood\Game\Data and got 41 light and 55 full, so the server's filtered list is 55 plus the 5 masters, exactly 60, and the 41 extras are precisely the light plugins. The first enabled entry in plugins.txt is Expressive Facegen Morphs.esl, so client slot 6 is that file while server slot 6 is the next full plugin, Water for ENB.esm, which is the divergence the entry quotes. The summary that contradicts it is at :139 ('you have every plugin the server wants') and the extras list at :143. One nuance worth knowing: the redeploy advice at :170 is conditional, 'If you cannot [connect], the server manifest is stale and needs regenerating', not an unconditional instruction, though in practice it is the last thing on screen after a red divergence line and reads as the conclusion.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Check-Manifest.ps1:112-172, the comparison block, which is the whole bug in sixty lines. 2. tools/Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:14-31 and :118-171, the comment explaining why light plugins get no index and the code that drops them, because the fix is to do exactly what this already does. 3. tools/docs-app/data/known.md:52-58, 'The load order rule', for why position matters at all.

**How:** Copy the light test out of tools/Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 into tools/Check-Manifest.ps1 and apply it to the client list before comparing. Concretely, in the else block starting at :117, after building $mine, drop any entry whose extension is .esl or whose file at Join-Path (Join-Path $Client 'Game\Data') $name has bit 0x200 set in the UInt32 at offset 8 of its TES4 header, which is the same read Deploy-Gamemode.ps1 does at :120-122. Build $mineFull from the surviving full plugins plus the five masters. Then the counts at :128-129 report 60 against 60, the extras list at :142-151 is empty, and the position pass at :154-171 reports identical. While you are there, change the wording at :168-170 so it does not end on redeploy advice after a comparison that has just been made correct. Better still, factor the light test into a small shared function so the two scripts cannot drift apart, which is the same failure mode as entry 222.

**Done when:** Run `.\tools\Check-Manifest.ps1` (it is read-only and safe, and only reads over ssh) and confirm it prints 'server wants 60 plugins', 'you have 60 enabled', 'you have nothing the server does not list', and 'POSITION BY POSITION: identical.' You can verify the expected numbers before you touch the script by running the header test yourself over C:\Thornswood\Game\Data against the 96 enabled names, which gives 41 light and 55 full. Also run `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[void][ScriptBlock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw .\tools\Check-Manifest.ps1))"` to confirm it still parses, since the pull request build check parses every script.

**Traps:** The script reads Profile\LocalAppData\plugins.txt while the launcher's own verifier reads Paths.Live (see the long comment at tools/app/Verify.cs:57-71 explaining that under a Vortex collection those are different files and reading the wrong one produced phantom problems). Whichever you pick, know which shape of install you are looking at. This script talks to the VPS over ssh but only reads, so it is on CLAUDE.md's allowed list for a Claude; Deploy-* and Publish-* are not. Do not change Deploy-Gamemode.ps1's filtering to match the check: the filtering is correct and the comment at :14-31 explains why (Game.getModCount skips light plugins by construction, so the server's indices must match). Every pull request gets a build check that parses the scripts, so a syntax error stops Ship at the build step.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/ , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format

## 214. Nothing tells a person with a load order error what to do about it, and the tester notes do not mention it

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** One attribution is wrong. The Fix button is not in tools/app/Verify.cs: it is declared in tools/ui.xaml:736 as <Button x:Name="BtnFix" ... Content="Fix"/> and wired up in tools/app/Shell.cs:535 (btnFix = F<Button>("BtnFix")). Verify.cs is the scanner that produces the Issue list the Fix acts on, and it does mention Fix in its issue wording at :80, :111, :120 and :274. Everything else is verified. The caption really is painted by the client and then abandoned: the shipped C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js calls updateText with 'LOAD ORDER ERROR!\nCheck console for details.' inside the catch of its load order check and returns, with a second variant for the ignoreLoadOrderMismatch setting. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md never mentions the caption at all, and known.md covers it only at :52-58 under the heading 'The load order rule', where :54 says 'One machine out of step gets LOAD ORDER ERROR and cannot connect.' Verify.cs:274 already words this well for a different case and is the model to copy.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Verify.cs:262-280, especially the issue text at :274 which already says 'The game shows that as LOAD ORDER ERROR ... Fix fetches the server's copy', because that is the sentence style this entry wants everywhere. 2. tools/Patch-Client.ps1:1-96 and the four block definitions at :97-249, because the caption lives in a bundle that is not built here and this is the only supported way to change it. 3. tools/docs-app/data/tests.md, the whole file, to see where a stop condition would go and how the other tests are worded.

**How:** Split it in two, because only one half is cheap. The cheap half, do it now: add a stop condition to tools/docs-app/data/tests.md saying that if you see LOAD ORDER ERROR you must quit, press Fix in the launcher, and not file any report from that session, and make it prominent rather than buried. Expand tools/docs-app/data/known.md:52-58 with the same instruction, since today it explains the rule but never says what to do. The expensive half is the caption itself: the text is inside the shipped bundle, so changing it means adding a fifth block to tools/Patch-Client.ps1 in the same shape as the four at :97-249, each of which is a hashtable with a Name and a Marker string that the script checks for at :250 before applying. Write the replacement so the marker is a string that exists in the current bundle, and add the sentence the entry proposes: your mods are out of step with the server, quit and press Fix in the launcher.

**Done when:** For the tests.md and known.md half, entirely offline: `grep -n "LOAD ORDER" tools/docs-app/data/tests.md tools/docs-app/data/known.md` returns the new stop condition in both, and the site renders locally with `node tools/docs-app/server.js`. For the patch half: run the script's own already-applied detection path by checking that the new Marker string is found in the bundle after patching, which is what :250 does, and confirm the bundle still parses as JavaScript with `node --check` against a copy.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md is explicit that skymp5-client.js is not built here and the copy in the game folder is patched by Patch-Client.ps1 and shipped as is, so editing the bundle by hand is out. Entry 215 is directly relevant: the bundle you are patching is another server's build, so a marker you match today may vanish the moment the bundle is rebuilt from Thornswood's own source, and Patch-Client has no hash pin to notice. Entries 40 and 41 remove the cause of the caption, so this is a stopgap by design and should be written as one. Patch-Client.ps1 is not on the do-not-edit list but Publish-Update.ps1 is, and any change to what ships in the game folder goes out as one complete release.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/

## 215. The SkyMP client you ship to everybody is another server's build, and your own equipment fix is not in it

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is real and serious, and I confirmed it by reading the shipped file. One count is low. C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js contains five DAEDRIC BEGIN/END block pairs, named exactly as the entry says: facesettle (patch-facesettle.js), loadin (patch-loadin.js), eqsafe (patch-eqsafe.js), edge-rebuild gate (patch-edgerebuild.js) and rebuild queue (patch-rebuildqueue.js). But there is a SIXTH Daedric marker the entry misses, and it is the one that matters most here: 'DAEDRIC gear diff (patch-geardiff.js, 2026-09-06) v2: the delta, not the whole outfit; worn removals take the full path', sitting immediately above applyEquipment. So six Daedric patches, and one of them is about equipment, which sharpens rather than weakens the entry's point. Confirmed absent: the string 'THORNSWOOD PATCH' appears zero times in the shipped bundle, so the fix at C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\skymp5-client\src\sync\equipment.ts:82-116 (which I read, and which is exactly the getEquipment function carrying that comment and the e.count > 0 filter) is not in what ships. Confirmed: tools/Patch-Client.ps1 defines exactly four blocks, at :97, :128, :159 and :230, named 'offline login', 'locks, activators and furniture', 'locked refs are not announced' and 'difficulty', and none is about equipment.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\skymp5-client\src\sync\equipment.ts:82-116, the getEquipment function with the THORNSWOOD PATCH comment, which explains in its own words why a negative count kills the whole Equipment message and why the server then keeps handing back the starter kit. 2. tools/Patch-Client.ps1, all of it, for the block format and the already-applied check at :250. 3. CLAUDE.md, the settled design bullet stating the bundle is not built here, because that is the rule this entry proposes changing and it cannot be changed quietly.

**How:** This is a #dev conversation before it is a branch, because it changes a settled design bullet. The proposal the entry makes is right: build the bundle from C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\skymp5-client with the equipment.ts fix in it, record the upstream commit the build came from, and hash-pin the input so tools/Patch-Client.ps1 refuses a bundle it does not recognise.
