The hash pin is the small, safe part and can go in first even while the bundle is still the foreign one: add the expected SHA-256 of the pre-patch bundle as a constant in Patch-Client.ps1, compute Get-FileHash on the input before applying any block, and fail loudly on a mismatch. That alone turns the next silent substitution into a stopped Ship. The rebuild is the larger job and needs the upstream commit recorded somewhere in the repo so the provenance question this entry raises can never be unanswerable again.

**Done when:** For the hash pin: run tools/Patch-Client.ps1 in whatever dry or check mode it supports against the current bundle and confirm it reports the recorded hash matching, then against a deliberately altered copy in the scratchpad and confirm it refuses. `Get-FileHash 'C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js' -Algorithm SHA256` gives you the value to record. For the rebuild: `grep -c 'THORNSWOOD PATCH' <new bundle>` returns at least one, `grep -c 'DAEDRIC' <new bundle>` returns zero, and `node --check <new bundle>` passes.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md states plainly that skymp5-client.js is not built here and is shipped as patched. Building it here contradicts that bullet, so it needs the problem, the approach and what it touches posted in #dev and agreed between Patrick and Djinn before any code. Entry 216 is the same blind spot and should be fixed in the same conversation, because the foreign-file rule currently decides this bundle is ours purely because we patched it. Entry 217 is what the foreign bundle costs, so the three are one story told three ways. Replacing the bundle changes what every person runs, so it goes out as one complete release with the launcher and the server, never a half. Do not delete the Daedric blocks by hand from the current bundle as an interim: they are load-bearing for behaviour nobody here has characterised, and the eqsafe block in particular is what the gear-diff shortcut falls back through.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://webpack.js.org/ , https://nodejs.org/api/crypto.html

## 216. The rule that keeps other servers' files out decides this one is ours because we patched it

**Category:** Leftovers  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every citation is exact, and I checked the byte counts myself. tools/New-GameFolder.ps1:205 is the line testing whether $rel is Data\Platform\UI\index.html or Data\Platform\Plugins\skymp5-client.js, and :206 is the body: try { return ([IO.File]::ReadAllText($full) -match 'thornswood') } catch { return $true }. tools/Ship-Rules.ps1:150 is if ($leaf -eq 'skymp5-client.js') { return $false }, inside the Data\Platform\Plugins\*.js block at :148-152, so the bundle is declared not-foreign outright. The assumption is written at :103-104: 'Ours are thornswood-*.js and the patched skymp5-client.js; anything else in Plugins is theirs.' And the count is right: 'thornswood' appears exactly five times in the shipped bundle, and all five are ours. Four are the Marker strings Patch-Client.ps1 looks for ('Thornswood: a locked door or chest keeps Skyrim', 'Thornswood: a locked door or chest is Skyrim', 'Thornswood: the launcher picks the character before the', 'Thornswood: Expert, not Legendary'), and the fifth is the identifier thornswoodLock inside the locked-refs patch. Nothing in that file is ours except what we wrote into it, which is the entry's whole point.

**Read first:** 1. tools/New-GameFolder.ps1:196-208, the ownership test, because the grep-for-our-own-name line is the bug in one statement. 2. tools/Ship-Rules.ps1:99-152, the foreign-file rules with the assumption spelled out in the comment, since the same blind spot appears twice with two different consequences. 3. tools/Patch-Client.ps1:97-249, the four blocks whose Marker strings are the only reason the test passes at all.

**How:** Replace identity-by-grep with identity-by-hash, in both places, and do it as one change because they share the assumption. Add a recorded SHA-256 of the upstream build the bundle came from, stored in a file in the repo next to Patch-Client.ps1 so it is reviewable, and have both tools/New-GameFolder.ps1:205-206 and tools/Ship-Rules.ps1:150 ask whether the file's hash matches a recorded build rather than whether it contains our name. Get-FileHash -Algorithm SHA256 is the whole mechanism. Until the bundle is actually built from Thornswood's own source (entry 215), the honest intermediate is to record the hash of the bundle currently shipping, note in a comment that its provenance is unconfirmed and is entry 215's job, and make an unrecognised hash a loud failure rather than a silent pass. Update the comment at Ship-Rules.ps1:103-104 in the same commit, because a wrong comment outlives a wrong line.

**Done when:** Offline and fully checkable. Copy the shipped bundle to the scratchpad, change one byte, and confirm the ownership test now returns foreign or unrecognised rather than ours. Confirm the unmodified bundle is still accepted. Parse-check both scripts the way the pull request build does: `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[void][ScriptBlock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw .\tools\New-GameFolder.ps1))"` and the same for Ship-Rules.ps1. Do not run Publish-Update.ps1 or any Deploy script to test.

**Traps:** CLAUDE.md names Publish-Update.ps1 on the do-not-edit-without-a-line-in-#dev list, and Ship-Rules.ps1 is what decides what never leaves a machine in a release, so a mistake here either leaks another server's files into a pack or excludes our own. Both are worth a #dev line. The entry's comparison to Djinn's Steam copy showing a Thornswood menu is the same class of error and worth reading up on before changing anything. Getting the hash pin wrong in the strict direction stops Ship at the build step, which is the correct failure but will surprise whoever ships next, so say in #dev when it lands. This is one fix and entry 215's rebuild is another: do not combine them in one pull request.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/module/microsoft.powershell.utility/get-filehash , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/powershell/

## 217. You load in naked, get dressed twice, and one unknown item in your pack makes the server strip you for the rest of the session

**Category:** Gamemode  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in detail, with one location refinement rather than a contradiction. Confirmed in the shipped bundle: applyEquipment runs ac.removeAllItems(null, false, true); ac.unequipAll(); ac.removeAllItems(null, false, true); then setInventory, in that exact order, on the full path. Confirmed it is called at Utility.wait(1).then(applyPcInv) and Utility.wait(1.3).then(applyPcInv) adjacently, and again later inside once('update', ...) followed by Utility.wait(.3).then(applyPcInv), so three schedulings in two places. Confirmed the shortcut is gated on __on && ac.getFormID() !== 20, where 20 is 0x14, the player, and __on comes from rpNetTuning.gearDiff === true; the strings rpNetTuning and gearDiff appear nowhere in this repo, so Thornswood's gamemode never sets it. Confirmed 'equipment' appears zero times in tools/gamemode.js. Confirmed logs/server.log:72 reads 'ActionListener::OnUpdateEquipment ff00002a - rejected equipment update: inventory does not contain item 13790'. Confirmed the rejection at ActionListener.cpp:306-316 is the unknown-id check exactly as described. REFINEMENT: the unequip is not in that block. :306-316 sets isAllowed = false and breaks. The unequip happens downstream in the else branch: :421-443 diffs current server equipment against the rejected set and pushes every newly worn item into itemIdsToUnequip, and :448 is the loop that sends them. So the entry's behaviour claim is right but a stranger going to :306-316 will not find the unequip there. Also confirmed: tests.md TEST 4 'Armour stays on' at :45-56 has six steps and no relog, so it cannot catch the strip.

**Read first:** 1. C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\skymp5-client\src\sync\equipment.ts:82-116, because the fix is already written there and its comment explains the whole failure in plain words: sumInventories makes a count negative on purpose, the server's count is unsigned, one negative number kills the message, the server then keeps handing back the starter kit. 2. C:\Thornswood-Dev\skymp\skymp5-server\cpp\server_guest_lib\ActionListener.cpp:295-450, the whole OnUpdateEquipment, so you see the reject at :306-316, the slot-flag gear strip at :318-399 (which already carries a THORNSWOOD comment and an equipmentSlotCheckEnabled gate), and the unequip diff at :421-448. 3. tools/gamemode.js:369-394 and :800-880, the inventory reads and writes, because making the gamemode own equipment starts from there and 'equipment' currently appears nowhere in that file.

**How:** Three fixes in the order the entry gives, and the order is right. One, the upload filter: get equipment.ts:82-116 into the bundle that ships. Until entry 215 rebuilds the bundle, that means a new block in tools/Patch-Client.ps1 in the same shape as the four at :97-249, replacing the getEquipment body in the shipped file so entries with a count of zero or less never go over the wire. Two, make the gamemode own equipment: nothing in tools/gamemode.js stores it, so the server hands back an empty set and nothing dresses you. Add an equipment property alongside SKILL_PROPERTY and CHARGEN_PROPERTY in ensureProperties at :304-318, store what the server accepts, and hand it back on spawn. Three, revisit the slot check: ActionListener.cpp:329-332 already reads worldState->equipmentSlotCheckEnabled, and ScampServer.cpp:277-280 reads it from server-settings.json, so turning the gear strip off is a server setting rather than a code change, and the comment at :318-329 argues for it. Fix the test too: add a relog step to TEST 4 in tools/docs-app/data/tests.md.

**Done when:** The test fix is done offline and immediately: TEST 4 in tests.md gains a step 7 that logs out and back in and checks what is still worn. The upload filter is done when `grep -c 'count > 0' <patched bundle>` increases and `node --check <patched bundle>` passes against a scratchpad copy. The gamemode half is done when the new property appears in ensureProperties and `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. The slot check is done when server-settings.json on the VPS carries equipmentSlotCheckEnabled false, which is Patrick's change to make, not a Claude's.

**Traps:** The gear-diff shortcut in the shipped bundle is opt-in and excluded for the player by form id, so do not expect it to help: even setting rpNetTuning.gearDiff would not apply to the person's own character because of the ac.getFormID() !== 20 guard. TEST 4 as written cannot fail on this bug, so a green test is not evidence, and both re-apply schedulings sit behind an isBadMenuShown guard that skips while the inventory, favourites, magic, container or crafting menu is open, which is precisely when a tester is looking. server-settings.json on the VPS holds secrets and is named by location only, never printed. Everything on the VPS is Patrick's to run: a Claude shows the command. This is the entry with the widest blast radius in the slice, touching the shipped bundle, the gamemode and a server setting, so it is three pull requests and one coordinated release, not one change.

**Links:** https://github.com/skyrim-multiplayer/skymp , https://nodejs.org/api/buffer.html

## 218. The rank manuals cannot be obtained by anybody, so every tier gate in the game refuses everybody

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The conclusion is right and is in fact stronger than stated, but one count is wrong. I parsed C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\Thornswood-Content.esp record by record. Confirmed: zero LVLI records anywhere (the file holds 235 REFR, 101 CELL, 43 BOOK, 6 COBJ, 6 WRLD, 4 FURN, 4 MESG, 4 MISC, 2 KYWD, 1 ACTI, 1 CONT, 1 FLOR). Confirmed: 235 placed references, exactly as claimed, and none of them places a manual. Confirmed: all sixteen manual FormIDs are BOOK records in this file, at mod index 0x05. Confirmed the single container: CONT 05001248, editor ID OreIngots, full name 'Ore and Ingots', a NobleChest01 model with five CNTO entries, none of which is a manual. WRONG: three of the sixteen FormIDs do not appear exactly once. 0501548a, 0501548b and 0501548c, the woodcutter manuals at tiers two, three and four, each appear twice. The second occurrence in each case is inside a CTDA condition on a charcoal recipe: COBJ 05015486 (KzlRecipeCharcoalTier2), 05015487 (Tier3) and 05015488 (Tier4). I decoded one: function index 47, which is GetItemCount, comparison value 1, param1 the manual FormID. So those recipes require you to be holding the manual. That makes the finding worse, not better: not only can nobody reach a tier, the three tiered charcoal recipes can never appear on a workbench either. The tier-one charcoal recipe, COBJ 05015480, has no condition, so it is the one crafting entry in the file anybody can actually use.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:25 (the MANUALS constant, all sixteen descriptors on one line) and :296-345 (the skill tracks comment, setSkill and tierOf), because the comment at :298 says manuals are plain BOOK records with no script and reading one is only a signal, which is why placement is the entire problem. 2. tools/gamemode.js:401-440, mineSalt and burnCharcoal, to see the two refusals at :404 and :424 that this causes. 3. Thornswood-Content.esp itself in xEdit, filtered to BOOK and COBJ, because six records and sixteen books is a small enough file to understand in one sitting.

**How:** Three options and they are not equal. Cheapest and best first: place them. Add REFR records putting each manual somewhere findable, or add the manuals to merchant inventories, which in this plugin means either the existing CONT or new vendor container records. Second option: sell them, which is the same edit plus a price and fits the profession fiction better. Third and least: have the server hand them out, which needs no plugin change at all and is a few lines in tools/gamemode.js next to setSkill at :334-340, awarding tier one on first login for the track matching the chosen trade. That third option is worth serious consideration because it needs no load order movement and closes the problem this week. Whichever you choose, do it for all four tracks, not just mining, or the charcoal kiln stays dead. And decide separately what to do about the three conditioned charcoal recipes, since they will remain unavailable until a woodcutter can hold the manual.

**Done when:** Fully checkable without the game. After the plugin edit, re-run the record scan: every manual FormID that previously appeared once should now appear more than once, with the extra occurrences inside REFR or CNTO records rather than CTDA conditions. In xEdit, use 'Referenced By' on each of the sixteen BOOK records and confirm each has at least one placement or container reference. If you take the server-side option instead, `node --check tools/gamemode.js` plus a new count in the load banner at :1858-1863 is the check, and no plugin scan is needed.

**Traps:** The Kzl editor IDs on every COBJ (KzlRecipeCharcoalTier1 through Tier4, KzlRecipeWeaponPickaxe, KzlRecipeWeaponAxe) show this content came from the other server, so treat anything here as another server's decision until proven otherwise. Any change to Thornswood-Content.esp changes a plugin every machine must carry with the same bytes, so it moves the load order in the sense that matters: known.md:54 says the launcher compares every plugin's size and checksum with the server's list at startup and the game repeats it at login, so an edited esp on one machine and not another means nobody connects. That makes this one complete release across launcher, game files and server, and it should be batched with the other plugin changes rather than shipped alone. The server-side option avoids all of that, which is its main argument. This corrects an earlier claim that the rank system was built and working, so whoever picks this up should also correct wherever that claim was repeated.

**Links:** https://tes5edit.github.io/ , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format , https://ck.uesp.net/wiki/Main_Page

## 219. Choosing Tailor silently turns you into a Warrior in Whiterun twenty seconds later, and takes the town you picked with it

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The bug is real and I traced it end to end, but one line cite is off by three. WRONG: the defaults are at tools/gamemode.js:592-593, not :589-590. Line 592 is const DEFAULT_TRADE = 'warrior'; and 593 is const DEFAULT_TOWN = 'whiterun';. Lines 589-590 are blank and the first line of the comment above them. Everything else verified exactly. Creation.cs:108 is new Trade(new Option("tailor", "Tailor", and the Professions array runs :74-111. gamemode.js:710-716 is the rejection: applyLauncherChoice looks up CLASS_BY_KEY[choice.trade] and TOWN_BY_KEY[choice.town], and if either misses it logs 'launcher sent an unknown choice' and returns false. 'tailor' is genuinely absent from CHARGEN.classes at :616, whose sixteen keys are warrior, hunter, mage, smith, miner, woodworker, alchemist, bard, cook, enchanter, farmer, fisher, merchant, priest, scholar, thief. The fallback path is confirmed: the caller at :1357-1361 only claims the choice when applyLauncherChoice returns true, so a rejected tailor falls through to :1374-1392, waits CHOICE_WAIT_MS (:588, 20000, hence the twenty seconds), then applies warrior in Whiterun. The comment at :1368-1373 even says out loud 'after a wait they start as a warrior in Whiterun and the log says so loudly', which is exactly the behaviour, just never reaching the person. And the no-kit branch at :1192-1199 is verified: it logs 'no kit for', sets stage done, closes the race menu and continues, cleanly.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:700-730, applyLauncherChoice, because the all-or-nothing return at :712-716 is the bug in five lines. 2. tools/gamemode.js:1350-1395, the chargen tick that calls it and the twenty-second fallback, including the comment at :1368-1373 which explains the design intent that this case violates. 3. tools/app/Creation.cs:60-112, the Professions array and the comment above it at :64-71, which already admits Tailor and Gatherer have no kit and says so on the card.

**How:** Do the right fix, not the cheap one, because the right fix is barely larger. In tools/gamemode.js, split applyLauncherChoice at :709-716 so a missing town is still fatal but a missing trade is not: resolve townDesc, apply the town, the half and the class unconditionally, and let a missing classDesc fall through to the same handling the no-kit branch at :1192-1199 already does, which is to log it, mark the stage done and close the race menu. That way choosing Tailor puts you in the town you picked as the person you chose, with no starter kit, which is what Creation.cs:66-67 already tells people to expect. Then make it loud: the log line at :713-714 is the only signal today, so add a notify() call (the route at :122) telling the person their kit is not built yet. If you also want the cheap fix, adding a tailor entry with a minimal kit to CHARGEN.classes is a few form ids, but it does not stop the next profession from doing the same thing, which is entry 222's point.

**Done when:** Checkable offline. `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes. Then assert the invariant directly: every trade key offered by the launcher must exist in CHARGEN.classes. Extract the seven keys from tools/app/Creation.cs:74-111 (warrior, mage, alchemist, cook, smith, enchanter, tailor) and the sixteen from gamemode.js:616, and confirm the launcher set is a subset, or that the code path for a non-subset key no longer loses the town. Add that count to the load banner at :1858-1863 so a deploy log shows the mismatch rather than hiding it.

**Traps:** Do not fix this by deleting Tailor from the launcher: the site's classes.md documents Tailor with two named halves at :33, so removing it makes the published page wrong instead, which is the same drift in the other direction. The rejected choice is never claimed (claimChoice at :1360 only runs on success), so it stays in launcherChoices and will be retried, which is harmless today but matters if you change the claim logic. gamemode.js and Creation.cs are on opposite sides of the client and server line, so a fix that changes the wire keys has to ship as one complete release with the launcher. Launcher code is C# 5 only. Entry 222 is the structural version of this bug and fixing that closes this one by construction, so consider whether this branch should exist at all or whether 222 should.

**Links:** https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Statements/const , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/whats-new/csharp-version-history

## 220. The Play button cannot see a wrong load order, so it lights up and the game fails thirty seconds later

**Category:** Load order  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The Shell.cs citations are exact and the conclusion is defensible, but the Verify.cs characterisation is wrong and it is the load-bearing claim. WRONG: 'tools\app\Verify.cs:84-131 compares by name only' is not true. Verify.cs does run a position-by-position pass, at :122-130: when want.Count == have.Count && issues.Count == 0 it walks both lists index by index and raises 'The load order does not match the server's from X onwards.' as a Blocking issue. It also catches the specific index-shifting case at :117-121 via Checks.NotLight(), which reads the TES4 header flag 0x200 for every plugin in Checks.MustBeLight (currently just MCMHelper.esp) and explains at :114-116 that a plugin without its light flag shifts everything after it by one. So the check is considerably smarter than the entry says. What IS true, and is the real gap worth writing on the row: the reference is the shipped plugins.txt at Paths.Golden, which is Profile\Default (tools/app/Paths.cs:57), not the server's live manifest, so the check is only as current as the last build; and the positional pass is short-circuited by that && issues.Count == 0 guard, so any earlier issue at all suppresses it. Verified exactly: Shell.cs:1536 is btnPlay.IsEnabled = all && !playing && !updatePending && !collectionBarUp && !ownGameBusy; Shell.cs:3416 is the Log line 'Not launching. N problems with your mod list would desync everyone.'; and Checks.cs:430-431 builds the Mod list check by counting Blocking issues from Verify.Run().

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Verify.cs:44-135, the whole Run method including the comment at :46-51 stating the shipped plugins.txt is the reference and the long comment at :57-71 about Paths.Live versus Paths.LocalAD, which records a real bug where the wrong file was read and Fix rewrote a file nothing had touched. 2. tools/app/Checks.cs:145-200, MustBeLight, IsLight and NotLight, because the light-flag machinery already exists and the fix should extend it rather than duplicate it. 3. tools/app/Shell.cs:3400-3430, the refusal path, so you can see the wording is already written and only the check feeding it needs work.

**How:** Two small changes, in tools/app/Verify.cs. First, remove the suppression: move the positional pass at :122-130 out from behind issues.Count == 0 so it runs whenever the two lists are the same length, since a reordering plus an unrelated issue is exactly the case that reaches the game today. Second, and this is the part the entry is really after, compare the way the server does. Generalise Checks.IsLight (:156-171) from the MustBeLight allow-list into a helper that answers 'is this plugin light' for any name, then in Verify build the full-plugin list by dropping .esl files and ESL-flagged plugins before comparing positions, which is exactly what tools/Deploy-Gamemode.ps1:118-171 does server side. That turns the client's 101 into the server's 60 and makes the comparison mean something. Raise any mismatch as new Issue(..., true) so it is Blocking, and the existing gate at Shell.cs:1536 and refusal at :3416 do the rest with no further change.

**Done when:** Compile the launcher with the .NET Framework csc exactly as .github/workflows/build.yml does; green there is the gate. Then verify the numbers offline against this machine: the reference list at C:\Thornswood\Profile\Default\plugins.txt has 96 enabled plugins, of which 41 are light and 55 are full by the 0x200 header test, so the derived full list plus the five Bethesda masters must come to 60, matching what the server wants. A quick way to confirm your helper agrees with Deploy-Gamemode's: run the same header test in PowerShell over C:\Thornswood\Game\Data and compare counts.

**Traps:** Shell.cs, Checks.cs and Verify.cs are all three on CLAUDE.md's list of files not to edit without a line in #dev first, because both machines touch them. This is the single most important trap on this row. Launcher code is C# 5 only: no string interpolation, no ?., no nameof, no expression-bodied members, no out var, which rules out the obvious `out bool isLight` signature. Reading TES4 headers means opening every plugin in Data on every scan, so open with FileShare.ReadWrite as Checks.IsLight already does at :161, and note its deliberate choice at :166 and :171 to answer true (light, so not reported) for anything unreadable, which keeps a locked file from producing a false blocker. Making the check stricter will stop Play for people it previously let through, which is the intent, but say so in #dev before it ships so the first report is expected. Entries 40 and 41 remove the underlying cause, and entry 213 is the same missing filter in a PowerShell script, so the light-plugin logic now exists in three places and should exist in one.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/whats-new/csharp-version-history , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format , https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/api/system.io.filestream

## 221. The shipped female body is the fully nude mesh with genital shapes, and the build script puts it back every time it runs

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Verified in full, including the parts the entry only asserts. I read the nif: C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\meshes\actors\character\character assets\femalebody_1.nif contains exactly three shape names, 3BA, 3BA_Vagina and 3BA_Anus, and zero occurrences of Bra, Panty, NeverNude or Underwear. femalebody_0.nif is byte-identical in size and both are dated March 2021 with a hard link count of 5, consistent with Vortex deployment. tools/Add-Bodies.ps1:192-193 is exactly as quoted: 'nude body with RaceMenu sliders and no underwear, which is what was asked / for. Every NeverNude and Underwear option is left out on purpose', inside the CBBE PourArchive call at :191-205. The male precedent is confirmed: malebody_1.nif holds the shapes 'HIMBO - Boxers' and 'HIMBO - Body HP' and is dated 6 March 2026, so the covered build is already what ships for men. And the claim that everything needed is on disk is true: C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\CalienteTools\BodySlide\SliderSets contains 'SE 3BBB Amazing NeverNude.osp', whose sets are CBBE 3BBB Amazing NeverNude and CBBE 3BBB Amazing NeverNude UniBoob, whose OutputPath is meshes\actors\character\character assets and OutputFile is femalebody with GenWeights true, so it writes the same two filenames, and whose shapes are 3BA_Anus, 3BA_Vagina, Bra and Panty.

**Read first:** 1. tools/Add-Bodies.ps1:189-230, the bodies section, because :192-193 is the decision being reversed and :220-223 shows how HIMBO was done for men. 2. The slider set file C:\Thornswood\Game\Data\CalienteTools\BodySlide\SliderSets\SE 3BBB Amazing NeverNude.osp, which names the two sets to build and confirms the output filenames. 3. tools/Ship-Rules.ps1, to confirm meshes under Data are shipped normally and that nothing here is classified as another server's file.

**How:** One BodySlide build and one script edit. In BodySlide, select the outfit/body 'CBBE 3BBB Amazing NeverNude' (or the UniBoob variant, pick one and be consistent), choose the preset the current body uses, tick Build Morphs to keep RaceMenu sliders working, and build. It writes femalebody_0.nif and femalebody_1.nif to meshes\actors\character\character assets, the same two files that are there now. Take the first-person meshes from the same Underwear option in the same pass, as the entry says. Then edit tools/Add-Bodies.ps1: change the comment at :191-194 to say the covered option is now deliberate, and add the NeverNude folder to the CBBE PourArchive list at :195-205 so the next run does not put the nude build back. Ship it in a normal release, not as an optional pack, for the reason the entry gives: a pack is opt-in and anyone who skips it stays nude.

**Done when:** Fully checkable without the game. After the build, read the shape names out of the new femalebody_1.nif and confirm Bra and Panty are present: in PowerShell, read the file bytes, convert with [Text.Encoding]::ASCII.GetString, and match on Bra and Panty. Confirm 3BA_Vagina and 3BA_Anus are still there, because the NeverNude set keeps them and covers them rather than removing them, so their presence is expected and not a failed build. Confirm the file timestamps changed and the hard link count is what your install shape expects. Then confirm the script change with `powershell -NoProfile -Command "[void][ScriptBlock]::Create((Get-Content -Raw .\tools\Add-Bodies.ps1))"`.

**Traps:** The single biggest trap is the one the entry names: Add-Bodies.ps1 will silently undo this on its next run, so the mesh build and the script edit are one change and must not be split across releases. The NeverNude set still contains the 3BA_Vagina and 3BA_Anus shapes underneath the Bra and Panty; if the requirement is that those shapes are gone rather than covered, this is not the right slider set and somebody should say so before the build. No plugin changes, so the load order does not move, which is why this can ship in an ordinary release rather than being batched with entries 52, 172 and 190. The meshes under Data may be Vortex hard links, so writing over them touches the staging copy too, which is the same behaviour Checks.FlagLight relies on at tools/app/Checks.cs:187-190 and is expected, not a bug. Everyone gets identical meshes by design (Add-Bodies.ps1:28 says mismatched bodies is the one thing that actually breaks co-op), so this is all-or-nothing across every machine and goes out as one complete release.

**Links:** https://github.com/ousnius/BodySlide-and-Outfit-Studio , https://en.uesp.net/wiki/Skyrim_Mod:Mod_File_Format

## 222. Nine professions with real starting kits cannot be picked, including the Woodworker the public page documents

**Category:** World  |  **Fact check:** partly-wrong

**Correction:** The finding is real and important, but the headline number is wrong: it is ten, not nine. tools/app/Creation.cs:74-111 offers seven professions, verified by reading the array: warrior, mage, alchemist, cook, smith, enchanter, tailor. tools/gamemode.js:616 holds sixteen trades with kits, verified: warrior, hunter, mage, smith, miner, woodworker, alchemist, bard, cook, enchanter, farmer, fisher, merchant, priest, scholar, thief. Six overlap. So TEN server trades with real, funded starting kits cannot be picked: hunter, miner, woodworker, bard, farmer, fisher, merchant, priest, scholar and thief. The seventh launcher option, tailor, exists on no server side at all, which is entry 219. tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md:35 does document the Woodworker, as a profession with two named halves, Charburner and Woodwright, verified. The comment at gamemode.js:625 is verified word for word: 'Sixteen trades and ten towns, generated from the same table the plugin and the message box records came from, so the three cannot drift apart.' EXTRA: that comment has drifted twice, not once. CHARGEN.towns holds nine towns, not ten (whiterun, riften, solitude, windhelm, markarth, falkreath, morthal, dawnstar, winterhold), and Creation.cs:146-157 also lists nine. So the comment is wrong about both halves of its own claim.

**Read first:** 1. tools/app/Creation.cs:60-160, the Professions array, the Classes array and the Towns array together, because the doc comment at :64-71 already explains that the key sent to the server is the profession's and that Tailor and Gatherer have no kit, which is most of the context. 2. tools/gamemode.js:605-640, the CHARGEN constant and the comment at :625 claiming the three cannot drift, plus CLASS_BY_KEY and TOWN_BY_KEY at :631-640 which do the key lookup. 3. tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md, the published page, which is the third of the three things meant to be generated and is currently the most complete of them.

**How:** The small fix is one entry: add woodworker to the Professions array in tools/app/Creation.cs beside the existing seven, with two halves matching classes.md:35 (Charburner and Woodwright), since the kit and the key already exist server side at gamemode.js:616 under the key 'woodworker'. That costs nothing and closes the published-page contradiction. The real fix is the one the comment at :625 already promises and never delivered: pick one source of truth, generate the other two from it. The natural source is whatever already generates the CHARGEN constant, since gamemode.js:616 is plainly a generated single line and something must be producing it. Find that generator (look for a script under tools/ that writes the constant), and extend it to emit the C# Trade array for Creation.cs and the markdown table for classes.md at the same time. Then a profession cannot exist in two of the three, and entry 219 becomes impossible by construction. Fix the ten-towns comment at :625 in the same pass.

**Done when:** Fully checkable offline and worth automating. Write a check that extracts the trade keys from tools/app/Creation.cs:74-111 and from the CHARGEN.classes object at tools/gamemode.js:616 and asserts the two sets are equal, and add the profession names in tools/docs-app/data/site/classes.md as a third set. Today that check fails with ten server-only and one launcher-only; done when it passes. `node --check tools/gamemode.js` and a green launcher compile under the .NET Framework csc are the other two gates, and the pull request build check runs the latter.

**Traps:** Creation.cs:118-121 warns that the flat Trades property exists because two other places turn a saved trade key back into a label and renaming the array broke the build in exactly those two places, so do not tidy that name away. Adding a profession changes what the launcher sends and what the gamemode must accept, so launcher and server ship together as one complete release; a launcher offering a profession an older gamemode does not know produces exactly entry 219's silent warrior-in-Whiterun. Launcher code is C# 5 only. Creation.cs is not on the do-not-edit-without-#dev list, but if the generator turns out to live in a script that also writes Thornswood-Content.esp descriptors, that touches the plugin and therefore the load order. Keep the small fix and the generator as two pull requests, per the one-fix-per-pull-request rule.

**Links:** https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/dotnet/csharp/whats-new/csharp-version-history , https://nodejs.org/api/fs.html , https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/JavaScript/Reference/Global_Objects/JSON

## 223. A professions menu in game is a smaller build than it looks, and the client cannot currently see its own profession

**Category:** Launcher  |  **Fact check:** verified

**Correction:** Every claim checks out. tools/gamemode.js:312-317 is exactly the block described: mp.makeProperty(CHARGEN_PROPERTY, { isVisibleByOwner: false, isVisibleByNeighbors: false, updateOwner: '', updateNeighbor: '' }), inside ensureProperties at :304-318. Note the contrast three lines above: SKILL_PROPERTY at :306-311 is created with isVisibleByOwner: true, so the pattern for a visible property is already in the same function. The emote wheel is real and built: tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-emotes.js is 'Thornswood emote wheel, game side' with an OPEN_KEY at :127 and a PAGE_KEY at :128, and tools/ts-ui/thornswood-emotes.js is the renderer, so the ring and the click reporting both exist. The transport claims hold too: notify() at gamemode.js:122 is the reply-to-one-person route, say() at :140 posts over loopback to the chat socket at :148, and the loopback command queue is at :2788 and :3337. Worth adding for a stranger: the roadmap Later column still carries 'Emote wheel' with the copy 'Wanted since the start, never started', which is stale, since it is plainly built. The entry's ordering advice is sound and the dependencies it names, entries 197 and 178, are the right ones.

**Read first:** 1. tools/gamemode.js:296-345, ensureProperties and the two makeProperty calls, because the visible and invisible patterns sit four lines apart and the fix is a one-word difference between them. 2. tools/ts-plugins/thornswood-emotes.js, particularly :127-135 for the key binding and :200-235 for the open and close handling, since the professions menu is the same renderer with different content. 3. tools/ts-ui/thornswood-emotes.js and tools/ts-ui/thornswood-bridge.js, for how the UI side talks back, noting the bridge comment at :140-150 about CEF and the 400ms guard that is the trap underneath both the chat and the wheel.

**How:** Do the two-line proof first, exactly as the entry says, and stop there. In tools/gamemode.js:313, change isVisibleByOwner from false to true for CHARGEN_PROPERTY. Then, wherever login is handled, print the person's profession to them with notify() (the route at :122) so the round trip is proven end to end without any UI work at all. That is the whole first change. Only once entry 197 gives a rank worth showing and entry 178 gives crafting gates worth listing is there anything for a menu to display, and at that point the menu is the emote wheel's renderer pointed at different data, which is genuinely the easy half.

**Done when:** `node --check tools/gamemode.js` passes and the diff is the single word at :313 plus the notify call. The proof that the route works is the login message appearing for the person, which needs the game and therefore a tester, not a Claude. What you can confirm offline is that the property is now created with the same visibility as SKILL_PROPERTY at :307, which is the property the skill system already relies on reaching the client.

**Traps:** mp.makeProperty is wrapped in a try/catch at :304-318 that swallows the error when a property already exists, with the comment 'already made, or made by a previous load. harmless.' That means flipping the visibility flag may not take effect until the server actually restarts, and a hot reload will silently keep the old visibility. Budget for a restart and do not conclude the change failed. The chargen property carries character creation state, so making it visible to its owner exposes it to the client; check nothing in it is meant to be hidden before flipping the flag, and say so in #dev if anything is. Building the menu before entries 197 and 178 produces a screen showing two words and a blank, which is the entry's own warning and is worth taking literally. gamemode.js ships to the VPS and goes out with the launcher and game files as one complete release. Also worth doing in passing: the roadmap Later row saying the emote wheel was never started is wrong and should come down.
